# PART III: FOURTH THROUGH SIXTH WEEKS OF THE GREAT FAST (ST. JOHN CLIMACUS, GREAT CANON, AKATHIST, AND PALM WEEK)

## FOURTH WEEK OF THE GREAT FAST

## On Monday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast
*At Matins:*  
*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first stichology, we say the penitential Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, of the occurring Tone.*  
*After the second stichology, we say the present Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, of Lord Joseph. Podoben: "Having arisen" (Воскре́съ є҆сѝ):*[^713]  
Let us, the faithful, bow down in worship before the all-honorable Tree whereon the Creator of all was lifted up; it is set forth, sanctifying those who draw near in soul and body, cleansing the defilement of sin from those who fast faithfully, and who ever praise Christ, the only Benefactor.

*Glory... the same. Both now... Theotokion, Podoben:*  
The Word of the Father came down upon the earth, and an Angel of light cried unto the Theotokos: Rejoice, O blessed one, who alone hast preserved the chamber, having received the conception of the pre-eternal God and Lord, that as God He may save the human race from deception!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^714]  
Having now reached the midpoint of self-control, and having anticipated the veneration of Thy life-bearing Cross, O Christ, falling down before Thee we cry aloud: Great art Thou, O Lover of mankind, and great are Thy works, in that Thou hast shown forth Thy precious Cross, before which bowing down in worship with fear we cry out: Glory to Thy boundless tender-heartedness!

*Glory... the same. Both now... Theotokion, Podoben:*  
As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou hast blossomed forth the most fair Cluster, which pours out for us saving wine that gladdens the souls and bodies of all; therefore, calling thee blessed as the cause of good things, we ever cry out unto thee with the Angel: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*Canon of the Menaion, and the present triode, and the Ode is sung with verses.*  
*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos: "The charioteer of Pharaoh" (Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на):*[^715]  
The radiant veneration of the Cross has appeared unto all, sending forth the sun-like ray of salvation, and illumining all who are held fast in the darkness of passions: let us hasten to embrace it in purity!

*At Matins:*

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed ourselves by the grace of the Fast, with a pure mind unto the Only Pure One, come, let us cry aloud with a voice of thanksgiving: Thou, O Word, hast given Thy Blood for us all, and by the Cross Thou dost sanctify us.[^716]

*Troparion:*
Tripped up by the assaults of the serpent, I ever lie in an awful fall, O Savior; Who by Thy Passion and Cross didst right the fall of the first-fashioned man, right Thou me, and guide me unto the fulfillment of Thy desires.

*Theotokion:*
Standing before Thy Cross, O Lord, the Maiden who knew not wedlock, beholding Thy wounds, O Master, was wounded and said: Woe is me, O Child! Having escaped pangs at Thy Nativity, now am I painfully wounded.

*Another [Canon], by Lord Theodore, Tone 3. Heirmos:*[^717]
*Let us sing unto the Lord, Who has wrought marvelous wonders:*

*Troparion:*
This holy and radiant week sets forth the all-honorable Cross unto the world: come, let us bow down in worship, having enlightened our souls with fear and love, glorifying in hymns Christ Who was nailed upon it.

*Troparion:*
Before Thy Cross we all bow down in worship, O Christ, celebrating the foretaste of Thy Resurrection; we sing of Thy nails, and we honor Thy spear, with lips and eyes, being ourselves purified in soul and body.

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O Trinity sung in one Essence, O Father, Son, with the Holy Spirit, unoriginate, ever-existing, uncreated Godhead, Source of light and of life: unto Thee do all of us men bow down in worship, Who hast brought all things into being.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, O Virgin, pure Theotokos, the Cherubic chariot from whom God was born; for thou alone hast been the fountain of incorruption, pouring forth life unto all, from which we draw and receive healing.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
Having reached the midpoint of the forty-stage stadium of the Fast,[^718] come, let us go in love with Christ unto the divine Passion, that having been crucified together with Him, we may be partakers of His Resurrection.

*Heirmos:*
*Let us sing unto the Lord, Who has wrought marvelous wonders in the Red Sea; for with the deep He covered the adversaries, and saved Israel; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: The furnace sevenfold:*[^719]
*Troparion:*
Unto him that has acquired both soul and mind wounded by venomous passions, O Lord, have mercy; show compassion by Thy Cross, protect me by the drops poured forth from Thy ribs, that I may fervently sing: O ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Upon the mountain of old Moses prefigured Thee, having lifted up his hands and overcome Amalek, O all-honorable Cross;[^720] bowing down before which today, we overcome the alien hosts of demons, and in faith we sing: O ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Bowed down by passions, tripped up by enemies, dragged along by evil custom, show compassion, O Tenderhearted One, protect me by Thy Cross, that with an unswerving heart I may sing: O ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Painfully groaning, crying out with motherly anguish, and unable to endure the grief from within, thou didst gaze upon the Cross at the One born from thy womb, O Child, crying aloud: What sight is this? How art Thou suffering, Who art by nature impassible, wishing by all means to deliver the human race from corruption?

*Another [Canon], Heirmos: The angels unceasingly:*[^721]
*Troparion:*
Beholding the Cross of Christ set forth in church, come, brethren, let us bow down in worship having approached with fear and faith, crying aloud unto it: Thou hast blossomed forth life unto men, O light-bearer!

*Troparion:*
Triumphant in battles, heaven has shown thee unto us as the life-creating Cross: an unconquerable weapon unto kings, the overthrow of enemies, the horn of the churches,[^722] and the salvation of the faithful.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Hymn:*
I glorify the Holy Father, and I honor the Holy Son, and I sing of the Holy Spirit: the simple consubstantial Trinity, God in each of These, Lights and Light, proceeding as from a single sun.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, impassable gate! Rejoice, unburnt bush! Rejoice, golden jar! Rejoice, mountain unhewn; rejoice, O Theotokos, first hope and wall unto them that trust in thee!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
Having reached the midpoint of the Fast, let us take courage in spirit toward what is to come with youthful vigor,[^723] running well with God, O brethren, so that we may also with joy behold the Pascha, when Christ has arisen!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
*The angels unceasingly in the highest praise God; the heavens of heavens, earth, and mountains, and hills, and the abyss, and all the human race, with hymns bless Him as Creator and Deliverer, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Every ear trembled:*[^724]
*Troparion:*
Blessing the grandsons of old, Jacob brightly prefigured the honorable Cross;[^725] by which do Thou bless us, O Merciful One, granting grace that bestows sanctification unto them that bow down before it, and that glorify Thee, the Deliverer of all.

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed ourselves in soul and mind with the water of the Fast, O faithful, let us embrace the life-giving and divine Wood; for it is manifestly set forth before all, pouring forth divine forgiveness, and heavenly light, and life, and true rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Knowing Thee as the Judge awesome unto all, coming in the glory of the Godhead, wishing then to reprove the hidden things of all, O Lord, therefore do I cry unto Thee, O God: I have sinned, forgive, reprove not my many and grievous offenses.

*Theotokion:*
Beholding of old the Lamb nailed upon the Cross, and the brightness of the sun being darkened, the Ewe-lamb weeping, said: O Sun of glory, Jesus, Thou hast set, being put to death; but make the light of the Resurrection shine forth unto them that love Thee!

*Another [Canon], Heirmos: On Mount Sinai:*[^726]
*Troparion:*
At the place where the feet of Thee, the Master and God, stood,[^727] let us all bow down in worship today before the all-honorable Cross, as David of divinely eloquent utterance cries aloud; for He Who holds all things in His fist was lifted up upon it.

*Troparion:*
Ye that dwell on earth, approach, pure through the Fast, and beholding the all-honorable Cross set forth today, bow down in worship with fear and faith; rejoice and be exceedingly glad, drawing sanctification for your souls.

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
The transcendent Trinity is in essence an indivisible Unity, joined by nature, properly divided into Persons; for indivisibly It is divided, and being One It is threefold: This is the Father, the Son, and the living Spirit, Who preserves all things.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Who has ever heard of a childbearing Virgin, and a Mother without a husband? O Mary, thou dost work a wonder! But tell me, how? Search not into the depth of my divine childbearing; this is entirely true, surpassing the comprehension of the human mind.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
Let all of us people offer grateful praise unto God, for beholding now the all-honorable Cross, we embrace it; understand, therefore, and submit yourselves at this, O demons! Ye barbarian nations, understand and submit yourselves, for God is with us![^728]

*Heirmos:*
*On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush, having conceived without burning the Fire of the Godhead in thy womb; and Daniel saw thee as the mountain unhewn; and Isaiah cried aloud: A rod that budded from the root of David.*

*Photagogicon of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, twice, Tone 6:*[^729]
Thou hast shown humility to be the preeminent way of exaltation, O Christ, having emptied Thyself and taken the form of a servant; Thou didst not give ear unto the vainglorious prayer of the Pharisee, but didst receive the contrite groaning of the Publican as an unblemished sacrifice in the highest; therefore do I also cry aloud unto Thee: Cleanse, O God, cleanse, O my Savior, and save me!

*Martyrikon:*
O Lord, in the memory of Thy saints all creation keeps festival; the heavens rejoice with the angels, and the earth makes merry with men; by their intercessions, have mercy upon us.

*Glory, both now, in the same tone:*
Thou didst receive the Archangel's word, and didst show thyself to be the Cherubic throne, and in thine arms thou didst bear, O Theotokos, the Hope of our souls.

*At the First Hour, we make veneration before the precious Cross, just as on Sunday, according to the prescribed pattern, singing instead of "Order my steps": "Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master." And at the Hours, after the "Our Father", the Kontakion: "No longer does the flaming sword."*[^730]

*Likewise also on Wednesday and Friday, these four days; and on Friday we take away the Cross, as is set down there.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 7:*[^731]
O Lord, reprove not the people that have sinned in Thine indignation, nor chasten us in Thy wrath, since Thou art good; for all the earth glorifies Thee, and we pray Thee: Spare us, O Holy One!

*Prokimenon, Psalm 61, Tone 6:*[^732]
In God is my salvation and my glory.  
*Verse:* Shall not my soul be subjected unto God?

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (14:24–32):*[^733]  
Thus says the Lord Sabaoth: As I have said, so will it be, and as I have purposed, so will it stand: [25] to destroy the Assyrians upon My land, and upon My mountains; and they will be for a trampling down, and their yoke will be taken away from them, and their glory will be taken away from their shoulders. [26] This is the purpose which the Lord has purposed upon the whole earth, and this is the hand stretched out over all the nations of the earth. [27] For what God the Holy One has purposed, who will bring to naught? And His high hand, who will turn it aside? [28] In the year that King Ahaz died, this word came to pass: [29] Rejoice not, all ye of alien race, because the yoke of him that smote you is broken; for out of the serpent's seed will come forth the offspring of asps, and from their offspring will come forth flying serpents. [30] And the poor will feed by Him, and needy men will rest in peace; and He will destroy thy seed with famine, and will kill thy remnant. [31] Howl, ye gates of cities! Let the cities cry out in confusion, all ye of alien race! For smoke is coming from the north, and there is none that will stand. [32] And what will the kings of the nations answer? That the Lord has founded Zion, and through Him the humble among His people will be saved.

*Prokimenon, Psalm 62, Tone 4:*[^734]
Thus will I bless Thee in my life.  
*Verse:* O God, my God, unto Thee do I rise early: my soul has thirsted for Thee.

## On Monday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried", Prosomoia Stichera:*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что́ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*[^735]
Passing today through the middle days of self-control by the power of the Cross, we glorify Christ our Savior and God Who was lifted up upon it in the midst of the earth, crying aloud: Show us to be beholders of Thy Passions, O Master, and of Thy venerable Resurrection, granting us cleansing and great mercy!

*Podoben:*
Let us humble the passions of the flesh by laying aside food and turning away from pleasures, and let us faithfully embrace the wood of the Cross; for it is seen manifestly set forth for veneration, and it sanctifies all things with divine grace; and let us cry aloud unto the Lord: We thank Thee, O Tenderhearted One, Who by this dost save our souls!

*Another, in the same tone, to the Theotokos:*
Now bowing down in worship before the Cross, let us all cry aloud: Rejoice, Tree of Life! Rejoice, holy scepter of Christ! Rejoice, eternal glory of men! Rejoice, boast of kings! Rejoice, stronghold of the faith! Rejoice, unconquerable weapon! Rejoice, driving away of enemies! Rejoice, radiant illumination saving the world! Rejoice, great glory of martyrs! Rejoice, strength of the righteous! Rejoice, splendor of angels! Rejoice, all-honorable!

*And 6 Stichera of the Menaion.*  
*Glory, both now, Theotokion.*  
*Prokimenon, Psalm 63, Tone 6:*[^736]
Hear my voice, O God, when I pray unto Thee.  
*Verse:* Deliver my soul from fear of the enemy.

*Reading from Genesis (8:21–9:7):*[^737]  
The Lord God said: Having considered, I will not again curse the earth for the deeds of men; for the imagination of man is set intently upon evils from his youth; therefore I will not again smite every living flesh as I have done. During all the days of the earth, seedtime and harvest, cold and heat, summer and spring, day and night [22] will not cease. [Chapter 9:1] And God blessed Noah and his sons, and said unto them: Increase and multiply, and fill the earth, and have dominion over it. [2] And the fear and dread of you will be upon all the beasts of the earth, (and upon all the cattle of the earth,) upon all the birds of heaven, and upon all that move upon the earth, and upon all the fish of the sea: into your hands have I delivered them. [3] And every moving thing that is alive will be food for you: as the green herb have I given you all things. [4] Only flesh with the blood of its life shall ye not eat. [5] For surely your blood of your lives will I require from the hand of every beast; and from the hand of man his brother will I require it. [6] Whoso sheds man's blood, in its place will his own be shed; for in the image of God made I man. [7] But as for you, increase and multiply, and fill the earth, and multiply upon it.

*Prokimenon, Psalm 64, Tone 6:*[^738]
Hear us, O God our Savior.  
*Verse:* Unto Thee is praise due, O God, in Zion.

*Reading from Proverbs (11:19–12:6):*[^739]  
A righteous son is born unto life, but the pursuit of the ungodly leads to death. [20] Perverse ways are an abomination unto the Lord, but acceptable unto Him are all they that are blameless in their ways. [21] He that unrighteously strikes hands will not be unpunished of evil; but he that sows righteousness receives a sure reward. [22] As a golden ring in a swine's snout, so is beauty to a woman without discretion. [23] The desire of the righteous is entirely good, but the hope of the ungodly perishes. [24] There are some who by scattering their own make it greater, and there are some who gathering what is not theirs are diminished. [25] Every sincere soul is blessed, but an angry man is uncomely. [26] He that hoards grain leaves it to the nations, and he that sells grain sparingly is cursed by the people; but the blessing of the Lord is upon the head of him that gives. [27] He that devises good things seeks good favor; but evils will overtake him that seeks them. [28] He that trusts in his riches, he will fall; but he that takes up the cause of the righteous, he will shine forth. [29] He that heeds not his own house will inherit the wind, and the fool will be servant to the wise. [30] From the fruit of righteousness grows the tree of life, but the souls of the lawless are taken away before their time. [31] If the righteous scarcely be saved, where will the ungodly and the sinner appear? [Chapter 12:1] He that loves instruction loves discernment, but he that hates reproof is foolish. [2] Better is he that has found favor from the Lord God, but a man who is a transgressor will be passed over in silence. [3] A man will not be established by wickedness, but the roots of the righteous will not be removed. [4] A virtuous woman is a crown unto her husband, but as a worm in wood, so does an evil woman destroy a man. [5] The thoughts of the righteous are judgments, but the ungodly manage deceit. [6] The words of the ungodly lie in wait for blood, but the mouth of the upright will deliver them.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 8, twice:*[^740]
The vainglorious Pharisee became abominable by boasting, and the humble-minded Publican was justified by praying in silence; therefore, my soul, seeing the difference between both, embrace and kiss humble-mindedness; for Christ has promised to give grace unto the humble, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*Martyrikon:*
O Martyrs of the Lord, pray unto our God, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and the cleansing of many offenses, we pray you!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, in the same tone:*
I flee unto thy protection, O holy Virgin Theotokos; I know that through thee I will obtain salvation, for thou art able, O Pure One, to help me.

## On Tuesday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins

*Triadica.*  
*After the first stichology, Penitential Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, we say the present Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "An unshakable foundation" (Непоколеби́мое ᲂу҆твержде́нїе):*[^741]
Having enlightened our souls by self-control, let us bow down in worship before the saving Cross upon which Christ was nailed, and let us cry aloud unto Him: Rejoice, sweetness of them that fast, and sure helper; rejoice, destroyer of passions, fellow-warrior of the faithful; rejoice, blessed Wood!

*Theotokion, Podoben:*
As an unshakable foundation of the faith and an honorable gift unto our souls, we faithful magnify the Theotokos in hymns: Rejoice, thou who in thy womb didst contain the Rock of Life; rejoice, hope of the ends of the earth, helper of the afflicted; rejoice, Bride unwedded!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, in the same tone.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "As the first fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*[^742]
As the life of creation, the inhabited earth kisses Thy Cross set forth, O Lord, and bowing down in worship it cries unto Thee: By its operation, keep in profound peace them that sing of Thee and that praise Thee in self-control, O Many-Merciful One!

*Theotokion, in the same tone:*
Rejoice, thou who by the Angel didst receive the joy of the world! Rejoice, thou who didst bear thy Creator and Lord! Rejoice, thou who wast accounted worthy to be the Mother of God!

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present Triode by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical ode is sung.*  
*Ode 2, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^743]
*See, see that I am God, Who of old led Israel through the Red Sea, and fed, and saved him, and delivered him from the bitter bondage of Pharaoh.*

*Troparion:*
In the midst of creation Thou didst endure the Cross, O Messiah, Word of God; before which today at the midpoint of the Fast we faithfully bow down in worship, asking to behold also Thy Resurrection.

*Troparion:*
Dividing the sea of passions with the rod of power prefigured by Moses,[^744] let us reach the promised land, and be richly filled with spiritual manna.

*Troparion:*
Who will not weep aloud for thee? Who will not mourn for thee, O my soul, that desirest evil things and seekest not good things with love, ever despising the righteous Judge Who is longsuffering toward thee?

*Theotokion:*
Having become flesh without change, O Virgin, thou didst bear Christ; Whom do thou fervently pray to transform from fleshly passions them that bow down before the Cross which He endured in the flesh.

*Another [Canon], in the same tone. Heirmos: See:*[^745]
*Troparion:*
See, see that I am God, Who grant unto the world My Cross as a weapon of salvation, that men may now bow down before it, so that they who kiss it may render powerless the wiles of the enemy.

*Troparion:*
Great art Thou, O Lord, Many-Merciful, for Thou hast granted us now to bow down before Thy life-creating Cross, where Thy hands and feet were nailed, and upon which the Blood was poured forth from Thy most pure side, welling forth life unto us.

*Trinity Hymn:*
All-perfect Unity, transcendent in Godhead, in three Hypostases, unbegotten Father, and only-begotten Son, Spirit proceeding from the Father and made manifest through the Son: one Essence and Nature, Lordship, Kingdom, save us all!

*Theotokion:*
Unutterable is the wonder of thy Nativity, O Virgin Mother! For how dost thou both give birth, and yet remain pure therein? How dost thou bear an Infant, and knowest not a man at all? As the Word of God, newly born from me, knows above nature.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*
Moses the great prefigured Thee unto us, O Cross, having stretched out his hands and overcome Amalek;[^746] depicting thee in shadows and in the air, we turn away and conquer the malicious enemy, singing praise unto Christ.

*Heirmos:*
*See, see that I am your God, born before all ages from the Father, and in these last days conceived without a man from the Virgin, and having loosed the sin of forefather Adam, as the Lover of mankind.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: He Who covers with waters:*[^747]
*Troparion:*
O God Who didst establish the foundations of all the earth, when lifted up upon the Wood, Thou didst grant unto all the faithful the immovable foundation, O Lord: the saving weapon of Thy Cross; before which we bow down in worship, and we supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the wood of the Cross shining more brightly than the rays of the sun, come, let us who are illumined by the Fast now embrace it, drawing light-bearing grace that drives away darkness from them that supremely exalt Christ unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou art the living and unchangeable Water unto eternal life, O Word; Thou, O Master, didst pour forth blood and water from Thy side upon the Cross,[^748] drying up the torrents of sin; therefore do I pray Thee: Dry up the wicked flowing of my passions!

*Theotokion:*
Thou, O holy pure Mother of God, didst give birth unto God Who rests in the saints as an intelligible overshadowing, Who sanctifies all the ends of the earth today, O Virgin, by divine veneration of the all-holy Wood upon which He was nailed in the flesh.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos: Angels and the heavens:*[^749]
*Troparion:*
Come, ye people, let us behold the life-bearing Wood now set forth, upon which Christ our God was affixed in the flesh; and singing with fear, let us bow down before it unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
We praise the three-part Cross as an image of the Trinity,[^750] and bowing down in worship with fear, let us cry aloud, blessing, singing, and supremely exalting Christ unto all ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Troparion:*
One God is the Trinity: neither has the Father passed into Sonship, nor has the Son changed into procession; but as distinct Persons, yet Both, Light, God, I glorify the Three unto all ages.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Thou alone dost incorruptibly give birth, thou alone dost nurse an Infant without wedlock, thou alone dost bear thy Creator and Master; though both Mother and handmaid, we sing of thee, O Virgin Mother, unto all ages.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*
Thou didst heal by a word the man born blind;[^751] open again my blinded eyes of the heart, that I may behold the light of Thy commandments, O Christ, unto all ages.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord:*  
*Heirmos:*
*Angels and the heavens, bless Him Who sits upon the throne of glory and is unceasingly glorified as God; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Blessed be the Lord God:*[^752]
*Troparion:*
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who by His Cross loosed the ancient curse and granted us this stronghold, a refuge and firm foundation, by which we now gore our enemies with horns.

*Troparion:*
As with fetters am I bound with boundless evils, and of my own will I wish not to understand Thy saving pathways, O Christ; convert me by Thy Cross, loosing my darkness, for the sake of the unutterable mercy of Thy tenderheartedness.[^753]

*Troparion:*
Having wickedly imitated the haughtiness of the Pharisee, I was brought down in a grievous and painful fall, and I lie trampled underfoot by enemies; but show compassion and save me, O Christ, Who for the sake of compassions didst humble Thyself, O transcendent King of glory!

*Theotokion:*
All generations call thee blessed, O Pure One; for Jesus alone, born from thy womb, has done great things with thee, O Virgin Maiden, as He knows; pray thou Him that thy flock and people may be saved!

*Another [Canon], the same Heirmos:*[^754]  
*Heirmos:*
*Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who by His Cross loosed the ancient curse, and set it forth unto us for veneration; kissing it faithfully and singing praise unto it, we unceasingly hymn Thy great mercy.*

*Troparion:*
Thou art the boast of the Church, O honorable Cross,[^755] the weapon of kings, and unto all the world the beneficent honor of the world, O Cross, the joy of the Orthodox, guardian of the inhabited earth: preserve and sanctify them that bow down before thee!

*Glory:*  
*Trinity Hymn:*
Holy Trinity, glory to Thee, O Unity of equal honor, divine Nature, one awesome Kingdom, Father, Son, with the Spirit, Light unapproachable unto all, unoriginate Godhead: save them that serve Thee, O Creator of all!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
As Thy Ewe-lamb stood beside the Cross, O Jesus, beholding her Shepherd and Master, she wept bitterly, and her womb was moved within her concerning Thee, O Christ, and she cried aloud: What strange sight is this?[^756] O Life, how diest Thou?

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*
As Thou didst save Manasseh when he repented, O God,[^757] save me also who repent bitterly with tears, and turn not away Thy servant; for I have turned aside my soul, like that Israel of old, living all my days in fornication.

*Heirmos:*
*Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who has raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of David His servant; wherein the Dayspring from on high has visited us, and has guided us into the way of peace.*

*Photagogicon of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 8:*[^758]
Reflecting, O my soul, upon the condemnation of the vainglorious Pharisee due to his haughtiness, and recognizing the justification of the humble-minded Publican through the confession of offenses: make haste to lay aside the bold confidence derived from virtue as though it were good, and arise to accept the confession of the evil things thou hast sinned, not adding haughtiness unto thyself through passion, from Christ Who has great mercy!

*Martyrikon:*
Greatly did ye strive, O holy ones, having valiantly endured torments from the lawless, confessing Christ before kings; and having departed from this life, ye work powers in the world, and ye heal the diseased from their passions, O holy ones: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, in the same tone:*
Thy protection, O Virgin Theotokos, is a spiritual remedy; for fleeing beneath it, we are delivered from diseases of the soul.

*At the Sixth Hour:*  
*Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*  
Before Thy Cross we bow down, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify.[^759]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 65, Tone 6:*  
Blessed be God, Who has not turned away my prayer, nor His mercy from me.[^760]  
*Verse:* Bless our God, ye nations!

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 25:1–9):*  
O Lord my God, I will glorify Thee, I will sing praise unto Thy Name; for Thou hast done marvelous things, even Thine ancient and true counsel. So be it, O Lord! For Thou hast made cities a heap of dust, even strong cities, so that their foundations should fall: the city of the ungodly shall never be built. Therefore shall the poor people bless Thee, and the cities of wronged men shall praise Thee. For Thou hast been a helper unto every humble city, and a shelter unto them that faint through want; Thou shalt deliver them from evil men, a shelter of them that thirst, and the spirit of wronged men. As faint-hearted men thirsting in Zion, even so shalt Thou deliver them from ungodly men to whom Thou didst deliver us. And the Lord of hosts shall make a feast for all the nations: upon this mountain they shall drink joy, they shall drink wine; they shall be anointed with myrrh upon this mountain. Give all these things unto the nations; for this is the counsel upon all the nations. Death swallowed up having prevailed; and again the Lord God has taken away every tear from every face: He has taken away the reproach of His people from all the earth, for the mouth of the Lord has spoken [these things]. And in that day they shall say: Lo, our God, on Whom we have hoped, and He will save us; this is the Lord, we have waited for Him, and we have rejoiced and been glad in our salvation.[^761]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 66, Tone 6:*  
Bless us, O God, our God, bless us, O God![^762]  
*Verse:* God be merciful unto us, and bless us!

## On Tuesday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichoi, and chant prosomoia stichera of the Triodion.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^763]  
In the midst of the earth Thou didst endure the Cross and Passion, O Compassionate One, granting dispassion and deliverance unto all at this mid-point of the fast; therefore we all set it forth for veneration and joyfully kiss it, that, enlightened by divine virtues, we all may be accounted worthy, O Word of God, to behold Thy Passion and Thy life-bearing Resurrection, O only Abundantly Merciful One.

*Podoben:*[^764]  
Thou wast stretched out dead upon the Cross and pierced with a spear, and given gall to drink, O Long-suffering One, Who by the hand of Moses didst turn the waters of Marah into sweetness; therefore I pray Thee and entreat Thee: drive away the most bitter passions from my mind, and sweetening my intellect with the honey of repentance, show me forth as a venerator of Thy precious Passion.

*Another, Tone 1. Podoben: "Of old Moses" (Дре́вле Мѡѷсе́й):*[^765]  
That great Cross which of old Moses prefigured by stretching forth his hands, overcoming Amalek, we people today, beholding it fearfully set forth before our eyes, let us touch it with purity, both in mind and with lips; for upon it Christ was lifted up, putting death to death, and having all been accounted worthy of such grace, praising the Savior of all with divinely inspired hymns, we pray that we may attain also unto His saving Resurrection itself.

*Other prosomoia stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^766]  
Come, let us bow down before the mediator of our life, the Cross of Christ our God manifested this day as the Tree, whereby death has been wounded and resurrection manifestly renewed unto us who are fallen, crying unto the Deliverer: O Thou Who didst willingly suffer for our sake, that Thou mightest save all things, our God, glory to Thee!

An invincible power has Thy Cross been given unto us Christians, O our Savior, whereby the multitudes of alien nations are conquered, and peace overshadows Thy Church which rightly glorifies Thee, O Christ; which now kissing, we cry warmly unto Thee: account us also worthy of the portion of Thy saints!

Having cleansed the senses of the soul with tears, and having purified ourselves by fasting, come, let us bow down before the Tree of the Cross, whereby the soul-corrupting uprisings of the flesh are brought to naught through self-restraint, crying unto Him Who was crucified: Account us worthy, O Savior, to bow down before the radiant brightness of Thy three-day Arising!

*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*[^767]  
Today the Master of creation and the Lord of glory is nailed to the Cross and pierced in the side; He Who is the Sweetness of the Church tastes gall and vinegar; He Who covers the heavens with clouds is compassed with a crown of thorns; He is clothed in a garment of mockery and smitten with a hand of clay, He Who fashioned man with His hand; He Who covers the heavens with clouds is beaten upon His shoulders; He receives spitting and wounds, reproaches and buffetings, and endures all things for the sake of me, the condemned one, my Deliverer and God, that He might save the world from deception, in that He is tenderhearted!

*We omit the Menaion, and we say the canon of the saint at Compline.*[^768]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 67, Tone 6:*  
Sing unto God, sing praises unto His Name![^769]  
*Verse:* Make a way for Him Who ascends upon the west.

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 9:8–17):*  
God spoke unto Noah and unto his sons with him, saying: Lo, I establish My covenant with you, and with your seed after you; and with every living soul that is with you, of the birds, and of the cattle, and with all the wild beasts of the earth, as many as [are] with you, of all that went out of the ark. And I will establish My covenant with you; and all flesh shall die no more by the water of a flood, and there shall be no more a flood of waters to destroy the whole earth. And the Lord God said unto Noah: This is the token of the covenant which I give between Me and you, and between every living soul that is with you, unto perpetual generations: I do set My bow in the cloud, and it shall be for a token of an [everlasting] covenant between Me and the earth. And it shall come to pass, when I bring clouds over the earth, that My bow shall appear in the cloud; and I will remember My covenant which is between Me and you, and between every living soul in all flesh; and the water shall no more become a flood to destroy all flesh. And My bow shall be in the cloud, and I will look upon it, that I may remember the everlasting covenant between Me and the earth, and between every living soul in all flesh that is upon the earth. And God said unto Noah: This is the token of the covenant which I have established between Me and all flesh that is upon the earth.[^770]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 68, Tone 6:*  
Let Thy salvation, O God, take hold on me![^771]  
*Verse:* Let the poor see and rejoice!

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 12:9–22):*  
The mouth of the prudent is praised by a man; but the fainthearted is held in derision. Better is a man in dishonor serving himself, than he that confers honor upon himself and lacks bread. A righteous man has compassion upon the lives of his beasts; but the tender mercies of the wicked are cruel. He that tills his land shall be filled with bread; but they that pursue vanity are void of understanding. He that finds sweetness in tarrying at wine shall leave dishonor in his own strongholds. The desires of the ungodly are evil, but the roots of the godly are in strong places. Through the sin of his lips a sinner falls into snares, but the righteous man escapes from them. He that looks gently shall obtain mercy; but he that contends in the gates will grieve souls. From the fruits of the mouth a man's soul shall be filled with good things, and the recompense of his lips shall be given unto him. The ways of fools are right before them, but the wise man listens to counsel. A fool presently declares his sin, but a prudent man conceals his own dishonor. A righteous man proclaims the open truth, but a deceitful witness is unjust. There are some who, speaking, wound like swords, but the tongues of the wise heal. True lips establish testimony, but a hasty witness has an unjust tongue. Deceit is in the heart of him that plots evil, but they that desire peace shall rejoice. Nothing unjust is pleasing unto the righteous, but the ungodly shall be filled with evil. Lying lips are an abomination unto the Lord, but he that acts faithfully is acceptable unto Him.[^772]

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, twice, Tone 3:*[^773]  
Surpassing the Publican in offenses, I do not emulate his repentance; and having acquired none of the righteous deeds of the Pharisee, I imitate his haughtiness; but O Christ our God, Who by the transcendence of Thy humility didst cast down demonic pride upon the Cross: alienate me from the malice of the former and from the madness of the latter, establishing the good purpose of them both within my soul, and save me!

*Martyrikon:*[^774]  
The prophets, and apostles of Christ, and the martyrs have taught us to sing of the consubstantial Trinity, and have enlightened the wandering nations, and have made the sons of men partakers with the angels.

*Glory, both now, Idiomelon, Tone 5:*[^775]  
Beholding Thee hanging naked upon the Cross, the Author and Creator of all, all creation changed with fear and lamented; the sun darkened its light and the earth quaked, the rocks were split and the radiant veil of the temple was rent in twain; the dead arose from the graves, and the angelic Hosts were struck with dread, crying aloud: O the wonder! The Judge is judged and willingly suffers for the salvation and renewal of the world!

## On Wednesday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins

*The Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*[^776]  
*After the first stichology, the sessional hymns of the Cross from the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, we say the present sessional hymn by Lord Joseph.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "O hope of the world" (Оу҆пова́нїе мі́ра):*[^777]  
Sanctifying the time of fasting, the divine and most honorable Cross lies forth for veneration; let us draw near with a pure conscience, let us draw forth sanctification and enlightenment, and with fear let us cry aloud: O Lover of mankind, our Savior, glory to Thy tenderhearted compassion! *(Twice)*

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben:*[^778]  
Standing by the Cross, the unwedded Mother, beholding Him Who was born of her without seed, cried aloud: A sword has pierced my heart, O Savior, unable to bear seeing Thee suspended upon the Tree, before Whom all creation trembles as Creator and God; O Long-suffering One, glory to Thee!

*After the third stichology, a sessional hymn by Lord Theodore.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O hope of the despairing" (Ѻ҆ча́ѧннаѧ):*[^779]  
Enlightened by virtues, having acquired the purity of self-restraint, let us draw near, and crying aloud let us bow down before the precious Cross: Sanctify our souls and bodies, and account us worthy to bow down also before Thy most pure Passion, O only God of all, and grant unto us Thy mercies! *(Twice)*

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Tone. Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^780]  
Beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine undefiled hands, O Compassionate One?

*Canon of the Cross, whose acrostic is: "Let us bow down before the all-honorable Tree."*[^781]  
*Composition of Lord Theophanes.*  
*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos: "I will open my mouth" (Ѿве́рзꙋ ᲂу҆ста̀ моѧ̑):*[^782]  
Before the holy Tree, upon which Christ stretched out His palms, conquering the opposing principalities, let us bow down, having purified ourselves by fasting, unto the praise and glory of the Almighty!

*Troparion:*  
The saving Cross is seen lying forth, bestowing sanctification; let us draw near unto it, having cleansed both body and heart, and let us draw forth saving grace.

*Troparion:*  
Cleanse me with the fire of Thy commandments, O Lover of mankind, and grant me now to behold Thy saving Passion and to venerate it with love, shielded and preserved by the Cross.

*Theotokion:*  
Beholding Thy lifting up upon the Cross, O Lover of mankind, she that bore Thee lamented, crying aloud: How is He that wills to judge all seen hanging as one condemned, the Lord of glory?

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Thy hymnographers" (Твоѧ̑ пѣсносло́вцы):*[^783]  
Having cleansed our hearts with the water of fasting, let us faithfully embrace the Tree of the Cross, upon which Christ hung, pouring forth for us the water of remission as our Benefactor.

*Troparion:*  
Winged with the sail of the Cross, the saving vessel, having reached the mid-point of the fast, O Messiah, Jesus our God: pilot us by it unto the haven of Thy Passion!

*Troparion:*  
Moses prefigured thee upon the mountain for the slaughter of enemies, O Cross; but we depict thee within our heart, and beholding thee and bowing down before thee, we conquer bodiless foes by thy power.

*Theotokion:*  
By Thine own will Thou becamest man, being God and Creator of all; and now I see Thee hanging upon the Cross, O my Son and Christ! she that bore Thee said, and my heart is wounded.

*Triode:*  
*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Being the Lord of all" (Гдⷭ҇ь сы́й всѣ́хъ):*[^784]  
Being the Lord of all and God the Creator, Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross in the midst of the earth, raising up unto Thyself human nature that had fallen by the evil counsel of the enemy; therefore we faithfully glorify Thee, being confirmed by Thy Passion.

*Troparion:*  
Having cleansed the senses by the light of fasting, let us believers be richly illumined by the spiritual rays of the Cross; and beholding it reverently set forth today, let us kiss it with pure lips, both with mouth and heart.

*Troparion:*  
At the place where the feet of Christ stood, let us bow down before the divine Cross, asking that the feet of our soul may be established upon the rock of God's commandments, and that its steps may be guided into the way of peace, by divine grace.

*Theotokion:*  
From the unwedded Maiden Thou camest forth, O Christ, having taken from her an intellective and animate flesh; and having destroyed the enemy by Thy Cross, Thou hast renewed corrupt human nature again; therefore we glorify Thy tenderhearted compassion.

*Another Triode, Tone 1. Heirmos: "Establish, O Lord" (Оу҆твердѝ Гдⷭ҇и):*[^785]  
Clap your hands with hymns, all ye ends of the earth, beholding the Tree that is bowed down unto, upon which Christ was suspended and the devil was wounded!

*Troparion:*  
Life-bearing joy is set forth today; come, let us all bow down with fear before the precious Cross of Christ, that we may receive the Holy Spirit!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
O thrice-sunned Sun, thrice-radiant Light, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, unoriginate Nature and Glory: deliver from perils them that hymn Thee!

*Theotokion:*  
The angelic ranks sing of thee, blessed all-hymned Theotokos and Virgin; with them we the human race now glorify thee as unwedded bride.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Coming to touch thee, O life-bearing Cross, I am filled with dread in tongue and mind, perceiving that upon thee the divine blood of my Lord was poured out.

*Heirmos:*  
*Establish, O Lord, Thy Church, which Thou hast acquired by the power of Thy Cross, whereby Thou didst conquer the enemy, and hast enlightened the universe.*

*Sessional Hymns, Tone 6. Idiomela:*[^786]  
Today the prophetic word is fulfilled: For lo, we bow down at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord; and having tasted of the Tree of salvation, we have obtained freedom from sinful passions, through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O only Lover of mankind!

*Glory:*  
Thy Cross, O Lord, is sanctified, for in it healings take place for them that are sick in sins; falling down before it, we cry unto Thee: Have mercy upon us!

*Both now:*  
No sooner was the Tree of Thy Cross set up, O Christ, than the foundations of death were shaken, O Lord; for whom Hades swallowed with greed, it let go in trembling; Thou hast shown forth Thy salvation unto us, O Holy One, and we glorify Thee, O Savior God: have mercy upon us!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "He Who sits in glory" (Сѣдѧ́й во сла́вѣ):*[^787]  
Prefiguring the sign of the Cross, glorious Jacob of old stretched forth his hands in crossing, blessing the grandchildren, and signifying the saving blessing that comes upon all of us.

*Troparion:*  
Preserved by the seal of the Cross, and kissing it lying forth with the joy of the Spirit, we go forward unto the saving Passions, having put to death the destructive passions of the flesh.

*Troparion:*  
We embrace thee as a saving weapon, an unconquerable victory, a sign of joy, whereby death was put to death, adorning ourselves with the glory of Him Who was nailed upon thee, O all-honorable Cross!

*Theotokion:*  
Seen by me, Thou wast invisible and unapproachable unto the angels, O Jesus my Savior, having borrowed flesh from me; and now beholding Thee nailed upon the Tree, I lament! the Mother of Christ said.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "All things were astounded" (Оу҆жасо́шасѧ):*[^788]  
Thou wast made dead being nailed upon the Cross, Who art the life and salvation of all; grant unto us therefore, O Savior, with purified soul to embrace it, and rejoicing to behold Thy saving Passion.

*Troparion:*  
The ranks of bodiless angels stand before thee trembling, O lifegiving Tree; for upon thee Christ poured forth His precious blood, flashing rays afar, bringing endless destruction upon the demons.[^789]

*Troparion:*  
Wounded by the weapon of the enemy, heal me with Thy blood, O Word, and with the spear speedily tear up the handwriting of my sins, O Savior, as I cry unto Thee: Inscribe me in the books of the saved, in that Thou art tenderhearted!

*Theotokion:*  
O ripe cluster, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree? O Sun of glory, how art Thou eclipsed, darkening the light of the sun by Thy Passion, O Savior? the Ewe-lamb who bore Thee of old cried unto Thee, crying aloud with maternal sorrow.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Prefiguring the three-day burial" (Возопѝ, прошбража́ѧ):*[^790]  
Being set up, thou didst shake the dwelling of Hades, but hast become an unshakable confirmation unto the faithful, a sure shelter, O all-honorable Cross of the Lord!

*Troparion:*  
Having become fruitful through virtues, let us embrace the life-bearing fruits of the divine Tree, which Jesus, the fruitful Vine, blossomed forth, having stretched Himself upon it.

*Troparion:*  
We hymn Thine abundant goodness, O Jesus, bowing down before Thy Cross, spear, and reed; for by them, O Compassionate One, Thou didst break down the middle wall of enmity.

*Theotokion:*  
The All-pure one gave birth unto Thee, the river of life-flowing waters, as the fountain of our renewal, Who wast stretched out upon the Cross and didst pour forth drops of salvation.

*Heirmos:*  
*The prophet Jonah, prefiguring the three-day burial, cried out, praying in the sea-monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!*

*Kontakion, Tone 7. Idiomelon:*[^791]  
No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden, for upon it has come a marvelous bond, the Tree of the Cross; the sting of death and the victory of Hades have been driven away, for Thou didst appear, O my Savior, crying unto them in Hades: Enter ye again into Paradise!

*Ikos:*[^792]  
Pilate set up three crosses upon Golgotha, two of the robbers, and one of the Giver of life; whom Hades beheld and said unto them below: O my servants and my powers! Who has driven nails into my heart, and suddenly pierced me with a wooden spear? And I am torn asunder, I am in pain within my inward parts, my bowels are wounded, my senses trouble my spirit, and I am compelled to cast forth Adam and them that came from Adam, who were given unto me through a tree; for a Tree leads these again into Paradise!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "They did not serve the creature" (Не послꙋжи́ша тва́ри):*[^793]  
The axe which Elisha retrieved from the Jordan, O Christ, prefigured the Cross, whereby Thou hast drawn the nations out of the depth of vanity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God and Lord of our fathers!

*Troparion:*  
Heavenly things rejoice with earthly things at the veneration of thy Cross; for through thee, O all-powerful one, a union was made for angels and men, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God!

*Troparion:*  
Bringing compassion like cypress, fragrant faith like cedar, and true love like pine, let us bow down before the Cross of the Lord, glorifying upon it the Deliverer Who was nailed thereon.

*Theotokion:*  
Without shaking the heavens, God took up His abode within thy womb, O chosen City of God; but hanging upon the Cross He shook creation; pray Him that I may be kept upon His immovable rock.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The pious youths" (Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ):*[^794]  
Thou didst stretch forth Thy hands upon the Tree, loosing the sin of unrestrained hands; and Thou wast pierced with a spear, wounding the adversary thereby, O Lord; Thou didst taste gall, taking away sensual malice, and wast given vinegar to drink, O Gladness of all!

*Troparion:*  
Drawing near with pure mind and bright conscience, let us bow down: the precious and holy Tree lies forth, whereby Christ brought about dishonorable death, placing the highest honor upon them that were grievously dishonored by transgression.

*Troparion:*  
By the tree of sin I was put to death, and by sensual tasting I was buried; revive me, O Lord, raise me up as I lie, show me forth as a venerator of Thy Passions, a partaker of Thy divine Resurrection, and an heir of them that love Thee!

*Theotokion:*  
Beholding Thee lifted up, having neither beauty nor form in Thy Passion, Whom when incarnate I knew to be fairer than the sons of men, Thee, O Only-begotten Son, the all-pure Virgin cried aloud: Show forth Thy glory, the salvation of all!

*Another Triode. Heirmos: "For the laws of the fathers" (За зако́ны ѻ҆те́ческїѧ):*[^795]  
When Thou wast willingly crucified in the midst of the earth at midday, O Compassionate One, Thou didst snatch the ends of the world out of the serpent's maw; therefore, in this middle week of the divine fasts, we bow down and glorify Thy precious Cross, crying aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Troparion:*  
Sign of joy, invincible weapon, wall of the Church, boast of martyrs, adornment of apostles, confirmation of hierarchs: having strengthened my weakened soul, account it worthy to bow down and sing unto Thee, crying aloud: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Troparion:*  
I lament and weep whenever I bring to mind that untroubled judgment-seat, O Long-suffering One, being myself condemned; therefore spare me, and lighten the heavy burden of my soul, that rejoicing I may cry: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Theotokion:*  
The bush prefigured the mystery of thy birthgiving that surpasses understanding, O all-immaculate Virgin; for even as that was, so didst thou remain unconsumed, having given birth unto Christ the Savior, the Fire, Who was lifted up upon the Cross; pray Him to deliver me from eternal fire, who cry aloud: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Another Triode. Heirmos: "This chosen and holy day" (Се́й нарече́нный и҆ ст҃ы́й де́нь):*[^796]  
Come, having purified ourselves by fasting, let us kiss with love the Cross of the Lord lying forth; for it is unto us a treasure of salvation and strength; therefore let us sing of it forever!

*Troparion:*  
This tripartite and great Cross, though small to the sight, is broader than the heavens by its power, ever leading men unto God; whereby let us bless Christ forever!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
Glorifying one Essence in three hypostases, I do not merge the three into one person, nor do I divide the form: the Father, and the Son, with the Spirit; for one is God over all in Trinity.

*Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou alone among mothers hast been shown forth, O Virgin Bride of God Mary, who without a husband gavest birth unto the Savior Christ, preserving the seal of purity; thee do we men bless forever!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Let this all-holy Tree be honored, which the prophet of old cried out to put into the bread of Christ, by the Israelites who crucified Him, Whom we exalt above all unto all ages.[^797]

*Heirmos:*  
*Cast into the pit of lions, Daniel, once great among the prophets, stretching forth his hands crosswise, was preserved unhurt from being devoured by them, blessing Christ our God forever.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Let every earthborn mortal" (Всѧ́къ земноро́дный):*[^798]  
With a spear Thou openest Thy side, spreading forth unto me a fountain of forgiveness; and Thou wast nailed upon the Tree, setting aside condemnation through a Tree, in the midst of the earth, O Lover of mankind; which we now kiss at the mid-point of the fast, singing of Thy goodness.

*Troparion:*  
Mountains, drop sweetness, hills, leap with joy in love; and trees of the field, cedars of Lebanon, dance, at the veneration today of the life-bearing Cross; prophets, martyrs, apostles, and spirits of the righteous, exult!

*Troparion:*  
Look down upon Thy people and Thine inheritance, O Lord, who sing of Thee in fear, for whose sake Thou didst endure voluntary death, lest the boundless multitude of our evils overcome Thy tenderhearted compassion; but save us all, O All-good One, by Thy Cross, in that Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*  
By Thy nod Thou shakest the visible world, yet being crucified Thou remainest hanging! the Theotokos weeping said, desiring to destroy the passionate devices of the first-formed man, O Christ, and to heal his crushing by Thy goodness.

*Another Triode. Heirmos: "Every tongue is at a loss" (Недоꙋмѣ́етъ всѧ́къ):*[^799]  
Elisha of old, drawing the iron axe-head out of the river, prefigured thee, O Cross, the lifegiving Tree, through wood; whereby Christ, having been nailed upon thee, drew the nations out of the depth of demonic idolatry; therefore, bowing down before thee, we glorify His dominion.

*Troparion:*  
The sun's rays, O Savior, were turned into darkness by Thy Crucifixion, and the shining of the moon was quenched, and the elements were all changed with trembling; therefore I cry unto Thee: Having transformed and enlightened by Thy right hand my passionate thoughts changed by me, O Word, save me!

*Troparion:*  
By Thy wound heal the passions of my soul; by the piercing of Thy side stay the painful piercings of the ages; with Thy nails, O Christ, nail through my lustful and passionate desires; grant me without passion to bow down before Thy precious Sufferings and Resurrection!

*Theotokion:*  
The fair Maiden gave birth unto Thee Who art fair in beauty; Whom beholding in suffering having neither form nor beauty, O Savior, weeping she said: Beyond understanding, O my Son, I marvel at Thy humility, whereby Thou savest humbled human nature!

*Another Triode. Heirmos: "Shine, shine" (Свѣти́сѧ свѣти́сѧ):*[^800]  
Make ready, be radiant, all ye people of God, and beholding the Tree of the Cross lying forth, kiss it with fear, and receive joy, ever glorifying the Lord of glory Who was lifted up upon it!

*Troparion:*  
A divine weapon art thou unto my life, O Cross; the Master, mounting upon thee, saved me; pierced in the ribs, He poured forth blood and water, of which I partake rejoicing: glory to Him!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
In three Persons, in one Essence, I bow down before Thee, O Holy Godhead: the Father, and the Son, with the Holy Spirit: one Principle, one Kingdom, holding dominion over all!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast been shown forth, O Virgin, as the great mountain in which Christ took up His abode, as divine David cries out, through whom we have been lifted up unto heaven, adopted as sons by the Spirit, O All-blessed Lady![^801]

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Divine scepter of the Church, O Cross, courage of the army! In reliance upon thee we vanquish the warriors; grant victories over the barbarians unto us who ever bow down before thee!

*Heirmos:*  
*O Mother and Virgin, and true Theotokos, who without seed gavest birth unto Christ our God, Who was lifted up in the flesh upon the Cross: thee do all we the faithful worthily magnify now together with Him.*[^802]

*Photogogic of the tone, and the present Exapostilarion:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "In the Spirit" (Дх҃омъ / Πνεύματι):*[^803]  
At the mid-point of the fasts, the all-honorable Tree summons all unto veneration, as many as by their passions worthily follow after the Passions of Christ: Come, all ye faithful, let us bow down before the Tree of the awesome mystery!

*Stavrotheotokion:*  
The unwedded and all-pure Mother of God the Word, crying aloud, weeping in lamentation, cried out: Were these the joys of good tidings that Gabriel brought unto me, O Child?[^804] Go forth to fulfill Thine ineffable counsel and divine dispensation!

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice. Tone 8:*[^805]  
Having reached the mid-point of the path of fasting, which leads unto Thy precious Cross, that we may behold Thy day, which Abraham saw and rejoiced, as he received Isaac alive as from the hill of the grave: be well-pleased also that we, having been delivered by faith from the enemy, may partake of the mystical supper, crying in peace: O our Enlightenment and Savior, glory to Thee![^806]

*For the Martyrs:*  
Invincible martyrs of Christ, having conquered deception by the power of the Cross, ye received the grace of eternal life! Ye feared not the threats of the tormentors; being wounded by tortures, ye rejoiced! And now your blood has become healings for our souls: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... in the same Tone [Tone 8]:*  
Having crossed the midst of the deep of abstinence, let us receive the haven of salvation, the time of Thy voluntary Passion, O Lord; but in that Thou art tenderhearted and compassionate, vouchsafe us also to behold in peace the day of Thy glorious Resurrection, O Lover of mankind!

*Both now... again the same.*

*The Veneration of the Cross takes place at the Sixth Hour, at which we sing the prescribed Sunday stichera, idiomela, Tone 2: "Come, ye faithful," and the rest. And the dismissal.*[^807]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*  
Before Thy Cross we bow down, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 69 [LXX 69:5; Slavonic Ps. 64], Tone 6:*[^808]  
Let all those who seek Thee rejoice and be glad in Thee, O God!  
*Verse: Let them be turned back and brought to shame.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 26:21–27:6):*[^809]  
Behold, the Lord comes forth from His holy place to bring wrath upon the inhabitants of the earth; and the earth shall disclose her blood, and shall not cover her slain. In that day the Lord shall bring His holy, and great, and strong sword upon the dragon, the fleeing serpent, upon the dragon, the crooked serpent; and He shall slay the dragon that is in the sea. In that day there is a fair vineyard, a desire to sing over it: I am a strong city, a besieged city; in vain shall I water it, for it shall be taken by night, and by day its wall shall fall; there is none who shall not take it. Who will set me to watch the stubble in the field? By reason of this enmity I have cast it away; therefore, the Lord God hath done all things whatsoever He purposed. And I am burnt up; those who dwell in it shall cry aloud: Let us make peace with Him, let us make peace! The children of Jacob that come shall sprout and blossom;

Israel shall blossom, and the world shall be filled with his fruit. Hath He smitten him as He smote those that smote him? And as he slew, shall he likewise be slain? Contending and reproaching, He will send them away: and wast thou not meditating with a harsh spirit, to slay them with a spirit of wrath? Therefore shall the iniquity of Jacob be taken away, and this is his blessing, when I take away his sin, when they make all the stones of the altars broken in pieces, as fine dust; and their trees shall not remain, and their idols shall be cut down, like a grove far away.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 70 [LXX 70:1–2], Tone 6:*[^810]  
In Thee, O Lord, have I hoped, let me never be put to shame!  
*Verse: Deliver me in Thy righteousness.*

## On Wednesday of the Fourth Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing the following idiomela and the Martyrikon, and in the Menaion 4.*

*Idiomelon, Tone 4:*[^811]  
Fasting, the mediator of good things, hath now brought in its mid-point, having pleased God by the days that are past, and setting forth benefit by those that remain; for the intensifying of good works produces greater beneficence; therefore, pleasing Christ the Giver of all good things, we cry aloud: O Thou Who didst fast for our sake and didst endure the Crucifixion, make us worthy without condemnation to partake also of Thy divine Pascha, having lived in peace, and worthily glorifying Thee together with the Father and the Spirit!

*Another [Idiomelon], Tone 5:*[^812]  
Those who practice virtues in secret, looking for spiritual rewards, do not display them in the midst of marketplaces and crossroads, but rather carry them within the heart; and He Who sees all things that are done in secret grants us the reward of abstinence. Let us accomplish the fast, not disfiguring our faces with gloomy countenance, but praying in the secret chambers of our souls, let us unceasingly cry aloud: Our Father Who art in heaven, lead us not into temptation, we pray, but deliver us from the evil one!

*For the Martyrs:*  
With an insatiable love of soul, ye did not deny Christ, O holy martyrs, who having endured diverse wounds of suffering, cast down the audacity of the tormentors! Having preserved the faith unbending and unharmed, ye were translated unto heaven; therefore, having gained boldness before Him, entreat that peace be granted unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Other Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы / Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες):*[^813]  
Let us all wash our souls with the water of fasting, and approaching the precious and lifegiving Cross of the Lord, let us bow down in faith, drawing forth divine enlightenment, and now acquiring eternal salvation, peace, and great mercy!

*Sticheron:*  
Boast of the apostles, O Cross, which Principalities, Powers, and Archangels encircle: save from every harm those who bow down before thee, and vouchsafe that we, well completing the divine stadium of abstinence, may attain unto the Saving Day, whereby we are saved!

*Another Podoben, Tone 6, Podoben: "Today He watches" (Дне́сь бди́тъ / Σήμερον γρηγορεῖ):*[^814]  
Today bowing down before the Cross of the Lord, let us cry aloud: Rejoice, O Tree of life, destroyer of Hades! Rejoice, joy of the world and consumer of corruption! Rejoice, driving away demons by thy power, establishment of the faithful, unbreakable weapon: preserve and sanctify those who kiss thee, we pray!

*And in the Menaion 4.*

*Glory... Both now... Idiomelon, Tone 8:*[^815]  
Today He Who is untouchable in Essence becomes touchable unto me, and suffers passions, He Who frees me from passions; He Who gives light unto the blind is spat upon by lawless lips, and gives His back unto stripes for the sake of captives! Beholding Him upon the Cross, the Pure Virgin and Mother spoke in pain: Woe is me, O my Child, what is this that Thou hast done? Thou Who art fair in beauty beyond all the sons of men appearest breathless, formless, having neither form nor beauty! Woe is me, O my Light! I cannot bear to behold Thee sleeping; I am wounded in my bowels, and a cruel sword passes through my heart! I hymn Thy Passions, I bow down before Thy tenderhearted compassion: O Long-suffering One, glory to Thee!

*Entrance: "O gladsome Light"*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 71 [LXX 71:18, 1], Tone 4:*[^816]  
Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, Who alone does marvelous deeds!  
*Verse: Give the king Thy judgment, O God, and Thy righteousness unto the king’s son.*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 9:18–10:1):*[^817]  
The sons of Noah who went forth from the ark were Shem, Ham, and Japhs; and Ham was the father of Canaan. These three are the sons of Noah; from these were people spread abroad over all the earth. And Noah began to be a husbandman of the earth, and he planted a vineyard. And he drank of the wine, and was drunken, and was uncovered in his house. And Ham, the father of Canaan, saw the nakedness of his father, and going outside he told his two brethren. And Shem and Japhs took a garment, and laid it upon both their shoulders, and walked backward, and covered the nakedness of their father; and their faces were backward, and they saw not the nakedness of their father. And Noah awoke from his wine, and knew what his younger son had done unto him. And he said: Cursed be the youth Canaan; a servant shall he be unto his brethren. And he said: Blessed be the Lord God of Shem; and the youth Canaan shall be a servant unto him. May God enlarge Japhs, and may he dwell in the tents of Shem; and may Canaan be a servant unto him. And Noah lived after the flood...

...three hundred and fifty years. And all the days of Noah were nine hundred and fifty years, and he died. Now these are the generations of the sons of Noah: Shem, Ham, and Japhs; and unto them were sons born after the flood.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 72 [LXX 72:28, 1], Tone 4:*[^818]  
But it is good for me to cleave unto God!  
*Verse: How good is God to Israel.*

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 12:23–13:9):*[^819]  
A prudent man is a throne of discernment; but the heart of fools meets with curses. The hand of chosen men shall rule easily; but the deceitful shall be in captivity. A terrifying word troubles the heart of a righteous man; but a good message cheers him. A righteous man, understanding, shall be a friend unto himself; but the thoughts of the ungodly are ungentle; evil things shall overtake sinners, but the way of the ungodly leads them astray. A deceitful man shall not catch prey; but a pure man is a precious possession. In the ways of righteousness is life, but the ways of the resentful lead unto death. A wise son is obedient unto his father, but a disobedient son is for destruction. From the fruits of righteousness a man eats of good things, but the souls of the lawless perish untimely. He that keeps his mouth preserves his soul; but he that is rash with his lips shall terrify himself. Every sluggard desires in his lusts, but the hands of the diligent are in active labor. A righteous man hates an unjust word; but the ungodly man is ashamed and shall have no boldness. Righteousness guards the innocent; but sin makes the ungodly wicked. There are those that make themselves rich, yet have nothing; and there are those that humble themselves in great riches. The ransom of a man's soul is his own wealth; but the poor man endures no threatening. The light of the righteous is always, but the light of the ungodly is quenched; deceitful souls wander in sins, but the righteous show compassion and pity.

*And the rest of the Order of the Presanctified Gifts.*

*From this day forth, the priest (or deacon, if there be one) begins the duplicated petitions for those preparing for illumination [the catechumens], and the prayer; and they are spoken even unto Great Wednesday, except on Saturdays and Sundays.*[^820]

## On Thursday of the Fourth Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the tone of the week.*[^821]

*After the first stichology, we say the Apostolic Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, we say the present Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй / Χορὸς Ἀγγελικὸς):*[^822]  
Being strengthened by the power of the Cross, ye truly destroyed all the enemy's deception, O glorious apostles, intercessors for our souls! Therefore today ye rejoice in its veneration, praying for us unto the only Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*Theotokion:*  
Mary, precious receptacle of the Master, raise us up who have fallen into the abyss of cruel despair, of transgressions, and of afflictions; for thou art the salvation and help of sinners, and a mighty intercession, and thou savest thy servants!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore, Tone 7, Podoben: "Lord, we are [Thy people]" (Гдⷭ҇и, мы̀ є҆смы̀ / Κύριε, ἡμεῖς ἐσμεν):*[^823]  
O Lord, beholding Thy Cross lying forth today, we approach in faith, kissing it in chants and hymns with fear and joy: Sanctify Thy servants, give peace unto Thy world by its manifestation, O Thou Who alone art plenteous in mercy! *(Twice)*

*Theotokion, the same Podoben:*  
O Lord, we are Thy people, and the sheep of Thy pasture; turn us back who have gone astray through wasting away; gather us who are scattered as a shepherd; have mercy upon Thy flock, O Lover of mankind, show compassion through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Thou Who alone art without sin![^824]

*Canon of the Menaion, and the present Triode by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the canticle is recited.*

*Ode 4, Tone 1. Heirmos: "On divine watch" (На бж҃е́ственнѣй стра́жи / Ἐπὶ τῆς θείας φυλακῆς):*[^825]  
Bestow upon us sanctification-giving grace, O holy Cross, establishment and boast of the apostles! Thou liest forth today for veneration by all the universe, lightening for us the time of fasting.

*Troparion:*  
Moses, having mystically inscribed the Cross by stretching forth his hands, vanquished Amalek; figuring which, we stretch forth our hands in fasting and supplications, that we may conquer the demonic hosts that ever war against us enviously![^826]

*Troparion:*  
With the divine plow of the Cross ye plowed the earth, O divine disciples, showing it forth fruitful, sprouting forth piety; therefore, praising you with prayerful voices, we ever glorify Christ!

*Theotokion:*  
O Mother of the Good God, sweeten my soul, which the evil one hath wretchedly afflicted by evil custom, having deceived me; that as the cause of salvation I may ever hymn thee who art much-praised!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], Tone 7. Heirmos: "Thy bodily coming, O Christ" (Пло́тское прише́ствїе твоѐ Хрⷭ҇тѐ):*[^827]  
The all-precious Cross is set forth today; let us approach, let us kiss it, for it is the horn of our salvation!

*Troparion:*  
Thy grace comes even by thine image, driving away the multitude of demons, O Cross of Christ, invincible weapon!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Thee as Unity in essence I hymn, and as Trinity in hypostases I revere: O Father, Son, and Most Holy Spirit!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou gavest birth, O unwedded Maiden, unto God, O All-immaculate Lady, Who holds the ends of the earth in His fist, and Who is more ancient than all!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O apostles, intercessors of the world, pray that we also may be saved who hold fast the weapon, the Cross of Christ!

*Heirmos:*  
*Being assured of Thy bodily coming, O Christ, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Ode 8, Tone 1. Heirmos: "This chosen [and holy day]" (Сѐй нарече́нный / Αὕτη ἡ κλητὴ):*[^828]  
Rejoice, O tripartite and divine Cross, upon which One of the Trinity, having borne flesh, was nailed! Deliver us who were plunged into the depth of impiety, who exalt Him above all forever!

*Troparion:*  
Having acquired the Cross as mighty power, the disciples of the Word freed those who were enslaved by the cruel tyranny of the evil one, singing: Thee do we exalt above all forever!

*Troparion:*  
Woe is me! What a fearsome throne is that one, whereon Thou, O Word, having sat, shalt reveal the secret deeds done by me, O Christ, reproving my insensibility! But being merciful by nature, spare me then!

*Theotokion:*  
Without departing from the Father's bosom, Thou didst recline in the bosom of the Virgin, for the recalling of the human race, whose likeness, O Jesus, Thou didst bear in Thy tender mercy; therefore do we mortals sing of Thee forever!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], Tone 7. Heirmos: "The only unoriginate" (Е҆ди́наго безначя́льнаго / Τὸν μόνον ἄναρχον):*[^829]  
We honor Thy Cross-bearing week as worthy of veneration, for in thee, bowing down before the precious Tree, we cry aloud: Bless, ye priests; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
O wonder! O King of the ages, that Thou hast vouchsafed us Thy servants to behold and bow down before the Tree whereon Thou wast crucified; therefore with fear do we sing of Thee forever!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
O consubstantial, O all-holy Trinity, Father, Son, and Most Holy Spirit, Godhead: I glorify Thine indivisible dominion, and I sing of Thy one Kingdom unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Him Who fashioned Adam in His image, and Who came forth from his seed, and Who destroyed the ancient curse: praise Him, ye priests; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O twelve-stringed harp of the Church, singing the word of wisdom: O apostles, for those who hymn you ask that they may behold the Passion of the Lord, and sing forever![^830]

*We praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto all ages.*  
*Heirmos:*  
*The only unoriginate King of glory, Whom the heavenly powers bless, and before Whom the angelic orders tremble: praise Him, ye priests; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.*

*Ode 9, Tone 1. Heirmos: "Shine, shine" (Свѣти́ся, свѣти́ся / Φωτίζου, φωτίζου):*[^831]  
Be clear, shine, O Church of God, with the rays of the Cross, which thou bearest lying forth unto all the faithful for veneration; and flee away, all ye demon host filled with darkness!

*Troparion:*  
The divine Cross is the strength of abstinence, the fellow-worker of those who watch, the fortification of fasters, the champion of those who do battle: approaching it in love, ye faithful, let us joyfully bow down!

*Troparion:*  
Divine foundations of the Church of Christ, preserve all things in the establishment of piety, and establish upon the firm rock of Him Who chose you, O glorious apostles!

*Theotokion:*  
O thou who didst receive joy at the archangel's voice, consume now the soul-destroying grief of my heart, and grant me, O Pure Lady, joy-producing mourning, that I may find divine consolation there!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], Tone 7. Heirmos: "Thee the most pure Mother and Virgin" (Тя̀ пречи́стꙋю Мт҃рь и҆ Дв҃ꙋ / Τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν):*[^832]  
Prefigured of old, thou didst sweeten the waters of Marah, O all-honorable Tree: sweeten thou my abstinence, O Cross of Christ, who art venerated today![^833]

*Troparion:*  
Thee as the invincible weapon of the Kingdom, the strength of the army, the establishment of the sanctuary, O Cross of Christ, do we all magnify in hymns!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Thee, the unoriginate Father and Master, and the co-unoriginate Word, and the Holy Spirit, piously do all we the faithful magnify!

*Theotokion:*  
Thee the glorious and only Theotokos, who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable Word, and didst bear Him in the flesh, do we magnify in hymns!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Thee the fiery sound, the apostles, as having burned up every matter of deception and planted piety in the world, do we magnify in hymns![^834]

*Heirmos:*  
*Thee the most pure Mother and Virgin, who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable Word, and didst bear Him in the flesh, do we magnify in hymns.*

*Photogogic of the tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, twice. Tone 7:*[^835]  
Let us the faithful emulate the Publican's repentance, and let us not boast Pharisaically; but from the depth of the heart let us offer groaning unto God the Benefactor of all; for He commanded, saying: Whosoever exalts himself shall be humbled, and he that humbles himself shall be exalted; therefore with one accord let us cry aloud unto Him: O God, be merciful unto us sinners, and save us![^836]

*For the Martyrs:*  
Breathing one thing, looking unto one thing, O passion-bearing martyrs, having found one path of life, even death for Christ's sake, in zeal depicting their departure one unto another, O wonder! As if snatching treasures beforehand they seized the tortures, and said one unto another: Even if we do not die now, we shall die in any case, and we shall repay what is owed unto existence; let us make necessity the cause of piety, regarding what is common as our own; let us buy life by death! By their prayers, O God, have mercy on us!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
By the intercessions of the Theotokos, grant peace unto the life of us who cry unto Thee, O Merciful One: Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*  
Before Thy Cross we bow down, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 73 [LXX 73:12, 1], Tone 4:*[^837]  
But God is our King before the ages; He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth!  
*Verse: O God, why hast Thou cast us off unto the end?*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 28:14–22):*[^838]  
Thus says the Lord: Give ear to the word of the Lord, ye afflicted men, and ye rulers of the people that are in Jerusalem! For ye said: We have made a covenant with Hades, and an agreement with death; if a driving storm pass through, it will not come upon us; we have made a lie our hope, and with a lie shall we be covered.
