Therefore thus says the Lord: Behold, I lay in the foundations of Zion a costly stone, chosen, an angular cornerstone, precious, for its reliance, and he that believes in Him shall not be put to shame![^839] And I will set judgment for hope, and My mercy in the balances; and ye that have trusted vainly in a lie: for the storm shall not pass by you, and it shall not remove from you the covenant of death, and your hope that was toward Hades shall not remain; if a rushing storm come on, ye shall be trampled down by it. Whenever it passes by, it shall take you; for every morning it shall pass through by day, and in the night there shall be an evil hope: learn to give ear in affliction: We cannot fight, and we ourselves are too weak to gather together. As the mountain against the ungodly the Lord shall arise, and it shall be as in the valley of Gibeon; with wrath shall He do His works, a work of bitterness; and His wrath shall be exercised strangely, and His destruction shall be marvelous! And rejoice ye not, neither let your bonds be strengthened; for I have heard of finished and shortened things from the Lord of Sabaoth, which He will perform upon all the earth.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 74 [LXX 74:10, 2], Tone 4:*[^840]  
But I will rejoice forever; I will sing praises unto the God of Jacob!  
*Verse: We will give thanks unto Thee, O God, we will give thanks unto Thee.*

## On Thursday of the Fourth Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing the present 3 prosomoia, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе / Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν):*[^841]  
Having been vouchsafed, O faithful, to kiss Thy Cross, O Lord, ever-blessed, whereby Thou hast saved us, we hymn Thy tenderhearted compassion, and we pray Thee diligently: Grant unto all, O Savior, the joy of Thy salvation, having granted us in repentance to behold Thy Resurrection and precious Sufferings!

*Sticheron:*  
Thou didst endure putting to death stretched upon the Cross, O Thou Who didst slay death and didst raise up the dead by Thy lifegiving word; therefore I pray Thee: Make alive my soul slain by sin, O Lord; grant me compunction and deliverance from evils in these holy days of Thy fast, O Lover of mankind!

*Another sticheron prosomoion by Lord Theodore, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the spiritual adamant" (Оу҆́мнагѡ а҆дама́нта):*[^842]  
Having been vouchsafed joyfully to behold and to kiss Thy holy Cross, we pray Thee, O God our Savior, that, strengthened by the fast, we may also reach Thine all-pure Passion; bowing down we hymn Thy Crucifixion, the lance, the sponge, and the reed, whereby having made us immortal, Thou hast brought us back unto the ancient sweet life, in that Thou art the Lover of mankind; therefore with songs we thankfully glorify Thee!

*And in the Menaion 3.*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 75 [LXX 75:12, 2], Tone 8:*[^843]  
Make your vows, and pay them unto the Lord our God!  
*Verse: In Judah God is known; His name is great in Israel.*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 10:32–11:9):*[^844]  
These are the tribes of the sons of Noah, according to their generations, according to their tongues: from these were the islands of the nations divided on the earth after the flood. And the whole earth was of one lip, and one speech was unto all. And it came to pass, as they journeyed from the east, they found a plain in the land of Shinar, and they dwelt there. And a man said unto his neighbor: Come, let us make bricks, and bake them with fire! And they had brick for stone, and clay instead of mortar. And they said: Come, let us build for ourselves a city and a tower, whose top shall reach even unto heaven; and let us make a name for ourselves, before we be scattered upon the face of all the earth! And the Lord came down to see the city and the tower, which the sons of men were building. And the Lord said: Behold, they are one race, and they all have one lip, and this they have begun to do; and now nothing will fail from them, of all whatsoever they may plan to do! Come, and having gone down, let us confuse their tongue there, that they may not understand each one the speech of his neighbor! And from thence the Lord scattered them over the face of all the earth; and they ceased building the city and the tower. Therefore its name was called Confusion, because there the Lord confused the speech of all the earth; and from thence the Lord scattered them over the face of all the earth.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 76 [LXX 76:2, 3], Tone 8:*[^845]  
With my voice have I cried unto the Lord, with my voice unto God, and He gave ear unto me!  
*Verse: In the day of my affliction I sought God.*

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 13:20–14:6a):*[^846]  
The desires of the pious sweeten the soul, but the deeds of the ungodly are far from understanding. He that walks with the wise shall be wise; but he that walks with fools shall be known as such. Evil things shall pursue sinners, but good things shall overtake the righteous. A good man shall inherit sons of sons, but the wealth of the ungodly is laid up for the righteous. The righteous shall enjoy wealth for many years, but the unrighteous shall perish quickly. He that spares his rod hates his son; but he that loves him chastens him diligently. The righteous man eating satisfies his soul, but the souls of the ungodly are in want. Wise women build houses, but a foolish woman digs them down with her hands. He that walks uprightly fears the Lord; but he that is perverted in his ways shall be dishonored. From the mouth of fools is a rod of insult, but the lips of the wise preserve them. Where there are no oxen, the mangers are clean; but where there is...

...much grain, the strength of the oxen is manifest. A faithful witness does not lie, but an unrighteous witness kindles falsehood. Thou shalt seek wisdom among the evil, and shalt not find it; but discernment is easy among the wise.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 6:*[^847]  
Having learned from the Lord Who humbled Himself even unto the Crucifixion and death for thy sake, O my soul, the humility that comes from lifting up, and the lifting up that comes from humility, boast not in virtues, neither judging thy neighbor in deeming thyself righteous, as the boastful Pharisee; but meditating only upon thy transgressions in a humbled understanding, cry aloud as the Publican: O God, cleanse me a sinner, and save me! *(Twice)*

*For the Martyrs:*  
Thy martyrs, O Lord, denied Thee not, neither did they depart from Thy commandments: by their prayers have mercy on us!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*[^848]  
When the All-pure One beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, she cried out weeping: What strange mystery is this that I behold, O my Savior? How dost Thou die upon the Tree, suspended in the flesh, O Giver of life?

## On Friday of the Fourth Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the tone.*[^849]

*After the first stichology, we say the Cross Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, we say the present Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ):*[^850]  
Sanctifying the time of abstinence, the all-honorable Cross is seen, bowing down before which today let us cry aloud: O Master, Lover of mankind, by its aid grant us to pass through the remaining time of fasting in compunction, and to behold Thy lifegiving Passions, whereby we have been delivered! *(Twice) Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*[^851]
She who in latter times gave birth unto Thee, O Christ, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, cried out: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! Crying she said: How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by angels, now voluntarily crucified by lawless men, O Savior? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^852]  
Beholding now the light of our souls, O Christ, Thy most precious Cross, and bowing down before it, with joy let us clearly cry aloud: Glory to Thee Who wast pleased to be lifted up upon it! Glory to Thee Who hast enlightened all creation thereby, in which we unceasingly glorify Thee with hymns!

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*  
When Thine unwedded Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, lamenting maternally she spoke: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Savior? How dost Thou, the Life of all, partake of death, wishing as the Tenderhearted One to make alive the dead?

*Psalm 50. And the Canon of the precious Cross. But that of the Menaion is sung at Compline.*  
*The Canon, whose acrostic is: We all sing of the Tree that is worshipped.*[^853]  
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos: "The chariot-driver Pharaoh" (Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на):*[^854]  
Having come today unto the Lord's Cross, with a pure mind and pious understanding, come, let us bow down! For it lies forth granting unto those that bow down before it a saving purification, radiance, and glory, and mercy.

*Troparion:*  
The lifegiving Cross, lying forth and beheld, pours forth a luminous radiance of grace; let us draw near and receive the illumination of gladness, and salvation, and forgiveness, offering praise unto the Lord.

*Troparion:*  
A strange sight lies forth unto those that behold: the precious Cross, and as a fountain it bubbles over with divine gifts, and quenches transgressions, and looses sicknesses, and strengthens understandings, unto those that purely come unto it.

*Troparion:*  
The rod dividing the sea prefigured the victory of the Cross, whereby through faith we sail unwetted across the unstable water of life, and escape the torrent of all sin, and are filled with divine quietness![^855]

*Theotokion:*  
When I ineffably gave birth to Thee, O Child, I escaped travails; and how am I now all filled with pangs? For I behold Thee hanging as a malefactor upon the Tree, Who hast suspended the earth unsupported, said the All-pure One, weeping.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens" (Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀):*[^856]  
The Cross, the bestower of all good things, is seen bowed down before, and all creation keeps festival in joy, being enlightened by the grace of our God Who voluntarily was lifted up upon it.

*Troparion:*  
Illumined by the light of the rays of the Cross at the mid-point of sacred abstinence, let us flee the darkening of sins, and cry aloud: O Tenderhearted Lord, the Enlightenment of all, glory to Thee!

*Troparion:*  
We hymn thee, O Cross, and kiss thee with faith, asking: By thy might deliver us from the snares of the enemy, and direct all of us who honor thee unto the haven of salvation!

*Theotokion:*  
Beholding Life put to death upon the Cross, and unable to bear the pain in her womb, the Pure Virgin lamented, crying aloud: Woe is me, O my Savior, what has the lawless people rendered unto Thee?

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:*[^857]  
No sooner was the Tree of Thy Cross planted, O Christ, than the foundations of death were shaken, O Lord. For man, whom Hades swallowed with longing, he released with trembling; Thou hast shown us Thy salvation, O Holy One, and we glorify Thee, O Savior and God: have mercy on us!

*Glory... the end. Both now... [Theotokion]:*[^858]  
Today the prophetic word is fulfilled: for behold, we worship at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord; and having tasted the Tree of salvation, we have obtained freedom from sinful passions, through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O only Lover of mankind!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "Thou art my strength" (Ты́ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость):*[^859]  
A mighty protection and restoration for men, an unconquerable weapon of faith, behold, the saving Cross is seen lying forth, and it sanctifies and enlightens with grace the hearts of all who come in faith.

*Troparion:*  
Lying forth at the mid-point of abstinence in the midst of all, the all-good, precious Cross in the midst of the earth, upon which Thou wast voluntarily lifted up, O Lover of mankind, sanctifies the world by its veneration, and puts to flight the hosts of demons.

*Troparion:*  
Let heaven and all the earth rejoice together! The passion-bearers, the martyrs, the apostles, the souls of the righteous now joyfully rejoice, seeing the God-given Tree that saves all lying forth in the midst, and sanctifying the faithful with grace.

*Troparion:*  
Having not kept Thy laws, O Lord, unconscionable that I am, I am to be condemned when Thou comest from heaven to judge human deeds; from now therefore I cry unto Thee: By the power of Thy Cross convert me and save me; grant me tears of repentance!

*Theotokion:*  
From a virginal womb, O Child, I gave birth unto Thee, and beholding Thee hanging upon the Tree I am in doubt, and comprehend not the height of the mystery and the depth of Thy great judgments, cried the All-pure One; whom with unceasing voices as the Mother of God we call blessed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Why hast Thou cast me off" (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ):*[^860]  
Shout aloud, ye nations! Sing, and make melody! Leap for joy, ye tribes, unto God Who has given the Cross, an unshakable foundation: whereof we now enjoy the fruit in the time of abstinence, nourishing soul and thought.

*Troparion:*  
All the noetic hosts honor thee, O all-honorable Cross, and we men touching thee today with clay lips draw forth sanctification and blessing in love, glorifying Him Who was nailed upon thee!

*Troparion:*  
Heal the temporal passions of my soul, O Tenderhearted Lord, and make me a worshipper of Thy holy Passions; show me better through the time of the fast, and grant me departure from evils, O All-good One!

*Theotokion:*  
Beholding upon the Cross Him Who for goodness' sake was born of thee beyond understanding, O All-immaculate One, thou wast wounded in thy bowels, and didst say: Woe is me, O divine Child, how dost Thou suffer for all? I bow down before Thy tenderhearted compassion, O Lord!

*Triode [by Lord Joseph], Tone 4. Ode 5. Heirmos: "All things were astonished" (Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́чєскаѧ):*[^861]  
The luminaries hid their light, seeing Thee the only Righteous One, Long-suffering, nailed unrighteously upon the Tree, and exposing by Thy might the dark powers of the deceitful one, O Savior.

*Troparion:*  
Let us wash our faces with the water of fasting, and kiss the Tree; for Christ the only One of all was lifted up upon it, clothed in mortal flesh, that He might slay him who had slain us.

*Troparion:*  
O all-honorable Cross, the boast of apostles, the weapon of passion-bearers, the glory of priests, the rampart of the venerable, the guardian of all the faithful: preserve and sanctify those who faithfully bow down before thee!

*Theotokion:*  
Seeing the Cluster of grapes, Whom she sprouted, hanging upon the Tree, the Vine, the Virgin, cried aloud: O Child, drop down sweetness that consumes the drunkenness of the enemies, who have crucified Thee, the Long-suffering One of all!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], the same Tone [Tone 4]. Ode 5. Heirmos: "Early in the morning from the night" (И҆зъ но́щи оу҆́тренююще):*[^862]  
Today Thy Cross is set forth, the worshipped Tree of life, and the world rejoices at the coming of the Spirit, kissing it.

*Troparion:*  
Seeing Thy Cross lying forth, the heavenly ministers hymn Thee, O Lord, and demons tremble, unable to endure Thy might!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Let us glorify the Trinity orthodoxly, with the Father and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: one Godhead, a tri-hypostatic Unity.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee as Mother and Virgin do all we the faithful orthodoxly confess, who hast ineffably given birth unto Christ our God, the only Plenteous in mercy.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Thou Who wast crucified upon pine, and cedar, and cypress, O Savior and God, sanctify all of us, and make us worthy to behold Thy lifegiving Passions![^863]

*Heirmos:*  
*Early in the morning from the night, enlighten us, O lifegiving Cross of Christ, who with fear bow down before thee, and ever make the saving day to shine forth unto us!*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Cleanse me, O Savior" (Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ Сп҃се):*[^864]  
When the Cross was planted upon the earth, the downfall of demons came to pass; beholding which now lying forth most gloriously, and kissing it, we rise up from sinful falls.

*Troparion:*  
Lifting Thee up, O Christ, as King and Lord, Who hast given us the Cross as an unbreakable rampart, by kissing it we rejoice at the mid-point of divine abstinence.

*Troparion:*  
Bestowing great gifts upon us all, the Lord's Cross is seen lying forth; let us draw near, O men, and draw forth the illumination of heart and soul!

*Theotokion:*  
Strengthen us, O Pure Lady, to be visited [by God] from all malice, and ever help us to abstain from evil and deceitful deeds, thou who art the intercession of all men!

*Kontakion, Tone 7:*[^865]  
No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden, for upon it has come a marvelous bond, the Tree of the Cross; the sting of death and the victory of Hades have been driven away; for Thou hast stood forth, O my Savior, crying unto those in Hades: Enter ye again into Paradise!

*Ikos:*[^866]  
Pilate set up three crosses upon Golgotha: two of the thieves, and one of the Giver of life, Whom Hades beheld and said unto those below: O my servants and my powers! Who, having driven nails into my heart, has suddenly pierced me with a wooden spear? And I am torn asunder, I ache in my inward parts, my belly is wounded, my senses trouble my spirit, and I am constrained to disgorge Adam and those descended from Adam who were given unto me by a tree; for behold, this Tree leads them again into Paradise!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The condescension of God" (Бж҃їѧ снизхожде́нїа):*[^867]  
He Who is above times appears in time bearing flesh, and heals our temporal passions for goodness' sake in the time of abstinence, wherein this divine Cross lies forth, sanctifying us.

*Troparion:*  
We sing, we glorify, we bow down, and we magnify Thy might, O Lord, in that Thou hast given unto us Thy servants the divine Cross as an unfailing delight, and the guardian of our souls and bodies.

*Troparion:*  
Show me not, O Lord, abased by evil things in the day of testing, nor cast me away from Thy presence put to shame; but show compassion, save me by Thy precious Cross, in that Thou art all-good!

*Troparion:*  
Moses prefigured thy grace, O Cross, sweetening the bitterest waters with wood; for from the bitterness of passions have we been delivered by thy power; therefore sweeten us who kiss thee now in compunction of soul![^868]

*Theotokion:*  
Enlarge the narrowness of my mind by thy prayer, O Lady, who hast constrained all the wiles of the adversary, and guide me by the narrow way to go unto the spaciousness of life, O Mother of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Sevenfold did the tyrant burn the furnace" (Седмери́цею пе́щь):*[^869]  
With a stick of wood of old the divine Elisha drew the axe out of the river, prefiguring thee from afar, O all-honorable Cross; for from the deep of deception have we surely been brought unto the faith by thee, being vouchsafed to behold this present day and in faith to bow down before thee, embracing salvation![^870]

*Troparion:*  
Jacob clearly prefigured thee from afar in his blessings, O all-honorable Cross; and we, having been vouchsafed by grace to behold thee, all approach with unhesitating faith, and touching thee, embrace abundant blessing, and light, and salvation, and forgiveness of transgressions![^871]

*Troparion:*  
Having made ourselves white with virtuous deeds, let us draw near, rejoicing at the mid-point of abstinence, and let us kiss the Lord's Cross in faith, that, guided by its power, we may finish the course in good purpose, and, illuminated, reach the divine Passion!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Consubstantial, co-unoriginate, co-everlasting, sharing one throne, an essence simple indeed, yet divided in Persons: the unbegotten Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, uncreated Being and Godhead, do all we hymn and sing: Ye priests, bless; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*  
Now as a gentle Lamb do I behold Thee hanging and nailed by the lawless unto the Cross, O my Savior Who art before all ages; I am torn with wailings, and held fast by motherly pangs, cried the All-pure One; whom with unceasing voices we hymn as is meet unto all ages.

*Triode [by Lord Joseph], Ode 8. Heirmos: "The pious youths in the furnace" (Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ):*[^872]  
Seeing the Passion of Thee Who art beyond passion, creation suffered with Thee, O Long-suffering One: the sun was darkened, the rocks were split, all the earth quaked, crying in fear: Praise the Lord, all ye His works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
Rebuke the wild beasts of the reeds, O my Compassionate God, which inwardly afflict me, O Savior Who didst endure dishonorable insult, beaten with a reed, O Christ, wishing to honor all who were dishonored by the ancient transgression!

*Troparion:*  
When I take into my mind Thine awesome Judgment-seat, O most righteous Judge, I tremble and am astonished, fearing and lamenting as I contemplate my deeds worthy of condemnation; therefore I pray Thy plenteous compassion, O Savior: let not the multitude of mine evils overcome Thee!

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth unto Thee without pain, I am subject unto pains at Thy Crucifixion, and my womb is burned up, for Thou art fastened with nails and pierced in Thy side with a spear, O Long-suffering One! Cried the All-pure Virgin; whom in one accord as the Theotokos we hymn!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], Ode 8. Heirmos: "All the works of God" (Всѧ̑ дѣла̀ Бж҃їѧ):*[^873]  
Let the mountains drop down righteousness with understanding, and let the hills rejoice, seeing the Tree of the Cross bowed down before, which we exalt above all, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*  
Awesome is the grace of Thy Cross: for it puts to flight the hosts of demons, bestowing waters of healings upon men; therefore we hymn Thee, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
Let us hymn the unoriginate Father, and the Son with the Holy Spirit: the consubstantial Trinity, one Principle, and praising God, singing unto Him piously unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*  
Every earthborn tongue hymns thee, O Virgin, for thou hast shone forth the unapproachable Light illuminating the world, Christ God: Whom we ever exalt above all unto the ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
The ends of the earth rejoice at the veneration of Thy Cross, O Lover of mankind; the angels in heaven celebrate today with us, singing unto Thee, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*All the works of God, and all creation, bless ye the Lord; for unto men from the earth has the Light shone forth, and enlightened all the universe, and granted eternal life unto the world: sing, ye people, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Every ear was astonished" (Оу҆жасе́сѧ):*[^874]  
Thou hast healed my crushing, O All-good One, being nailed of old upon the Cross with nails in Thy hands and feet, O King of all, and pierced in the ribs, given vinegar and gall to drink, O Delight of all, Glory of sweetness, eternal Redemption!

*Troparion:*  
Fairer than sapphire and gold, radiant as the sun art thou, O divine Cross, circumscribed indeed by place as thou liest, and terribly encompassed everywhere by noetic powers, yet with the divine rays of thy strength enlightening the fulness of the universe![^875]

*Troparion:*  
The Cross is the haven of the storm-tossed, the guide and confirmation of those who go astray, the glory of Christ, the strength of the apostles and prophets, the might of fasters, the refuge of all men: beholding it lying forth in the midst, let us all kiss it in abstinence!

*Troparion:*  
When Thou shalt come upon the earth to judge the world which Thou didst create, O Lord, while Thy host of angels goes before Thee, and the Cross shines forth brighter than the rays of the sun: by its prayer show compassion upon me, and save me who have sinned above all men!

*Theotokion:*  
Without corruption I gave birth unto Thee from the womb, Whom the Father begat before all ages: and how do corruptible men tear Thee, O Savior, and dig through Thy ribs with a spear, and nail Thy hands and feet inhumanly? Cried the All-pure One; whom worthily we magnify.

*Triode [by Lord Joseph], Ode 9. Heirmos: "Let every earthborn mortal" (Всѧ́къ земноро́дный):*[^876]  
By the Tree of the Cross Thou hast withered the flame of sin, O Christ; and stretching out Thy hands, Thou hast delivered from the hand of the adversary him who unrestrainedly stretched out his hands to eat the fruit: whose instigation alone Thou hast ordained to flee, O plenteous in mercy Christ!

*Troparion:*  
Vouchsafe us purely to behold Thy Passion, O Christ, which pours forth dispassion unto all men, as in dispassion we bow down before Thy precious Cross, overlooking our sins and making us sons of the true Resurrection!

*Troparion:*  
Having lifted up us who had fallen into death, by Thy Resurrection Thou hast dissolved the battle, and hast reconciled us unto Thy Father, having poured forth Thy blood for our sake, O Benefactor: therefore we glorify Thee as God, the all-powerful Redeemer!

*Theotokion:*  
O thou who alone didst give birth unto the Hypostatic Wisdom of God, make me wise, I pray thee, to flee the counsels, and snares, and subtle craft of the wise one of malice, O Theotokos, unwedded Virgin, steadfast refuge of the faithful!

*Another Triode [by Lord Theodore], Ode 9. Heirmos: "The seedless conception" (Безсѣ́менное Ржⷭ҇тво̀):*[^877]  
The precious Cross, which the hosts of angels embrace, we also bowing down do magnify!

*Troparion:*  
The all-pure Tree, whereby we who were formerly dead have become partakers of life, the Savior's Cross do we magnify!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
The unoriginate Father, the co-unoriginate Son, and the Holy Spirit sharing one throne, the Holy Trinity do we magnify!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
The unwedded Mother and Virgin do all we hymn, O Pure One: for without seed hast thou given birth unto the Creator!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Having vouchsafed us to behold thee and to bow down before thee, prosper us to anticipate the most holy Passion, O Cross of Christ!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*Thy seedless conception, O Pure Theotokos, Christ our God, with unceasing hymns do we magnify!*

*Photagogic of the tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 4:*[^878]  
O Searcher of truth and Knower of secret things, O Lord, Thou didst condemn the Pharisee overcome by vainglory and seeking justification by deeds of virtues, but the Publican praying with compunction and condemned by that other Thou didst justify; of whose repentance show us also to be emulators, O Thou Who wast crucified, and make us worthy of forgiveness, in that Thou art the Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*For the Martyrs:*[^879]  
Who is not astonished beholding the good fight wherein ye contended, O holy martyrs? How being in the flesh, ye conquered the bodiless enemy! Having confessed Christ, having armed yourselves with the Cross, therefore worthily have ye appeared as expellers of demons and warriors against barbarians: unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Idiomelon, Tone 8:*[^880]  
Today the Master of creation and Lord of glory is nailed upon the Cross, and is pierced in the ribs; He tastes gall and vinegar, the Sweetness of the Church; He is encompassed with a crown of thorns, Who covers the heaven with clouds; He is clothed with a garment of mockery, and struck by a clay hand, He Who created man with His hand; He is beaten upon His shoulders, Who arrays the heaven with clouds; He receives spitting and wounds, reproaches and buffetings, and endures all things for the sake of me who am condemned, my Redeemer and God, that He may save the world from deception, in that He is tenderhearted!

*The First Hour, and the dismissal.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*  
Before Thy Cross we bow down, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 77 [LXX 77:38, 1], Tone 6:*[^881]  
And He is compassionate, and will forgive their sins!  
*Verse: Give ear, O My people, unto My law.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 29:13–23):*[^882]  
Thus says the Lord: This people draws near unto Me with their mouth, and with their lips they honor Me, but their heart is far from Me; but in vain do they worship Me, teaching commandments and doctrines of men. Therefore, behold, I will proceed to remove this people, and I will remove them, and I will destroy the wisdom of the wise, and hide the understanding of the prudent. Woe unto them that make counsel deeply, and not by the Lord! Woe unto them that take counsel in secret, and their works shall be in darkness, and they will say: Who has seen us? And who shall understand us or what we do? Shall ye not be counted as the clay of the potter? Shall the thing formed say unto him that formed it: Thou hast not formed me? Or the work to him that made it: Thou hast not made me wisely? Is it not yet a very little while, and Lebanon shall be turned like Mount Carmel, and Carmel shall be counted as a forest? And in that day the deaf shall hear the words of this book, and the eyes of the blind out of darkness and out of gloom shall see. And the poor shall rejoice because of the Lord with gladness, and men that were in despair shall be filled with joy. For the lawless man has failed, and the proud has perished, and all that commit iniquity in malice are cut off; and they that make men to sin in word, and they will lay a stumbling-block for all that reprove in the gates, for they have turned aside the righteous in unjust things. Therefore thus says the Lord concerning the house of Jacob, which He separated from Abraham: Jacob shall not now be put to shame, neither shall Israel now change his face! But when their children see My works, for My sake they shall sanctify My name, and they shall sanctify the Holy One of Jacob, and shall fear the God of Israel.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 78 [LXX 78:9, 1], Tone 5:*[^883]  
Help us, O God our Savior!  
*Verse: O God, the heathen are come into Thine inheritance.*

*And the veneration of the Cross takes place after the dismissal of the Hours, after the final Trisagion, and we sing the stichera, Tone 2: "Come, ye faithful" (see fol. 293):*[^884]

*And the precious Cross is lifted up, and they carry it into the skeuophylakion.*

## Friday of the Fourth Week of the Holy Great Fast: Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 stichoi; and we sing the Idiomelon twice; and 4 Martyrika of the tone, and in the Menaion 4.*

*Idiomelon, Tone 7:*[^885]  
Having enslaved the dignity of my soul to the passions, I became as a beast; and I am unable to look up unto Thee, the Most High; but bowing down low, O Christ, as the Publican I pray, crying unto Thee: O God, cleanse me, and save me! *(Twice)*

*Glory: For the departed, by John of Damascus.*[^886] *Both now: Theotokion of the 1st tone.*

*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light".*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 79 [LXX 79:2, 3], Tone 4:*[^887]  
Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, Thou that leadest Joseph like a sheep!  
*Verse: Thou that sittest upon the Cherubim, show Thyself!*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 12:1–7):*[^888]  
The Lord said unto Abram: Go forth out of thy land, and from thy kindred, and from the house of thy father, and go into the land which I shall show thee. And I will make of thee a great nation, and I will bless thee, and magnify thy name, and thou shalt be blessed. And I will bless them that bless thee, and curse them that curse thee; and in thee shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed. And Abram went, as the Lord spoke unto him, and Lot went with him; and Abram was seventy and five years old when he departed from Haran. And Abram took Sarai his wife, and Lot his brother's son, and all their substance that they had acquired, and every soul that they had gotten in Haran; and they went forth to go into the land of Canaan. And Abram traversed the land in the length thereof unto the place of Shechem, unto the high oak; and the Canaanites were then dwelling in the land. And the Lord appeared unto Abram, and said unto him: Unto thy seed will I give this land. And Abram built there an altar unto the Lord Who appeared unto him.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 80 [LXX 80:2, 3], Tone 4:*[^889]  
Rejoice in God our Helper!  
*Verse: Take a psalm, and bring the timbrel!*

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 14:15–26):*[^890]  
The simple believes every word, but the prudent comes unto repentance. A wise man fearing turns aside from evil; but the fool, trusting in himself, mingles with the lawless. A quick-tempered man acts without counsel; but a prudent man endures many things. Fools divide malice; but the prudent shall retain understanding. The evil shall stumble before the good, and the ungodly shall minister at the gates of the righteous. Friends will hate poor friends; but the friends of the rich are many. He that dishonors the needy sins; but he that has mercy on the poor is blessed. The erring (unrighteous) devise evil things; but the good devise mercy and truth; workers of evil know not mercy and faith, but merciful deeds and faith are with workers of good. In everyone that is careful there is abundance; but the pleasure-lover and careless shall be in want. The crown of the wise is their wealth; but the way of fools is evil. A faithful witness shall deliver a soul from evil; but a deceitful man kindles falsehoods. In the fear of the Lord is hope of strength; and unto His children He leaves a confirmation (of peace).

## Saturday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast: Matins

*Alleluia, and Troparia, Tone 2:*  
*"Apostles, martyrs..." Glory: "Remember, O Lord..." Both now: "Holy Mother..." And the stichology.*[^891]

*Then the Martyria Sessional Hymns of the tone.*

*And after the Amomos [Psalm 118] we sing the Troparia: "The choir of the saints..." And after the litany, the Sessional Hymn for the departed, Tone 5: "Give rest with the righteous, O our Savior..." Glory: the end thereof. Both now: Theotokion: "O Thou Who didst shine forth from the Virgin..."*[^892]

*Then the Canon from the Menaion, and of the patron saint of the monastery, and the present Four-Ode Canons [Tetraodia] according to their order.*[^893]

*The Four-Ode Canon, whose acrostic is: A song for the martyrs.*[^894]  
*Ode 6, by Lord Joseph. And the biblical canticles are chanted with stichology.*  
*Tone 4. Heirmos: "By the tempest of life" (Пꙋчи́ною жите́йскою):*[^895]  
*Troparion:*  
Ye surpassed the bounds of the flesh, O sufferers, by much patience, having endured torments and afflictions; therefore do ye relieve every sickness and affliction of those who praise you in song.

*Troparion:*  
The host of holy passion-bearers was joined unto myriads of angels, and prays God the King of all to deliver us from myriad transgressions, as having been well-pleasing unto Christ.

*For the Departed:*  
Having been put to death and having fallen asleep in the tomb, O Christ, Thou didst raise up the dead; and unto those that have died in the faith Thou dost grant the wealth of Thy goodness, O Christ: give them rest with all the saints!

*Theotokion:*  
God the Divine Word, seeking to renew man, O Pure One, is incarnate of thee and is seen as earthly; Whom unceasingly beseech that we may find mercy in the time of answering.

*Another [Canon] by Lord Theodore, the same tone. Heirmos: "In the tempest of sin" (Бꙋ́рею грѣхо́вною):*[^896]  
*Troparion:*  
Sparing not flesh and blood, O holy ones, ye stood undaunted before every torment, not denying Christ; therefore from heaven Christ sent down crowns unto you.

*Troparion:*  
Let us adorn the solemn feast of the martyrs, having been enlightened by deeds, and let us cry aloud with divinely-inspired hymns: Ye are truly daystars upon the earth, O martyrs of Christ!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn [Trinity Hymn]:*  
O Holy Trinity, I glorify Thee, unoriginate Essence, one God, one Lord, three Persons: Father, Son, and Spirit: unbegotten, begotten, proceeding, identical and ever-existing!

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
O blessed Bride of God! How didst thou give birth without a husband, and didst remain even as before? For thou didst bear God, a fearful wonder; but pray that they who hymn thee may be saved!

*Verse: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
*Martyrikon:*  
Beholding the severing of your limbs, ye delight in blood, rejoicing; but diligently pray to the Lord for us, O ever-glorious martyrs!

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
*For the Departed:*  
Thou Who didst fashion me from the earth and make me alive, and didst say unto me to return again unto the earth: the servants whom Thou hast taken, give rest, and raise them up from mortal corruption, O Lord!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*Submerged by the tempest of sin, as though held fast in the belly of the sea monster, with the prophet I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Lord, and save me!*

*Kontakion: "With the saints give rest..."*[^897]  
*Ikos: "Thou alone art immortal..." (see fol. 17).*[^898]

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "Unto the golden image" (Ѻ҃́бразꙋ злато́мꙋ):*[^899]  
*Troparion:*  
Arrayed in divine incorruption by the stripping off of corruptible flesh, O sufferers, ye now stand radiant before Him Who for our sake received flesh from the incorruptible Woman; therefore clothe me, stripped bare by malice, in the garment of salvation!

*Troparion:*  
Having lived in abstinence, O company of sufferers, strengthen us to run unimpeded the course of abstinence, as having bravely proclaimed Christ in the arena, and standing crown-bearing before the throne, mentally delighting with the angels.

*For the Departed:*  
By the prayers of Thy holy martyrs, O God, show Thy servants who have fallen asleep in the faith to be dwellers of Paradise, and make them worthy of the spiritual light, unceasingly crying unto Thee: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
Thee alone who art good do we beseech, O Virgin: beatify us who are afflicted, and diligently pray Christ, Who is by nature good, that doing good deeds we may fulfill the time of abstinence, who sing unto Him: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "He Who spoke upon the mountain" (Глаго́лавый на горѣ̀):*[^900]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou Who hast magnified all Thy saints, having made them marvelous with signs in the world: blessed art Thou unto ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*  
Having passed through every kind of wound, and having not submitted to bend the knee to Baal, ye received crowns of glory from God, O martyrs of Christ!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn [Trinity Hymn]:*  
O Essence worshipped in the Trinity of Unity, Father, Son, and Spirit: preserve those who hymn Thee, O God of our fathers!

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Mother, all-radiant Maiden, our sole intercession before God: cease not, O Sovereign Lady, to pray that we may be saved!

*Verse: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord has shown wonders.*  
*Martyrikon:*  
Having soldiered for the Immortal King, and having shown perfect faith toward Him, ye poured out your blood for Him, O martyrs!

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.*  
*For the Departed:*  
Where Thy light of life wells forth, transfer Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast translated from temporal things, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*Thou Who didst speak on the mountain with Moses, and didst manifest the image of Virginity in the bush: blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "In the fiery flame" (Во ѻ҃́гни́ пла́меннѣмъ):*[^901]  
*Troparion:*  
O renowned sufferers of Christ, and honorable in God: by your great prayers unto Him deliver us who praise your memory from great transgressions and from the torments there!

*Troparion:*  
O company of martyrs, chosen hundredfold array of Christ God and truly steadfast army: sanctify our mind and heart during these holy days of the fast by your holy prayers!

*For the Departed:*  
Deliver from the tormenting worm, and the gnashing of teeth, O Lord, and the unillumined outer darkness, all whom Thou hast received in faith; and appoint them where the light of Thy face, O Christ, shines unto ages!

*Theotokion:*  
Having beheld the precious Cross of Christ, O Pure Theotokos, and having worshipped from the heart, vouchsafe us by thy prayers unto the Master to behold also the Honorable Passions, having been cleansed from the passions!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "The earth, and all that is therein" (Землѧ̀, и҆ всѧ̑ ꙗ҆́же на не́й):*[^902]  
*Troparion:*  
O good exchange whereby ye found life through death, O hieromartyrs of Christ, not fearing fire and sword, frost and beasts, and crying aloud: Praise ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
The angelic choir above and we earthly mortals below praise the marvelous sufferings and the exploits of your courage, O martyrs of Christ, blessing and praising the Lord, and supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn [Trinity Hymn]:*  
Light and Life and Lives, I revere Thee, Father, Son, and proceeding Spirit: one Nature, three Hypostases; hymning the one God, I bless Thee, I praise Thee, O Lord, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages!

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Who of earthborn mortals shall not hymn thee, O undefiled and pure dove? For thou didst bear for us the great Light, the Wealth of life, Jesus the Savior: Whom we praise and laud as Lord, and supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Verse: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
*Martyrikon:*  
Glorifying your marvelous exploits, O martyrs, we bless and worship the Benefactor and God Who strengthened you in the arena of contest: Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
*For the Departed:*  
Being the Lord and God of death and life, Who dost raise up those who have piously fallen asleep, translate them thither into the habitations of the righteous, who bless and praise Thee, O Lord, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*The earth, and all that is therein, the sea and all fountains, the heavens of heavens, light and darkness, frost and heat, ye sons of men, ye priests: bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "For He that is mighty has done" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ сотвори́ мнѣ̀):*[^903]  
*Troparion:*  
Being unbeguiling beacons, O sufferers of Christ, illuminate our thoughts, establish us to perform the radiant and pure desires of God!

*Troparion:*  
Ye appear as weapons slaying the enemies, O valiant sufferers of Christ; but deliver us from the arrows of the crafty one by your intercessions!

*For the Departed:*  
Give rest in Abraham's bosom, O Compassionate One, unto Thy servants who have departed in faith from us unto Thee, the Creator and all-good Lord of all!

*Theotokion:*  
Mortify the stirrings of my flesh, O thou who beyond understanding didst bear God in the flesh; and grant illumination to my mind, being the pure Cloud of light!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "We all magnify" (Велича́емъ всѝ):*[^904]  
*Troparion:*  
We all hymn your memories, O all-praised martyrs, beholding the exploits of your sacred sufferings, and having wondered, we magnify Christ!

*Troparion:*  
Suffering, the passion-bearers said one to another: Let us not spare the flesh; come, let us die for Christ, that we may live rejoicing unceasingly unto all ages!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn [Trinity Hymn]:*  
O Trinity in one Nature: unbegotten Father, and begotten Son, and proceeding Spirit: preserve unharmed by Thy mercy those who hymn Thee!

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, all-honorable and pure one, boast of virginity, confirmation of mothers, help of men, and joy of the world, Mary Mother and handmaid of our God!

*Verse: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
*Martyrikon:*  
O choir of saints, receive my supplication, and as ye have vouchsafed me to kiss the Cross and to bow down before the saving Passion, pray to Christ for me!

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.*  
*For the Departed:*  
Pardon, remit, O Compassionate One, unto those who have been translated unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and give them rest in the habitations of the elect; for Thou art the Life and the Resurrection!

*Katavasia [Heirmos]:*  
*We all magnify Thy love for mankind, O Christ our Savior, the glory of Thy servants and crown of the faithful, Who hast magnified the memory of Her that gave Thee birth!*

*The Exapostilaria are written on the Second Saturday of the Fast (see fol. 107).*[^905]

*At the Praises [Lauds], the Martyrika of the tone.*

*And at the Aposticha, prosomoia by Lord Theophanes.*[^906]

*At the Liturgy, the Typika, and the Beatitudes of the tone.*

*Prokeimenon of the Apostle for the departed.*[^907] *The Apostle, Pericope 390 [Hebrews 6:9–12].*[^908]

*And for the departed, to the Corinthians, Pericope 163 [1 Corinthians 15:58–16:3].*[^909]

*Alleluia, for the departed.*[^910]

*Gospel from Mark, Pericope 31 [Mark 7:31–37].*[^911] *And for the departed, from John, Pericope 16 [John 5:24–30].*[^912]

*Communion Hymn: "Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous" [Psalm 32:1].*[^913] *Another, for the departed: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord, and their memorial is from generation to generation" [Psalm 64:5; 101:13].*[^914]

Christ hangs upon the Cross beneath the titulus 'INRI'; to the right stands His All-Holy Mother with the holy women; to the left stands the Beloved Disciple John with the Roman centurion; and at the base of the Cross kneels Mary Magdalene.]

## FOURTH SUNDAY OF THE GREAT FAST: COMMEMORATION OF OUR VENERABLE FATHER JOHN OF THE LADDER (CLIMACUS)

*On Saturday evening at "Lord, I have cried", we sing 3 Resurrection stichera, and 4 Anatolian [Eastern] stichera, and 3 from the Menaion.*

*But other later typika have appointed the office of our Venerable Father John of the Ladder to be sung.*[^915]

*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^916]  
*Troparion:*  
O venerable Father John, truly didst thou continually bear the high praises of God in thy throat, meditating all-wisely upon the practical, divinely-inspired words, and wast enriched by the grace flowing thence, becoming blessed, and overturning the counsels of all the ungodly.

*The same Podoben:*  
O all-glorious Father John, having cleansed thy soul with fountains of tears, and having propitiated God by all-night standings, thou didst soar, O blessed one, unto His love and beauty, which thou now worthily enjoyest, rejoicing unceasingly with thy fellow sufferers, O divinely-wise and venerable father!

*The same Podoben:*  
O venerable Father John, having winged thy mind unto God by faith, thou didst abhor the unsteady confusion of the world, and taking up thy cross, didst follow the All-Seeing One: having enslaved the unbridled body to the mind through the exploits of ascetic training, by the power of the Holy Spirit.

*Glory, Tone 5:*[^917]  
O venerable Father, having listened unto the voice of the Lord's Gospel, thou didst forsake the world, counting wealth and glory as nothing; whence thou didst cry aloud unto all: Love God, and ye shall find eternal grace; prefer nothing before His love, so that when He comes in His glory, ...ye shall find rest with all the saints: by whose prayers, O Christ, preserve and save our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion of the 1st tone.*

*At the Aposticha, stichera of the Octoechos.*

*Glory: For the Venerable One, Tone 2, Idiomelon:*[^918]  
Let us honor John, an angel on earth and a man of God in the heavens, the adornment of the world, the sweetness of good things, the boast of ascetic virtues; for planted in the house of God, he flourished righteously, and like a cedar in the wilderness he multiplied the flocks of Christ's rational sheep in holiness and righteousness!

*Both now: Theotokion, the same tone: "O new wonder" (Ѡ҆ чꙋдесѐ но́вагѡ).*

*"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Troparion, Tone 1:*[^919]  
A dweller of the wilderness and an angel in the body, and a wonderworker wast thou shown to be, O our God-bearing father John: having received heavenly gifts by fasting, vigilance, and prayer, thou healest the diseased and the souls of them that with faith have recourse unto thee: Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who works healings for all through thee!

*At Matins:*  
*According to the usual order.*

*The Canons: Sunday Resurrectional with Theotokion, to 6; and the present of the Triodion, to 4; and of the Saint, to 4.*[^920]

*Canon of the Triodion, Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos: "Unto God the Savior" (Спаси́телю Бг҃ꙋ):*[^921]  
*Troparion:*  
I am become like unto him that fell into the hands of robbers, and by their beatings was left half-dead by them, O Savior: and I too am thus wounded by my sins.

*Troparion:*  
Despise me not grievously suffering, he cried weeping, O Savior, having squandered Thy wealth upon robbers: and I too thus pray: Have compassion and save me!

*Troparion:*  
Having healed my mind slain by sins in wounds from the unrighteous robbers of wicked thoughts, O Christ Savior, save me in that Thou art plenteous in mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
All-pure Mother of Christ, unceasingly beseech God Who was incarnate of thee, and Who was not separated from the bosom of the Father, to save from every circumstance those whom He created!

*Another Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Having traversed the water as dry land" (Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ):*[^922]  
*Troparion:*  
Ascending into the immaterial and spiritual light from the grief of material things, O venerable John, by thy prayers unto the Lord illuminate me!

*Troparion:*  
Having sucked the sweetness of abstinence, thou didst cast away the bitterness of the passions; therefore more than honey and the honeycomb dost thou sweeten our senses, O father.

*Troparion:*  
Having ascended unto the height of virtues, having spat upon pleasures dragging downward, thou wast shown to be the sweetness of salvation, O venerable father, unto thy flock.

*Theotokion:*  
O thou who ineffably didst give birth unto the Wisdom and Word of the Father: heal the grievous wound of my soul, and quiet the affliction of my heart!

*Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth..."*[^923]

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Establish us, O God" (Оу҆тверди́ ны Бж҃е):*[^924]  
*Troparion:*  
Traveling grievously the road of this life, O Christ, I am wounded by robbers in the passions: but raise me up, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*  
The robbers shattered my mind, and left me in wounds, half-dead through my transgressions: but heal me, O Lord!

*Troparion:*  
The passions stripped me bare of Thy commandments, O Savior Christ, and I am wounded with pleasures: but pour out mercy upon me!

*Theotokion:*  
Unceasingly pray, O Pure One, unto Him Who proceeded from thy loins, that they who hymn thee as the Mother of God may be delivered from the deception of the devil!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "Thou art the confirmation" (Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе):*[^925]  
*Troparion:*  
With the live coal of ascetic training thou didst all-gloriously burn up the thorns of the passions, and thou warmtest the assemblies of monastics.

*Troparion:*  
Having compounded thyself wholly into an odor of divine fragrance, O venerable one, as myrrh of sanctification from ascetic spices.

*Troparion:*  
Meditating upon the laws of ascetic training, thou didst drown the passions in the streams of thy tears, even as other Pharaohs now.

*Theotokion:*  
Restrain the unsteady confusion of my thoughts, O Pure One, Mother of God, directing their motion unto Thy Son!

*Sessional Hymn [of the Cross], Tone 5, Podoben: "Strange wonder" (Стра́нное):*[^926]  
Holding fast Thy most pure Cross, O our Savior, as a weapon of salvation, through it we cry unto Thee: Save us, O God of all, Who didst voluntarily suffer for us, in that Thou art plenteous in mercy!

*Glory: For the Venerable One, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ):*[^927]  
Having ascended unto heaven by virtues, manifestly established, thou didst piously ascend unto the unmeasured depth of divine vision: having overcome all demonic snares, thou dost protect men...

...men from their harm, O John, ladder of virtues; and now thou dost pray that thy servants may be saved.

*Both now: Theotokion, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ):*[^928]  
He Who sits upon the Cherubic Throne, and dwells in the bosom of the Father, sits as upon His holy throne, O Lady, in thy bosom according to the flesh; for God has thus become King over all the nations, and with understanding we now sing unto Him: Whom do thou also entreat, O Pure One, that thy servants may be saved!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "I have heard, O Lord, Thy report" (Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭ҇и слꙋ́хъ тво́й):*[^929]  
*Troparion:*  
Robbers stole away my divine work, and left me tormented with wounds.

*Troparion:*  
My unstable thoughts drew me away from Thy commandments, O Savior, and therefore I am wounded with transgressions.

*Troparion:*  
When the Levite saw me wounded with stripes, he passed by, O my Savior: but do Thou save me!

*Theotokion:*  
As Sovereign Lady we glorify thee, O unwedded Mother of God, and unto thy haven do we, the faithful, flee for refuge.

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "I have heard, O Lord" (Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭ҇и):*[^930]  
*Troparion:*  
As a most fragrant garden of flowers and an ensouled paradise of virtues, thou didst blossom forth self-control, whereby thou hast nourished all who honor thee.

*Troparion:*  
Having acquired thee, O father, as a lawgiver of ascetic discipline and a most gentle rule for monastics, truly like Moses and David, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*  
Planted beside the waters of self-control, thou didst appear, O blessed father, as a fair-flowering vine-branch, bearing the clusters of piety.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast borne for us, O Mother of God, Him Who is subject to time, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father: Whom entreat to save those who sing of thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Rising early at the dawn, we cry unto Thee" (Оу҆́треннююще вопїе́мъ тѝ):*[^931]  
*Troparion:*  
O Jesus, visit the wounds of my soul, as of old Thou didst visit him that fell into the hands of robbers, and heal my sickness, O Christ, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*  
Painfully have I grown faint with the wounds of my transgressions, O my Christ, and thence lie naked of divine virtues: save me, I pray!

*Troparion:*  
When the priest saw me, and the Levite was manifestly unable to help, they passed by me; but Thou Thyself, being tender-hearted, hast now granted salvation and saved me.

*Theotokion:*  
Despise me not, O Master, I pray Thee, wretched and painfully wounded in mind by robbers; but have compassion, O Savior, through the prayers of Her that bore Thee!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "Why hast Thou cast me off" (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ):*[^932]  
*Troparion:*  
Having quenched all passions with the dew of thine ascetic labors, O blessed father, thou didst abundantly kindle with the fire of love and faith the lamp of self-control and the illumination of dispassion, and becamest a son of the day.

*Troparion:*  
By thy divine husbandry, O father, thou didst cultivate the cluster of faith and didst cast it into the winepresses, and didst work the labors of discipline; and filling the spiritual cup of self-control, thou gladdenest the hearts of thy flock.

*Troparion:*  
Having nobly endured the assaults and wounds of adversary foes, thou didst appear as a pillar of endurance, strengthening thy flock with the divine staff, and didst nourish them, O blessed one, upon the pasture of self-control and beside the water.

*Theotokion:*  
Following the utterances of thy lips, O all-pure Lady, we call thee blessed; for the Lord Who truly did great things with thee has magnified thee, and being born from thy womb, showed thee forth as the true Mother of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "The abyss encompassed me" (Ѡ҆би́де мѧ̀ бе́здна):*[^933]  
*Troparion:*  
I wasted a godly life in passions, O Master, and grievously wounded in every way by transgressions, I have fled unto Thee: have compassion on me, I pray!

*Troparion:*  
Robbers plundered my wealth and left me as dead, having wounded my mind with passions: but have compassion and save me, O Lord!

*Troparion:*  
When the Levite saw the pain of my wounds, not enduring the sores, he passed by me; but Thou Thyself, O Lover of mankind, didst pour out Thy rich mercy upon me.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee, the unburnt bush, and the mountain, and the ensouled ladder, and the heavenly gate, do we worthily glorify, O most glorious Mary, boast of the Orthodox!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "Cleanse me, O Savior" (Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ Сп҃се):*[^934]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou didst receive in thy soul the divine wealth of the Spirit: blameless prayer, purity, integrity, constant vigilance, and the labors of self-control, through which thou wast known as the house of God.

*Troparion:*  
Wisely didst thou pass beyond the baseness of material things below, and having winged thy mind with immaterial prayer, thou didst appear as an heir of heavenly rest by reason of the perfection of thy life.

*Troparion:*  
With the sweat of ascetic training thou didst truly quench the burning coals of the enemy's darts, and having kindled the fire of faith, didst burn up the insolence of unbelief and heresies.

*Theotokion:*  
From Zion on high the majesty of beauty shone forth, clothed from thee in the presentation of the flesh according to an ineffable union, O unwedded Bride, and illumined the world.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^935]  
Upon the height of self-control the Lord truly set thee, as an unerring star guiding the ends of the earth with light, O instructor John, our father!

*Ikos:*  
As a house of God in truth didst thou make thyself, O father, by thy divine virtues, manifestly adorning as radiant gold faith, hope, and true love, setting forth the divine laws, disciplined in self-control as one bodiless; having acquired understanding, courage, temperance, and humility, whereby thou wast exalted; therefore thou wast also enlightened by unceasing prayers, and didst receive the heavenly mansions, O instructor John, our father!

*Then is read the Synaxarion of the day.*[^936]  
*We read the Life of our Venerable Father John of the Ladder [Climacus], who reposed in peace.*  
*Verses:*  
*John, who in the flesh while alive was dead,*  
*Lives eternally, even when appearing dead and breathless;*  
*Leaving the writing of the ladder of ascent,*  
*He shows forth the way of his ascent.*  
*John died on the thirtieth day.*

Being sixteen years of age, and endowed with keen intellect, he offered himself unto God as a most sacred sacrifice, having ascended Mount Sinai. Then after nineteen years, arising, he came unto the stadium of hesychia, five miles from the place of Cyriacus, reaching the hermitage of Palestra, at the place called Tholas, where he lived for forty years, ever inflamed with love and the fire of divine charity. He ate of all things which the blameless commandments allowed, but in great moderation, and in this most wisely broke the horn of pride. But what mind could recount the fountain of his tears? Of sleep he partook only so much that the nature of his mind might not be harmed by lack of vigil. His course was constant prayer, and boundless love toward God. Having lived in a manner well-pleasing unto God by all these achievements, and having composed the *Ladder*, setting forth words of instruction, and being filled with goodness, he worthily fell asleep in the Lord, leaving many other writings also. Through his prayers, O God, have mercy on us and save us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The all-exalted God of the fathers" (Превозноси́мый Ѻ҆ц҃е́въ):*[^937]  
*Troparion:*  
Robbers fell upon me, the wretched one, and left me dead with wounds, as one breathless; therefore I pray Thee: O God, visit me!

*Troparion:*  
Unstable thoughts took captive my mind, and wounding me with passions, left me dead with a multitude of transgressions: but heal me, O Savior!

*Troparion:*  
When the Levite saw me suffering with sores, not enduring the wounds because they were incurable, he passed by me, O my Savior: but do Thou Thyself heal me!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou Who wast incarnate of the Virgin hast saved me, pouring out upon my wounds the rich mercy of Thy compassions, O Christ: therefore do I glorify Thee!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "The children coming from Judea" (Ѿ І҆ꙋде́и доше́дше):*[^938]  
*Troparion:*  
Upon the grass of the Kingdom on high, O father, thou didst nourish thy flock, and driving away the filth of heresies with the staff of dogmas, thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber of Christ's Kingdom, clothed in a garment worthy of Him that called thee, wherein thou didst also recline, crying out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*  
Defiled not by sins, thou didst appear as a river of self-control, O father, drowning evil thoughts and cleansing the defilement of those who cry out in faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
From thy womb came forth the Lord of all, incarnate, O Virgin; therefore, confessing thee in Orthodox manner as Mother of God, we cry unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto Thee, the Creator of all" (Тебѣ̀ Вседѣ́телю):*[^939]  
*Troparion:*  
By the robbers of my thoughts, O Savior, I corrupted my life with the wounds of sins, therefore I was stripped naked of the divine Image of Thee, the Lover of mankind and God: but have compassion on me!

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst come down to earth from on high, O Savior, having compassion on me who was wholly wounded with stripes by the sores of transgressions, O Compassionate One, and didst pour out Thy mercy upon me, O Christ.

*Troparion:*  
Body and soul, O Master and Savior, didst Thou give as a ransom for me, and didst save me, who was wounded with the sword of sins in incurable wounds, as Thou art merciful.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee, who beyond understanding didst bear the Lord by the divine Word and remainest a virgin, do all works bless and supreme exalt throughout all ages, O Virgin.

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "The King of heaven" (Цр҃ѧ̀ нбⷭ҇нагѡ):*[^940]  
*Troparion:*  
Having acquired thee, O father, as truly an ensouled pillar and an image of self-control, we all honor thy memory, O John.

*Troparion:*  
The multitude of monks rejoices, and the assembly of the venerable and righteous keeps festival; for with them hast thou worthily received a crown.

*Troparion:*  
Adorned with virtues, thou didst enter into the bridal chamber of ineffable glory, singing a hymn unto Christ unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*  
Despise not those who have need of the help that is from thee, O Virgin, who sing of thee and exalt thee throughout all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah" (И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й):*[^941]  
*Troparion:*  
Having failed to keep Thy commandments with my understanding, O Master, and having fallen into the passion of pleasures, I was stripped bare of grace, and cast out naked with wounds; therefore I pray Thee, O Savior, save me!

*Troparion:*  
Neither could the Levite wash away my ulcers; but Thou didst come unto me, O God, with compassion, and didst pour out the mercy of Thy compassions upon me, O Savior, healing me as an all-excellent physician.

*Troparion:*  
Being tender-hearted, Thou didst have compassion and didst save me, O Christ, grievously slain by the robbers with wounds, O Savior; and Thy soul and body, like two denarii, didst Thou give as a ransom.

*Theotokion:*  
Beyond understanding is thy Childbearing, O Mother of God; for conception was in thee without a man, and virginal was the birth: for He that was born is God; Whom magnifying, we call thee blessed, O Virgin!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Heirmos: "Let us gloriously honor the pure one" (Чи́стꙋю сла́внѡ почти́мъ):*[^942]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou wast shown to be a physician from God, O blessed one, of those ailing in transgressions, and a destroyer and driver-away of evil spirits: therefore we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst leave the earth as an abode of corruption, O father, and didst dwell in the land of the meek; and with them thou rejoicest, enjoying divine sweetness.

*Troparion:*  
Today is a festive day, for it summons all the flocks of monastics unto a spiritual choir, a table, and the food of incorruptible life.

*Theotokion:*  
Having dwelt in thee, O all-immaculate Lady, He Who cast down the murderer of men that of old maliciously deceived our first ancestors, was born, and saved us all.

*Exapostilarion of the Resurrection, and of the Venerable One. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^943]  
Thou didst turn aside from the worldly pleasures of the passions as harmful, and having withered the flesh through fasting, thou didst renew the strength of thy soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O most glorious one: therefore cease not to pray for us, O John!

*Theotokion:*  
As Sovereign Lady and Mother of God we confess thee, saved through thee; for thou didst ineffably give birth unto God, Who destroyed death by the Cross, and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable, with whom we praise thee, O Virgin!

*At the Praises, Resurrection Stichera of the Octoechos, and Anatolian [Eastern] Stichera. Then we say the verse:*  
*Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up!*  
*And we sing the Idiomelon, Tone 1:*[^944]  
Come, let us work in the mystical vineyard, bringing forth in it the fruits of repentance, laboring not for food and drink, but accomplishing virtues in prayers and fastings: being pleased by these, the Lord grants the denarius of work, whereby He delivers souls from the debt of sin, He Who alone is plenteous in mercy!  
*Glory: again the same. Both now: "Most blessed art thou..." Great Doxology and Dismissal.*  
*Glory... Both now... The Gospel Sticheron, and the Catechesis, and the First Hour, and the rest of the order as usual.*

*At the Liturgy, the Typika, and the Beatitudes of the tone.*  
*Prokeimenon of the Apostle, of the occurring tone; and for the Venerable One, Psalm 149 [LXX 149:5, 1], Tone 7:*[^945]  
The venerable shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds!  
*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song!*  
*Epistle of the day: to the Hebrews, Pericope 314 [Heb. 6:13–20]; and another for the Venerable One: to the Ephesians, Pericope 234 [Eph. 5:9–19].*  
*Alleluia of the tone; and for the Venerable One.*  
*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord... Gospel from Mark, Pericope 40 [Mk. 9:17–31]; and for the Venerable One, from Matthew, Pericope 10 [Mt. 4:25–5:12].*  
*Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens! Another: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be!*

## On the Fourth Sunday of the Great Fast: Vespers

*After the introductory psalm, at "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 stichoi; and we sing compunctionate stichera: 4 of the Octoechos, and 3 Prosomoia of the Triode by Lord Joseph.*

*Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord" (Ве́лїа крⷭ҇та̀):*  
During this season, O faithful, let us show forth great labors of self-control, that we may receive great glory, being delivered from the fire of Gehenna by the mercy of the great God.

Having now reached the midpoint of the season of fasting, let us manifestly show forth the beginning of a godly life, and let us fervently strive to attain unto the end of a virtuous conversation, that we may receive sweetness that never grows old.

*Another, by Lord Theodore, Tone 7, Podoben: "Beneath thy protection" (По́дъ кро́въ тво́й):*[^946]  
Having reached the midpoint of this sacred course of fasting, let us run with joy unto what is to come, having anointed our souls with the oil of well-doing, that we may be accounted worthy to worship Christ our God and His divine Passions, and to anticipate both the awesome and Holy Resurrection.

*And in the Menaion 3. Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*  
Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant... (see fol. 73)[^947]

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, Tone 7:*  
The Savior is near, Who planted the vineyard and called the laborers: come, ye champions of fasting, let us receive our wages, for the Giver is rich and merciful; having worked a little, let us receive mercy for our souls!

*Another Idiomelon, Tone 6:*  
Falling into robber-like thoughts, Adam was deceived in mind and wounded in soul, and lay naked of protection; neither did the priest before the Law attend unto him, nor did the Levite according to the Law look upon him; but only Thou, O God, Who camest not from Samaria, but from the Mother of God: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Martyrikon:*  
Thy martyrs, O Lord, denied Thee not, nor did they depart from Thy commandments: by their prayers, have mercy on us!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 6:*  
With the Archangel let us, the faithful, hymn the heavenly Chamber and truly sealed Door: Rejoice, for whose sake Christ, the Savior of all, the Giver of life and God, budded forth for us! Cast down the tyrants, our godless enemies, by thy hand, O all-pure Lady, hope of Christians!

*And the rest of the sequence according to custom.*  
*At Compline, we sing the office of the occurring saint on Thursday of the Great Canon.*

## On Monday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast: Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the occurring tone.*  
*After the first stichology, we sing two compunctionate Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, with the Theotokion.*  
*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn of Lord Joseph, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^948]  
A most fair time has arrived, a praiseworthy day has shone forth, the day of self-control, O brethren: let us strive to be cleansed, that we may appear pure unto Him Who made us, and may obtain His beauty, through the prayers of Her that bore Him, the only pure Mother of God.  
*Glory: the same. Both now: Theotokion, Podoben:*  
Gabriel was amazed at the beauty of thy virginity and the surpassing brightness of thy purity, and cried aloud unto thee, O Mother of God: What worthy praise shall I offer unto thee? What shall I call thee? I am in perplexity and amazed; therefore, as I was commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn of Lord Theodore, Tone 7, Podoben: "Lord, we are Thy people" (Гдⷭ҇и, мы̀ є҆смы̀):*[^949]  
O Lord Who hast granted us to anticipate this day, the holy week brightly foreshadowing the awesome rising of Lazarus from the dead: vouchsafe Thy servants in Thy fear to pass through the stadium of fasting, completing it all.  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou art more honorable than the glorious Cherubim, O all-pure Virgin; for they, unable to endure the divine power, cover their faces with wings and accomplish their ministry; but thou didst behold with thine own eyes and didst bear the incarnate Word: Whom unceasingly entreat for our souls!

*Canon in the Menaion, and the Triode by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical canticle is chanted.*  
*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos: "The waters of old" (Во́ды дре́вле):*[^950]  
*Troparion:*  
Having cultivated our thoughts with the divine plow of divine fasting, let us yield the ear of virtues as fruit, that we may not hunger unto the ages, enjoying ineffable sweetness and rejoicing.

*Troparion:*  
Bearing within me passions of many years which darken my wretched soul, I fall down with contrite heart and pray unto Thine invincible power, O timeless Word of the unoriginate Father: have compassion, and save me!

*Troparion:*  
Good fasting nourishes hearts, anointing God-pleasing thoughts, drying up the abyss of passions, and cleansing with showers of compunction those who bring praise in faith unto the Almighty.

*Theotokion:*  
O Maiden of many names, rejoice, O holy Virgin, Mary Mother of God, boast of the faithful, deliverance from the curse, heavenly ladder, incomprehensible wonder, unburnt bush, unsown earth!

*Another [Triode] by Lord Theodore, Tone 7. Heirmos: "Let us sing unto the Lord" (Пои́мъ Гдⷭ҇ви):*[^951]  
*Troparion:*  
Having venerated the life-bearing Tree, we go forth hence on the way of fasting unto the Passion of Christ, rejoicing.

*Troparion:*  
Following Christ, let us renounce the world and take the cross upon our shoulder, being conformed unto His divine Passions.

*Triadikon:*  
In three Persons, the one supersubstantial Godhead let us all hymn: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*  
Whom thou didst bear incomprehensibly, having known no corruption, O Pure One, Jesus the Savior: make Him merciful now toward thy servants!

*Troparion:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
When Thou shalt come in glory with the angels to judge all things, vouchsafe us to give a good answer unto Thee, O Christ!

*Heirmos:*  
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who drowned all the host of Pharaoh in the sea, a song of victory, for He has been glorified!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The Babylonian furnace" (Вавѷлѡ́нскаѧ пе́щь):*[^952]  
*Troparion:*  
Dragged away by the harmful deceits of the serpent and deceived in mind all the day, adding wound unto my wound, O Savior, I cry unto Thee, the physician of the ailing: Heal, and save me!

*Troparion:*  
By fasting Elisha of old raised the child of the Shunammite, being manifestly strengthened in spirit; and we who are deadened by voluptuousness, O faithful, let us be brought to life by life-bearing fasting!

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst save the Ninevites, O Lord and Lover of mankind, who of old repented in weeping and fasting, as Thou art tender-hearted; have compassion on us also through Thy mercy, who know not how to confess ourselves unto Thee by deeds.

*Theotokion:*  
O all-immaculate Lady, who after childbearing didst remain incorrupt, deliver thy servants from corruption, we pray, O Pure One, who faithfully sing and with oneness of mind in soul cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another [Triode]. Heirmos: "Him Who is fearful unto the Cherubim" (Стра́шнаго Херꙋві́мѡмъ):*[^953]  
*Troparion:*  
Having with God the strength of self-control, O faithful, let us youthful run the rest of the holy course, and receive crowns!

*Troparion:*  
Beholding the fruitlessness of my much-suffering soul, have patience still, O Christ, and cut me not down as that cursed fig tree!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Triadikon:*  
O Trinity of one origin and Unity in three hypostases, Father, Son, and Spirit, deliver from all temptations and perils those who sing of Thee!

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, Mountain of God; rejoice, ever-radiant Lamp; rejoice, new Heaven; rejoice, flashing Mind; rejoice, Temple of the Lord; rejoice, all-hymned Lady!

*Troparion:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Meditating on Thine awesome scrutiny, O Christ, I am dismayed, I tremble and cry aloud: Having immeasurable, great mercy, save me, the wretched one!

*We praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing and supreme exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Heirmos:*  
Him Who is fearful unto the Cherubim, and marvelous unto the Seraphim, and Creator of the world, do ye priests, and servants, and spirits of the righteous, sing, bless, and supreme exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "A new wonder" (Но́вое чꙋ́до):*[^954]  
*Troparion:*  
Having now filled the divine cup with compunction, the grace of the Fast manifestly summons all the faithful, crying with gladness: Come, enjoy, having put aside the drunkenness of the passions, that ye may be accounted worthy of future comfort!

*Troparion:*  
Near is the judgment, take heed, O my soul! Take care with thy conscience, and work what is good, which ever keeps thee uncondemned; for if we judge ourselves here, in nowise shall we be condemned there without witnesses, where the guilty are condemned.

*Troparion:*  
Principalities, Powers, Thrones, Seraphim, Dominions, Authorities, and Cherubim, Angels, Archangels: Pray God that we may pass through the time of the Fast, doing things well-pleasing unto Him, that as well-pleasing servants we may receive glory!

*Theotokion:*  
With the martyrs, with the prophets, with all the venerable ones, O Christ, receive Her that bore Thee, who ever prays for the servants who have provoked Thee to anger, the only Master and Lover of mankind, before Whom the heavenly ranks tremble!

*Another [Triode]. Heirmos: "Beyond nature" (Па́че є҆стества̀):*[^955]  
*Troparion:*  
Having sailed the calm voyage of fasting by the breath of the Spirit, let us pray to reach the harbor of the Passions of Christ!

*Troparion:*  
Emulating the Canaanite woman, I cry unto Thee: Have mercy on me, O Son of David and God, and heal my languishing soul, even as hers daughter!

*Triadikon:*  
The Nature of the Godhead, united as a Unity, and triadically distinguished by three hypostatic marks: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, let us sing!

*Theotokion:*  
O thou who didst conceive the uncontainable Word in thy womb, and without seed didst bear Him in the flesh: pray diligently that our souls may be saved!

*Troparion:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
O Thou Who didst justify the publican, cleanse me as I cry out: and be merciful unto me, a sinner, O Judge of all, forgiving my transgressions!

*Heirmos:*  
A Mother beyond nature, and a Virgin according to nature, the only blessed one among women, in hymns do we, the faithful, magnify as the Mother of God.

*Exaposteilarion of the tone.*  
*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, twice, Tone 8:*[^956]  
I have likened myself unto him that fell among robbers, O Master of all, having fallen through my transgressions, and by them was pitilessly wounded: but leave me not incurable, O Thou Who camest not from Samaria, but from the most pure Virgin, Jesus, saving Name: have mercy on me! *(Twice)*

*Martyrikon:*[^957]  
What shall we call you, O holy ones? Cherubim? For Christ rested upon you; Seraphim? For unceasingly ye glorify Him; Angels? For ye turned away from the body; Powers? For ye work miracles! Many are your names, and greater are your spiritual gifts: pray ye that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8:*  
The heavenly hosts sing of thee, O joyful Mother unwedded, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Mother of God; pray that our souls may be saved!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 3:*[^958]  
For our iniquities have risen up against us, arise, O Lord, help us; for Thou art our Father, and besides Thee we know no other!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 83 [LXX 83:4, 2], Tone 4:*[^959]
