Thine altars, O Lord of hosts, my King and my God!  
*Verse: How beloved are Thy dwellings, O Lord of hosts!*

*At the Sixth Hour (continued):*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 37:33–38:6):*[^960]  
**Reader:** Thus saith the Lord concerning the king of the Assyrians: He shall not enter into this city, nor shoot an arrow there, nor cast up a mount against it, nor surround it with an entrenchment. But by the way that he came, by the same shall he return, and into this city he shall not enter, saith the Lord. For I will defend this city to save it for Mine own sake, and for David My servant's sake. And the Angel of the Lord went forth, and smote in the camp of the Assyrians a hundred and fourscore and five thousand; and when they arose in the morning, behold, they were all dead corpses. So Sennacherib king of the Assyrians departed, and returned, and dwelt in Nineveh. And it came to pass, as he was worshipping in the house of Nasarach his ancestral god, that Adramelech and Sharezer his sons smote him with swords; and they fled into Armenia, and Esarhaddon his son reigned in his stead. In those days Hezekiah was sick unto death. And Isaiah the prophet the son of Amoz came unto him, and said unto him: Thus saith the Lord: Set thine house in order; for thou shalt die, and not live. Then Hezekiah turned his face to the wall, and prayed unto the Lord, saying: Remember now, O Lord, how I have walked before Thee in truth and with a true heart, and have done that which is pleasing before Thee. And Hezekiah wept with a great weeping. Then came the word of the Lord to Isaiah, saying: Go, and say unto Hezekiah: Thus saith the Lord, the God of David thy father: I have heard thy prayer, I have seen thy tears; behold, I will add unto thy days fifteen years. And I will deliver thee and this city out of the hand of the king of the Assyrians, and I will defend this city.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 84 [LXX 84:8, 2], Tone 8:*[^961]  
Show us Thy mercy, O Lord, and grant us Thy salvation.  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land; Thou hast turned back the captivity of Jacob.*

*Inasmuch as it is necessary to know concerning the kathismata of Thursday of the Great Canon, which are three: note that they are to be read beforehand, and not omitted; look ahead, and as they are arranged in their proper places, so read.*[^962]

## On Monday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers[^963]

*Instead of "Unto the Lord", we recite the appointed Kathisma of the Psalter, that is, the tenth.*[^964]  
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses, and chant the present prosomoia, 3, and from the Menaion, 3.*

*Prosomoia Stichera of the Triodion, by Lord Joseph, Tone 3, Podoben: "In the likeness of the Cross" (Крⷭ҇тоꙗвле́ннѡ):*[^965]  
O Word of God, Who hast given us the time of fasting to turn back and live, and in nowise perish: grant that all may well-please Thee, and serve Thee in fervent compunction, O Christ; even as of old that chaste and honorable harlot, having received remission of transgressions through myrrh and the pouring forth of warm tears.

As the blind man, from my heart I cry unto Thee: O Son of God, enlighten the eyes of my heart! Even as the faithful Canaanite woman, I cry aloud unto Thee: Have mercy on me, O Compassionate One! For I have a soul possessed by lusts; changing it from the darkness of passions, grant that henceforth I may live in purity, that I may glorify Thy manifold goodness.

*Another Sticheron, by Lord Theodore, Tone 8, Podoben: "The God-called martyr" (Бг҃озва́нный мч҃нкъ):*[^966]  
The divinely-radiant grace of abstinence, having shone forth upon us today brighter than the sun, enlightens our souls, driving away the clouds of sinful passions. Therefore let us all run to it, embracing it with glad heart; and with joy let us accomplish its divine course, partaking of its delights, as we cry aloud unto Christ: O God, sanctify those who faithfully accomplish this course!

*And from the Menaion, 3. Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 85 [LXX 85:11, 1], Tone 4:*[^967]  
Guide me, O Lord, in Thy way, and I will walk in Thy truth.  
*Verse: Incline Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me.*

*The Reading from Genesis (Genesis 13:12–18):*[^968]  
**Reader:** Abram dwelt in the land of Canaan; and Lot dwelt in a city of the surrounding country, and pitched his tent toward Sodom. But the men of Sodom were evil and sinners before God exceedingly. And God said unto Abram, after Lot was separated from him: Lift up now thine eyes, and look from the place where thou now art northward, and southward, and eastward, and seaward; for all the land which thou seest, to thee will I give it, and to thy seed for ever. And I will make thy seed as the dust of the earth; so that if anyone can count the dust of the earth, then shall thy seed also be numbered. Arise, walk through *At Vespers (continued):*

the land in the length of it and in the breadth of it; for I will give it unto thee, and unto thy seed for ever. And Abram removed his tent, and came and dwelt by the oak of Mamre, which was in Hebron, and built there an altar unto the Lord.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 86 [LXX 86:2, 1], Tone 4:*[^969]  
The Lord loves the gates of Zion more than all the dwellings of Jacob.  
*Verse: His foundations are in the holy mountains.*

*The Reading from Proverbs (Proverbs 14:27–15:4):*[^970]  
**Reader:** The fear of the Lord is a fountain of life, and causes men to turn aside from the snare of death. In a multitude of people is the king's glory; but in the failing of the people is the destruction of the mighty. He that is longsuffering is great in understanding; but he that is faint-hearted is strongly foolish. A gentle man is a physician of the heart; but a sensitive heart is a moth to the bones. He that oppresses the poor provokes his Maker; but he that honors Him has mercy on the needy. The ungodly shall be cast out in his wickedness; but the righteous has hope in his own holiness. Wisdom rests in the good heart of a man; but in the heart of fools it is not known. Righteousness exalts a nation; but sins diminish peoples. An intelligent servant is acceptable to a king, and by his good conduct he removes dishonor. Wrath destroys even the prudent; but a soft answer turns away wrath, while a grievous word stirs up anger. The tongue of the wise knows good things; but the mouth of fools publishes evils. In every place the eyes of the Lord behold both the evil and the good. The healing of the tongue is a tree of life; and he that guards it shall be filled with the Spirit.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 6, once:*[^971]  
As he that fell among robbers and was wounded, so have I fallen by reason of my sins, and my soul is wounded: unto whom shall I flee to be healed, save only unto Thee, the Physician of souls and bodies? Pour out upon me, O God, Thy great mercy!

*Another Idiomelon, Tone 4, once:*[^972]  
Having sinned through the first transgression, we were led from the sweetness and delight of paradise into a life of dishonor; for having stripped off the worthy and praised life of virtues through transgressions, we fell as among robbers, and are half-dead, having erred from Thy saving teachings: but we pray Thee, O Word Who appeared from Mary and didst approach the Passion without suffering: bind up the wounds which have come upon us from sin, and pour out upon us Thy measureless tender-heartedness and Thy healing care, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*For the Martyrs:*[^973]  
O Thou Who didst accept the endurance of the holy martyrs, accept also from us our hymnody, O Lover of mankind, granting us by their prayers great mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
From all dangers preserve Thy servants, O blessed Mother of God, that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls!

## On Tuesday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins[^974]

*Triadica.*[^975]  
*Then we chant Kathismata 11, 12, and 13.*[^976]  
*After the first stichology, we recite the compunctive Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, 2, with their Theotokion.*  
*After the second stichology, the Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph.*

*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^977]  
With the warmth of faith, through abstinence let us burn up the passions of intemperance, and flee from the pit of sin, and with streams of tears quench the eternal flame, crying: O All-good One, we have sinned against Thee; cleanse us, and grant us great mercy! *(Twice)*

*Theotokion, Podoben:*  
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O all-immaculate Virgin Mother, surpassing the Angels in purity; cleanse me, who beyond all others have become dust and am defiled by bodily transgressions, through thy prayers with divine water, granting me great mercy, O Pure One!

*After the third stichology, the Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "Most blessed art thou" (Пребл҃гослове́нна є҆сѝ):*[^978]  
All-glorious is the grace of the venerable fast, whereby Elijah the prophet found a fiery chariot, and Moses received the tablets, and Daniel became marvelous, and Elisha raised the dead, and the Children quenched the fire, and everyone became united unto God: feeding thereon, let us cry aloud: Blessed is Christ our God Who art thus well-pleased; glory to Thee! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Podoben: "Unto Thy most pure icon" (Пречи́стомꙋ):*[^979]  
Having obtained thine invincible mercy in grievous troubles, I am delivered from those who afflict me beyond hope in marvelous manner, O Mother of God! For thou art ever swift to aid those who ask of thee in faith, and dost loose surrounding temptations; therefore thankfully I cry unto thee: Accept, O Sovereign Lady, these brief words of thanksgiving, since thou art unto me a help in all things, O Virgin!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present Triodion [triode] by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical canticle is recited.*

*Ode 2, Tone 3. Heirmos:*[^980]  
Give ear, O earth and heaven, and give ear unto my words; for upon earth I will tell the wonders of God.

*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
With unceasing prayers, with fasting and contemplation, let us wing our souls unto divine desire!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Having fled the headlong falls of intemperance, let us all be made bright by the light of abstinence in the divine Spirit!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Having cast off the rottenness of sin with the salt of divine virtues, O my soul, cleave unto God!

*Theotokion:*  
Christ found rest in Thee, the only Blessed One, and took His flesh from Thee.

*Another Canon, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Give ear, ye people":*[^981]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Pure fasting, prayers, tears, contemplation of divine things, and having gathered every other virtue, let us now offer unto the Master Christ!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Eve brought ruin through the fruit: take heed, O my soul, be not deceived, if at any time the serpent take counsel with thee to eat luscious fruits!

*To the Trinity:*  
Three Persons of one form do I glorify: the Father, the Son, and the Spirit; one dominion of the Godhead, the Kingdom of all, and Divinity.

*Theotokion:*  
Thy childbearing, O Pure One, was awesome; for it is God Who became man, begotten without beginning from the Father, and born of thee in these last times without a man.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thou didst heal the woman with the issue of blood through the touching of the hem of Thy garment, O my Christ; make me also whole from passions, who touch the mercy of Thy pity in faith!

*Heirmos:*  
Give ear, ye people, unto the signs of the deeds of My divine power, and thereby understand that I alone am God of all!

*Ode 8, Tone 3. Heirmos: "Incessantly by angels":*[^982]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Lying in wait secretly all the days, the all-crafty one seeks to seize me and make me his prey: deliver me from his malice, O Savior, Who didst save Jonah from the whale!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Purified by fasting, let us ascend the mountain of virtues, and we shall clearly hear what God will speak in us; for He will speak peace and illumination, and the healing of the soul's afflictions!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Troparion:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Ever blinded by the darkening of sin, I cannot comprehend Thy wonders, O Lord: open therefore the eyes of my heart, O Jesus, Giver of light!

*Theotokion:*  
With the sprinklings of thy mercies, O undefiled Ever-Virgin, wash away the passionate defilements of my heart, granting me streams of tears that bestow purity and cleansing of soul!

*Another Canon, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Of old Him Who bedewed":*[^983]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
The fire is not quenched, and the worm, it is said, dies not: fear the threatening, O my soul, well-pleasing Christ! that thou mayest find delight where the dwelling of all those who rejoice is.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
My soul, inflamed by the burning of desires, do Thou raise up, O Lord, I pray Thee, by Thy touch, even as Peter's mother-in-law, that she may turn to serve Thee in a manner well-pleasing, confessing Thee unto all ages!

*Verse: We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*To the Trinity:*  
The ever-existing, unoriginate Father let us, the faithful, praise in hymnody, and the Son co-unoriginate, and the Spirit co-glorified with the Father: consubstantial Persons, being of one all-powerful beginning and dominion.

*Theotokion:*  
O Mary, called of God, truly the cleansing of the faithful, for from thee remission is richly bestowed upon all: cease not to entreat thy Son and Lord on behalf of those who hymn thee!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Open the ears of my soul which have grown deaf, O Christ, and make clear my tongue, as of old Thou didst for him that had an impediment in his speech and was deaf: that giving ear to Thy words I may sing, and praise Thee with my tongue unto ages!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and supremely exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Heirmos:*  
Him Who of old bedewed the Hebrew Children in the flame, and Who as Lord gloriously consumed the Chaldeans therein, let us praise, saying: Bless and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9, Tone 3. Heirmos: "Thee, the unconsumed":*[^984]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Bearing in mind the flame of the unquenchable fire, with fervent thought of repentance let us now draw near to the fire, consuming the passions!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
The fervent word that came forth with zeal from the mouth of those who fasted of old bent their limbs: emulate this, O my soul, and live well!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Reflecting upon the fearful judgment-seat, O my all-wretched soul, prepare thy ways ever unto the fulfillment of the desires of the Deliverer!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Theotokion:*  
My soul, darkened by the blackness of lusts, do thou enlighten, O Pure One who didst bear the Light: that in love and faith I may ever magnify thee!

*Another Canon, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Supernaturally in the flesh":*[^985]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Let us present every member of our flesh unto Christ as weapons of righteousness, lifting up, as it is said, holy hands without wrath and disputings!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thou Who didst once appear to the disciples in the ship, O my Christ, and didst calm the raging sea: still also the surging waves of my thoughts!

*To the Trinity:*  
Unto one nature do I bow down; three Persons do I hymn: the one God of all, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the ever-existing Beginning.

*Theotokion:*  
As a pure mother thou bearest a child, and as a virgin dost suckle: how dost thou perform both in one in giving birth? It is God Who hath done it; ask me not how!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Heal me, defiled in soul by robber-like passions, O my Christ, pouring out Thy mercy, even as Thou didst unto him that fell into the hands of robbers!

*Heirmos:*  
Thee Who didst supernaturally conceive in the womb in the flesh the Word Who before all ages shone forth from the Father, in unceasing hymns do we, the faithful, magnify!

*Exaposteilarion of the tone.*  
*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 7:*[^986]  
Visit my wounded and humble soul, O Lord, Thou Physician of the sick and of the desperate, Thou tempest-free haven; for Thou art the Deliverer of the world Who camest to raise up him that fell from hidden places: raise up me also, who fall down before Thee, according to Thy great mercy! *(Twice)*

*For the Martyrs:*[^987]  
In the midst of the tribunal of the lawless, the passion-bearers cried out rejoicing: Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
We hymn thee, O all-pure Mother of God, and we glorify Him Whom thou didst bear, God the Word, crying: Glory to Thee!

*At the First Hour, we recite Kathisma 14. At the Third Hour, 15. At the Sixth Hour, 16.*[^988]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 3:*[^989]  
The God of Jacob is our helper, and He is a protector in the day of affliction.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 87 [LXX 87:16, 2], Tone 6:*[^990]  
Poor am I, and in troubles from my youth.  
*Verse: O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried by day, and in the night before Thee.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 40:18–31):*[^991]  
**Reader:** Thus saith the Lord: To whom have ye likened the Lord, and to what likeness have ye compared Him? Hath a craftsman made an image, or a goldsmith, having melted gold, overlaid it with gold, or made it in a likeness? For a craftsman chooss wood that will not rot, and wisely seeks how he may set up his image so that it be not shaken. Know ye not? Have ye not heard? Hath it not been told you from the beginning? Have ye not understood the foundations of the earth? He that holds the circle of the earth, and the inhabitants thereof are as grasshoppers; Who hath set up the heaven as a chamber, and stretched it out as a tent to dwell in; Who gives princes as nothing to rule, and hath made the earth as nothing. For they shall not plant, neither shall they sow, neither shall their root be established in the earth; He hath blown upon them, and they are withered, and a tempest shall take them away like stubble. Now therefore to whom have ye likened Me, and unto what shall I be exalted? saith the Holy One. Lift up your eyes on high, and behold: Who hath created all these things? Bringing out their host by number, He calls them all by name, from the greatness of His glory and in the dominion of His power, nothing is hidden from Thee. Why sayest thou, O Jacob, and why hast thou spoken, O Israel: My way is hid from God, and my God hath taken away judgment and departed? And now hast thou not known? or hast thou not heard? The everlasting God, the God Who hath formed the ends of the earth, faints not, neither is weary; nor is there any searching out of His wisdom. He gives power to the faint, and grief to them that are not afflicted. For the younger shall faint, and the youths shall grow weary, and the chosen shall be without strength; but they that wait upon the Lord shall renew their strength!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 88 [LXX 88:16, 16b], Tone 6:*[^992]  
Blessed are the people that know the joyful sound.  
*Verse: O Lord, in the light of Thy countenance shall they walk.*

*At the Ninth Hour, Kathisma 17.*[^993]

## On Tuesday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers[^994]

*Instead of "Unto the Lord", we recite Kathisma 18.*[^995]  
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses, and chant the prosomoia stichera of the Triodion, 3, and from the Menaion, 3.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*[^996]  
Making love our food, through abstinence let us restrain the passions, O ye faithful; and toward God Who was lifted up upon the Cross for our sake and was pierced in the side with a spear, let us be guided to live in a manner well-pleasing, that we may enjoy eternal and better delight, glorifying the Savior of our souls!

*Podoben:*  
Through the tree of old we found death, but now again through the Tree of the Cross life: let us, the faithful, mortify the surges of passions, and pray that we may attain unto the saving Resurrection of the Benefactor of all, shining with divine deeds, and adorned with virtues, glorifying the Savior of our souls!

*Another Prosomoion Sticheron, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2, Podoben: "Higher than mind of the good things" (Па́че ᲂу҆ма̀ бл҃ги́хъ):*[^997]  
Thy life-bearing Cross do we praise, O Lord, and the sacred sufferings of Thy flesh for our sake: the spear, the slaying, the mockings, the spittings, the beatings, and the neglect with the purple robe, and the crown woven of thorns, whereby, delivering all from the curse, Thou hast saved us: therefore we pray Thee, grant us to finish the time of the fasts in peace!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 89 [LXX 89:1-2], Tone 4:*[^998]  
Lord, Thou hast been our dwelling-place in generation and generation.  
*Verse: Before the mountains were brought forth, or ever the earth and the world were formed.*

*The Reading from Genesis (Genesis 15:1–15):*[^999]  
**Reader:** The word of the Lord came unto Abram in a vision by night, saying: Fear not, Abram: I defend thee, and thy reward shall be exceedingly great. And Abram said: Sovereign Lord, what wilt Thou give me, seeing I go childless, and the son of Masek my handmaid, this Eliezer of Damascus, is my heir? And Abram said: Since Thou hast given me no seed, behold, one born in my house is mine heir. And straightway the voice of the Lord came unto him, saying: This shall not be thine heir; but he that shall come forth out of thee shall be thine heir. And He brought him forth abroad, and said unto him: Look up now toward heaven, and tell the stars, if thou be able to number them.

*At Vespers (continued):*

and He said: So shall thy seed be. And Abram believed God, and it was counted unto him for righteousness. And He said unto him: I (am) God Who brought thee out of the land of the Chaldeans, to give thee this land to inherit it. And he said: Sovereign Lord, whereby shall I know that I shall inherit it? And He said unto him: Take Me a heifer of three years old, and a she-goat of three years old, and a ram of three years old, and a turtledove, and a pigeon. And he took unto Him all these, and divided them in the midst, and laid each piece one against another; but the birds divided he not. And the birds came down upon the carcasses that were divided; and Abram sat down by them. And when the sun was going down, a deep sleep fell upon Abram; and lo, an horror of great darkness fell upon him. And it was said unto Abram: Know of a surety that thy seed shall be a stranger in a land that is not theirs, and shall serve them; and they shall afflict them and humble them four hundred years. And also that nation, whom they shall serve, will I judge; and afterward shall they come out hither with great substance. And thou shalt go to thy fathers in peace, nourished in a good old age.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 90 [LXX 90:1, 2], Tone 4:*[^1000]  
He that dwells in the help of the Most High shall abide in the shelter of the God of heaven.  
*Verse: He will say to the Lord: Thou art my protector.*

*The Reading from Proverbs (Proverbs 15:7–19):*[^1001]  
**Reader:** The lips of the wise are bound with knowledge; but the hearts of fools are not firm. The sacrifices of the ungodly are an abomination to the Lord; but the vows of the upright are acceptable unto Him. The ways of the ungodly are an abomination to the Lord; but He loves those that follow after righteousness. The instruction of the innocent is known by them that pass by; but they that hate reproofs die ignominiously. Hell and destruction are manifest before the Lord; how much more also the hearts of men? An uncorrected man will not love them that reprove him, neither will he converse with the wise. When the heart is merry, the countenance blooms; but when it is in sorrow, it is dejected. An upright heart seeks knowledge; but the mouth of the uncorrected knows evils. At all times the eyes of the wicked receive evil things; but the good are ever at peace. Better is a little with the fear of the Lord than great treasures without fear. Better is an entertainment of herbs with love and grace than a stalled ox with hatred. A wrathful man stirs up strife; but he that is longsuffering calms even that which is to come; a patient man will quench strife, but an ungodly man stirs it up all the more. The ways of the slothful are strewn with thorns; but those of the valiant are made smooth.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 5, once:*[^1002]  
Having slipped from Thy right path, wretch that I am, I fell through passions into a pit; and the Levite and the priest, having seen me, passed by; but Thou, O Christ, didst have mercy on me, and tearing up the handwriting of sin with the weapon of the Cross, Thou didst illuminate me with freedom from passion, and madest me to sit with the Father; therefore do I cry unto Thee: O Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!

*Another Sticheron, Tone 8, once:*[^1003]  
I slipped and went down from Jerusalem, from Thy commandments written therein by the tongues; and going to Jericho by an imitative impulse, in it of old for malice from Thee, unto murder being delivered by Thy people in disobedience, I fell among soul-corrupting passions as among robbers: wounded by them and half-dead, O Thou Who of Thine own will wast wounded in the flesh with nails and spear for the sake of man's sin, and accomplishedst the common salvation by the Cross in Jerusalem: heal me, O Lord, and save me!

*At the Aposticha (continued):*

*For the Martyrs:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, forgetting that which is in life, and not caring for torments for the sake of the future life, were shown forth as inheritors thereof; therefore they rejoice with the angels: by their prayers grant Thy people great mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
O Lord, when the sun beheld Thee hanging upon the Tree, O Sun of righteousness, it hid its rays, and the moon changed its light into darkness; and Thine all-immaculate Mother was wounded in her maternal heart.

## On Wednesday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins[^1004]

*Triadica.*[^1005]  
*Then Kathismata 8, 1, and 2.*[^1006]  
*After the first stichology, the Sessional Cross Hymns of the Octoechos, 2, with their Stavrotheotokion.*  
*After the second stichology, we say the present Sessional Hymn, by Lord Joseph.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^1007]  
Having tasted of the tree unseemly, Adam found the bitter fruits of intemperance; but lifted up upon the Tree, Thou didst deliver him, O Tender-hearted One, from bitter condemnation: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: Grant us, O Master, to abstain from corrupting fruit, and to do Thy will, that we may find mercy! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*  
Beholding Thee, Who from thy pure blood wast incarnate and beyond understanding born of thee, O Pure One, hanging upon the Tree in the midst of two transgressors, thou wast pained in thy maternal heart, and weeping as a mother didst cry aloud: Woe is me, my Child! What divine and ineffable dispensation is this of Thine, whereby Thou hast brought to life Thy creation? I praise Thy tender-heartedness!

*After the third stichology, the Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "Unto Thy most pure icon" (Пречи́стомꙋ твоемꙋ̀ ѻ҆́бразꙋ):*[^1008]  
Singing of the all-holy Tree of the Cross, we ever bow down before Thy supreme goodness, O Christ God; for thereon didst Thou abolish the hostile powers, and gavest a sign unto those who believe in Thee;

*At Matins (continued):*

therefore thankfully we cry aloud unto Thee: Vouchsafe all who live in peace and good cheer to fulfill the time of the fast! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*  
The Ewe-lamb, beholding Thee, the Blameless Shepherd, lifted up upon the Tree, weeping as a mother cried aloud: O Son, the ungrateful people have condemned Thee to death, in return for Thine having spread out a cloud for their passage: woe is me, I am made childless, who knew no man! But arise, and shine forth, O Sun, and I shall be glorified among the sons of the earthborn!

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present Triodion [triode], by Lord Joseph. And the biblical canticle is recited.*

*Ode 3, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Of the heavenly vault" (Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га):*[^1009]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Having condemned enmity, upon the Cross Thou didst stretch out Thy hands, O Righteous Judge: now also save me, the prodigal, who am condemned by transgressions, O Savior, who have embittered Thee, the Longsuffering One!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
I desired a beast-like life that produces passions, I wandered far from Thy commandments, O All-good Savior, enslaved by strange and unclean citizens; but now as I turn back, receive and save me!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
As of old Thou didst open the ears of the deaf-mute, open the ears of my soul which are deafened by darkness, and vouchsafe me to give ear unto Thy saving word, O Jesus, the only Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*  
Saving Gate, Bridge unto God, Intercessor for Christians, All-immaculate Sovereign Lady: as I am encompassed by the afflictions of life and tempest-tossed, guide me, O Maiden!

*Another Canon, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2. Heirmos: "My barren mind" (Непло́дствовавшїй мо́й ᲂу҆́мъ):*[^1010]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Because of the tender mercy of Thy compassions having ascended the Cross, Thou didst draw me out from the pit of passions, and hast raised me, O Christ, unto heavenly things!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Stretching out Thy hands upon the Cross, O Christ, Thou hast gathered together all nations that were far off from Thee, to stand near unto Thy dominion!

*To the Trinity:*  
O Tri-hypostatic Unity, transcendent Trinity, one Godhead: Father, and Son, and upright Spirit: save those who honor Thee!

*Theotokion:*  
Who among the earthborn is able worthily to praise thee, O Virgin Mother? For among women thou alone hast appeared chosen and all-blessed!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross, Thou wast pierced in the side, Thou didst taste of gall for my sake, O Jesus, embittered by the transgression!

*Heirmos:*  
My barren mind show Thou fruitful, O God, Cultivator of good things, Planter of what is good, through Thy tender-heartedness!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 8, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Sevenfold the furnace" (Седмери́цею пе́щь):*[^1011]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Looking upon me with Thine unslumbering eye, have compassion, O Thou Who didst bow Thy head upon the Cross and willingly sleepest, and didst dispel the darkness of sin, O Christ, Who art the Light of righteousness, upon me who am held fast by the slumber of despondency, and slave unto sleep upon the bed of pleasurable passions!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Having been adorned with wealth-bestowing gifts from baptism, I rather loved the poverty of evil things, and became an outcast from virtues, wretch that I am, having gone far away into the land of malice: therefore turn me back, and receive me, O Savior, protecting me by Thy Cross unto all ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Cast off the drunkenness of passions, and seek the cleansing of tears through fasting, O soul: wine that gladdens the heart, withers away pleasures, and reduces the bodily furnace unto ashes; and hasten to be crucified together with Christ Who for thy sake was nailed unto the Tree, and live unto ages!

*Theotokion:*  
All-pure Mother of God, cleanse the wounds and the sinful corruptions of my soul, washing them with the springs from the side of Thy Child, and purifying them with streams from them; for unto thee do I cry, and unto thee do I flee, and upon thee do I call, O Full of Grace!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "With voices angelic" (Гла́сы а҆́гг҃льскими):*[^1012]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Him Who was crucified in the flesh, and in His Godhead suffered not, Jesus, the Angels praise, and we, the earthborn, unto all ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
In Thy crucifixion, O Christ, having accepted a reproachful and cursed death, Thou hast saved us, delivering us from corruption!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
In Cherubic fashion, O Trinity: Holy, Holy, Holy, do I sing of Thee, the one Godhead, without beginning, simple, and by all incomprehensible!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
With praises, O Pure One, all generations brightly honor thee; for thou hast given birth unto the Creator: O awesome wonder, and all-blessed work!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thou didst endure to suffer all things for one reason: that I might be saved, O Christ: I sing of Thy crucifixion, the nails, and the slaying, unto all ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Heirmos:*  
Him Who is glorified with voices angelic in the heavens, let us, the earthborn, praise, saying: God, unto all ages!

*Ode 9, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Heaven was dismayed at this" (Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о):*[^1013]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Beholding Thee stretched out upon the Cross, the sun hid its rays, and all the earth was shaken with trembling, O All-merciful One, seeing Thee willingly suffering, Who art by nature impassible; therefore I pray Thee: Heal the passions of my soul, as Thou art the Physician, O Christ!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Leaving the paths of salvation, I walked in those that lead unto hades, having around me deep darkness of pleasure, and the slippery slopes of passions, and a storm of temptations; therefore I pray Thee: Save me by Thy Cross, O Christ, as Thou alone art plenteous in mercy!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Held fast by the winter of temptations, and drowning in the surges of passions, and tempest-tossed grievously by the storm of pleasures, I have reached the calm and quiet deep of fasting, wherein pilot me by Thy Cross, O Tender-hearted One, and guide me unto salvation!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst conceive without seed, O Virgin, the Word of God Who fashioned all things, without fleshly desires; and without corruption hast thou given birth, without motherly pangs: therefore confessing thee as the Mother of God with tongue and heart, we magnify thee!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "Thy virginity, O Theotokos" (Дѣ́вство твоѐ Бцⷣе):*[^1014]  
*Troparia:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Willingly hast Thou endured to be crucified, O our Savior, that Thou mightest grant life unto all, delivering them from death!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
By the tree was I slain, and by the Tree of the Cross have I been made alive, upon which my Christ was nailed, and slew mine enemy!

*To the Trinity:*  
Unto the Son Who is with the Father, and unto the Holy Spirit enthroned with Them, with one mind let us bow down!

*Theotokion:*  
A most glorious wonder, and an awesome report: how and as a Mother dost thou give birth, O Pure One, and knewest no corruption, as thou art a Virgin?

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
I praise Thy spear, I sing also of Thy nails, the sponge, the reed, the Cross, whereby Thou hast saved me, O Jesus God!

*Heirmos:*  
Thy virginity, O undefiled Theotokos, which the divine fire consumed not, we magnify!

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 3:*[^1015]  
Having left the height of virtues, O my soul, thou hast descended into the depth of sin, having fallen among wicked robbers; and being full of foul-smelling wounds, thou liest cast down and helpless: therefore cry aloud, O soul, unto Christ God, Who was crucified for thee, and willingly received wounds: Care for me, O Lord, and save me!

*Another [Idiomelon], Tone 8:*[^1016]  
By robber-like thoughts was I wounded, wretch that I am, and was left half-dead, O Lord; and the choir of prophets disregarded me, seeing me half-dead and incurable by human arts; therefore, suffering sorely, in humility of heart I cry unto Thee, O Christ God: Pour out, as Thou art tender-hearted, Thy great mercy upon me!

*At Matins (continued):*

*For the Martyrs:*[^1017]  
Unconquered martyrs of Christ, having conquered deception by the power of the Cross, ye received the grace of eternal life; ye feared not the threats of the torturers, and wounded with torments ye rejoiced; and now your blood has become healing for our souls: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*  
O All-immaculate One, when she beheld Thee hanging in the flesh upon the Tree, her heart was wounded, and weeping she cried aloud, O Word: Whither hast Thou set, O my most dearly beloved Jesus, my Son and Lord? Leave me not alone, O Christ Who barest Thee!

*At the First Hour, Kathisma 3; at the Third Hour, Kathisma 4; at the Sixth Hour, Kathisma 5.*[^1018]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 1:*[^1019]  
By the sufferings of the saints which they endured for Thee, be Thou entreated, O Lord, and heal all our sicknesses, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 91 [LXX 91:2, 3], Tone 4:*[^1020]  
It is a good thing to give praise unto the Lord, and to sing unto Thy name, O Most High.  
*Verse: To proclaim Thy mercy in the morning, and Thy truth by night.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 41:4–14):*[^1021]  
**Reader:** Thus says the Lord: [4] I (am) God, the first, and unto the things that are to come I am. [5] The nations saw, and feared; the ends of the earth drew near, and came together. [6] Each one judging for his neighbor, and to help his brother, and he will say: [7] The carpenter is strong, and the metalsmith that smites with the hammer, forging together; at one time he will say: The soldering is good; they have fastened them with nails, they will set them up, and they will not be moved. [8] But thou, Israel, art My servant Jacob, whom I have chosen, the seed of Abraham, whom I have loved; [9] whom I have taken from the ends of the earth, and called thee from the watch-posts thereof, and said unto thee: Thou art My servant; I have chosen thee, and have not cast thee away. [10] Fear not, for I am with thee; be not deceived, for I am thy God, Who has strengthened thee, and helped thee, and established thee with My righteous right hand. [11] Behold, all that oppose thee shall be ashamed and confounded; for they shall be as though they were not, and all thine adversaries shall perish. [12] Thou shalt seek them, and shalt not find the men that mock thee; for they shall be as though they were not, and they that war against thee shall not be. [13] For I am thy God Who holds thy right hand, Who says unto thee: [14] Fear not, Jacob, O little Israel; I have helped thee, says thy God, the Holy One of Israel Who redeems thee.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 92 [LXX 92:1, 1b], Tone 6:*[^1022]  
The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty.  
*Verse: The Lord is clothed with strength, and has girded Himself.*

*At the Ninth Hour, Kathisma 6.*[^1023]

## On Wednesday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers[^1024]

*We chant instead of "Unto the Lord in my affliction": Kathisma 7.*[^1025]

*At "Lord, I have cried," the Idiomelon Sticheron, which we say twice. And we begin the verses from: "The wicked shall fall into their own net":*  
*Tone 8:*[^1026]  
Having fallen among robbers by my thoughts, I, the wretched one, was taken captive in mind, and was sorely wounded, and have injured my whole soul, and lie utterly stripped of virtues on the pathway of life; and the priest, seeing me grieving with incurable wounds, passed me by without looking upon me; and the Levite again, not enduring the soul-corrupting pain, he also seeing me passed by; but Thou Who wast well-pleased to become incarnate not from Samaria but from Mary, O Christ God, in Thy love for mankind grant me healing, pouring out upon me Thy great mercy!

*Verse: With my voice have I cried unto the Lord, with my voice unto the Lord have I made my supplication.*  
*Again the same.*

*Verse: I will pour out my supplication before Him, I will declare mine affliction before Him.*  
*For the Martyrs:*[^1027]  
If any virtue, and if any praise befits the saints? For with the sword they bowed their necks for Thy sake Who didst bow the heavens and descend; they poured out their blood for Thy sake Who didst empty Thyself and take the form of a servant; they humbled themselves even unto death, imitating Thy poverty: by whose prayers, according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies, O God, have mercy on us!

*Verse: When my spirit was fainting within me, then Thou knewest my paths.*  
*Other Stichera, by Lord Joseph, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Lord, though before the judgment seat" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи):*[^1028]  
O Lord, Thou didst show Thy sacred disciples to be noetic heavens: by their sacred intercessions deliver me from earthly evils, ever lifting up through abstinence my contemplation of mind above passions, as Thou art tender-hearted and the Lover of mankind!

*Verse: In this way wherein I walked have they hidden a snare for me.*  
*Podoben:*  
Having the whole time of the fast as a co-worker in divine deeds, let us weep from our whole heart, and cry aloud unto the Savior: Through Thy disciples, O most merciful Lord, save those who praise Thee with love, as Thou art tender-hearted and the Lover of mankind!

*At Vespers (continued):*

*Another Sticheron, by Lord Theodore, in the same tone, Podoben: "The glorious and most pure" (Сла́внꙋю и҆ Пречⷭ҇тꙋю):*[^1029]  
*Verse: I looked on my right hand and beheld, and there was no man that knew me.*  
All-laudable Apostles, intercessors for the world, physicians of the sick, guardians of health: preserve us in that which is your own as we pass through the time of the fast; divinely making peace in your own people, keeping the mind untroubled by passions, that we all may sing a hymn unto Christ Who is risen, as the Conqueror!

*Other Stichera according to the alphabet, 24, of the Great Canon. The composition of Lord Andrew of Crete. Tone 4, Podoben: "I desired with tears" (Хотѣ́хъ слеза́ми):*[^1030]

*Verse: Refuge has failed me, and there is no man that cares for my soul.*  
**1 (*А*):** All my life have I spent with harlots and publicans: shall I then be able at least in old age to repent of that wherein I have sinned, O Creator of all and Physician of the diseased? O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*And at each verse we make 3 prostrations.*[^1031]

*Verse: I cried unto Thee, O Lord; I said: Thou art my hope, my portion art Thou in the land of the living.*  
**2 (*Б*):** By the weight of negligence am I held fast, I wallow in the mire, shot through with the arrow of Belial, and I defile that which is in Thine image, O Converter of the negligent and Redeemer of them that have sinned: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Give ear unto my supplication, for I am brought very low.*  
**3 (*Г*):** I have become a stumbling-block unto men, having tilled as an earthly man earthly things; I was joined in marriage by Thy command, and I transgressed, defiling my bed: O Thou Who didst fashion man from the earth, despise not Thine own creation: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Deliver me from them that persecute me, for they are stronger than I.*  
**4 (*Д*):** A schemer against mine own soul have I become, taking thought for my flesh; I was set as a mockery for demons, slaving for pleasures and unseemly deeds: in Thy tender-heartedness spare me, O Driver-away of demons: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Bring my soul out of prison, that I may give praise unto Thy name.*  
**5 (*Є*):** Willingly have I sinned more than all, therefore am I abandoned; I have an adversary unto my soul, the wisdom of the flesh, and it darkens me, O Illumination of them that are in darkness, and Guide of them that go astray: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: The righteous shall wait for me, until Thou shalt recompense me.*  
**6 (*Ѕ*):** My soul shall live, said the prophet, O Lord, and it shall praise Thee: seek me, the lost sheep, and number me with Thy flock; grant me time for repentance, that with groaning I may cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*At Vespers (continued):*

*Verse: Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice.*  
**7 (*И*):** I have sinned, I have sinned, I have transgressed Thy commandments, O Christ God; be merciful unto me, O Benefactor, that with inward eyes I may receive sight, and flee from darkness, and with fear cry aloud: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Let Thine ears be attentive to the voice of my supplication.*  
**8 (*Ѳ*):** Wild beasts have compassed me about, but rescue me from them, O Master; for Thou willest that all men should be saved and come unto the knowledge of the truth, as Thou art the Creator, and with all men save me also: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee there is cleansing.*  
**9 (*І*):** Be Thou healing unto me, O Benefactor, Redeemer, my Savior, and cast me not away; behold me lying in iniquities, and raise me up, as Thou art almighty, that I also may declare Thy deeds, and cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: For Thy name's sake have I waited for Thee, O Lord; my soul has waited for Thy word, my soul has hoped in the Lord.*  
**10 (*К*):** Having hidden the talent given unto me, like an unwise servant I buried it in the earth; for as unprofitable have I been condemned, and I dare not anymore ask of Thee; as Thou art without malice, have pity on me, that I also may call out: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: From the morning watch until night, from the morning watch let Israel hope in the Lord.*  
**11 (*Л*):** O Thou Who didst dry up the lake of passions by the touching of the hem of Thy garment for the woman with the issue of blood, grant me forgiveness of transgressions, who run unto Thee with unhesitating faith, and receive me as her, and heal my sickness: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption, and He shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities.*  
**12 (*М*):** Thou art to sit upon the throne, O Thou Who didst make heaven and earth by Thy word: we all are to stand before Thee, declaring unto Thee our sins; before that day, receive me in repentance: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: O praise the Lord, all ye nations; praise Him, all ye peoples.*  
**13 (*Н*):** Look down with a tender-hearted eye, and be merciful unto me, O only Savior; grant streams of healings unto my poor and wretched soul; wash me clean from the mire of my deeds, that I may sing: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: For His mercy has been established upon us, and the truth of the Lord abides for ever.*  
**14 (*Ѯ*):** Belial has prepared swords, striving to ensnare my humble soul; he has made me a stranger, O Tender-hearted One, unto the illumination of the knowledge of Thy countenance: O Mighty in strength, snatch me from his vessels: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven. Behold, as the eyes of servants look unto the hands of their masters, as the eyes of a maiden unto the hands of her mistress: so our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have pity on us.*  
**15 (*О*):** Unto all passions have I been enslaved, having left the law and the divine scriptures; heal me wholly, O Benefactor, Who for my sake becamest like me, O God; turn me back, O Tender-hearted Destroyer of passions: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*At Vespers (continued):*

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: for we are utterly filled with contempt, exceedingly filled is our soul with the reproach of the prosperous and the contempt of the proud.*  
**16 (*П*):** The harlot wets with tears Thy most pure and honorable feet, exhorting all to draw near, and to receive the forgiveness of their transgressions: grant her faith unto me also, O Savior, that I may cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*We add as a refrain unto the rest:*  
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

**17 (*Р*):** Cleanse the defilement of my soul, O Thou Who for my sake becamest poor, and didst become an infant in the flesh; having sent down a drop of Thy mercy upon me, the feeble and wretched one, O Christ: wash me from defilement, heal me who am diseased: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**18 (*С*):** Strengthen my soul, O Master, to run unto Thee, and to serve Thee ever; for Thou art my protection and guardian, and defense and help; grant me, O God the Word, to cry unto Thee with boldness: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**19 (*Т*):** Be Thou an unbreakable wall unto us, O Jesus Savior, and merciful God; for deceived in morals and deeds, we have fallen: but raise up Thy creation, as Thou art the Benefactor, and be reconciled, as Thou art tender-hearted: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**20 (*У*):** A prodigal son have I become, who wasted his wealth; with hunger do I now pine away; under Thy shelter do I flee: like him receive me, O Good Father, and make me a partaker of Thy table, that I may cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**21 (*Ф*):** Through envy the author of evil cast down the first-formed from paradise; having said: "Remember me," upon the Tree the robber received paradise; but I with faith and fear, crying: "Remember me," call unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**22 (*Х*):** Stretch forth Thy hand unto me as unto Peter, and lead me up out of the depth, O God; grant me grace and mercy, through the prayers of Thine all-immaculate Mother, who bare Thee without seed, and of all Thy saints: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!`

*Verse: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
**23 (*Ѱ*):** Receive me as I sing unto Thee every day, O Lamb Who takest away my sin; my soul and body wholly do I offer into Thy hands, and by night and by day as is due I cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*At Vespers (continued):*

**24 (*Ѡ*):** O Thine ineffable tender-heartedness, O All-good, Malice-free Lord! O Sinless and Tender-hearted One! Cast me not away from Thy presence, that I also thankfully, rejoicing and singing, may cry unto Thee: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O ineffable condescension! O marvelous and awesome Childbearing! O how the Virgin carries Thee, an Infant, in her embrace, the Creator and God! O Thou Who wast pleased to be incarnate of her, O Benefactor: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me!

*Entrance: "O Joyful Light":*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 93 [LXX 93:1, 2], Tone 4:*[^1032]  
The Lord is the God of vengeances, the God of vengeances has spoken freely.  
*Verse: Lift up Thyself, Thou that judgest the earth, render a recompense to the proud.*

*The Reading from Genesis (Genesis 17:1–9):*[^1033]  
**Reader:** [1] Abram was ninety-nine years old; and the Lord appeared to Abram, and said unto him: I am thy God; be well-pleasing before Me, and be blameless. [2] And I will establish My covenant between Me and between thee, and will multiply thee exceedingly. [3] And Abram fell upon his face; and God spoke unto him, saying: [4] As for Me, behold, My covenant is with thee, and thou shalt be a father of a multitude of nations. [5] And thy name shall no more be called Abram, but thy name shall be Abraham; for a father of many nations have I made thee. [6] And I will increase thee exceedingly, and I will make nations of thee, and kings shall come out of thee. [7] And I will establish My covenant between Me and between thee, Abraham, and between thy seed after thee throughout their generations for an everlasting covenant, to be a God unto thee, and unto thy seed after thee. [8] And I will give unto thee, and unto thy seed after thee, the land wherein thou sojournest, all the land of Canaan, for an everlasting possession; and I will be their God. [9] And God said unto Abraham: Thou therefore shalt keep My covenant, thou, and thy seed after thee throughout their generations.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 94 [LXX 94:1, 2], Tone 4:*[^1034]  
Sing unto the Lord a new song.  
*Verse: Sing unto the Lord, bless His name.*

*The Reading from Proverbs (Proverbs 15:20–16:9):*[^1035]  
**Reader:** [20] A wise son makes a glad father; but a foolish son scorns his mother. [21] The paths of a senseless man lack understanding; but a prudent man walks uprightly. [22] They that honor not assemblies put off counsels; but in the hearts of counselors counsel abides. [23] The wicked man will not give ear unto it, nor speak anything useful and good for the common good. [24] The thoughts of the wise are paths of life, that he may turn aside from hell and be saved. [25] The Lord tears down the houses of scorners; but He establishes the border of the widow. [26] An unrighteous thought is an abomination to the Lord; but the words of the pure are honorable. [27] A taker of bribes destroys himself; but he that hates the receiving of gifts shall be saved. By alms and faiths are sins cleansed; and by the fear of the Lord every man turns away from evil. [28] The hearts of the righteous meditate upon faith; but the mouth of the ungodly answers evil things. The ways of righteous men are acceptable before the Lord; through them even enemies become friends. God is far from the ungodly; but He hears the prayers of the righteous. Better is a small income with righteousness, *At Vespers (continued):*

*The Reading from Proverbs (continued from Proverbs 15:20–16:9):*  
**Reader:** than great revenues with unrighteousness. [9] The heart of a man thinks righteous things, that his steps may be directed by God. [30] The eye that sees good makes the heart glad; and a good report makes the bones fat. [31] He that hears the reproofs of life shall lodge in the midst of the wise. [32] He that rejects instruction evil-hates himself; but he that keeps reproofs loves his own soul. [33] The fear of the Lord is instruction and wisdom; and the beginning of glory will answer to it; [and glory shall go before the humble]. [16:1] To man belongs the preparation of the heart; and from the Lord is the answer of the tongue. [2] All the works of the humble are manifest before God; and the Lord strengthens spirits. [3] Commit thy works unto the Lord, and thy thoughts shall be established. [4] The Lord has made all things for Himself; but the ungodly shall perish in the evil day. [5] Every one that is high-hearted is unclean before God, and he that puts hand to hand unrighteously shall not be unpunished. [6] The beginning of a good way is to do righteous things; and it is more acceptable before God than to sacrifice sacrifices. [7] He that seeks the Lord shall find knowledge with righteousness; and they that seek Him uprightly shall find peace. [8] All the works of the Lord are with righteousness; but the ungodly man is kept for the evil day.

*Then, "Let my prayer be set forth": and the rest of the Order of the Presanctified Gifts.*[^1036]  
*If, however, there is no Presanctified Liturgy:*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," we sing three Prosomoia of the Triodion, and the 24 Stichera of the Great Canon.*[^1037]  
*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice, and the Martyrikon. Glory... Both now... Theotokion: and the rest.*  
*Prokeimenon and Readings.*  
*At the trapeza, we partake of oil, and partake of wine on account of the labor of the vigil that is to be. Those who take heed to be saved apply themselves to temperance, and the majority fast without hypocrisy.*[^1038]  
*We say Small Compline in the cells without prostrations.*  
*After the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Canon of Andrew. Likewise also the Midnight Office.*[^1039]

[Engraving: The Golgotha Cross with the instruments of the Passion (the spear, the reed, the crown of thorns, the ladder, and the skull of Adam), with a domed church in the background.]

[Engraving: Christ the Good Shepherd with sheep, enclosed in an ornate floral frame.]

## On Thursday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast[^1040]

*The klepalo is struck at the 4th hour of the night.*[^1041] *And when we have gathered into the church, the beginning is made by the priest according to custom. And we say: "O Heavenly King," the Trisagion, and after "Our Father": "Lord, have mercy," 12. Glory... Both now... "Come, let us worship," and the other two Psalms, 19 and 20 [LXX 19 and 20], and the usual Troparia, and the Litany. And thereafter we say the Six Psalms, and after the Psalms we sing "Alleluia," and the Triadica of the Tone, as is the custom. And we read the stichology of one Kathisma, the eighth.*[^1042]  
*Then we say the Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos. And we read the Life of Venerable Mary in two staseis.*[^1043]  
*Then Psalm 50 [LXX 50].*  
*We do not sing the biblical Odes.*[^1044]  
*And immediately we begin the Great Canon, slowly, and with a contrite heart and voice, making three metanias at every troparion.*[^1045]

*The Great Canon, the work of our Holy Father Andrew of Crete, of Jerusalem:*[^1046]  
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^1047]  
A Helper and Protector has He become unto me for salvation; He is my God, and I will glorify Him, the God of my father, and I will exalt Him: for gloriously has He been glorified!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*[^1048]

*Troparia:*  
Whence shall I begin to weep for the deeds of my wretched life? What beginning shall I make, O Christ, unto my present lamentation? But as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me remission of transgressions!

Come, O wretched soul, with thy flesh, confess unto the Creator of all, and abstain henceforth from thy former irrationality, and offer tears of repentance unto God!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 1 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Having rivaled the transgression of first-formed Adam, I have recognized myself stripped naked of God, and of the everlasting kingdom and delight, on account of my sins.[^1049]

Woe is me, O wretched soul! Why hast thou become like unto the first Eve? For thou hast looked evilly, and wast bitterly wounded, and didst touch the tree, and didst taste audaciously of the irrational food!

Instead of sensible Eve, an intelligible Eve has arisen within me: the passionate thought in the flesh, showing forth sweet things, yet ever tasting of the bitter drink.

Worthily was Adam driven out of Eden, because he kept not Thy one commandment, O Savior; what then shall I suffer, who ever reject Thy life-giving words?

Surpassing the murder of Cain, by deliberate choice have I become a murderer of my soul's conscience, giving life to the flesh and warring against my soul by my wicked deeds.

I have not resembled the righteousness of Abel, O Jesus; never have I offered Thee an acceptable gift, nor divine actions, nor a pure sacrifice, nor a blameless life.

Like Cain, we also, O wretched soul, have offered together unto the Creator of all defiled deeds, and a blemished sacrifice, and a useless life; therefore we also have been condemned.

Having quickened clay into life, O Fashioner, Thou didst place within me flesh and bones, and breath and life; but, O my Creator, my Redeemer, and Judge, receive me who repent!

I declare unto Thee, O Savior, the sins which I have committed, and the wounds of my soul and body, which murderous thoughts within have laid upon me like robbers.

Though I have sinned, O Savior, yet I know that Thou art the Lover of mankind: Thou chastisest mercifully, and hast fervent compassion; Thou seest him that weeps, and runnest to meet him like the Father, calling back the prodigal.

Cast down before Thy gates, O Savior, at least in old age cast me not away empty into hades; but before the end, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, grant me remission of transgressions!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 1 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
I am he who fell among robbers through my own thoughts; wholly by them am I wounded now, and filled with sores; but do Thou Thyself draw near, O Christ Savior, and heal me![^1050]

The priest, seeing me beforehand, passed by, and the Levite, beholding me in afflictions naked, looked past me; but O Jesus, Who didst shine forth from Mary, do Thou draw near and have compassion on me!

O Lamb of God, Who takest away the sins of all, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me tears of compunction!

It is the time of repentance; I come unto Thee, my Creator: take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me tears of compunction!

Abhor me not, O Savior, cast me not away from Thy face; take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me remission of sinful falls!

My voluntary and involuntary transgressions, O Savior, manifest and secret, known and unknown, having forgiven them all as God, cleanse and save me!

From youth, O Christ, have I transgressed Thy commandments; carelessly, with all passions and in despondency have I passed my life; therefore I cry unto Thee, O Savior: at least at the end, save me!

Having wasted my wealth in prodigality, O Savior, I am empty of pious fruits; hungering, I cry aloud: O Father of mercies, anticipating me, do Thou have compassion on me!

Unto Thee do I fall down, O Jesus: I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me; take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me tears of compunction!

Enter not into judgment with me, bringing forward my deeds, seeking out words, and correcting impulses; but in Thy mercies overlooking my dreadful sins, save me, O Almighty!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Another Canon of our Venerable Mother Mary of Egypt, in the same tone and Heirmos:*[^1051]  
*Whose acrostic is: Do thou, Venerable Mary, help.*[^1052]  
*Refrain: Venerable Mother Mary, pray to God for us.*[^1053]  
*Troparia:*  
Grant me radiant grace from divine Providence on high, Mary, to flee from the darkness of the passions, and fervently to sing the fair accomplishments of thy life!

Bowing down unto the divine laws of Christ, unto Him hast thou drawn near, having abandoned the unrestrained impulses of pleasures, and with all reverence didst accomplish every virtue as one!

*To Saint Andrew:*  
*Refrain: Venerable Father Andrew, pray to God for us.*[^1054]  
*Troparion:*  
By thy prayers, Andrew, deliver us from shameful passions, and show forth partakers of Christ's kingdom now those who praise thee with faith and love, O glorious one, we pray thee!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Super-essential Trinity, worshipped in Unity, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art tender-hearted, grant me tears of compunction!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Theotokos, hope and protection of those who praise thee, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as pure Sovereign Lady, receive me who repent!

*Ode 2. Heirmos:*[^1055]  
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and will sing of Christ, Who came in the flesh from the Virgin!

*Troparia:*  
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak; O earth, give ear unto the voice of one repenting before God, and praising Him!

Give ear unto me, O God my Savior, with Thy merciful eye, and receive my fervent confession!

I have sinned more than all men; I alone have sinned against Thee: but as God, O Savior, have compassion upon Thy creation!

A tempest of evils holds me fast, O Tender-hearted Lord: but stretch forth Thy hand unto me also, as unto Peter!

The tears of the harlot do I also offer, O Merciful One: cleanse me, O Savior, by Thy tender-heartedness!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 2 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
I have darkened the soul's beauty with the pleasures of passions, and in every way have turned my whole mind into dust.[^1056]

I have rent now my first garment, which the Fashioner wove for me from the beginning, and thenceforth I lie naked.

I have clothed myself in a rent garment, which the serpent wove for me by his counsel, and I am ashamed.

I looked upon the beauty of the garden, and was deceived in my mind; and thenceforth I lie naked, and am ashamed.

All the rulers of passions plowed upon my back, lengthening their iniquity upon me.

I have destroyed my first-formed comeliness and my goodly beauty, and now I lie naked, and am ashamed.

Sin sewed coats of skins for me, stripping me of the first God-woven garment.

I am compassed about with a vesture of shame, as with fig leaves, unto the reproof of my self-willed passions.

I have clothed myself in a shameful garment, and one shamefully stained with blood by the flux of a passionate and pleasure-loving life.

I have defiled the garment of my flesh, and sullied that which is according to Thine image and likeness, O Savior.

I fell into passionate ruin and into material corruption; and thenceforth until now the enemy torments me.

Having preferred a material-loving and possessions-loving life through intemperance, O Savior, I am now burdened with a heavy load.

I have adorned the fleshly image with the diverse covering of defiled thoughts, and I am condemned.

I took diligent care for outward adornment alone, having neglected the inner God-fashioned tabernacle.

Having depicted the deformity of my passions by pleasure-seeking impulses, I destroyed the beauty of the mind.

I have buried the comeliness of the first image with passions, O Savior, which do Thou seek and find, as once the lost coin!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 2 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
I have sinned! As the harlot I cry unto Thee: I alone have sinned against Thee; receive my tears also as myrrh, O Savior!

I have slipped lustfully like David, and have been defiled; but wash me also, O Savior, with tears!

Cleanse me! As the publican I cry unto Thee, O Savior, cleanse me! For none of those born of Adam has sinned against Thee as have I!

I have neither tears, nor repentance, nor compunction: do Thou Thyself grant these unto me, O Savior, as God!

Shut not Thy door against me then, O Lord, Lord: but open it unto me who repent before Thee!

O Lover of mankind, Who desirest all to be saved, do Thou recall me and receive me who repent, as Thou art good!

Give ear unto the sighings of my soul, and receive the drops from mine eyes, O Savior, and save me!

*Theotokion:*  
All-immaculate Virgin Theotokos, alone all-hymned, pray fervently that we may be saved!

*Another Heirmos:*[^1057]  
See, see that I am God, Who rained manna, and made water gush from the rock of old in the wilderness for My people, by My right hand alone and by My might!

*Troparia:*  
"See, see that I am God": give ear, O my soul, unto the Lord crying aloud; and depart from thy former sin, and fear Him as incorruptible, and as Judge and God!

Unto whom hast thou become like, O much-sinning soul? Only unto the first Cain, and unto that Lamech, having stoned the body with evil deeds, and killed the mind with irrational impulses!

Passing through all that were before the Law, O soul, thou hast not resembled Seth, nor imitated Enos, nor Enoch by translation, nor Noah; but hast been shown destitute of the life of the righteous.[^1058]

Thou alone, O my soul, hast opened the floodgates of the wrath of thy God, and hast drowned all thy flesh, deeds, and life as the earth, and hast remained outside the saving ark!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 2 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
"I have slain a man unto my wound, and a young man unto my hurt," cried Lamech weeping; but thou, O my soul, tremblest not, having sullied thy flesh and defiled thy mind![^1059]

O how have I rivaled Lamech, the former murderer: having slain the soul as a man, the mind as a young man, and as a brother mine own body, like Cain the murderer, by pleasure-seeking impulses!

Thou didst devise to build a tower, O soul, and to establish a fortress by thy lusts, had not the Fashioner confounded thy counsels and dashed thy contrivances to the earth!

I am wounded, I am pierced: behold, the arrows of the enemy have pierced my soul and body! Behold, ulcers, putrefactions, and dark spots cry aloud, the wounds of my willful passions!

The Lord once rained down fire from the Lord upon angering iniquity, consuming the Sodomites; but thou, O soul, hast kindled the fire of Gehenna, in which thou shalt be burned!

Understand and see that I am God, Who searches hearts and tests thoughts, Who reproves deeds, burns up sins, and judges the orphan, the humble, and the poor!

*To the Venerable Mother Mary:*  
Thou didst stretch forth thy hands unto the merciful God, Mary, when submerged in the abyss of evils; and as unto Peter He lovingly stretched forth His divine hand, in every way seeking thy conversion.

*To Mary:*  
With all fervor and love didst thou hasten unto Christ, having turned away from the former way of sin, feeding thyself in untrodden wildernesses, and purely fulfilling His divine commandments.

*To Saint Andrew:*  
We see, we see the love of mankind, O soul, of God and Master: therefore before the end let us fall down before Him with tears, crying aloud: By the prayers of Andrew, O Savior, have mercy on us!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 2 (continued):*  
*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Beginningless, Uncreated Trinity, Undivided Unity: receive me repenting, save me who have sinned; I am Thy creation, despise me not, but spare me, and deliver me from the condemnation of fire!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O All-immaculate Sovereign Lady, Parent of God, hope of those who flee unto thee, and haven of those who are in storm: by thy prayers make the merciful Creator and thy Son propitious unto me!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1060]  
Upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish my mind!

*Troparia:*  
The Lord once rained down fire from the Lord, burning up the land of Sodom aforetime.[^1061]

Flee for safety to the mountain, O soul, like Lot of old, and make haste into Zoar!

Flee the conflagration, O soul! Flee the burning of Sodom! Flee the destruction of the divine flame!

I confess unto Thee, O Savior: I have sinned, I have sinned against Thee! But remit, forgive me, as Thou art tender-hearted!

I alone have sinned against Thee, I have sinned more than all, O Christ Savior: despise me not!

Thou art the Good Shepherd: seek me, the lamb, and despise me not who have gone astray!

Thou art sweet Jesus, Thou art my Creator: in Thee, O Savior, shall I be justified!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Trinity, Unity, O God! Save us from deception, temptations, and adversities!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O womb that received God! Rejoice, O throne of the Lord! Rejoice, O Mother of our Life!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 3 (continued):*  
*Another Heirmos:*[^1062]  
Establish, O Lord, upon the rock of Thy commandments my shaken heart, for Thou alone art holy and the Lord!

*Troparia:*  
I have acquired Thee as the Fountain of life, the Destroyer of death, and I cry unto Thee from my heart before the end: I have sinned, cleanse, save me![^1063]

I have imitated those who lived in fornication in the time of Noah, O Savior, inheriting their condemnation of being submerged in the flood.

I have sinned, O Lord, I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me; for there is none among men who has sinned, whom I have not surpassed by my transgressions!

Having imitated that Ham, the scorner of his father, O soul, thou hast not covered the shame of thy neighbor, walking backward with averted face!

The blessing of Shem thou hast not inherited, O wretched soul, nor hast thou possessed a spacious domain on the earth of forgiveness, like Japhs!

From the land of Haran depart from sin, O my soul: come into the land welling forth ever-living incorruption, which Abraham inherited!

Thou hast heard of Abraham, O my soul, who of old left the land of his fatherland and became a sojourner: imitate his deliberate choice!

Having entertained the angels at the oak of Mamre, the patriarch inherited in old age the prize of the promise.

Understanding Isaac, O my wretched soul, as a new sacrifice mystically offered as a whole burnt offering unto the Lord, imitate his deliberate choice!

Thou hast heard of Ishmael, cast out as the offspring of a bondwoman: be sober, O my soul; take heed lest thou suffer anything like unto him through wanton indulgence!

Thou hast become like unto Hagar the Egyptian of old, O soul, having enslaved thyself by deliberate choice and given birth to a new Ishmael: insolence!

Thou hast understood the ladder of Jacob, O my soul, shown from earth unto heaven: why hast thou not held fast to the firm step of piety?

Imitate the priest of God and solitary king, the likeness of Christ in the world of life among men!

Become not a pillar of salt, O soul, by turning back: let the Sodomite image terrify thee; save thyself high up in Zoar!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 3 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Fleeing from the burning like Lot, flee, O my soul, from sin; flee from Sodom and Gomorrah; flee from the flame of every irrational desire!

Have mercy, O Lord, have mercy upon me, I cry unto Thee, when Thou shalt come with Thine angels to render unto all according to the worth of their deeds!

Cast not away the supplication of those who hymn Thee, O Master, but show compassion, O Lover of mankind, and grant forgiveness unto those who ask in faith!

*To St. Mary:*  
I am possessed by storm and the surge of transgressions; but do thou thyself now save me, O mother, and lead me unto the haven of divine repentance!

*To St. Mary:*  
Having offered servile supplication even now, O venerable one, unto the tenderhearted Mother of God through thy prayers, open unto me the divine entrances!

*To St. Andrew:*  
By thy prayers grant unto me also the remission of debts, O Andrew, president of Crete, for thou art an eminent initiate of the mysteries of repentance!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O simple, uncreated Trinity, beginningless nature, hymned in a Trinity of hypostases: save us who worship Thy dominion in faith!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God, who without knowing man hast given birth in time unto the timeless Son from the Father: a strange wonder, having remained a virgin while nursing!

*Heirmos:*  
Establish, O Lord, upon the rock...

*Sessional Hymn, by Lord Joseph, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thou hast risen from the tomb" (Воскре́слъ є҆сѝ ѿ гро́ба):*[^1064]  
O radiant luminaries, apostles of the Savior, enlighten us in the darkness of life, that we may now walk becomingly as in the day, fleeing by the light of temperance from nocturnal passions, and may behold with joy the radiant Passions of Christ!

*Glory... Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^1065]  
O God-chosen twelve-fold choir of the Apostles, offer now supplication unto Christ, that all who perform their prayers in compunction...

*At Matins (continued):*

...may pass through the course of the Fast, zealously practicing virtues, so that we may thus come beforehand to behold the glorious Resurrection of Christ God, offering glory and praise!

*Both now... Theotokion, Podoben:*[^1066]  
O Mother of God, together with the Apostles, entreat the incomprehensible Son and Word of God, Who was ineffably born of thee beyond comprehension, to grant pure peace unto the universe, and before the end to grant us forgiveness of transgressions, and for the sake of His extreme goodness, to make Thy servants worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom!

*Then the Triodion, without prostrations.*[^1067]  
*Ode 4, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^1068]  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity!

*Troparia:*  
Having lived in self-mastery, the enlightened apostles of Christ calm for us the season of fasting through their divine intercessions.

A twelve-stringed instrument, the divine choir of the disciples, sang a saving song, confounding evil voices.[^1069]

With spiritual rain ye have watered the whole world under the sun, having driven away the drought of polytheism, O all-blessed ones!

*Theotokion:*  
Save me who have humbled myself, having lived in high-mindedness, O all-pure Virgin, who gavest birth unto Him Who hath exalted our humbled nature!

*Another Canon, in the same tone. Heirmos: I have heard, O Lord:*[^1070]  
*Troparia:*  
O all-honorable assembly of the Apostles, praying unto the Creator of all: ask that He have mercy upon us who praise you!

Being husbandmen of Christ, O apostles, who have cultivated the whole world by the divine word, ye ever bring forth fruits unto Him.

Ye became the vineyard of Christ, truly beloved, for ye have poured forth spiritual wine unto the world, O apostles!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O transcendentally-originate, co-equal, all-powerful Holy Trinity, Father, Word, and Holy Spirit, God, Light and Life: preserve Thy flock!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O fiery-visaged Throne! Rejoice, O light-bearing Lampstand! Rejoice, O Mountain of sanctification, Ark of life, Holy of holies Tabernacle!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 4 of the Great Canon. Another Heirmos:*[^1071]  
The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid: that Thou wast to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: I have heard the report of Thee and was afraid: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*  
Despise not Thy works, abandon not Thy creation, O Righteous Judge, even though I alone have sinned as a man more than any man, O Lover of mankind; yet as Lord of all, Thou hast authority to forgive sins!

The end is drawing near, O soul, it is drawing near, and thou dost not care, nor dost thou prepare; the time is growing short, arise: the Judge is near at the doors! Like a dream, like a flower, the time of life runs on; why do we trouble ourselves in vain?

Awake, O my soul! Consider thy deeds which thou hast committed; bring them before thy face, and shed a drop of thy tears; speak with boldness of thy deeds and thoughts unto Christ, and be justified!

There has been no sin in life, nor deed, nor wickedness, which I have not committed, O Savior, having sinned in mind and word, in deliberate choice and intention, in thought and deed, as no one else ever has!

Therefore am I condemned, therefore am I convicted, wretched that I am, by mine own conscience, than which nothing in the world is more compelling: O Judge, my Redeemer and Knower of hearts, spare and deliver, and save me, Thy servant!
