The ladder which the great one among the patriarchs saw of old is a pattern, O my soul, of practical ascent and of noetic elevation; if then thou desirest to live in practice, in knowledge, and in contemplation, be renewed![^1072]

The patriarch endured the heat of the day for the sake of privation, and bore the frost of the night, daily making provision, pasturing, laboring, and serving, that he might obtain two wives.

Understand the two wives to mean practice and contemplation in knowledge: Leah as practice, since she had many children, and Rachel as knowledge, since she was acquired with much labor; for without labors, O soul, neither practice nor contemplation will succeed!

Watch, O my soul, excel as of old the great one among the patriarchs, that thou mayest acquire practice with knowledge, that thou mayest be a mind seeing God, and reach the unwaning darkness in vision, and be a great merchant!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 4 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Having begotten the twelve patriarchs, the great one among the patriarchs mystically established for thee, O my soul, a ladder of active ascent, having wisely set his children as foundations and his steps as an ascent![^1073]

Thou hast imitated hated Esau, O soul, and hast surrendered to thy seducer the birthright of the first beauty, and hast fallen away from the paternal prayer, and twice hast thou slipped, wretched one, in practice and in knowledge: therefore repent now!

Esau was called Edom because of his extreme frenzy for women; for burning ever with intemperance and defiled by pleasures, he was named Edom, which is interpreted as the inflamed burning of a sin-loving soul.

Having heard of Job on the dungheap who was justified, O my soul, thou hast not emulated his courage, nor hast thou possessed a steadfast purpose in all that thou knowest and wherewith thou hast been tempted, but thou hast shown thyself impatient![^1074]

He who was before upon a throne is now naked and festering on a dungheap; he who had many children and was glorious is suddenly childless and homeless: for he accounted the dungheap as a palace, and his sores as pearls!

Clothed in royal dignity, with a crown and purple, a man of great possessions and righteous, abounding in wealth and herds, suddenly grew poor and was deprived of his wealth and the glory of his kingdom.

If that man was righteous and blameless more than all, yet did not escape the hunter's snare and net: what wilt thou do, O wretched soul, that lovest sin, if something unexpected should come upon thee?

The body is defiled, the spirit is soiled, and I am wholly covered with sores: but as a physician, O Christ, heal both by my repentance; wash, cleanse, and show me forth, O my Savior, whiter than snow!

Being crucified for all, Thou didst offer Thy Body and Blood, O Word: Thy Body, that Thou mightest renew me; Thy Blood, that Thou mightest wash me; and Thou didst surrender Thy Spirit, O Christ, that Thou mightest bring me unto Thy Father!

Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O Compassionate One, that we might be saved: willingly wast Thou crucified upon the Tree; Eden that was shut was opened; the things above and the things below, and all the saved nations worship Thee!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 4 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
May the Blood from Thy side be a font for me, and the water that flowed forth a drink of forgiveness together, that I may be cleansed on both sides, anointed and drinking, as an anointing and drink, O Word, Thy life-flowing words![^1075]

I am naked of the bridal chamber, I am naked of the marriage, and together of the banquet: the lamp is extinguished, as having no oil; the bridal chamber is shut while I slept; the supper has been eaten; and I, bound hand and foot, am cast outside!

The Church has acquired as a chalice Thy life-bearing side, from which it poured forth for us two-fold streams of forgiveness and knowledge, in the image of the Old and the New, the two Covenants together, O our Savior!

The time of my life is short, and filled with pain and wickedness: but receive me in repentance, and summon me unto knowledge, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: do Thou Thyself have compassion on me, O Savior!

I am high-spoken now, and also hard of heart, in vain and to no purpose: condemn me not with the Pharisee; but rather grant me the humility of the Publican, O only Compassionate One, Righteous Judge, and number me with him!

I have sinned, having outraged the vessel of my flesh, I know it, O Compassionate One: but receive me in repentance, and summon me unto knowledge, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: do Thou Thyself have compassion on me, O Savior!

I have become an idol unto myself by passions, harming my soul, O Compassionate One: but receive me in repentance, and summon me unto knowledge, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: do Thou Thyself have compassion on me, O Savior!

I did not listen to Thy voice, I disobeyed Thy Scripture, O Lawgiver: but receive me in repentance, and summon me unto knowledge, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: do Thou Thyself have compassion on me, O Savior!

*To St. Mary:*[^1076]  
Passing thy life in the flesh like the bodiless powers, O venerable one, thou hast truly received great grace with God; intercede faithfully for those who honor thee: therefore we pray thee, deliver us also from all adversities by thy prayers!

*To St. Mary:*  
Having been dragged down into the depth of great iniquities, thou wast not held fast; but with a higher thought thou didst hasten unto the highest virtue by deeds openly, having marvelously amazed the angelic nature, O Mary!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 4 (continued):*  
*To St. Andrew:*  
O Andrew, paternal boast, cease not to pray with thy supplications, standing before the all-divine Trinity, that we may be delivered from torment, who with love invoke thee as a divine advocate, O all-blessed one, the ornament of Crete!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
Undivided in essence, unconfused in persons, I theologize Thee, the one Trinitarian Godhead, as of one kingship and co-enthroned; I cry unto Thee the great hymn, sung thrice in the highest!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou both givest birth and remainest a virgin, and abidest in both manners a Virgin by nature: He Who is born renews the laws of nature, and the womb gives birth without having conceived: for where God wills, the order of nature is overcome; for He does whatsoever He wills!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1077]  
Rising early from the night, O Lover of mankind, enlighten me, I pray Thee, and guide me in Thy commandments, and teach me, O Savior, to do Thy will!

*Troparia:*  
In the night have I passed my life continually, for the night of sin was unto me darkness and thick gloom; but show me forth as a son of the day, O Savior!

Imitating Reuben, wretched that I am, I have committed an unlawful and transgressing counsel against the most high God, having defiled my bed as he did his father's![^1078]

I confess unto Thee, O Christ the King: I have sinned, I have sinned, like the brothers of old who sold Joseph, the fruit of purity and chastity!

By his kinsmen was the righteous soul bound, the sweet one was sold into slavery, as a type of the Lord: but thou, O soul, hast wholly sold thyself by thine evil deeds!

Imitate the righteous and chaste mind of Joseph, O wretched and untried soul, and do not be defiled by continually breaking the law with irrational desires!

Even though Joseph dwelt in a pit of old, O Master and Lord, yet it was as a type of Thy burial and resurrection; but what such thing shall I ever offer unto Thee?

Thou hast heard, O soul, of the ark of Moses, borne by the waters and the waves of the river, as if in a bridal chamber, fleeing from the deed of the bitter Pharaonic decree of old!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 5 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
If thou hast heard of the midwives who of old killed the immature male child, O wretched soul, which is the practice of chastity: now, like great Moses, suckle wisdom![^1079]

Like great Moses who struck the Egyptian, thou hast not slain the mind wounded by sin, O wretched soul; and how, tell me, wilt thou dwell in the wilderness of passions through repentance?

Great Moses dwelt in the wilderness: come then, imitate his life, that in the bush of the Divine Epiphany, O soul, thou mayest also be in contemplation!

Picture to thyself the rod of Moses, O soul, striking the sea and solidifying the deep, as a type of the divine Cross, through which thou also canst accomplish great things!

Aaron offered unto God a blameless and unbeguiling fire; but Hophni and Phinehas, like thee, O soul, offered unto God a strange, defiled life!

Like Pharaoh bitter and hard in disposition, have I become, O Master, Jannes and Jambres in soul and body, and submerged in mind: but do Thou help me!

Wretched that I am, I have mingled with mire in my mind: wash me, O Master, in the bath of my tears, I pray Thee, making the garment of my flesh whiter than snow!

If I examine my deeds, O Savior, I see that I have surpassed every man in sins, for I have sinned consciously with deliberate thought, not in ignorance!

Spare, spare Thy creation, O Lord: I have sinned, forgive me, for Thou alone art pure by nature, and none other besides Thee is without defilement!

Being God, Thou didst take form in me for my sake; Thou hast shown forth wonders, healing lepers, and strengthening the paralytic: Thou didst stop the flow of blood of the woman with the issue of blood, O Savior, by the touch of Thy garments![^1080]

Imitate the woman with the issue of blood, O wretched soul: hasten, lay hold of the hem of Christ's garment, that thou mayest be delivered from afflictions, and hear from Him: Thy faith has saved thee!

Imitate the bowed woman, O soul: come, fall down at the feet of Jesus, that He may straighten thee, and that thou mayest walk uprightly in the paths of the Lord!

Even though Thou art a deep well, O Master, pour forth water for me from Thy most pure veins, that like the Samaritan woman, drinking I may thirst no more, for Thou pourest forth the streams of life!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 5 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
May my tears be as Siloam unto me, O Master and Lord, that I also may wash the pupils of my heart, and see Thee noetically, the pre-eternal Light![^1081]

*To St. Mary:*  
With incomparable desire, O all-wealthy one, having desired to worship the Life-bearing Wood, thou wast deemed worthy of thy desire: make me worthy also to obtain the glory on high!

*To St. Mary:*  
Having crossed the stream of the Jordan, thou didst find painless rest, having escaped the pleasures of the flesh; from which deliver us also by thy prayers, O venerable one!

*To St. Andrew:*  
As an eminent pastor, O all-wise Andrew, being chosen, with great love and fear I pray thee: by thy prayers may I obtain salvation and eternal life!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
Thee, the Trinity, we glorify, the one God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Father, Son, and Spirit, simple Essence, Unity ever worshipped!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
From thee, O incorrupt, unwedded Virgin Mother, did God Who created the ages clothe Himself in my clay, and united unto Himself human nature!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
I cried with my whole heart unto the compassionate God, and He heard me from the lowest hell, and brought up my life from corruption!

*Troparia:*  
The tears of mine eyes and sighs from the depth I offer in purity, O Savior, while my heart cries: O God, I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me!

Thou hast turned away from thy Lord, O soul, like Dathan and Abiram; but spare, cry out from the lowest hell, lest the chasm of the earth cover thee!

Like a heifer that has grown wild, O soul, thou hast become like Ephraim: as a gazelle from the snares, preserve thy life, having winged thy mind with practice and contemplation!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 6 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Let the hand of Moses convince us, O soul, how God can whiten and cleanse a leprous life; and despair not of thyself, even if thou art leprous![^1082]

The waves of my transgressions, O Savior, returning as in the Red Sea, have suddenly overwhelmed me, as of old the Egyptians and their captains!

Thou hadst an irrational choice, O soul, like Israel of old: for thou didst irrationally prefer the voluptuous surfeit of passions to the divine manna!

Thou hast preferred the wells of Canaanite thoughts, O soul, rather than the vein of the rock, from which wisdom, like a cup, pours forth streams of theology!

Swine's flesh and cauldrons, and Egyptian food, hast thou preferred above heavenly food, O my soul, as of old did the senseless people in the wilderness!

When Thy servant Moses struck the rock with his rod, he typically prefigured Thy life-creating side, from which we all draw the drink of life, O Savior!

Search and inspect, O soul, like Joshua the son of Nun, what the land of promise is like, and settle therein through good obedience to the law!

Arise and war, as Joshua against Amalek, against the passions of the flesh, ever conquering the Gibeonites, the deceitful thoughts!

Pass through the flowing nature of time, as the Ark of old, and take possession of that land of promise, O soul: God commands it!

As Thou didst save Peter when he cried out: Save me, come beforehand, O Savior, deliver me from the beast, stretching forth Thy hand, and lead me up from the depth of sin![^1083]

I know Thee as a tranquil haven, O Master, Master Christ: but coming beforehand, deliver me from the inaccessible depths of sin and despair!

I am the coin with the royal image which Thou didst lose of old, O Savior: but having lit the lamp, Thy Forerunner, O Word, seek and find Thine image!

*To St. Mary:*  
That thou mightest extinguish the flame of passions, thou didst ever shed drops of tears, O Mary, being inflamed in soul: the grace thereof grant unto me also, thy servant!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 6 (continued):*  
*To St. Mary:*  
Thou hast acquired heavenly dispassion by thy supreme life on earth, O mother; therefore pray that those who hymn thee may be delivered from passions by thy prayers!

*To St. Andrew:*  
Knowing thee as the pastor and president of Crete, and an intercessor for the universe, I flee unto thee, Andrew, and cry unto thee: deliver me, O father, from the depth of sin!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
"I am the Trinity, simple, undivided, divided in persons, and I am the Unity united by nature," says the Father, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thy womb gave birth unto God for us, formed in our likeness; pray unto Him as the Creator of all, O Mother of God, that by thy prayers we may be justified!

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*[^1084]  
My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end is drawing near, and thou shalt be confounded! Awake then, that Christ God may spare thee, Who is everywhere present and fills all things!

*Ikos:*  
Beholding Christ's healing opened, and health flowing therefrom unto Adam, the devil suffered and was wounded, and as in distress he lamented, and cried out unto his friends: "What shall I do unto the Son of Mary? The Bethlehemite kills me, Who is everywhere present and fills all things!"

*Synaxarion first of the Menaion, then the present one:*[^1085]  
*Verses:* Grant examples of compunction, O my Christ, unto those who now chant the Great Canon unto Thee.

On this day, according to ancient tradition, we chant the office of the Great Canon. This, truly the greatest of all canons, was excellently and artfully composed and written by our father among the saints, Andrew, Archbishop of Crete, who is also called "of Jerusalem." He originated from Damascus, and at the fourteenth year of his age, having devoted himself to grammatical training, and having mastered the complete cycle of liberal education, he arrived in Jerusalem, where he passed through the monastic life, living venerably and God-pleasingly in quietude and unperturbed living. He also left to the Church of God many other writings profitable for life, discourses and canons, showing himself and appearing particularly valiant in festival celebrations. Together with many others, he also composed the present Great Canon...

*At Matins (continued):*

...having boundless compunction; for having recorded and compiled every narrative of the Old and New Testaments, he composed the present sweet hymnody, clearly from Adam even unto Christ's very Ascension and the apostolic preaching.[^1086] He thereby rouses every soul to emulate and imitate according to its power whatever good stories are told, and to flee whatever evil things, and continually run unto God through repentance, tears, confession, and other clear pleasing of God. Yet it is so vast and sweet-sounding that it is able to soften even the hardest soul and awaken it unto noble vigilance, if only it be sung with a contrite heart and fitting attentiveness. He composed this also at the time when the great Sophronius, Patriarch of Jerusalem, wrote the Life of St. Mary of Egypt. For this Life also offers boundless compunction and grants great consolation unto those who have transgressed and are sinning, if only they wish to depart from evils. They were appointed to be sung and read on the present day for the following reason: because the Holy Forty Days is drawing near to its end, lest men, having grown slothful toward spiritual labors, neglectfully forget themselves and entirely cease from chastity. The great Andrew, therefore, like an instructor, by the narratives of the Great Canon recounted the virtue of great men and again the turning away from evil, as if one might say: he prepares those who labor most valiantly and bravely stretch forward unto what lies ahead. And the holy Sophronius, by his supernatural discourse, causes men to become chaste once more, raises them unto God, and urges them neither to fall down nor despair, even if they have at times been overtaken by certain transgressions. For how great is God's love for mankind and mercy toward those who whole-souledly choose to turn away from their former transgressions, the narrative concerning the Egyptian woman shows forth. It is called the Great Canon, as one might say, both because of these thoughts and recollections—for its composer is worthy of this, having composed them excellently—and because, while other canons contain about thirty troparia or a few more, this one reaches two hundred and fifty, each one dripping with ineffable sweetness. Fittingly and appropriately, therefore, this Great Canon acquired great compunction, for which reason it was appointed unto the Great Forty Days. This excellent and Great Canon, and the discourse on the venerable Mary, the same father of ours Andrew first brought unto the city of Constantine, when he came to assist, being sent by Patriarch Theodore of Jerusalem unto the Sixth Council. For having then contended excellently against the Monothelites, while still remaining among the monastics, he was enrolled in the clergy of the Church in the city of Constantine; then he was ordained deacon and appointed curator of orphans therein. And a little while later having become Archbishop of Crete, afterward, having reached near a place called Hieros toward Mytilene, he departed unto the Lord, having well shared in his see. By his prayers, O God, have mercy upon us.

*Then the Beatitudes with prostrations. Tone 6:*[^1087]  
In Thy Kingdom, when Thou comest, remember us, O Lord.  
Thou didst make the thief an inhabitant of paradise, O Christ, when he cried unto Thee upon the Cross: "Remember me!" Count me also, an unworthy servant, worthy of his repentance.

*Verse: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the Kingdom of heaven.*  
Having heard of Manoah of old, O my soul, when God appeared, and who then received the fruit of promise from a barren woman, imitate his piety.

*Verse: Blessed are those who mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
Having rivaled the indolence of Samson, thou hast shorn the head of thy works, O my soul, having surrendered through sensual pleasure unto foreigners thy chaste and blessed life.

*At Matins (continued):*

*Verse: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*[^1088]  
He who of old conquered the foreigners with the jawbone of an ass is now found captive to passionate self-indulgence; but flee, O my soul, such imitation, action, and softness.

*Verse: Blessed are those who hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*  
Barak and Jephthah the military commanders, judges of Israel, were preferred, with them Deborah of manly mind; being strengthened, O soul, by their valiance, be thou made manly!

*Verse: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
Thou hast known the bravery of Jael, O my soul, who of old pierced Sisera and wrought salvation with a sharp wooden peg—hearest thou?—whereby the Cross is prefigured for thee.

*Verse: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
Offer, O soul, a sacrifice of praise; bring action as a daughter purer than Jephthah's, and slay as a sacrifice the carnal passions unto thy Lord.

*Verse: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called sons of God.*[^1089]  
Consider Gideon's fleece, O my soul; receive the dew from heaven, and lap like a dog, and drink the water flowing from the Law by the wringing out of the letter.

*Verse: Blessed are those who are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the Kingdom of heaven.*  
Thou hast received the condemnation of Eli the priest, O my soul, through lack of discernment having acquired passions for thyself, just as he his children, to work iniquities.

*Verse: Blessed are you when they reproach you and persecute you, and say all kinds of evil against you falsely for My sake.*  
Among the judges, the Levite through neglect divided his wife unto the twelve tribes, O my soul, that he might expose the unlawful defilement of Benjamin.

*Verse: Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in the heavens.*  
The wise Hannah while praying moved her lips indeed in praise, but her voice was not heard; yet being barren, she gives birth to a worthy son of prayer.

*At Matins (continued):*

*Verse: Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*[^1090]  
Among the judges was numbered the offspring of Hannah, the great Samuel, whom Ramah nurtured in the house of the Lord; emulate him, O my soul, and judge thine own works before those of others.

*Verse: Remember us, O Master, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
David being chosen for the kingdom was anointed kingly with the horn of divine chrism; thou therefore, O my soul, if thou desirest the Kingdom on high, be anointed with the chrism of tears.

*Verse: Remember us, O Holy One, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
Have mercy on Thy creation, O Merciful One; show compassion unto the work of Thy hands, and spare all who have sinned, and me above all, who have despised Thy commandments.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
Unto the unoriginate One, and unto the begetting and proceeding: I worship the Father Who begat, I glorify the Son Who was begotten, and I hymn the Spirit Who shone forth together with the Father and the Son.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
We worship Thy supernatural childbearing, not dividing according to nature the glory of Thy Child, O Birth-giver of God; for He Who is one in Person is confessed in two natures.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1091]  
We have sinned, we have transgressed, we have done unrighteously before Thee, neither have we kept nor done as Thou hast commanded us; but deliver us not up to the end, O God of the fathers.

*Troparia:*  
I have sinned, I have transgressed, and I have rejected Thy commandment, for I was brought forth in sins and have added wounds to my sores; but do Thou Thyself have mercy upon me, as Thou art tenderhearted, O God of the fathers.

I have confessed the secrets of my heart unto Thee, my Judge; see my humility, see also my affliction, and give heed unto my judgment now; and do Thou Thyself have mercy upon me, as Thou art tenderhearted, O God of the fathers.

Saul of old, when he lost his father's asses, O soul, suddenly found a kingdom for renown;[^1092] but take heed, forget not thyself, having preferred thy brutish lusts over the Kingdom of Christ.

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 7 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
David of old, the forefather of God, though he sinned doubly, O my soul, struck by the arrow of adultery and captive to the spear of torment for murder; yet thou art sick with even heavier deeds by thine own voluntary impulses.[^1093]

David of old joined iniquity to iniquity, mingling murder with adultery, yet he immediately showed forth twofold repentance; but thou, O most wicked soul, hast done worse deeds without repenting unto God.

David of old depicted it, composing as on an icon a hymn whereby he exposes the deed he had done, crying: "Have mercy on me, for against Thee only have I sinned, the God of all: do Thou Thyself cleanse me!"

When the Ark was being carried on a cart, that Uzzah, when the calf turned aside, merely touched it and experienced the wrath of God; but fleeing his presumption, O soul, revere divine things honorably.

Thou hast heard of Absalom, how he rose up against nature; thou hast known his foul deeds whereby he defiled the bed of David his father; yet thou hast imitated his passionate and pleasure-loving impulses.

Thou hast subjected thy free dignity unto thy body; for having found another Ahithophel as an enemy, O soul, thou didst condescend to his counsel; but Christ Himself scattered these things, that thou by all means mightest be saved.

Solomon the marvelous, full of the grace of wisdom, having once done evil before God, departed from Him; unto whom thou, O soul, hast become like by thy cursed life.[^1094]

Dragged by the pleasures of his passions, he was defiled—woe is me! The lover of wisdom became a lover of wanton women and an alien from God; whom thou hast imitated in mind, O soul, by foul sensual pleasures.

Thou hast rivaled Rehoboam, who listened not unto the counsel of his father, and likewise that most wicked servant Jeroboam, the former apostate, O soul; but flee imitation, and cry unto God: "I have sinned, show compassion unto me!"

Thou hast rivaled the defilements of Ahab, O my soul—woe is me! Thou hast become a dwelling-place of carnal defilements and a shameful vessel of passions; but groan from thy depths, and tell thy sins unto God.

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 7 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Elijah of old burned twice fifty of Jezebel's men, when he destroyed the shameful prophets to the rebuke of Ahab; but flee the imitation of both, O soul, and be strengthened.[^1095]

Heaven was shut unto thee, O soul, and a famine from God overtook thee, when like Ahab thou didst not submit of old to the words of Elijah the Tishbite; but become like the woman of Zarephath, feed the prophet's soul!

Thou hast gathered the sins of Manasseh by deliberate choice, setting up passions as abominations and multiplying wrath, O soul; but warmly emulating his repentance, acquire compunction.

I fall down before Thee, and offer Thee my words like tears: I have sinned as the harlot did not sin, and I have transgressed as no other on earth; but show compassion, O Master, unto Thy creation, and call me back!

I have buried Thine image and corrupted Thy commandment; all my beauty is darkened, and by passions the lamp is quenched, O Savior; but showing compassion, restore unto me, as David sings, joy![^1096]

Turn back, repent, uncover hidden things, say unto God Who knows all things: "Thou knowest my secret things, O only Savior; but do Thou Thyself have mercy upon me, as David sings, according to Thy mercy!"

My days have vanished like the dream of one who awakens; therefore like Hezekiah I weep upon my bed that years of life may be added unto me; but what Isaiah shall stand before thee, O soul, if not the God of all?

*To St. Mary:*  
Crying aloud unto the all-pure Mother of God, thou didst first cast away the fury of passions that violently afflicted thee, and didst confound the enemy who tripped thee up; but grant help now from affliction unto me also, thy servant!

*To St. Mary:*  
Whom thou didst love, Whom thou didst desire, for Whose sake thou didst wear out thy flesh, O venerable one: pray now unto Christ for thy servants, that being merciful unto us all, He may grant a peaceful estate unto those who honor Him.

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 7 (continued):*  
*To St. Andrew:*  
Establish me upon the rock of faith by thy prayers, O father, enclosing me with divine fear; and grant me repentance, O Andrew, I pray thee: deliver me from the snare of the enemies who seek after me!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O simple, undivided Trinity, consubstantial and of one nature, Lights and Light, and three Holies, yet one Holy is God the Trinity hymned; sing then, O soul, and glorify Life and Lives, the God of all!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
We hymn thee, we bless thee, we worship thee, O Birth-giver of God, for of the inseparable Trinity thou hast brought forth the one Christ God, and hast opened the heavenly realms unto us who are on earth.

*Triodion. Tone 8. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1097]  
The beginningless King of glory, before Whom the heavenly hosts tremble: praise Him, O priests, and highly exalt Him, O people, unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
As coals of immaterial fire, burn up my material passions, O apostles, kindling in me now the desire of divine love!

Let us honor the sweet-voiced trumpets of the Word, whereby the infirm walls of the enemy fell down, and the ramparts of the knowledge of God were established!

Shatter the idols of passion in my soul, O consecrated temples, apostles of the Lord, who shattered the temples and pillars of the enemy!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst contain Him Who is uncontainable by nature; thou didst bear Him Who bears all things; thou didst nurse, O pure Virgin, Him Who feeds creation: Christ the Giver of life.

*Another Triodion. Heirmos the same:*[^1098]  
Having built the whole Church through the master-craftsmanship of the Spirit, O apostles of Christ, bless Christ within her unto the ages!

Having sounded the trumpet of teachings, the apostles cast down all idolatrous deception, highly exalting Christ unto all ages!

O apostles, noble colony, overseers of the world, and heavenly citizens: deliver from tribulations those who continually praise you!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 8 (continued):*  
*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O thrice-radiant, all-bright Godhead, nature of one glory and one throne: O Father Almighty, Son, and Divine Spirit, I hymn Thee unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
As an honorable and most exalted throne let us hymn the Mother of God unceasingly, O people, who alone after childbearing remains Mother and Virgin.

*Great Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1099]  
Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and before Whom the Cherubim and Seraphim tremble, let every breath and creature praise, bless, and highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Have mercy upon me who have sinned, O Savior; raise up my mind unto conversion; receive me who repent; show compassion unto me who cry: "I have sinned against Thee, save me! I have transgressed, have mercy upon me!"

The charioteer Elijah, having ascended into the heavens as upon a chariot of virtues, was once borne high above earthly things; meditate then, O my soul, upon his ascent![^1100]

The stream of the Jordan of old, by Elijah's mantle, stood still on this side and on that through Elisha; but thou, O my soul, hast not partaken of this grace because of intemperance.

Elisha of old, receiving the mantle of Elijah, received a double portion of grace from the Lord; but thou, O my soul, hast not partaken of this grace because of intemperance.

The Shunammite woman of old entertained the righteous man, O soul, with a noble disposition; but thou hast not brought into thy house stranger or traveler; therefore thou shalt be cast out weeping from the bridal chamber!

Thou, wretched soul, hast always imitated the foul mind of Gehazi; cast away his love of money, at least in thine old age; flee the fire of Gehenna, departing from thine evil deeds!

Having rivaled Uzziah, O soul, thou hast acquired his leprosy doubly in thyself; for thou thinkest unfitting things and doest unlawful things: leave what thou hast, and run unto repentance!

Thou hast heard, O soul, of the Ninevites who repented before God in sackcloth and ashes; thou hast not imitated them, but hast appeared more wicked than all who have sinned before the Law and after the Law!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 8 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
In the pit of mire thou hast heard Jeremiah, O soul, crying with lamentations for the city of Zion and seeking tears; imitate his lamenting life, and thou shalt be saved![^1101]

Jonah fled unto Tarshish, foreseeing the conversion of the Ninevites; for as a prophet he understood the lovingkindness of God, therefore he was zealous that his prophecy should not prove false.

Thou hast heard how Daniel in the lions' den stopped the mouths of the beasts, O soul; thou knowest how the young men with Azariah quenched the burning flame of the furnace through faith.

I have brought before thee all of the Old Testament, O soul, as an example; imitate the God-loving deeds of the righteous, and again flee the sins of the wicked!

O righteous Judge and Savior, have mercy and deliver me from the fire and the threatening which I must justly endure at the judgment; grant me remission before the end through virtue and repentance!

Like the thief I cry unto Thee: "Remember me!"[^1102] Like Peter I weep bitterly: "Pardon me, O Savior!" I call like the publican, I shed tears like the harlot: receive my weeping, as once that of the Canaanite woman!

Heal the ulcer of my humble soul, O Savior, the only Physician; apply a plaster unto me, and oil and wine: the deeds of repentance, and compunction with tears!

Imitating the Canaanite woman, I also cry: "Have mercy on me, O Son of David!" I touch the border of Thy garment like the woman with the issue of blood; I weep like Martha and Mary over Lazarus.

Pouring out an alabaster jar of tears like myrrh upon Thy head, O Savior, I call unto Thee like the harlot seeking mercy; I bring supplication, and ask to receive forgiveness.

Though none has sinned against Thee as I have, yet receive even me, O tenderhearted Savior, repenting in fear and calling in love: "I have sinned against Thee only; have mercy on me, O Merciful One!"

Spare Thy creation, O Savior, and seek out what is lost like a shepherd; go before him who has gone astray, snatch him from the wolf, and make me a sheep in the pasture of Thy flock!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 8 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
When as Judge Thou shalt sit, as Thou art tenderhearted, and shalt display Thy dread glory, O Savior: oh, what fear will there be then, while the furnace burns, for all who fear Thine unbearable judgment-seat![^1103]

*To St. Mary:*  
The Mother of the Unwaning Light, having enlightened thee, loosed thee from the darkening of passions; having therefore entered into spiritual grace, enlighten, O Mary, those who faithfully praise thee!

*To St. Mary:*  
Seeing a new wonder, the godly Zosimas was truly astonished in thee, O mother; for he beheld an angel in the flesh and was filled with all wonder, singing unto Christ unto the ages!

*To St. Andrew:*  
As one who hast boldness before the Lord, O Andrew of Crete, venerable boast: pray, I beseech thee, that I may now find through thy prayers release from the bonds of iniquity, for thou art a teacher of repentance and the glory of the venerable!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
O Beginningless Father, co-beginningless Son, O good Comforter, upright Spirit: Begetter of God the Word, Word of the Beginningless Father, Living and Creating Spirit: O Trinity, Unity, have mercy upon me!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
As from the dipping of purple dye, O All-Pure One, the spiritual purple robe of Emmanuel, His flesh, was woven within thy womb; therefore we honor thee as truly the Mother of God!

*We praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing and highly exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*And again the Heirmos.*

*Triodion. Tone 8. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1104]  
Thee we confess to be truly the Mother of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee.

*Troparia:*  
Having appeared as springs of saving water, O apostles: bedew my soul, withered by sinful thirst!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 9 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Swimming in the abyss of destruction, and already submerged, save me, O Lord, with Thy right hand, as Thou didst Peter!

As salt of delicious teachings, dry up the rottenness of my mind, and drive away the darkness of ignorance!

*Theotokion:*  
As thou hast given birth unto Joy, grant me weeping, whereby I may be able to find divine consolation, O Lady, on the coming day!

*Another Triodion. Heirmos: Thee, mediatress of heaven and earth:*[^1105]  
Thee, glorious apostolic assembly, we magnify with hymns; for ye have appeared unto the universe as bright luminaries, driving away deception!

Having caught rational fish with your net of the Gospel, ye ever offer these as food unto Christ, O blessed apostles!

Remember us before God by your supplication, O apostles, that we may be delivered from every temptation, we pray, who hymn you with love!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
Thee, three-hypostatic Unity—Father, Son, with the Spirit—one consubstantial God, I sing: a Trinity equal in power and beginningless!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thee, who didst bear a child and remain a virgin, all generations call blessed, for through thee we have been delivered from the curse; for thou hast given birth unto the Lord, our Joy!

*Great Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1106]  
The childbearing of a seedless conception is ineffable, the incorrupt fruit of a husbandless Mother; for the birth of God renovates natures; therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as the Bride of God and Mother.

*Troparia:*  
My mind is covered with sores, my body is diseased, my spirit is sick, speech has grown feeble, life is deadened, the end is at the doors; what therefore shalt thou do, O my wretched soul, when the Judge shall come to search out what is thine?[^1107]

I have brought before thee, O soul, Moses' account of the creation of the world, and therefrom all the testamentary Scripture, which tells thee of the righteous and the unrighteous; of whom thou hast imitated the second, O soul, and not the first, having sinned against God!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 9 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
The Law has grown weak, the Gospel is idle, and all the Scripture has been neglected in thee; the prophets have grown feeble, and every word of the righteous; thy wounds, O soul, have multiplied, there being no physician to heal thee.[^1108]

I bring before thee examples from the New Scripture to lead thee, O soul, unto compunction; emulate therefore the righteous, turn away from sinners, and propitiate Christ by prayers and fastings, and by purity and reverence.

Christ became man, calling robbers and harlots to repentance; repent, O soul; the door of the Kingdom is already opened, and Pharisees, publicans, and repenting adulterers seize it beforehand!

Christ became man, partaking of my flesh, and by His will He fulfilled all things that belong to nature, except sin, showing forth unto thee, O soul, an example and image of His condescension.

Christ saved the Magi, summoned the shepherds, showed the multitudes of infants as martyrs, glorified elders and aged widows; whom thou hast not emulated, O soul, neither in deed nor in life; but woe unto thee when thou shalt be judged!

Having fasted forty days in the wilderness, the Lord afterward hungered, showing His human nature; be not idle, O soul, if the enemy assault thee; by prayer and fasting let him be repulsed from thy feet!

Christ was tempted, the devil tempted Him, showing stones that they might become bread; he led Him up into a mountain to see all the kingdoms of the world in a moment; fear the snare, O soul; be sober, pray at every hour unto God!

The wilderness-loving turtle-dove, the voice of one crying, Christ's lamp cried aloud, preaching repentance, while Herod transgressed the law with Herodias; see, O my soul, that thou be not entangled in the snares of lawlessness, but embrace repentance!

The Forerunner of grace settled in the wilderness, and all Judea and Samaria, having heard, ran and confessed their sins, being eagerly baptized; whom thou, O soul, hast not imitated!

Marriage is honorable and the bed undefiled, for Christ blessed both beforehand, eating in the flesh, and at the wedding in Cana turning water into wine and displaying His first miracle, that thou mightest be transformed, O soul!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 9 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Christ strengthened the paralytic, who took up his bed, and raised the dead youth, the widow's child, and the centurion's servant, and appeared unto the Samaritan woman, prefiguring for thee, O soul, worship in spirit.[^1109]

By the touching of the hem of His garment the Lord healed the woman with an issue of blood, cleansed lepers, and having enlightened the blind and straightened the lame, healed with a word the deaf, the dumb, and the bowed down to the earth, that thou mightest be saved, O wretched soul!

Healing infirmities, Christ the Word evangelized the poor, cured the afflicted, ate with publicans, conversed with sinners, and restored the departed soul of Jairus' daughter by the touch of His hand.

The publican was saved, and the harlot was made chaste, while the boasting Pharisee was condemned; for the first said: "Cleanse me"; and the second: "Have mercy upon me"; but the third was magnifying himself, crying: "O God, I thank Thee," and other foolish words.

Zacchaeus was a publican, yet he was saved; and Simon the Pharisee was scandalized, while the harlot received forgiving release from Him Who has the power to forgive sins; strive to imitate her, O soul!

Unto the harlot, O my wretched soul, thou hast not been zealous, who, taking the alabaster jar of myrrh, with tears anointed the Savior's feet, and wiped them with her hair, as He tore up the handwriting of her ancient sins!

Thou hast learned, O my soul, how the cities unto which Christ gave the Gospel were cursed; fear their example, lest thou become like them, whom the Master, having likened unto the Sodomites, condemned even unto Hades!

Mayest thou not appear worse through despair, O my soul, having heard of the faith of the Canaanite woman, whose daughter was healed by the word of God; cry aloud from the depth of thy heart, as she did unto Christ: "O Son of David, save me also!"

Have compassion, save me; O Son of David, have mercy, Thou Who didst heal by a word those possessed by demons; and speak unto me as unto the robber that compassionate voice: "Amen, I say unto thee, thou shalt be with Me in Paradise when I come in My glory!"

A robber reviled Thee, a robber confessed Thee as God, for both were hanging upon the cross; but, O Compassionate One, as unto Thy faithful robber who recognized Thee as God, open also unto me the door of Thy glorious Kingdom!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 9 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Creation trembled seeing Thee crucified, mountains and rocks split from fear, and the earth shook, and Hades was stripped bare, and the light darkened at midday, beholding Thee, O Jesus, nailed to the cross!

Exact not from me worthy fruits of repentance, for my strength has failed within me; grant me ever a contrite heart and spiritual poverty, that I may offer these unto Thee as an acceptable sacrifice, O only Savior!

My Judge and my Knower, Who art to come again with the angels to judge the whole world, beholding me then with Thy merciful eye, spare me, and have compassion upon me, O Jesus, who have sinned more than all human nature!

*To St. Mary:*  
Thou didst astonish all by thy marvelous life, the ranks of angels and the assemblies of men, having lived immaterially and transcended nature, whereby with seemingly bodiless feet stepping, O Mary, thou didst cross the Jordan!

*To St. Mary:*  
Entreat the Creator on behalf of those who praise thee, venerable Mother, that we may be delivered from mistreatments and afflictions attacking us on every side, that having been delivered from tribulations, we may unceasingly magnify the Lord Who has glorified thee, O saint!

*To St. Andrew:*  
O honorable Andrew, thrice-blessed father, shepherd of Crete, cease not praying for those who hymn thee, that we all may be delivered from wrath, affliction, corruption, and immeasurable transgressions, who honor thy memory with faith!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
Consubstantial Trinity, three-hypostatic Unity, Thee we hymn, glorifying the Father, magnifying the Son, and worshipping the Spirit: one nature truly God, Life, and living endless Kingdom!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Preserve thy city, O most pure Mother of God; for reigning faithfully in thee, it is also established in thee, and conquering through thee, it overcomes every temptation, captures warriors, and accomplishes obedience!

*And immediately the Heirmos. Exapostilarion, as usual.*

*At the Aposticha, automelon, twice. Tone 8:*[^1110]  
Having fallen into robber-infested paths, O my soul, thou hast been sorely wounded by thine own sins, having surrendered thyself unto mindless enemies; but as...

*At Matins (continued):*

*At the Aposticha (continued):*  
...thou hast time, cry aloud in compunction: O hope of the hopeless, life of the despairing, Savior, raise me up and save me!

*Martyricon:*  
Having clad yourselves well in the breastplates of faith, and armed yourselves with the sign of the Cross, ye appeared as valiant soldiers; ye manfully resisted tormentors, and destroyed the devil's deception; having become victors, ye were counted worthy of crowns: pray unto Christ for us, that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Receive the voices of thy servants, O all-pure Virgin Mother of God, and unceasingly pray that forgiveness of sins and peace may be granted unto us.

*We combine also the First Hour without stichologia.*  
*After the Trisagion, at all the Hours, the Kontakion:* My soul, my soul...  
*At the Third Hour, Kathisma 14; at the Sixth, 19.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*  
Compassionate, long-suffering, Almighty Lord, send down Thy mercy upon Thy people!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 96, Tone 6:*[^1111]  
The Lord is King, let the earth rejoice!  
*Verse: Let many islands be glad.*

*Reading of the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 42:5–16):*[^1112]  
Thus says the Lord God, Who made heaven and fixed it fast, Who established the earth and what is upon it, and gives breath to the people upon it, and spirit to them that walk upon it:  
[6] I the Lord God have called Thee in righteousness, and will hold Thy hand, and will strengthen Thee; and I have given Thee for a covenant of the race of Israel, for a light of the Gentiles:  
[7] To open the eyes of the blind, and to lead out from bonds those that are bound, and from the prison-house those that sit in darkness. I am the Lord God: this is My name; My glory will I not give to another, nor My praises to graven images.  
[8] The things from the beginning, behold, have come to pass, and new things do I declare; and before they are proclaimed,  
[9] they are manifested unto you. Sing unto the Lord a new song: His dominion is from the end of the earth; glorify His name from the ends  
[10] of the earth, ye that go down to the sea, and sail upon it, the islands and they that dwell therein!  
[11] Be glad, O wilderness, and its villages, the courts, and they that dwell in Kedar; they that dwell upon  
[12] the rock shall rejoice, from the top of the mountains they shall cry aloud! They shall give glory unto God, and declare His praise in the islands.  
[13] The Lord God of hosts shall go forth, and shall shatter battle; He shall stir up zeal, and shall shout  
[14] against His enemies with might: I have kept silence; shall I also forever hold My peace, and endure? I have endured like a woman in travail; I will destroy and dry up together;  
[15] I will lay waste mountains and hills, and dry up all their grass; and I will make the rivers into islands, and dry up the pools;  
[16] And I will bring the blind by a way that they knew not, and in paths that they have not known will I make them walk; I will make darkness into light for them, and crooked things into straight paths: these words will I do, and will not forsake them.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 97, Tone 6:*[^1113]  
Sing unto the Lord a new song, for marvelous things has the Lord done, His right hand has saved Him!  
*Verse: All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God.*

*At the Ninth Hour, Kathisma 11.*

## On Thursday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers[^1114]

*After the Prooemiac Psalm, instead of "Unto the Lord when I was afflicted," Kathisma 12. The lamplighting semantron strikes softly on account of the labor of the vigil.*[^1115]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses. And we sing the automelon, twice. Tone 8:*  
Of mine own free will I stripped myself of the beauty of virtues through my first transgression, yet I was immediately clothed therein by Thy condescension toward me, O Word of God; for Thou didst not despise me when I had fallen into grievous passions and had been trampled in robber-fashion upon the path; but having protected me by Thine all-powerful might, Thou hast counted me worthy of protection, O Plenteous in mercy!

*Martyricon:*  
O martyrs of the Lord, ye sanctify every place and cure every infirmity; and now pray that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy, we pray you!

*Other Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃енницы):*[^1116]  
Nailed, O Lord, upon the Cross, Thou didst tear up Adam's handwriting with the divine spear; therefore tear asunder my bonds, O Word, that in faith I may sacrifice unto Thee a sacrifice of praise, rejoicing that I have now found the acceptable time of fasting, which Thou hast shown forth unto all for salvation!

Moses of old, shining with the brightness of fasting, beheld the glory of God; emulating him, O my humble soul, by deeds, self-mastery, and prayer, be pleasing unto Him Who through goodness stretched out His palms upon the Cross for thy sake, that thou mayest attain divine delight!

*Another Sticheron, Tone 6, Podoben: "Archangelically let us hymn" (А҆рха́гг҃льски воспои́мъ):*[^1117]  
Worshipping Thy life-giving Cross of Thine ineffable and immeasurable goodness toward us, O Christ, and enlightened in our souls thereby, we unceasingly hymn Thee, asking with good courage to complete with joy the course of the fast, and to attain to singing of Thy Passions, O Lord, whereby Thou hast saved us!

*And in the Menaion, 4.*  
*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion.*  
*Entrance: O Gladsome Light.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 98, Tone 7:*[^1118]  
Exalt the Lord our God, and worship the footstool of His feet, for He is holy!  
*Verse: The Lord is King, let the peoples rage.*

*At Vespers (continued):*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 18:20–33):*[^1119]  
The Lord said: The cry of Sodom and Gomorrah is multiplied unto Me, and their sins are exceedingly great.  
[21] Having gone down, therefore, I will see whether their actions accord with their cry which comes unto Me; and if not,  
[22] that I may know. And the men, having turned away from thence, came unto Sodom; but Abraham was still standing  
[23] before the Lord. And Abraham, drawing near, said: Wilt Thou destroy the righteous with the ungodly,  
[24] and shall the righteous be as the ungodly? If there be fifty righteous in the city, wilt Thou destroy them?  
[25] Wilt Thou not spare the whole place for the sake of the fifty righteous, if they be in it? In no wise shalt Thou do according to this word, to slay the righteous with the ungodly, so that the righteous should be as the ungodly: in no wise!  
[26] Shalt Thou not, Who judgest all the earth, execute judgment? And the Lord said: If there be in Sodom fifty righteous within the city, I will forgive the whole city and the whole place for their sake. And Abraham, answering,  
[27] said: Now have I begun to speak unto my Lord, who am but earth and ashes:  
[28] What if the fifty righteous should lack five? Wilt Thou destroy the whole city for the sake of the forty-five? And He said: I will not destroy it if I find forty-five there.  
[29] And he continued yet to speak unto Him, and said: What if forty should be found there? And He said:  
[30] I will not destroy it for the sake of the forty. And he said: What, O Lord, if I speak: what if thirty should be found there?  
[31] And He said: I will not destroy it for the sake of the thirty. And he said: Since I have taken upon me to speak unto the Lord: what if twenty should be found there? And He said: I will not destroy it if twenty should be found there.  
[32] And he said: What, O Lord, if I speak yet once more: what if ten should be found there? And He said:  
[33] I will not destroy it for the sake of the ten. And the Lord departed, as He ceased speaking unto Abraham; and Abraham returned unto his place.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 99, Tone 6:*  
Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth!  
*Verse: Serve the Lord in gladness.*

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 16:17–33):*  
[17] He that receives instruction shall be among the good, and he that regards reproof shall be made wise. He that guards his ways preserves his own soul; and he that loves his life spares  
[18] his mouth. Insolence goes before destruction, and an evil mind before  
[19] a fall. Better is a mild-spirited man with humility than one who divides spoil with  
[20] the insolent. A man prudent in affairs is a finder of good things; and he that trusts in the Lord is blessed.  
[21] Men call the wise and understanding evil, but those sweet in speech shall be heard more.  
[22] Understanding is a wellspring of life unto them that possess it, but the instruction of fools is evil.  
[23] The heart of the wise understands what proceeds from his mouth, and upon his lips he bears understanding.  
[24] Good words are honeycombs, and the sweetness thereof is the healing of the soul. There are ways that seem  
[25] right unto a man, yet the end thereof looks toward the bottom of Hades. A man in labor labors  
[26] for himself, and forces his own destruction: the perverse man bears destruction in his mouth.  
[27] A foolish man digs evil for himself, and upon his lips he treasures up fire.  
[28] A perverse man scatters evil, and kindles a torch of deceit for the wicked, and separates  
[29] friends. A lawless man entices friends, and leads them away into paths that are not good.  
[30] He that fixes his eyes devises perverse things, and biting his lips he determines  
[31] all evils: this man is a furnace of malice. Old age is a crown of praise, and it is found  
[32] in the ways of righteousness. Better is a long-suffering man than a strong one, [and a man having understanding than a great husbandman;

*At Vespers (continued):*

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 16:32–17:17, continued):*  
and he that holds back anger is better than one that takes a city.]  
[33] All things come into the bosom of the unrighteous, but from the Lord are all righteous things.

*[Chapter 17]*  
[1] Better is a morsel of bread with pleasure in peace than a house full of many good things and unjust sacrifices with strife.  
[2] A prudent servant shall rule over foolish masters, and shall divide the inheritance among brethren.  
[3] As silver and gold are tried in the furnace, so are chosen hearts with the Lord.  
[4] The evil man listens to the tongue of transgressors, but the righteous man heeds not lying lips.  
[5] He that mocks the poor provokes Him that made him, and he that rejoices over one perishing shall not be held guiltless; but he that shows mercy shall obtain mercy.  
[6] The crown of old men is children's children, and the boast of children is their fathers. To the faithful man the whole world is riches, but to the faithless not even a penny.  
[7] Faithful lips are not fitting for a fool, nor are lying lips for a righteous man.  
[8] Instruction is a reward of graces unto them that use it; and whithersoever it turns, it prospers.  
[9] He that conceals wrongs seeks love; but he that hates to hide them separates friends and kinsmen.  
[10] A threat crushes the heart of the wise, but a fool though beaten feels not the strokes.  
[11] Every evil man stirs up contention, but the Lord shall send a merciless angel against him.  
[12] Care falls upon a man of understanding, but fools devise evils.  
[13] Whoso rewards evil for good, evil shall not depart from his house.  
[14] Authority gives words the beginning of righteousness, but strife and contention lead the way to want.  
[15] He that judges the righteous unrighteous, and the unrighteous righteous, is unclean and an abomination with the Lord.  
[16] To what purpose is wealth to a fool? For a senseless man cannot acquire wisdom. He that builds his house high seeks destruction, and he that refuses to learn shall fall into evils.  
[17] Let a friend love thee at all times, and let brethren be profitable in adversities.

*And the rest of the Order of the Presanctified, wherein we partake also of the holy Mysteries of Christ.*

*At the table we eat oil and drink wine on account of the labor of the vigil. But in the Studite Typikon it commands to eat dry food the whole of that week, more than others, for they are days of mourning and not of feast; for on these days indeed it is also appointed to sing "Alleluia." But in the Typikon of the Holy Mountain it directs on the holy Forty Martyrs, on the Finding of the Forerunner's Head, on Wednesday of the Cross, on the Great Canon, and on the Akathist to eat two cooked foods with oil, and to drink two cups of wine. These things have we noted for your charity from both, that he who desires to be saved may choose what is most well-pleasing before God.*[^1120]

*Note: It is necessary to know that if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos happens to fall on this day, the order of the Great Canon is chanted beforehand, from Monday to Tuesday, that is, on Monday evening; and the Feast remains immutable.*[^1121]

## On Friday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins[^1122]

*The Triadica of the Tone.*  
*Then Kathismata 19, 14, 15.*  
*After the First Stichologia we say two Stavrosima sessional hymns of the Octoechos, and with the Theotokion.*  
*After the Second Stichologia we say the present Sessional Hymn.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^1123]  
Thou didst stretch out Thy palms upon the tree, O Christ, healing Adam's wound by Thy wounds; therefore I pray Thee: heal my wounds which the deceiver has inflicted upon my soul, and count me worthy in prayer and fasting, O Savior, to be well-pleasing unto Thee!  
*Twice.*

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben:*  
Seeing Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-blameless Mother cried aloud: O My Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what ineffable dispensation of Thine is this, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Merciful One?

*After the Third Stichologia, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "O hope of the world" (Оу҆пова́нїе мі́ра):*[^1124]  
O Cross, guardian of the world, driver-away of demons, invincible protection unto all who have acquired thee: grant us with a pure conscience to traverse the remainder of the fast, making our souls right before Christ, O blessed Wood!  
*Twice.*

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben:*  
O pure and honorable Virgin, glory of angels, when thou didst stand by the Cross of thy Son and God, unable to bear the sight of the enemies' mockings, thou didst cry aloud, lamenting with a mother's grief: How dost Thou endure the threats of all, O Lover of mankind? Glory to Thy long-suffering!

*Triodion by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the canticle is recited.*  
*Ode 5, Tone 1. Heirmos: Thou Who hast enlightened with the radiance of Thy coming:*[^1125]  
*Troparia:*  
Having stretched out heaven like a curtain, Thou didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Cross, O most good Jesus; therefore I pray Thee: have compassion upon me who am stretched out by the enemy's temptations!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 5 (continued):*  
*Troparia:*  
Thou didst fall asleep upon the Cross, O most good Jesus, granting vigilance of salvation unto us who lie in lowest destruction, O Lord; therefore in faith we glorify Thee!

Count Thy servants worthy, enlightened with the brightness of heart, to see the day of Thy Passions and Thy life-bearing Resurrection, O Savior, as we sing the power of Thy Kingdom!

*Theotokion:*  
Seeing Thee lifted up upon the Cross, the All-Pure One cried aloud, wounded in her maternal heart, and said: Out of the loving-kindness of Thy compassions, O Lord, Thou hast endured suffering, granting dispassion unto all!

*Another Triodion, by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Heirmos: From the night rising early, O Lover of mankind:*[^1126]  
*Troparia:*  
Upon the Cross Thou didst spread out Thy hands, O Lover of mankind, and the Judeans pierced Thee with nails and pierced Thy side with a spear, and Thou endurest all things, O Christ, that we might be saved!

By the eating of the tree Adam died of old, but through the tree of the Cross he found life again, whereby he enjoys the food of the compassions within Paradise!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
One in nature I hymn Thee, O Trinity: unoriginate, uncreated, sovereign, royal, most perfect Unity God, Light and Life, Creator of the world!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
In thy supernatural childbirth, O Pure One, the laws of nature are manifestly dissolved through thee; for without seed thou givest birth unto the pre-eternal God, Who was begotten of the Father.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee:*  
Thy Cross, the spear, and Thy nails we venerate, O Master; for by them Thou hast delivered us from corruption, who have been made immortal by Thy Passion, O Christ!

*Heirmos:*  
Rising early from the night, O Lover of mankind, enlighten me, I pray, and guide me also in Thy commandments, and teach me, O Savior, to do Thy will!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: The marvel beyond nature:*[^1127]  
*Troparia:*  
Thou didst blunt the stings of the evil one, O Savior, nailed with nails upon the tree; Thou wast clothed with a crown of thorns of reproach, uprooting the thorns of transgression; therefore, hymning Thee, we cry: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Cross, O Christ, gathering mankind unto knowledge of Thee; and Thou didst endure having Thy side pierced with a spear, pouring forth for us a fountain of salvation, as we sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

With streams of Thy compassion, O Christ, Thou Merciful One, cleanse my heart, which has been defiled by the biting of sin, and count me worthy, O Christ Jesus, to pour forth rivers of compunction, that I may cry unto Thee: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*  
We have recognized thee, O Maiden, as the divine jar holding the manna of the Godhead, the ark, the table, the lampstand, the throne of God, the palace, and the bridge leading unto divine life for those who sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Another Triodion, Ode 8, by Lord Theodore. Heirmos: The song of the youths:*[^1128]  
*Troparia:*  
Christ is crucified, and I am brought to life, singing with the Children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

The world delivered from corruption by Thy crucifixion sings with the Children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
Equally-glorified Trinity, transcendent Unity—Father, Son, and Spirit—save those who sing of Thee in faith!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Bowing before the childbearing of the Pure Mother of God, I sing with the Children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee:*  
I hymn Thy Cross, whereby Thou hast saved me, O Christ, singing with the Children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Heirmos:*  
Let us sing the song of the youths unto Christ, singing with them: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: The type of the pure childbearing:*[^1129]  
*Troparia:*  
Elijah saw the Lord in a subtle breeze, having first attenuated his flesh by prayers and fasting; emulating him, O my soul, cast away the grossness of pleasures, that thou mayest behold the Desired One!

Moses of old, having lifted up the serpent upon the tree, O Savior, prefigured Thy lifting up upon the Cross, O Most Good One, whereby Thou hast delivered from the venomous malice of the serpent all the nations that worship Thee!

Lying in the tomb of despondency, having the weighing-down stone of perplexity, not understanding Thy ever-living word, O Savior, and feeling not Thy fear: have compassion upon me and save me by Thy goodness, O Plenteous in mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
O Lady higher than all, show me higher than the malice of passions, who glorify thee as truly the Mother of God, and sing of thy childbearing, O Pure One, which is honored in incomprehensibility, O woman full of divine joy!

*Another Triodion, Ode 9, by Lord Theodore. Heirmos: The childbearing of a seedless conception is ineffable:*[^1130]  
*Troparia:*  
Thou art crucified, and Thou savest me; Thou diest, and Thou givest me life. O Thy compassion, O Thy love for mankind! Who has seen, who has heard of a Master accepting a reproachful death for a servant? But glory to Thine ineffable goodness, O Lord!

*At Matins (continued):*

*Ode 9 (continued):*  
*Troparion:*  
When the sun beheld Thee crucified, it set; for how could it shine, beholding the reproach of its Creator? For all creation also, bowing down, cried out in silence to the crucifiers: Truly Thou art the God of all, though Thou sufferest in the flesh.

*Glory:* *Trinitarian:*  
O Father of the Only-begotten Son, Thou Sole Begetter of the One; and O Sole Light of the One, Radiance of the Light; and Thou One, Holy Spirit of the One God, truly being the Lord of the Lord: O Holy Trinity, Unity, save me who theologize Thee.

*Both now:* *Theotokion:*  
The wonder of thy childbearing amazes me, O All-immaculate one: how dost thou conceive without seed Him Whom naught can contain? Tell me, how art thou a virgin, having given birth as a Mother? Having received with faith that which is beyond nature, bow down before the One Who was born; for whatsoever He wills, He is also able to do.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
For our sake, each of Thy members underwent suffering: Thy head, blows; Thy cheeks, buffeting; Thy hands, nailing; and Thy ribs, the spear; and above all, the Cross; but glory to Thine ineffable compassion, O our Savior!

*Heirmos:*  
Ineffable is the Childbearing of a seedless conception, the incorrupt fruit of a husbandless Mother; for the birth of God renews nature; therefore, all generations, as the Bride of God and Mother, we orthodoxly magnify thee.

*Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, twice, Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, Who wast nailed to the Cross with robbers, and by Thy wound didst heal human nature, despise me not who have fallen into the hands of spiritual waylayers and bodiless robbers, and have been stripped by them of the robe of grace-given adoption, and lie with wounds as one lifeless; and the priest having come, seeing the wound, paid no heed; and the Levite also, abhorring it, passed by; but Thou, O Lord, Who wast ineffably incarnate of the Virgin, having poured forth from Thy saving side, which abundantly poured out blood and water, like oil dripping down, O Christ God, bind up the sores of my wounds with softness, and unite me unto the heavenly choir, as Thou art compassionate.[^1131]

*Martyrion:*  
What shall we call you, O saints? Cherubim? For Christ rested upon you. Seraphim? For unceasingly you glorified Him. Angels? For you turned away from the body. Powers? For you work miracles. Many are your names, and greater are your gifts; pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, both now:* *Staurotheotokion, in the same tone:*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*  
O marvelous wonder! O new mystery! O dreadful undertaking! The Virgin spoke, when she beheld Thee suspended upon the Cross between two robbers, Whom without pain she had wonderfully brought forth, weeping and saying: Woe is me, most beloved Child! How has a fierce and ungrateful people nailed Thee to the Cross?

*At the First Hour, there is no Kathisma. At the Third Hour, Kathisma 14; at the Sixth Hour, Kathisma 15.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of Prophecy, Tone 8:*  
O Thou Who art sung by all, O Christ our God, Who didst shake the earth that Thou mightest turn and save those dwelling upon it; and Who again dost establish it, on account of Thy goodness and ineffable compassion: through the prayers of the Theotokos, have mercy on us.[^1132]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 100, Tone 4:*  
Of mercy and judgment will I sing unto Thee, O Lord.  
*Verse:* I will sing, and I will understand in a blameless way.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 45:11–17):*  
Thus says the Lord God, the Holy One of Israel, Who made the things that are to come: Ask Me concerning My sons and concerning My daughters, and command Me concerning the works of My hands. [12] I made the earth and man upon it; I by My hand established heaven; I commanded all the stars. [13] I have raised him up with righteousness as king, and all his ways are right. He shall build My city, and he shall turn back the captivity of My people, not for price nor for reward, says the Lord of Sabaoth. [14] Thus says the Lord of Sabaoth: Egypt has labored, and the merchandise of the Ethiopians, and the Sabaeans, men of tall stature, shall come over unto thee, and they shall be thy servants; and they shall follow after thee, bound with hand-chains, and they shall pass over unto thee, and they shall bow down unto thee, and in thee shall they pray, for God is in thee, and they shall say: There is no God besides thee. [15] For Thou art God, and we knew it not, the God of Israel, the Savior. [16] All that oppose Him shall be ashamed and confounded, and they shall walk in shame; be renewed unto Me, O islands. [17] Israel is saved by the Lord with an everlasting salvation; they shall not be ashamed, neither shall they be confounded unto the age anymore, says the Lord.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 101, Tone 4:*  
O Lord, give ear unto my prayer, and let my cry come unto Thee.  
*Verse:* Turn not Thy face away from me.

*At the Ninth Hour, there is no Kathisma.*

## On Friday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*We chant Kathisma 18.*

*And after the customary Psalter reading, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the Idiomelon:*

*Tone 6, twice:*  
As I went forth from the Jerusalem of Thy divine commandments, and arrived at the passions of Jericho, drawn by the glory of dishonor of worldly cares, I fell among robber-like thoughts, and was stripped by them of the robe of grace-given adoption, and lie with wounds as one lifeless; and the priest having come, seeing the wound, paid no heed; and the Levite also, abhorring it, passed by; but Thou, O Lord, Who wast ineffably incarnate of the Virgin, having poured forth from Thy saving side, which abundantly poured out blood and water, like oil dripping down, O Christ God, bind up the sores of my wounds with softness, and unite me unto the heavenly choir, as Thou art compassionate.[^1133]

*Martyrion:*  
Thy martyrs, O Lord, did not deny Thee, nor did they depart from Thy commandments; through their prayers, have mercy on us.

*Then we sing the Prosomoia Stichera of the Most Holy Theotokos, 3, repeating them to make 6:*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*  
Revealing the pre-eternal Counsel unto thee, O Maiden, Gabriel stood before thee, greeting thee and proclaiming: Rejoice, untilled earth; rejoice, unburnt bush; rejoice, unfathomable depth; rejoice, bridge leading unto the heavens, and lofty ladder which Jacob beheld; rejoice, divine jar of manna; rejoice, dissolution of the curse; rejoice, restoration of Adam: the Lord is with thee.

Thou appearest unto me as a man, says the undefiled Maiden unto the Archistrategos, and how dost thou proclaim words surpassing man? For thou saidst that God shall be with me, and shall dwell in my womb; and how shall I be, tell me, a spacious domain and place of sanctification for Him Who surpasses the Cherubim? Deceive me not with guile, for I have not known pleasure, I am a stranger to marriage; how then shall I bring forth a Child?

Where God wills, the order of nature is overcome, says the bodiless one, and things surpassing man are accomplished; believe my true words, O All-holy, All-immaculate one! And she cried out: Be it done unto me now according to thy word, and I shall give birth unto the Bodiless One, Who borrows flesh from me, that He may lead man up, as He alone is mighty, unto his first dignity, through union.

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*  
The mystery hidden from the ages is revealed today, and the Son of God becomes the Son of Man, that taking the worse, He may impart unto me the better. Adam of old was deceived, and desiring to become God, he became not so; God becomes man, that He may make Adam god. Let creation rejoice, let nature dance, for the Archangel stands with fear before the Virgin, and brings the "Rejoice," the counter-weight to sorrow. O our God, Who in Thy tender mercy wast made man, glory to Thee![^1134]

*If the Office of the Presanctified is not celebrated, we sing the Stichera of the Theotokos on 8.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice, and the Martyrion.*

*Glory, both now: "A language which he knew not," written at the Praises on Saturday.*

*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light."*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 102, Tone 4:*  
Compassionate and merciful is the Lord, long-suffering and of great mercy.  
*Verse:* Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me, bless His holy name.

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 22:1–17):*  
And it came to pass after these words, that God tempted Abraham, and said unto him: Abraham, Abraham! And he said: Behold, here am I. [2] And He said: Take thy beloved son whom thou hast loved, Isaac, and go into the high land, and offer him there for a whole-burnt offering upon one of the mountains that I will tell thee of. [3] And Abraham rose up early in the morning, saddled his donkey, and took with him two of his servants, and Isaac his son; and having split wood for a whole-burnt offering, he arose and went, and came to the place of which God had told him, on the third day. [4] And Abraham, lifting up his eyes, saw the place afar off. [5] And Abraham said unto his servants: Sit ye here with the donkey; and I and the lad will go yonder, and having worshipped, we will return unto you. [6] And Abraham took the wood of the whole-burnt offering, and laid it upon Isaac his son; and he took in his hands both fire and the knife, and they went both of them together. [7] And Isaac spoke unto Abraham his father, and said: Father! And he said: What is it, my child? And he said: Behold the fire and the wood; where is the sheep for a whole-burnt offering? [8] And Abraham said: God will provide for Himself a sheep for a whole-burnt offering, my child. And having gone both of them together, [9] they came unto the place of which God had told him; and there Abraham built an altar, and laid the wood in order; and having bound Isaac his son, he laid him upon the altar on top of the wood. [10] And Abraham stretched forth his hand to take the knife, to slay his son. [11] And the angel of the Lord called unto him out of heaven, and said: Abraham, Abraham! And he said: Behold, here am I. [12] And he said: Lay not thine hand upon the lad, neither do thou anything unto him; for now I know that thou fearest God, and hast not spared thy beloved son for My sake. [13] And Abraham lifted up his eyes and looked, and behold, a ram caught by its horns in a thicket of Sabek; and Abraham went and took the ram, and offered it for a whole-burnt offering in the place of Isaac his son. [14] And Abraham called the name of that place, "The Lord saw"; that they might say today: "In the mountain the Lord was seen." [15] And the angel of the Lord called unto Abraham a second time out of heaven, saying: [16] By Myself have I sworn, says the Lord, because thou hast done this thing, and hast not spared thy beloved son for My sake, [17] in blessing I will truly bless thee, and multiplying I will multiply thy seed as the stars of heaven, and as the sand which is along the seashore; and thy seed shall inherit the cities of their adversaries. [18] And in thy seed shall all the nations of the earth be blessed, because thou hast obeyed My voice.[^1135]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 103, Tone 4:*  
How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all.  
*Verse:* Bless the Lord, O my soul; O Lord my God, Thou hast been magnified exceedingly.

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 17:17–18:5):*  
Brethren should be helpful in distress; for unto this end are they born. [18] A foolish man claps his hands and rejoices in himself, as he who gives surety also makes his friend surety; [19] and upon his lips he treasures up fire. [20] A lover of sin rejoices in quarrels, and the hard-hearted shall not meet with good things. [21] A man fickle of tongue shall fall into evils; and the heart of a fool is sorrow to him that has gotten him. [22] A father rejoices not over an undisciplined son; but a wise son brings joy to his mother. [23] A cheerful heart brings well-being; but the bones of a sorrowful man dry up. [24] The paths of him that receives unjust gifts in the bosom do not prosper; and the ungodly man perverts the ways of righteousness. [25] The face of a prudent man is wise, but the eyes of a fool are on the ends of the earth. [26] A foolish son is wrath unto his father, and grief unto her that bore him. [27] It is not good to lay fines upon a righteous man, neither is it holy to plot against righteous rulers. [28] He that spares to utter a harsh word is understanding; and a long-suffering man is very wise, better than he that seeks instruction. [29] Unto a senseless man asking for wisdom, wisdom shall be imputed; and he that keeps silent shall be deemed to be prudent. [18:1] A man seeking to separate from friends seeks pretexts; and at all times he shall be reproached. [2] A man void of understanding has no need of wisdom, for he is rather led by folly.
