[3] When an ungodly man comes into the depth of evils, he cares not; and dishonor and reproach come upon him. [4] The word in a man's heart is deep water, and a leaping river and fountain of life. [5] To accept the person of the ungodly is not good, neither is it holy to turn aside the righteous in judgment.

*And the rest of the Office of the Presanctified.*

*At the refectory, we drink wine on account of the impending labor of the vigil.*

*Small Compline and the Midnight Office we chant in the cells.*

*The Canon for the Departed of the Tone we chant in the crypt.*

## On Saturday of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast, at Matins: The Supplicatory Office of the Akathist

*The semantron sounds at the fourth hour of the night, for the sake of the supplicatory office of the Akathist.*

*And having assembled in church, the priest having given the blessing, we begin Matins, as the beginning of Matins was prescribed on Thursday of the Great Canon (see fol. 338).[^1136]*

*After the Six Psalms, "God is the Lord" in Tone 8, and we sing the Troparion thrice, slowly. Tone 8:*  
Having received the secret command in spirit, the bodiless one stood with haste in the abode of Joseph, saying unto the Unwedded One: He Who bowed the heavens by His descent is contained wholly and unchangeably in thee; Whom also beholding in thy womb having taken the form of a servant, I am amazed to cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded![^1137]

*Then we chant Kathisma 16 of the Psalter: "The Lord said unto my Lord:"*

*And after its completion, the Little Litany, and we sing the Kontakion in the midst of the church, with sweet singing, slowly. Tone 8:*  
To thee, the Champion Leader, the trophies of victory, as ones delivered from evils, we thy servants ascribe unto thee thank-offerings, O Theotokos; but as thou hast power invincible, free us from all dangers, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*During which the deacon incenses the holy icons and the brethren according to rank; but at the remaining ones, only the superior and the choirs. And we read 6 Ikoses and Kontakia. We stand during the reading. These Ikoses are according to the alphabet, that is, 24, and they are pronounced by the priest in the holy sanctuary.*

*Ikos 1 [A]:*  
An Archangel was sent from heaven to say unto the Theotokos: Rejoice! And beholding Thee, O Lord, taking flesh along with the bodiless voice, he was amazed and stood, crying unto her such things as these:  
Rejoice, through whom joy shall shine forth;  
Rejoice, through whom the curse shall vanish!  
Rejoice, restoration of fallen Adam;  
Rejoice, deliverance from the tears of Eve!  
Rejoice, height difficult to climb for human thoughts;  
Rejoice, depth difficult to fathom even for angelic eyes!  
Rejoice, for thou art the King's throne;  
Rejoice, for thou bearest Him Who bears all things!  
Rejoice, star displaying the Sun;  
Rejoice, womb of divine incarnation!  
Rejoice, through whom creation is renewed;  
Rejoice, through whom we worship the Creator!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded![^1138]

*Kontakion 2 [B]:*  
Beholding herself in purity, the Holy One spoke boldly unto Gabriel: The strangeness of thy voice appears hard for my soul to accept; for how speakest thou of a childbearing from a seedless conception, crying: Alleluia!

*Ikos 2 [G]:*  
Seeking to know the knowledge unknown, the Virgin cried unto the ministering spirit: Tell me, how is it possible for a Son to be born from a pure womb? Unto her he spoke with fear, yet crying thus:  
Rejoice, initiate of the ineffable Counsel;  
Rejoice, faith of those requesting silence!  
Rejoice, prelude of the miracles of Christ;  
Rejoice, headstone of His decrees!  
Rejoice, heavenly ladder by which God descended;  
Rejoice, bridge leading those from earth unto heaven!  
Rejoice, wonder much-marvelled-at by angels;  
Rejoice, wound much-lamented by demons!  
Rejoice, thou who ineffably didst give birth to the Light;  
Rejoice, thou who didst teach the "how" unto no one!  
Rejoice, thou who surpassest the knowledge of the wise;  
Rejoice, thou who illuminest the minds of the faithful!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 3 [D]:*  
The power of the Most High then overshadowed the Virgin who knew not wedlock unto conception, and showed forth her fruitful womb as a sweet field unto all who desire to reap salvation, when chanting thus: Alleluia!

*Ikos 3 [E]:*  
Having received God within her womb, the Virgin ran unto Elizabeth; and her babe, immediately recognizing her greeting, rejoiced, and with leapings as with songs cried out unto the Theotokos:  
Rejoice, branch of the unfading vine;  
Rejoice, possession of immortal fruit!  
Rejoice, thou who didst cultivate the Husbandman, the Lover of mankind;  
Rejoice, thou who didst bear the Planter of our life!  
Rejoice, field growing a richness of mercies;  
Rejoice, table bearing an abundance of propitiations!  
Rejoice, for thou makest the meadow of delight to bloom;  
Rejoice, for thou preparest a haven for souls!  
Rejoice, acceptable incense of prayer;  
Rejoice, cleansing of the whole world!  
Rejoice, good-pleasure of God toward mortals;  
Rejoice, boldness of mortals toward God!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 4 [Z]:*  
Having within a tempest of doubtful thoughts, the chaste Joseph was troubled, looking upon thee, the unwedded, and suspecting a secret union, O Immaculate one; but learning that thy conceiving was of the Holy Spirit, he said: Alleluia!

*And again the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader." We read from the Homily of the Akathist, dividing it in two.*

*Then we chant the Blameless (Kathisma 17), and the Litany.*

*And again we say the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader," and the second 6 Ikoses and Kontakia.*

*Ikos 4 [H]:*  
The shepherds heard the angels hymning the incarnate coming of Christ, and running as unto a Shepherd, they beheld Him as an innocent Lamb, pastured in Mary's womb, whom hymning they said:  
Rejoice, Mother of the Lamb and Shepherd;  
Rejoice, fold of rational sheep!  
Rejoice, torment of invisible enemies;  
Rejoice, opening of paradise's doors!  
Rejoice, for the heavenly rejoice with the earthly;  
Rejoice, for the earthly dance together with the heavenly!  
Rejoice, unceasing mouth of the Apostles;  
Rejoice, invincible courage of the passion-bearers!  
Rejoice, firm support of the faith;  
Rejoice, radiant knowledge of grace!  
Rejoice, through whom Hades was stripped bare;  
Rejoice, through whom we are clothed with glory!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded![^1139]

*Kontakion 5 [Th]:*  
Beholding the star pointing toward God, the Magi followed its radiance; and holding it as a lamp, through it they searched for the mighty King; and having reached the Unreachable, they rejoiced, crying unto Him: Alleluia!

*Ikos 5 [I]:*  
The sons of the Chaldeans saw in the hands of the Virgin Him Who made man with His hands; and discerning Him as Master, though He had taken the form of a servant, they hastened with gifts to serve Him, and to cry unto the Blessed one:  
Rejoice, Mother of the unwaning Star;  
Rejoice, dawn of the mystical day!  
Rejoice, thou who didst quench the furnace of deception;  
Rejoice, thou who illuminest the initiates of the Trinity!  
Rejoice, thou who castest out the inhuman tyrant from dominion;  
Rejoice, thou who hast shown forth Christ, the Lord and Lover of mankind!  
Rejoice, thou who deliverest from barbarian worship;  
Rejoice, thou who drawest out from the mire of deeds!  
Rejoice, thou who didst extinguish the worship of fire;  
Rejoice, thou who frees from the flame of passions!  
Rejoice, guide of the faithful unto chastity;  
Rejoice, gladness of all generations!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 6 [K]:*  
Having become God-bearing heralds, the Magi returned unto Babylon, fulfilling Thy prophecy; and having preached Thee, the Christ, unto all, they left Herod as a babbler, who knew not how to sing: Alleluia!

*Ikos 6 [L]:*  
Having shone forth in Egypt the enlightenment of truth, Thou didst dispel the darkness of falsehood; for its idols, O Savior, not enduring Thy strength, fell down; and they who were delivered from them cried unto the Theotokos:  
Rejoice, restoration of men;  
Rejoice, downfall of demons!  
Rejoice, thou who didst trample down the dominion of delusion;  
Rejoice, thou who didst expose the deceit of idols!  
Rejoice, sea that drowned the spiritual Pharaoh;  
Rejoice, rock that gave drink unto those thirsting for life!  
Rejoice, pillar of fire guiding those in darkness;  
Rejoice, protection of the world, broader than a cloud!  
Rejoice, sustenance, successor to the manna;  
Rejoice, minister of holy delight!  
Rejoice, land of promise;  
Rejoice, out of which flows milk and honey!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 7 [M]:*  
When Simeon was about to depart from this present deceitful age, Thou wast given unto him as an infant; but Thou wast recognized by him also as perfect God; therefore, that righteous elder marveled at Thine ineffable wisdom, crying: Alleluia!

*And again the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader." Then we read the rest of the Homily of the Akathist.*

*Psalm 50. And the Canons: of the Saint of the monastery or temple with the heirmos to 6, and of the Most Holy Theotokos to 6.*

*The composition of Lord Joseph, the acrostic whereof is: Receptacle of joy, to thee alone it is fitting to rejoice. Joseph.*[^1140]

*(If it be a temple of the Theotokos, we sing the canon of the feast of the Theotokos with the heirmos to 12. Heirmoi twice; Troparia to 10.)*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*  
*I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour out a word to the Queen Mother, and I shall be seen radiantly celebrating, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.*

*Troparia:*  
[Χ] Beholding thee, O Pure one, as the living Book of Christ, sealed by the Spirit, the great Archangel proclaimed unto thee: Rejoice, vessel of joy, through whom the curse of our first mother shall be loosed!

[Α] Rejoice, O Virgin Bride of God, the restoration of Adam, the slaying of Hades; rejoice, O All-immaculate one, palace of the King of all; rejoice, fiery Throne of the Almighty!

[Ρ] Rejoice, O unfading Flower, who alone didst sprout forth the sweet-smelling Apple; rejoice, thou who didst bear the sweet fragrance of the only King; rejoice, unwedded one, salvation of the world!

[Α] Rejoice, treasury of purity, through whom we rose from our fall; rejoice, sweet-smelling lily, O Lady, who perfumest the faithful, fragrant censer, and costly myrrh!

*Katavasia:*  
*I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour out a word to the Queen Mother, and I shall be seen radiantly celebrating, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
*Establish thy singers, O Theotokos, living and ungrudging Fountain, who form a spiritual choir, in thy divine glory, vouchsafing them crowns of glory.*

*Troparia:*  
[Σ] Rejoice, living table, who didst sprout forth the divine Ear of corn manifestly as an untilled field, containing the Bread of life; rejoice, inexhaustible fountain of living water, O Lady!

[Δ] Rejoice, heifer who bore the spotless Young Bull for the faithful; rejoice, ewe who bore the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world; rejoice, fervent mercy-seat!

[Ο] Rejoice, radiant morning, who alone didst bear Christ the Sun, dwelling of the Light; rejoice, thou who didst dispel the darkness and utterly drive away the gloomy demons!

[Χ] Rejoice, only door through which the Word alone passed, who by thy childbearing, O Lady, didst shatter the bars and gates of Hades; rejoice, divine entrance of the saved, O Bride of God!

*Katavasia:*  
*Establish thy singers, O Theotokos, living and ungrudging Fountain, who form a spiritual choir, in thy divine glory, vouchsafing them crowns of glory.*

*Little Litany. And after the exclamatory prayer, the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader."*

*And we read the other six Ikoses and Kontakia.*

*Ikos 7 [N]:*  
A new creation did the Creator display, showing Himself unto us whom He had made, sprouting from a seedless womb, and preserving it incorrupt as it was, that seeing the wonder, we may hymn her, crying aloud:
Rejoice, flower of incorruption;  
Rejoice, crown of continence!  
Rejoice, flashing forth the type of the Resurrection;  
Rejoice, displaying the angelic life!  
Rejoice, tree of splendid fruit, from which the faithful are nourished;  
Rejoice, tree of well-shaded leaves, under which many are sheltered!  
Rejoice, thou who bearest in thy womb the Redeemer of captives;  
Rejoice, thou who didst give birth to the Guide of the straying!  
Rejoice, supplication before the righteous Judge;  
Rejoice, forgiveness of many transgressions!  
Rejoice, robe of confidence for the naked;  
Rejoice, love that overcomes all longing!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded![^1141]

*Kontakion 8 [X]:*  
Seeing a strange childbearing, let us become strangers to the world, transferring our mind unto heaven; for unto this end did the high God appear on earth as a humble man, wishing to draw to the height those who cry unto Him: Alleluia!

*Ikos 8 [O]:*  
The uncircumscribed Word was wholly in things below, and yet nowise departed from things above; for it was a divine condescension, and not a change of place, and a childbearing from a God-receiving Virgin, who heard such things as these:  
Rejoice, dwelling-place of the uncontainable God;  
Rejoice, doors of the venerable mystery!  
Rejoice, doubtful hearing of the unbelieving;  
Rejoice, undoubted boast of the faithful!  
Rejoice, all-holy chariot of Him Who is upon the Cherubim;  
Rejoice, most glorious habitation of Him Who is upon the Seraphim!  
Rejoice, thou who didst bring opposites into one;  
Rejoice, thou who didst unite virginity and childbearing!  
Rejoice, through whom transgression was loosed;  
Rejoice, through whom paradise was opened!  
Rejoice, key of the Kingdom of Christ;  
Rejoice, hope of eternal good things!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 9 [P]:*  
Every angelic nature was amazed at the great work of Thine incarnation; for they beheld Him Who is unapproachable as God, accessible to all as man, dwelling among us, and hearing from all: Alleluia!

*Ikos 9 [R]:*  
Orators of many words we behold mute as fishes before thee, O Theotokos; for they are at a loss to tell how thou remainest a virgin and wast able to give birth; but we, marveling at the mystery, faithfully cry out:  
Rejoice, vessel of God's wisdom;  
Rejoice, treasury of His providence!  
Rejoice, thou who showest the philosophers to be unwise;  
Rejoice, thou who provest the crafty-worded to be wordless!  
Rejoice, for the fierce seekers have become foolish;  
Rejoice, for the myth-makers have withered away!  
Rejoice, thou who tearest asunder the Athenian webs;  
Rejoice, thou who fillest the nets of the fishermen!  
Rejoice, thou who drawest up from the depth of ignorance;  
Rejoice, thou who illuminest many in understanding!  
Rejoice, ship of those who desire to be saved;  
Rejoice, haven of the voyages of life!  
Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 10 [S]:*  
Desiring to save the world, the Adorner of all came unto it of His own promise; and being the Shepherd as God, for our sake He appeared as a man among us; for having called like by like, as God He hears: Alleluia!

*And again the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader."*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^1142]
The great warrior of the bodiless Angels stood before thee in the city of Nazareth, proclaiming Thy King and Lord of the ages, O Pure One, crying out unto thee: Rejoice, blessed Mary, incomprehensible and unutterable depth, recalling of mankind!

*Glory, both now: the same. And again a reading.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead:*

[Σ] In voices of song, O Virgin, we cry aloud unto thee, O All-praised One: Rejoice, fertile mountain, curdled by the Spirit; rejoice, candlestick, and jar bearing the manna, sweetening the senses of all the pious!

[Ο] Propitiation for the world, rejoice, O Most Pure Lady; rejoice, ladder that by grace hast raised all from the earth; rejoice, bridge that truly leads all who praise thee from death unto life!

[Ι] Higher than the heavens, rejoice, foundation of the earth, who without toil didst bear in thy womb, O Most Pure One; rejoice, purple dye that from thy blood didst dip the divine purple robe for the King of hosts!

[Π] Having given birth unto the true Lawgiver, rejoice, O Lady, Who cleanses freely the transgressions of all; depth unfathomable, height ineffable, unwedded one, through whom we have been deified!

[Ρ] We hymn thee who didst weave for the world a crown not fashioned by hands, crying unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, fortress, and rampart of all, and confirmation, and sacred refuge!

*Katavasia:*
*He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the transcendent God, came on a light cloud, with His incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: All things were amazed:*[^1143]

[Ε] Having given birth unto the Way of life, rejoice, O All-immaculate One, who didst save the world from the flood of sin; rejoice, Bride of God, dreadful report and telling; rejoice, dwelling-place of the Sovereign Master of creation!

[Π] Strength and confirmation of men, rejoice, O Most Pure One, place of the sanctification of glory, putting to death of hades, bridal chamber all-radiant; rejoice, joy of the Angels; rejoice, help of those who pray unto thee in faith!

[Ι] Fiery chariot of the Word, rejoice, O Lady, living Paradise, having in thy midst the Tree of life, the Lord, Whose sweetness vivifis those who partake in faith, though they had bowed down to corruption!

[Χ] Strengthened by thy might, we faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, city of the King of all, of which glorious and worthy things were clearly spoken, mountain unquarried; rejoice, depth immeasurable!

[Α] Spacious tabernacle of the Word, rejoice, O Most Pure One; vessel that didst produce the divine Pearl; rejoice, all-marvelous reconciliation unto God for all who ever call thee blessed, O Mother of God!

*Katavasia:*
*All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb Him Who is God over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.*

*Here follows the canon of the holy patron of the monastery.*[^1144]

*Ode 6. Heirmos: This divine and all-honorable:*

[Π] Undefiled bridal chamber of the Word, cause of the deification of all, rejoice, O Most Pure One, proclamation of the Prophets; rejoice, adornment of the Apostles!

[Ε] From thee distilled the dew that quenched the flame of polytheism; therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, living fleece, which Gideon, O Virgin, foresaw!

[Ι] Lo, unto thee [O Virgin] we cry: Rejoice! Be thou a haven unto us who are tempest-tossed, and a calm in the deep of afflictions, and the overthrow of all snares of the adversary!

[Χ] Cause of joy, grace our thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, unburnt bush, cloud all-radiant, continually overshadowing the faithful!

*Then the Four-Ode Canon, whose acrostic is: This song is by Joseph:*

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos: The sea of life surging with tempest:*

Having suffered, O martyrs, and rolling upon the ground like chosen stones, you utterly brought to an end all building of the enemy, and showed yourselves to be temples of the living God!

We pray you, O martyrs, who have finished the good course: strengthen us to run well the course of the fast, who have shone forth by the perfection of virtue!

*For the Departed:*
Make Thy servants, O Master, who have passed away from the earth unto Thee, the All-Good, partakers of Thy Kingdom, through the sacred intercessions of Thy divine martyrs, O Many-merciful One!

*Theotokion:*
O only All-praised One, pray to the all-hymned Word that remission of sins and a share of divine gifts may be granted unto those who faithfully hymn thee, O Virgin Mother!

*Another Canon. Ode 6, Tone 5. Heirmos: Out of the whale, the Prophet:*[^1145]

Celebrating the memory of the martyrs, O Lord, we send up hymns today, rejoicing divinely!

Not fearing swords or fire, daring in faith, O passion-bearers, in this you wounded the tormentors!

*Glory: Trinity:*
In Persons I hymn Thee as the Trinity, in essence I worship Thee as the Unity: O Father, Son, and Holy Spirit!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thou art the praise of Angels and of men, O Virgin, for thou didst bear Christ, the Savior of our souls!

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [Psalm 67:36].*[^1146]

Having exchanged death for life, you rejoice, dwelling in the heavens, O most glorious passion-bearers of Christ God!

*Verse: Their souls shall abide in prosperity [Psalm 24:13].*

Ruling over death and life, O Christ, give rest with Thy saints unto those who in faith have departed from this life!

*Katavasia:*
*Thou didst deliver the Prophet from the sea-monster; bring me up also, O Lord, from the depth of sins, and save me!*

*Little Litany. And again we sing the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader."*

*And we read the remaining six Ikoses and Kontakia.*[^1147]

*Ikos 10:*

[Τ] A wall art thou unto virgins, O Virgin Theotokos, and unto all who flee unto thee; for the Creator of heaven and earth fashioned thee, O Most Pure One, dwelling in thy womb, and teaching all to cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, pillar of virginity; rejoice, gate of salvation; rejoice, leader of spiritual restoration; rejoice, dispenser of divine goodness; rejoice, for thou didst regenerate those conceived in shame; rejoice, for thou didst give counsel unto those robbed of understanding; rejoice, who didst bring to naught the corrupter of minds; rejoice, who didst bear the Sower of purity; rejoice, bridal chamber of seedless espousal; rejoice, who didst join the faithful unto the Lord; rejoice, good nourisher of virgins; rejoice, adorner of holy souls: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 11:*

[Υ] Every hymn falls short, striving to measure up to the multitude of Thy many compassions; for if we should offer Thee songs as many in number as the sand, O Holy King, we accomplish nothing worthy of what Thou hast given unto us who cry unto Thee: Alleluia!

*Ikos 11:*

[Φ] A light-receiving lamp, appearing unto those in darkness, do we behold in the holy Virgin; for kindling the immaterial Light, she guids all unto divine knowledge, illuminating the mind with its beam, and honored by these cries: Rejoice, ray of the spiritual Sun; rejoice, luminary of the unwaning Light; rejoice, lightning illuminating souls; rejoice, like thunder striking down the enemies; rejoice, for thou causest the many-lighted illumination to dawn; rejoice, for thou gushest forth a copious river; rejoice, living image of the font; rejoice, who takest away the defilement of sin; rejoice, bath washing clean the conscience; rejoice, chalice drawing forth joy; rejoice, fragrance of the sweet savor of Christ; rejoice, life of mystical gladness: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 12:*

[Χ] Desiring to grant release from ancient debts, the Releaser of all debts of men came of Himself unto those who had departed from His grace; and tearing up the handwriting against them, He hears from all: Alleluia!

*Ikos 12:*[^1148]

[Ψ] Singing thy childbearing, we all praise thee as a living temple, O Theotokos; for the Lord Who holds all things in His hand, having dwelt in thy womb, sanctified and glorified thee, and taught all to cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, tabernacle of God the Word; rejoice, Holy of Holies greater than all; rejoice, ark gilded by the Spirit; rejoice, inexhaustible treasury of life; rejoice, precious diadem of pious kings; rejoice, venerable boast of reverent priests; rejoice, unshakeable tower of the Church; rejoice, invincible rampart of the Kingdom; rejoice, through whom victories are raised up; rejoice, through whom enemies are cast down; rejoice, healing of my body; rejoice, salvation of my soul: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Kontakion 13:*

(*Ѡ*) O All-praised Mother, who didst bear the Word, the Holiest of all the saints! Having accepted our present offering, deliver all from every affliction, and rescue from the torment to come those who cry unto thee: Alleluia!

*This Kontakion is said thrice.*

*And again Ikos 1 is read: "An Archangel was sent..." And again the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader."*

*Then the Synaxaria are read: first that of the Menaion, then the present one:*

*Verses: With sleepless songs of thanksgiving,*
*The City praises the vigilant Champion in battles.*

On this day we celebrate the Akathist Hymn of our Most Holy Lady Theotokos, for the following reason:[^1149] While Heraclius was ruling the imperial realm of the Greeks, Khosrow, the Persian king, seeing the Greek scepter brought low by Phocas the tyrant emperor, sent one of his grandees named Sarbaros with many thousands of troops to subjugate all the eastern lands unto himself; for Khosrow had previously destroyed ten times ten thousand Christians, selling them to the Jews who bought them and put them to death. The chief grandee Sarbaros, having conquered all the East, reached even to the Golden City, which is now called Scutari. Emperor Heraclius, having become impoverished in gold, converted the sacred church vessels into gold coins with the promise to return them in greater and more complete measure; and sailing through the Black Sea with ships to the Persian lands, he laid them waste. Khosrow was utterly defeated by him along with his remaining troops; and Siroes, Khosrow's son, learning of this, rebelled against his father, seized the rule for himself, slew his father Khosrow, and formed an alliance with Emperor Heraclius. But the Chagan of Mysia and ruler of the Scythians, learning that the emperor had departed across the Pontic Sea, broke the peace with the Greeks, moved countless legions, and came from the western parts against Constantinople, crying out blasphemous words against God. Immediately the sea was filled with ships, and the land was full of countless foot-soldiers and horsemen. But Sergius, the Patriarch of Constantinople, greatly comforted the people, urging them not to lose heart or weaken, but from their soul to place all their hope in God and in His most pure Mother, the Theotokos. Likewise Bonus the Patrician, who was then governing the city, prepared what was needed to equip the warriors; for it behooves us also, with help from on high, to act fittingly. The Patriarch, bearing the divine icons of the Mother of God, along with the whole multitude, went in procession along the walls from above, giving them courage from thence. For while Sarbaros from the east and the Chagan from the west were advancing to burn the environs of the city, the Patriarch went along the walls carrying the Icon of Christ Not-Made-by-Hands, the precious and life-creating Wood of the Cross, and the venerable Robe of the Mother of God. The Chagan, a Scythian, attacked the walls of Constantinople by land with an innumerable multitude, fortified with strong siege weapons; and so great was their number that one Greek had to fight against ten Scythians. But the invincible Champion, through the few soldiers who were near Her church called Pigi [the Spring], destroyed the greater part of them. Hence the Greeks, taking courage and rejoicing, by the aid of their unconquerable Leader, the Mother of God, continually defeated them unto the end. And when the citizens looked toward reconciliation, they were rejected; for the Chagan answered them: "Be not deceived in the God in Whom you believe, for tomorrow by all means I shall take your city." Hearing this, the citizens stretched out their hands unto God. The Chagan and Sarbaros having agreed together, attacked the city by land and sea, seeking to work destruction by their wiles; but they were so defeated by the Greeks that the living were not sufficient to burn the dead. And their monoxyla [canoes], filled with armed men, in the gulf called the Golden Horn, near the church of the Mother of God at Blachernae, were suddenly struck by a violent tempest that fell upon the sea and tore it asunder, so that all the ships of the enemies were scattered. And one could see that most glorious and marvelous sight of the Most Pure Mother of God: all were cast upon the seashore at Blachernae. The people quickly opened the gates and slew them all together, and even the children and women fell upon them. Their commanders returned weeping and wailing together. Therefore the God-loving people of Constantinople, rendering thanks unto the Mother of God, rendering thanks, sang unto Her an all-night and unseated song [the Akathist], as unto Her who had kept vigil over them and by supernatural power had wrought victory over their enemies. From that time forth, in remembrance of so great and supernatural a wonder, the Church received this feast, to offer it unto the Mother of God at this present season, wherein the victory also was won by the Mother of God. And it was called "unseated" [Akathistos] because the city choir and all the people then stood upright while singing it.[^1150]

After the passage of thirty-six years, in the reign of Constantine Pogonatus, the Hagarenes, leading a countless army, attacked Constantinople again; and warring against it for seven years, during which they wintered in the regions of Cyzicus, they lost many of their men. Then, giving up the war and returning with their army, when they were near the sea called Syllaeum, they were all drowned through the intercession of the most pure Mother of God.

And yet a third time, under Leo the Isaurian, the Hagarenes, in numbers far exceeding the former, first laid waste the Persian kingdom, then Egypt and Libya; and having overrun India, Ethiopia, and Spain, finally they waged war against the Imperial City itself, borne upon one thousand eight hundred ships. Having encircled it, they expected immediately to plunder it. But the sacred citizens, bearing about the precious Wood of the venerable and life-creating Cross and the venerable icon of the Mother of God Hodigitria, circled the wall, propitiating God with tears. Then it seemed good to the Hagarenes to divide themselves into two parts: some made war against the Bulgarians and there fell more than twenty thousand; while the others remained to take Constantinople, but were prevented by the chain that stretched from Galata unto the walls of the Imperial City. Having sailed up as far as a place called Sosthenes, there a north wind blew upon them, and many of their ships were shattered and destroyed; and those who remained fell into severe famine, so that they even tasted human flesh and kneaded dung and ate it. Then fleeing, when they reached the Aegean deep, all their light vessels and they themselves were delivered unto the depth of the sea; for a violent hailstorm fell from heaven, stirring up the sea, and melted the pitch of the ships. And so that countless multitude of ships perished, only three remaining to bring the tidings.

For the sake of all these supernatural wonders of the All-Pure Mother of God, we celebrate the present feast. And it was called "unseated" because all the people, standing upright on that night, sang the hymn unto the Mother of the Word; and whereas it is customary to sit during all others, during these hymns of the Mother of God we all listen standing upright.

By the prayers of Thine unconquerable Mother, our Champion, deliver us also, O Christ God, from surrounding afflictions, and have mercy upon us, for Thou alone art the Lover of mankind!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: The godly-minded youths worshipped not creation:*[^1151]

[Α] We hymn thee, crying aloud: Rejoice, chariot of the spiritual Sun; true Vine, who didst cultivate the ripe Cluster, distilling wine to gladden the souls of those who glorify thee in faith!

[Ι] Having given birth unto the Healer of all mankind, rejoice, Bride of God, mystical rod that didst bud forth the unfading Flower; rejoice, O Lady, through whom we are filled with joy and inherit life!

[Ρ] No eloquent tongue can praise thee worthily, O Lady; for thou wast exalted higher than the Seraphim, having given birth unto Christ the King; pray Thou unto Him that this city may be delivered from many temptations!

[Ε] The ends of the earth praise thee, calling thee blessed, and with love cry unto thee: Rejoice, scroll wherein by the Father's finger the Word was written, O Pure One; pray Thou unto Him, O Theotokos, that thy servants may be written in the book of life!

[Ι] We, thy servants, pray thee and bow the knee of our heart: Incline thine ear, O Pure One, and save those continually submerged in sorrows, and preserve thy city, O Theotokos, from every capture by the enemy!

*Another Canon. Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos: Dew-giving furnace:*

The all-radiant assembly of Thy divine martyrs, O Many-merciful One, now dwells in Thine unwaning light; by their prayers grant illumination and cleansing from sins unto all, O Christ!

How beautiful is the season of abstinence which Thou hast given unto us, O Lord, wherein do Thou show mercy unto our souls, as Thou art good, by the prayers of the holy sufferers who loved Thy fair and honorable Passions!

Guide Thy servants who have traversed the tempestuous sea of this painful life unto the haven of life, O Master, that they may cry with all the elect: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O Ever-Virgin who didst give birth unto the Lawgiver, pray that all transgressions may be taken away in this present season from those who have well chosen to crown themselves through diligence in the divine fast!

*Another Canon. Ode 7, Tone 5. Heirmos: Blessed art Thou:*[^1152]

Blessed art Thou, O God, Who hast shown forth marvelous things through the martyrs suffering for Thy sake, all-hymned and all-glorified!

Blessed art Thou, O God, Who grantest unto us the passion-bearers as intercessors before Thee, all-hymned and all-glorified!

*Glory: Trinity:*
Blessed art Thou, O God, One indeed in essence, but known in three Persons, all-hymned and all-glorified!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Blessed art Thou, O God, Who wast born without seed, and didst show forth Her who bare Thee remaining pure, all-hymned and all-glorified!

*Verse: Unto the saints who are in His earth, marvelous hath the Lord made all His desires in them [Psalm 15:3].*

Blessed art Thou, O God, Who didst tame wild beasts and quench fire unto the glory of Thy saints, all-hymned and all-glorified!

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord [Psalm 64:5].*

Blessed art Thou, O God, Who hast translated from death unto unending life those who in life have served Thee in faith!

*Heirmos:*
*Blessed art Thou, O God, Who beholdest the depths and sittest upon the throne of glory, all-hymned and all-glorious!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: The pious youths in the furnace:*

[Ν] In thy womb thou didst receive the Word; Him Who bears all things thou didst bear; with milk thou didst nourish Him Who by His nod nourishes the whole universe, O Pure One; unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[Μ] Moses understood in the bush the great mystery of thy childbearing; and the youths prefigured this most clearly, standing in the midst of fire and not being consumed, O incorrupt and holy Virgin; therefore we honor thee unto all ages!

[Ο] Stripped of old through deceit, we have been clothed in a garment of incorruption through thy childbearing; and we who sat in the darkness of transgressions have seen the Light, O Maiden, dwelling-place of the Light; therefore we hymn thee unto all ages!

[Ν] Through thee the dead are restored to life, for thou didst bear the hypostatic Life; those previously mute become eloquent; the lepers are cleansed; diseases are driven away; the multitudes of aerial spirits are defeated, O Virgin, salvation of mankind!

[Η] Having given birth unto the Salvation of the world, through Whom we have been lifted up from the earth unto the height: Rejoice, All-Blessed One, protection and dominion, rampart and confirmation of those who sing, O Pure One: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another Canon. Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos: Out of the flame for the venerable:*[^1153]

At the right achievement of the holy martyrs, the heavenly minds marvelled; by their prayers, O Master, show forth Thy mercies richly upon us, O Compassionate One!

You who quenched the fire by divine dew, O praiseworthy sufferers of the Lord, deliver us from the tormenting fire by your fervent prayers unto the Master!

*For the Departed:*
O All-Good Word, vouchsafe the life that is in the heavens and holy brightness unto those who have departed from us in the Orthodox faith, by the prayers of the gloriously triumphant martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
By the prayers of Her who bare Thee, O Compassionate One, and of the holy martyrs, and of Thine Apostles, illuminate our souls, that we may glorify Thee in spiritual gladness unto ages of ages!

*Another Canon. Ode 8, Tone 5. Heirmos: The Creator of creation, Whom the Angels dread:*

O holy choirs of martyrs, remember those who sing of you in love and exalt Christ unto ages of ages!

When severed with the sword, the martyrs rejoiced, singing unto Christ and exalting Him above all unto all ages!

*Let us bless the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Trinity:*
In marvelous manner is the Trinity distinguished, yet abides undivided as God, Whom we exalt above all unto all ages!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
A Virgin bare an Infant, for God became man from her; let all creation sing unto Him unto ages of ages!

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [Psalm 67:36].*

The martyrs showed themselves to be pillars of courage, rejoicing at the downfall of the devil, singing unto Christ and exalting Him above all unto ages of ages!

*Verse: Their souls shall abide in prosperity [Psalm 24:13].*

Vouchsafe Thy servants, O Lord, who have departed unto Thee in faith and hope, to dwell in the bosom of Abraham!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*

*Heirmos:*
*The Creator of creation, Whom the Angels dread, hymn, O ye people, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Every earthborn mortal:*[^1154]

[Ι] That we, the faithful, may cry unto thee: Rejoice! having through thee become partakers of everlasting joy, deliver us from afflictions, from barbarian captivity, and from every other wound coming upon sinning men by reason of the multitude of our transgressions, O Maiden!

[Ω] Thou hast been shown forth as our enlightenment and confirmation; therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, star unwaning, introducing into the world the great Sun; rejoice, Pure One, who didst open Eden that was shut; rejoice, vessel that received the inexhaustible Myrrh poured out upon thee!

[Σ] Let us stand with reverence in the house of our God, and cry aloud: Rejoice, Sovereign Lady of the world; rejoice, Mary, Mistress of us all; rejoice, only immaculate and fair one among women; rejoice, pillar of fire leading mankind into the life on high!

[Η] Dove that didst bear the Merciful One, rejoice, Ever-Virgin; rejoice, boast of all the venerable, crown of the sufferers; rejoice, divine adornment of all the righteous, and salvation for us, the faithful!

[Φ] Spare Thine inheritance, O God, overlooking all our sins now, having as an intercessor before Thee on this account Her who on earth bare Thee without seed, Who for Thy great mercy's sake wast pleased to take human form, O Christ!

*Another Canon. Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos: God it is impossible for men to see:*

Shining forth like light, like the sun, the martyrs illuminated the whole earth with the rays of piety and the beacons of miracles, and dispersed the darkness of polytheism; by their prayers, O God, have mercy upon us!

Invincible army of the martyrs, strengthen all of us to contend well and to complete the course of the fast, that having accomplished the work of divine life, we may be partakers of life, rejoicing!

*For the Departed:*
Let Thy goodness anticipate, O Lord, those who have departed from us by Thy dread command; let Thy mercy comfort them, and guide them unto enlightened dwellings by the light of Thy countenance!

*Theotokion:*
At the Angel's voice, ineffably, O Theotokos, thou didst bear the Angel of the Father's Counsel; receive then the voices of thy servants which we offer in the season of the fast, and offer these unto God as incense!

*Another Canon. Ode 9, Tone 5. Heirmos: Rejoice, O Isaiah:*

Celebrating the triumph of the martyrs, today, O people, let us cry aloud in hymns, singing unto Christ the Arbiter of the contest, Who granted them victories over enemies, Whom in hymns we magnify!

Torn with iron claws and severed with the sword limb by limb, you were lovingly joined unto Christ, O all-praised martyrs; therefore now rejoicing in the heavens, pray for all!

*Glory: Trinity:*
One in essence I hymn Thee, O beginningless Trinity, venerable, life-originating, indivisible Unity: unbegotten Father, and begotten Word and Son, Holy Spirit: save us who hymn Thee!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is thy childbearing, O Mother of God; for conception was in thee without man, and the birth was virginal; for He Who was born is God, Whom magnifying, we call thee, O Virgin, blessed!

*Verse: Unto the saints who are in His earth [Psalm 15:3].*

Let us, O people, crown with hymns the valiant army of the King of all and God, the martyrs; for they clearly conquered the demonic legions, and chanting of them, we magnify the Master!

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord [Psalm 64:5].*

When Thou shalt raise up all Thy creation to be judged, O Christ, have mercy on Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast received, forgive them their transgressions in this life, and give them rest with Thy saints unto all ages!

*Heirmos:*
*Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin hath conceived in her womb and borne a Son, Emmanuel, both God and man; the Orient is His name; Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.*

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^1155]
The mystery hidden from the ages is made known today: the divine God the Word, the Son of the Virgin Mary, becomes man through compassion, and Gabriel announcs the joy of the Annunciation, with whom let us all cry aloud: Rejoice, O Mother of the Lord!

*At the Praises [Lauds], let every breath praise the Lord. We set 4 verses, and we chant Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

The Archangel Gabriel confirms the hidden mystery unknown to the Angels, and he comes now unto thee, the only undefiled and fair Dove, the recalling of our race, and he cries aloud unto thee, O All-Holy One: Hail! Prepare thyself by thy word to receive God the Word in thy womb! *(Twice)*

A light-bearing chamber is prepared for Thee, O Master, the pure womb of the Child of God; come unto her and descend, show compassion upon Thy creation, which through envy received war and was held in the servitude of the deceiver, and lost its primal beauty, and awaits Thy saving descent!

The Archangel Gabriel comes visibly unto thee, O All-Immaculate One, and cries aloud unto thee: Rejoice, release from the curse, resurrection of the fallen; rejoice, who alone wast chosen for God; rejoice, chariot of the Sun of glory: receive the Bodiless One Who wills to dwell in thy womb!

*Glory, both now: Idiomelon, Tone 4:*
The Mother of God heard a tongue which she knew not, for the Archangel spoke unto her the words of the Annunciation; therefore having accepted the salutation in faith, she conceived Thee, the pre-eternal God; therefore we also, rejoicing, cry aloud unto Thee: O God Who wast incarnate of her without change, grant peace unto the world, and unto our souls great mercy!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Typika, and from the Canon of the Akathist, Odes 3 and 6.*[^1156]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Song of the Theotokos:*
*My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Savior [Luke 1:46–47].*

*Verse: For He hath regarded the lowliness of His handmaiden; for behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed [Luke 1:48].*

*Epistle of the day: to the Hebrews, Pericope 322 [Hebrews 9:24–28]. And of the Theotokos: from the same Epistle, Pericope 320 [Hebrews 9:1–7].*

*Alleluia, Tone 8: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest [Psalm 131:8].*

*Verse: Remember David, O Lord, and all his meekness [Psalm 131:1].*

*Gospel of the day: from Mark, Pericope 35 [Mark 8:34–9:1]. And of the Theotokos: from Luke, Pericope 54 [Luke 10:38–42; 11:27–28].*

*Communion Hymn:*
*I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord [Psalm 115:4].*

*At the refectory we eat two cooked dishes with oil, and we drink wine, giving thanks unto our all-holy Sovereign Lady the Theotokos for the wonder wrought by Her.*

Christ hangs upon the Cross beneath the titulus 'INRI'; to the right stands His All-Holy Mother with the holy women; to the left stands the Beloved Disciple John with the Roman centurion; and at the base of the Cross kneels Mary Magdalene.]

## On the Fifth Sunday of the Great Fast: Commemoration of our Venerable Mother Mary of Egypt[^1158]

*The order of service is similar to that of the other Sundays: in which we also sing the Menaion office of the saint of the day.*
*The later fathers, however, handed down that on this Sunday we sing the office of our venerable mother Mary of Egypt.*

*At the Lamplighting Psalms, after the Introductory Psalm and the usual kathisma of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses. And we sing 3 Resurrectional stichera and 3 Anatolian stichera of the Octoechos, and 3 stichera of the venerable woman, repeating them to make 6:*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

The pollution of past defilements held thee back from beholding the precious sights; but thy perception and thy God-wise conscience concerning what had been committed wrought thy turning unto better things. For looking upon the Icon of the blessed Maiden of God, having repented of all thy former transgressions, O all-lauded one, with boldness thou didst venerate the precious Wood.

Having with joy venerated the holy places, from thence thou didst receive the most saving viaticum of virtue, and didst run exceedingly well on thy noble journey. And crossing the stream of the Jordan, thou didst eagerly dwell in the habitation of the Forerunner, and by thy way of life didst darken the fierceness of the passions, boldly refining away, O ever-memorable mother, the properties of the flesh.

Having settled in the wilderness, thou didst banish from thy soul the images of thy passions, inscribing upon thy soul the most Godlike depiction: the forms of virtues. And so greatly didst thou shine forth, O blessed one, that thou didst even walk lightly over the waters, and was lifted up from the earth in thy supplications unto God. And now, O all-glorious Mary, standing before Christ with boldness, pray for our souls!

*Glory...*

*Glory: Tone 4:*
The power of Thy Cross, O Christ, hath wrought a marvel, for even she who was formerly a harlot strove in ascetic contest; therefore, casting aside weakness, she valiantly stood against the devil; therefore, bearing the trophy of victory, she prays for our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion of the Tone [Tone 4, first Theotokion / Dogmatikon].*

*At the Aposticha, the Stichera of the Octoechos.*

*Glory, of the venerable woman: Tone 2:*
The snares of the soul and the passions of the flesh hast thou severed with the sword of abstinence; the thoughts of transgression hast thou suppressed by the silence of discipline; and with the streams of thy tears thou didst water all the wilderness, and didst make the fruits of repentance bud forth for us; therefore we celebrate thy memory, O venerable mother!

*Both now: Theotokion, in the same Tone: "O new wonder!"*[^1159]

*Dismissal Troparion: The Resurrectional Troparion of the Tone; Glory, of the venerable woman, Tone 8:*
In thee, O mother, that which was according to the image was preserved in safety; for taking up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thine actions thou didst teach to despise the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, a thing immortal; therefore, O venerable Mary, thy spirit rejoices together with the Angels!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thou Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin...

*The same also at "God is the Lord."*
*And the rest of the order of service as usual.*

[Headpiece: Horizontal ornamental decorative band of interlinked foliage motifs]

*At Matins:*

*After the whole usual order of service, 3 Canons: the Resurrectional Canon with its Theotokia for 6; the present Canon of the Triodion for 4; and for the venerable woman for 4.*[^1160]

*Canon on the Rich Man and on Lazarus:*[^1161]

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Having traversed the water:"*
Thou hast left me unto the riches of pleasures, like unto the rich man who rejoiced each day in sweetness; therefore I pray Thee, O Savior: deliver me from the fire, even as Thou didst deliver Lazarus!

I have clothed myself in pleasures, O Savior, even as he who put on fine linen and gold and golden raiment; but send me not into the fire, as Thou didst send him!

The rich man of old exulted in wealth and sweet pleasure in this corruptible life, therefore was he condemned unto torments; but Lazarus the poor was bedewed with refreshment.

*Theotokion:*
The ranks of Angels and men, O unwedded Mother, praise thee unceasingly; for thou didst bear in thine arms the Creator of them both as an Infant.

*Second Canon of the venerable woman, for 4:*

*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos: "The wave of the sea:"*
Unto me who celebrate in love thy light-bearing and divine memory, send down light, O venerable woman, as thou standest before Christ the unapproachable Light, delivering me from all temptations of this life!

He Who went down into Egypt in the flesh, uncircumscribed and pre-eternal, shows thee forth from Egypt as an all-radiant star, the Lord Who knows all things before their being.

Knowing not the divine commandments, O honorable one, thou didst defile the divine image of God; yet by divine providence thou didst cleanse it again, O all-lauded one, having been deified by thy divine deeds, O venerable mother.

*Theotokion:*
O the great compassion and the ineffable condescension of Thee, my God! How by the intercessions of Thy Mother hast Thou likened her who was formerly a harlot unto the Angels, as pure and undefiled!

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Thou art the confirmation:"*
Even as Thou didst save Lazarus from the flame, O Christ, so deliver me, Thine unworthy servant, from the fire of Gehenna!

I am the rich man in passions and pleasures, O Lord, and Lazarus the poor in lack of virtues; but save me!

The rich man clothed himself in purple and fine linen through pleasures and sins; for this cause is he tormented in the flames.

*Theotokion:*
Grant us help by thy prayers, O All-Pure One, repelling the assaults of grievous adversities!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "Thee Who upon the waters:"*
Unto her who drew near unto the gates of destruction through unseemly deeds, He Who of old broke down the gates of Hades by the power of His Divinity opens the gates of repentance unto thee, O all-honorable one, being Himself the Door of Life.

Her who was formerly a weapon of sin, O Long-suffering and Compassionate One, Thou didst show forth, through the veneration of the weapon of the divine Cross, as conquering all the weapons and wiles of the demons!

He Who of old shed His own blood as the ransom for all makes thee pure by drops of tears, thee who was diseased with the grievous leprosy of evil deeds, He Who gave being in every way unto all!

*Theotokion:*
Higher than any word is that which hath come to pass in thee, O Virgin; for the Word of the Father dwelt in thee in a manner befitting God, granting by a single word the remission of sins unto all who transgress.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрость и҆ Сло́во):*
Having bridled all the surges of the flesh by ascetic labors, thou didst show forth the courageous disposition of thy soul; for longing to behold the Cross of the Lord, O all-honorable one, thou didst crucify thyself unto the world. Therefore, O all-blessed one, thou didst lovingly direct thyself toward the desire for the angelic life. Pray thou unto Christ God that He grant remission of sins unto them that honor with love thy holy memory!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Let us praise the Heavenly Gate and Ark, the All-Holy Mountain, the Radiant Cloud, the Unburnt Bush, the Spiritual Paradise, the Recalling of Eve, the Great Treasure of all the universe; for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore we cry aloud unto her: Pray unto thy Son and God that He grant remission of sins unto them that piously worship thine all-holy Offspring!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "I heard, O Lord:"*
The rich man was delighting in meats and making merry in clothing; but Lazarus was desiring to be fed with the crumbs from his table.

The dogs licked with their tongue the sores of Lazarus the poor, being more compassionate to the needy man than the mind of the rich man.

Of old before the gates of the rich man, O Savior, lay Lazarus tormented by the wounds of poverty, and for this is he now glorified!

*Theotokion:*
Him Whom thou didst bear, O All-Pure One, pray that they who praise thee may be saved from the bondage of the deceiver, for thou alone art our protectress!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "Thy divine emptying upon the Cross:"*
As the Creator of human nature, as the Fountain of mercy and the Riches of compassion, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst show compassion upon her who fled unto Thee, and didst snatch her away from the destructive beast!

Having hastened to behold the Cross, O Mary, thou wast illumined with the illumination of the Cross by the divine command of Him Who conversed through the Cross, having crucified thyself unto the world, O laudable one!

Though she was formerly the cause of evil, a deceitful pleasure unto many, yet having shone forth as the sun, the venerable woman appeared as a guide unto all that sin!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast surpassed even the heavenly mind, being the spiritual Heaven of the King of all; for outside the laws of nature, O Pure One, thou didst bear the Lawgiver and Creator of all!

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Why hast Thou cast me off:"*
When the rich man saw Lazarus in the bosom of Abraham, rejoicing in light and glory, he cried aloud: Father Abraham, have mercy on me who am condemned in the fire, and grievously tormented in my tongue!

Thou didst enjoy the riches of life, rejoicing, Abraham said unto the rich man; for this cause art thou tormented here eternally, being in the fire, but Lazarus the poor rejoices in unending joy!

I have become rich through the deceit of life, even as the rich man who spent his whole life in pleasures, O Lover of mankind; but I entreat Thy compassions, that I may be freed from the fire, even as Lazarus was saved.

*Theotokion:*
Having acquired maternal boldness toward thy Son, O All-Pure One, despise not, we pray, a kin-like providence for us; for we Christians offer thee, and thee alone, as an acceptable propitiation unto the Master.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "Of Thy manifestation, O Christ:"*
Moses was glorified of old on Sinai, having mystically seen the back parts of God, depicting an astonishing mystery; and now Mary, having warmly fallen down before the most pure image of the jar that received the Manna, leads an angelic life.

Desiring to see the temple of Thy majesty after the manner of the Psalms, and the spiritual habitation of Thy glory, she who had defiled Thy temple: by the spiritual prayers, O Christ, of Her that was Thy temple without knowing man, make me a temple of the Creative Spirit!

She who with the hook of the flesh caught the eyes of many through fleeting pleasure, making them food for the devil, was herself truly caught by the divine grace of the Precious Cross, having become most sweet food for Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Taught the mystery concerning thee through mystical divine utterances, O All-Pure One, the choir of prophets foretold thee in diverse manners; and Mary, having fallen down before the most pure image of the jar that received the Manna, is now a surety for sinners unto God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Cleanse me, O Savior:"*
The rich man condemned himself to the fiery flame by a life of pleasure; but Lazarus the poor, having chosen poverty in this life, was accounted worthy of unending joy.

Lazarus was accounted worthy of the bosom of Abraham, enjoying eternal life; but the rich man was condemned to the fire, suffering in soul and body.

The rich man is condemned to the fire because of Lazarus; condemn not me, the wretched one, I pray Thee, O Lord Lover of mankind; but make me worthy of Thy light, even as Lazarus!

*Theotokion:*
May we be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy prayers, O pure Mother of God; and may we obtain the divine illumination of the Son of God, Who was ineffably incarnate of thee!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "The lowest abyss:"*
The angelic hosts rejoice, O Mary, beholding in thee a way of life equal to their own, O venerable woman, and they shout glory unto the Lord.

The legions of dark demons are dismayed at the steadfastness of thy patient endurance, seeing how a woman, gloriously naked and all alone, was able to prevail.

Thou hast shone forth like the sun, O all-lauded Mary, and with wonders didst illumine all the wilderness; therefore shine upon me also with thy light!

*Theotokion:*
The Angels, enlightened by the glory of thy Nativity, cried aloud, O Virgin: Peace on earth unto all of us, and good will among men!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Though formerly filled with all manner of defilement, today through repentance she is shown forth as the Bride of Christ; imitating the angelic way of life, she destroys the demons with the weapon of the Cross; for this cause hast thou been revealed as a Bride of the Kingdom, O Mary all-glorious!

*Ikos:*
The ewe-lamb and daughter of Christ let us praise now in hymns, the ever-memorable Mary, who appeared as a lamb from Egypt, and having escaped from all their delusion, she alone was offered to the Church as a perfect growth, striving through abstinence and prayer beyond the measure of human nature; therefore the one Sovereign Ruler exalted the life and deeds of Mary all-glorious!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The Children coming forth from Judea:"*
Even as Job of old lay in a dunghill of worms and filth, so also did Lazarus sit before the gates of the rich man, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Lazarus, rolling of old before the gates of the unmerciful man, desired the crumbs of his table, but no one gave unto him; and instead of these, he found the bosom of Abraham.

Deliver me, I pray, O my Christ, from the portion of the unmerciful rich man; and joining me unto Lazarus the poor, make me worthy to cry unto Thee in thanksgiving: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Having been incarnate from a virginal womb, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore, knowing Thy Mother to be the Theotokos, we cry in thanksgiving: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "The Children in Babylon:"*
The greatest among the fathers, the wise Zosimas, wandering through the wilderness, was accounted worthy to behold the venerable woman; and he cries: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

"Why, O Father, hast thou come to see a strange dwelling of every active virtue?" the venerable woman cried unto the elder; and she cries: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

Having put to death the surges of thy passions, O blessed one, thou hast now sped unto the haven of dispassion, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst conceive ineffably, remaining a virgin, O All-Pure One, and didst bear unto the world salvation, Christ our God; therefore we, all the faithful, magnify thee with hymns!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Sevenfold the furnace:"*
Of old the rich man, the wretched one, was brightly arrayed in scarlet raiment, and fine linen, and purple; but Lazarus the poor lay grievously at his gates, desiring to be filled with the crumbs falling from the table, and no one gave unto him; therefore he reigns together with Christ in glory!

Lazarus lay at the gates of the rich man, rotting in body with sores, and he desired to be satisfied with food, and no one gave unto him; but even the dogs compassionately licked his rot and sores with their tongue; therefore was he accounted worthy of the paradise of delight!

I have grown rich in pleasures, even as the rich man of old, clothing myself every day in purple; and I, O Many-Merciful One, condemn myself unto the sweetness of this life by pleasures and delusions; therefore I pray Thee, O Christ: deliver me from the eternal fire, unto all ages!

*Triadikon:*
The Thrice-Radiant Godhead, the single shining Ray from one three-hypostatic Nature: the Unoriginate Father, the Consubstantial Word with the Father, and the Co-existing, Consubstantial Spirit: bless, ye children; praise in song, ye priests; let the people supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "Be astonished in fear, O heaven:"*
Thou Who searchest the depths of the heart, Who foreknowest all that is ours before being: Thou didst snatch away from the violent life her who fled unto Thee, O Savior, who urgently and unceasingly cries unto Thy love for mankind: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt unto all ages!

O the honorable change, the turning of thyself unto that which is better, O honorable one! O divine love, which hated carnal pleasures! O fervent and divine faith, O all-lauded Mary! Whom we faithfully praise and supremely exalt unto the ages!

Thou hast found the recompense of thy sufferings and the reward of thy labors, O honorable Mary, whereby thou didst cast down the murderous enemy; and now with the Angels thou criest aloud, unceasingly chanting a hymn and supremely exalting Christ unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Renew me entirely for the sake of goodness in thy womb, O Pure One, having not corrupted the properties of His nature, as Master of all the ages; therefore as the cause of our salvation we sing of thee in hymns unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Thee, who knewest not wedlock:"*
Make me poor Lazarus, O Christ, I pray Thee, destroying my desires for pleasures, as Thou art God by nature; and make me rich in virtues, that in faith with hymnody I may magnify Thee!

Rich and unmerciful, in my mind I have despised Thy commandments through lack of faith, O Lover of mankind, being cast down grievously before the gates; but as Thou art compassionate and overflowing in mercy, raise me up, even as of old Thou didst raise Lazarus, Thy friend of four days!

We have all learned well the instructive parable; let all of us who are faithful therefore hate the unmercifulness of the rich man, that we may escape torment and ever rejoice in the bosom of Abraham!

*Theotokion:*
Thee who didst hold in thine arms the invisible God Who is praised in the heavens by all creation, and Who through thee ever grants salvation unto us, we magnify in faith!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos: "Weep not for Me, O Mother:"*
Easily didst thou endure the toil of the wilderness, O mother, being strengthened by the mighty power of Christ; for the foul thoughts that assailed thee, thou didst extinguish by streams of divine tears, O pure one, summit of ascetics, boast of the venerable!

With the most radiant beams the Virgin and Pure One, who alone bore Christ the Light, enlightens thee; she makes thee fearsome unto enemies, O honorable one, and manifests thee clearly unto all, O Mary, beauty of sufferers, confirmation of the venerable!

Having wisely abandoned all earthly things, thou wast shown to be an honorable habitation of the Spirit; pray thou unto Christ, the only Deliverer, that they who faithfully celebrate thy divine memory may be freed from worldly evils!

*Theotokion:*
Having hidden thyself beyond nature from the laws of nature, O Virgin, thou didst bear a new Child upon earth, O Pure One, Who is the Lawgiver and the Ancient of Days, the spiritual Heaven of the Creator of all; therefore with faith and love we call thee blessed!

*Exapostilarion:*
*The Resurrectional Exapostilarion of the Tone.*
*Then for the venerable woman, Podoben: "Thou Who didst adorn heaven with stars" (Нб҃о ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Having thee as an image of repentance, O all-venerable Mary, pray thou unto Christ that in this time of the Fast it may be granted unto us, so that in faith and love we may praise thee in hymns!

*Theotokion:*
O sweetness of the Angels, joy of the afflicted, protectress of Christians, O Virgin Mother of the Lord: intercede for us, and deliver us from eternal torments!

*At the Praises, 4 Resurrectional Stichera and 4 Anatolian Stichera of the Octoechos.*

*Then we say the Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up [Psalm 9:33 LXX].*

*And we sing the Idiomelon, Tone 1:*
The Kingdom of God is not food and drink, but righteousness and abstinence with holiness; therefore neither shall the rich enter into it, but those who place their treasures into the hands of the poor. These things David the prophet also teaches, saying: Righteous is the man that is merciful all the day long, who delights in the Lord...

...and walking in the light he will not stumble; all these things were written for our instruction, that while fasting we may practice kindness, and the Lord will give us heavenly things instead of earthly!

*Glory, the same again.*
*Both now:* Most blessed art thou...
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal. Glory, both now: the Evangelical Sticheron, and the First Hour.*[^1162]

*At the Liturgy:*
*The Beatitudes of the Tone. Prokeimenon according to the Tone of the Apostle.*[^1163]
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 321 from the midpoint [Hebrews 9:11–14]. And for the venerable woman, to the Galatians, Pericope 208 [Galatians 3:23–29]. Alleluia of the Tone.*
*Gospel from Mark, Pericope 37 [Mark 10:32–45]. And for the venerable woman, from Luke, Pericope 33 [Luke 7:36–50]. Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens [Psalm 148:1].*[^1164]
*At the refectory we dine as on previous Sundays.*[^1165]

[Headpiece: Rectangular ornamental woodcut vignette depicting Christ teaching]

## On the Fifth Sunday of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing 4 penitential stichera of the Tone from the Octoechos, and prosomoia of the Triodion:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
Having become poor, O Christ, Thou rich One, Thou didst enrich men with immortality and radiance; therefore, enrich me who have become impoverished by the pleasures of life with virtues, and join me unto Lazarus the poor, delivering me from the torment of the rich man and from the Gehenna that lies before me!

I have become grievously rich in wickedness, and have loved sweetness, and have enjoyed the pleasures of this life, O Lord, and I am condemned to the fire of Gehenna; wasting away in mind from hunger, cast down before the gates of divine deeds, like Lazarus despised, have compassion upon me, O Master!

*Another Sticheron, in the same Tone, Podoben: "A radiant cloud" (Ѻ҆́блакъ тѧ̀):*[^1166]
Eagerly beginning the sixth week of the venerable Fasts, let us, the faithful, offer unto the Lord a prefestal hymn of the Palms, as He comes in glory by the power of His Divinity to Jerusalem to put death to death; let us therefore piously prepare the emblems of victory, the branches of virtues, crying aloud: "Hosanna" unto the Creator of all!

*And 3 stichera of the Menaion.*
*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*
*Entrance: "O Gladsome Light."*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: "Thou hast given an inheritance" [Psalm 60:6 LXX], with its verses (see fol. 175)[^1167].*
*And the rest as usual.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice: Tone 1:*
Marvelous is the Savior's disposition of love toward mankind for our sake: possessing the knowledge of things to come as though present, He set forth the life of Lazarus and of the rich man; contemplating the end of both, let us flee the unmercifulness and inhumanity of the one, and let us emulate the patience and greatness of soul of the other, so that, being comforted with him in the bosom of Abraham, we may cry aloud: O righteous Judge, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Martyrikon:*
By the prayers of all the Saints and of the Theotokos, O Lord, grant us Thy peace, and have mercy on us, as Thou alone art compassionate!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O joy of the heavenly ranks, strong defense of men upon earth, O All-Pure Virgin: save us who flee unto thee, for in thee, after God, have we placed our hope, O Theotokos!

[Headpiece: Rectangular ornamental woodcut vignette depicting Christ enthroned in glory]

## On Monday of Palm Week, at Matins[^1168]

*The Hymns to the Trinity [Triadikoí].*
*After the first stichology of the Psalter, the penitential sessional hymns of the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Thou didst roll away the stone of callousness, O Lord, from my heart; raise up my soul put to death by passions, O Good One, to offer Thee the palms of virtues as unto the Conqueror of Hades; make me worthy of compunction, O Master, that I may obtain eternal life, hymning Thy dominion and Thy compassion, O only Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*Theotokion:*
Beholding the wonder of wonders in thee, O Full of Grace, creation rejoices; for thou didst conceive without seed, and didst bear ineffably Him Whom the chief ranks of Angels cannot behold; pray Him, O Theotokos, for our souls!

*After the third stichology...*

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*[^1169]
Holding the sixth week of the Fast, let us offer unto Christ a prefestal hymn, palms, as He comes for our sake to sit upon the foal of an ass, to subdue the irrationality of the Gentiles as King unto the Father; let us all prepare for Him the branches of virtues, that rejoicing we may behold His Resurrection!

*Glory, the same again. Both now, Theotokion:*
Having stretched forth thy divine hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Creator, O All-Holy Virgin, incarnate in His Divinity, pray that we may be delivered from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love, crying aloud unto thee: Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us by thy Childbirth!

*Triodion, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos: "A song of victory:"*[^1170]
*Troparion:*
Let us put to death the passions by abstinence, and let us bring the spirit to life by divine deeds, that with a pure mind we may behold the precious Passion of Christ!

Emulating the disposition of the unmerciful rich man, I pray Thee, O Compassionate One, Lover of mankind: reckon me with Lazarus the poor, delivering me from the flame and unquenchable fire!

I have fasted from all good deeds, and have taken my fill of transgressions, O Lord; therefore, now satisfy me who hunger with saving and precious food!

*Theotokion:*
O All-Pure Mistress, who didst bear the all-compassionate God: save and have compassion upon me who am condemned in condemnation, cast down by my deeds!

*Another [Canon], Tone 1. Heirmos: "Unto God Who helped:"*
Appropriately keeping the Forefeast of the Flower-bearing feast brightly today, O ye faithful, let us be accounted worthy to behold the life-bearing Passion!

Christ advances and comes unto Jerusalem as King, sitting upon the foal of an ass, to subdue the irrationality of the Gentiles beneath the yoke of the Father!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
O Three-Hypostatic Unity in one Godhead, Unbegotten Father, God, and Only-Begotten Son, and One Holy Spirit, Consubstantial Dominion: save us all!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Most glorious things were spoken concerning thee everywhere, for in the flesh thou didst bear the Creator of all, O Mary Theotokos, unwedded and knowing not wedlock!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Behold, Christ comes unto the village of Bethphage; rejoice, O Bethany, homeland of Lazarus, for unto thee shall He show a great wonder, raising Lazarus from the dead!

*Heirmos:*
Unto God Who helped Moses in Egypt, and thereby drowned Pharaoh with his whole army, let us sing a song of victory, for He hath been glorified!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Whom the Angels and all the hosts tremble before:"*
I, the wretched one, have grown rich in unfruitful thoughts; I have defiled my soul with unlawful pleasures, and the darkness of despair surrounds me; shine upon me, O my God, the radiance of repentance!

Even as of old Thou didst enlighten the man blind from birth, enlighten my soul, O Merciful One, which sees not Thy light, but is buried in the darkness of grievous forgetfulness and worldly cares!

Having fasted, Elijah opened the heavens and watered the thirsty earth with showers; let us fast, drawing streams of tears of the soul, that we may find mercy!

*Theotokion:*
As a living vine, thou didst blossom forth for us the ripe Cluster, O Pure Virgin, gushing forth for us the wine of forgiveness, which drives away the drunkenness of sin!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "A song we bring unto Thee:"*[^1171]
The threshold of the quickening of Lazarus has opened; for Christ comes to raise the dead one as from sleep, to conquer death by life!

Great is thy praise, O Bethany! For thou wast accounted worthy to receive the Creator as a guest, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Triadikon:*
Thee, O Superessential, Threefold Unity, joined in separation by the form of persons, the Father, the Son, and the Divine Spirit, do we glorify!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
With the Angel we address unto thee the "Rejoice," for thou didst raise up the Joy, the Savior of the world; pray Thou Him, O praised Virgin!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
A song we bring unto Thee, for Thou art borne upon a foal, Who in the highest mountest upon the Cherubim, that Thou mayest subdue all men, O Christ, unto Thy dominion!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
A song we bring unto Thee of the bodiless ones, as the Children in the furnace, and singing we say: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "The Life-receiving Spring:"*
I have been put to death by many transgressions of negligence, and I am held fast in the tomb, having the stone of despair lying upon me; breaking it asunder by Thy mercy, O Christ, even as Thou didst raise Lazarus of old, now raise me up!

Let us flee the imitation of the rich man who was condemned to the unquenchable flame; and let us love the patience of Lazarus in his afflictions, so that when we depart from hence, Abraham may receive us into his bosom!

Let us love the fast that slays the passions, and let us diligently attend unto prayers; let us weep, lament, and fervently sigh, that Jesus may comfort us and make us partakers of His Kingdom!

*Theotokion:*
The divine treasure of virginity, the only restoration of our forebears, the spring that gushes forth the abyss of tender-heartedness, the beauty of Jacob, the Theotokos, through whom we are saved, let us hymn in song!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "Thee, the door seen by Ezekiel:"*
Today the sickness of Lazarus is made known unto Christ as He walks on the other side of the Jordan; and as the Foreknower He says: This sickness is not now unto death!

Make ready, O Bethany; adorn thy divine entrances, expand thy thresholds; for behold, the Master comes with the Apostles, quickening His sheep!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
Thee, the Three-Hypostatic Unity and Undivided Essence, Whom the six-winged hosts praise, do we, the human race, magnify in hymns!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thee Who wast timelessly begotten of the Father, and ineffably born in time of the Mother for the salvation of all, as Creator and Master do we magnify in hymns!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
With branches of well-doing and bearing palms of purity, let us all make ready to meet Christ as our God, as He comes unto Jerusalem!

*Heirmos:*
Thee, the door seen by Ezekiel the prophet, through which no one passed except God alone, O Virgin Theotokos, do we honor with hymns!

*It is truly meet: And the Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon, twice: Tone 5:*[^1172]
Condemn me not, O Christ, unto the fire of Gehenna, as the rich man, for the sake of Lazarus; but grant also unto me, who ask with weeping, drops of Thy love for mankind, O God, and have mercy on me!

*Martyrikon:*
Blessed is the army of the Heavenly King: for though the passion-bearers were earth-born, yet they hastened to attain the angelic dignity, disregarding their bodies, and through their sufferings were accounted worthy of the honor of the bodiless ones; by their prayers, O Lord, save our souls!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
O thou who art full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and the cleansing of our many transgressions, we beseech thee!

[Headpiece: Rectangular ornamental woodcut vignette depicting the Holy Mandylion with flanking angels and foliate scrollwork]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*[^1173]
This is, O God, the fearful day, the evening of which we did not expect to reach, and of this day Thou hast accounted us worthy to see in Thy love for mankind: O Trinity, glory to Thee!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 104 [Psalm 104:3 LXX], Tone 6:*
Let the heart of them rejoice that seek the Lord!
*Verse [Psalm 104:1 LXX]:* Give praise to the Lord, and call upon His name!

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Isaiah 48:17–49:4]:*
Thus says the Lord, Who delivered thee, the Holy One of Israel: I am the Lord thy God, Who taught thee that thou shouldst find the way wherein thou shouldst walk. And if thou hadst listened to My commandments, then would thy peace have been as a river, and thy righteousness as a wave of the sea. And thy seed would have been as the sand, and the offspring of thy womb as the dust of the earth; neither now shalt thou be utterly destroyed, nor shall thy name perish before Me. Go forth from Babylon, flee from the Chaldeans; proclaim with a voice of joy, and let this be heard, proclaim it even unto the ends of the earth; say: The Lord has redeemed His servant Jacob! And if they thirst, He will lead them through the wilderness, and bring forth water out of the rock for them; the rock shall be cleft, and the water shall flow, and My people shall drink. There is no joy for the ungodly, says the Lord.
Give ear unto Me, ye islands, and attend, ye nations; after a long time it shall stand, says the Lord; from the womb of My mother He called My name.
