And He made My mouth as a sharp sword, and under the shadow of His hand He hid Me; He made Me as an elect arrow, and in His quiver He concealed Me. And He said unto Me: Thou art My servant, Israel, and in Thee will I be glorified. And I said: In vain have I labored; for naught and for nothing have I given My strength; therefore My judgment is before the Lord, and My labor before My God.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 105 [Psalm 105:48 LXX], Tone 6:*
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel from eternity unto eternity!
*Verse [Psalm 105:1 LXX]:* Give praise to the Lord, for He is good!

[Headpiece: Rectangular ornamental woodcut headpiece band of floral rosettes and vine foliage]

## On Monday of Palm Week, at Vespers[^1174]

*After the usual kathisma of the Psalter.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing the following prosomoia of the triodion, the composition of Master Joseph:*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
By many transgressions and the pleasures of life I, the wretched one, have acquired a sick soul, and I ever lie upon the bed of slothfulness, O Master, and cry unto Thee: O Merciful One, come and visit me, grant me health and mercy, and forsake me not, lest at any time I fall asleep into death, and the enemy rejoice over my destruction, who ever seeks, O Savior, to cast me down into the depth of hades!

Emulating the unmerciful rich man in my madness, I took sweet delight, sinking into pleasures and passions; and knowing that, like Lazarus, my mind ever lies before the gates of repentance, O Lord, I pass by it without feeling, while it suffers from hunger and is covered with the sores of passions; therefore I am subject to the fire of Gehenna, from which deliver me, O Master, Who alone art rich in mercy!

*Another Sticheron, of Master Theodore:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Lord, in the days of Moses" (Гдⷭ҇и, при Мѡѷсе́и):*
Lord, passing in the flesh on the other side of the Jordan, Thou didst foretell that the sickness of Lazarus was not unto death, but to be for Thy glory as our God; glory to the greatness of Thy works, and to Thy sovereign power: for Thou hast overthrown death for the sake of Thy great mercy, O Lover of mankind!

*And of the Menaion [3 stichera].*
*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 106 [Psalm 106:1 LXX], Tone 8:*
Give praise to the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures forever!
*Verse [Psalm 106:2 LXX]:* Let the redeemed of the Lord say so!

*Reading from Genesis [Genesis 27:1–6]:*[^1175]
And it came to pass after Isaac was old, that his eyes were dimmed so that he could not see; and he called Esau his elder son, and said unto him: My son! And he said: Behold, here am I. And Isaac said: Behold, I am old, and I know not the day of my end. Now therefore take thy weapons, thy quiver and thy bow, and go out into the field, and hunt game for me. And make me savory meat, such as I love, and bring it to me, that I may eat; so that my soul may bless thee before I die. And Rebekah heard Isaac speaking to Esau his son. And Esau went to the field to hunt game for his father. And Rebekah spoke unto Jacob...

...unto her younger son, saying: Behold, I heard thy father speaking unto Esau thy brother, saying: Bring me game, and make me savory meat, that I may eat, and bless thee before the Lord, before my death. Now therefore, my son, obey my voice according to what I command thee. Go now to the flock, and fetch me from thence two tender and good kids of the goats; and I will make them savory meat for thy father, such as he loves. And thou shalt bring it to thy father, that he may eat, so that thy father may bless thee before his death. And Jacob said to Rebekah his mother: Behold, Esau my brother is a hairy man, and I am a smooth man. What if my father feel me, and I seem unto him as a deceiver, and I bring a curse upon myself, and not a blessing? And his mother said unto him: Upon me be thy curse, my son; only obey my voice, and go fetch them to me. And he went, and fetched them, and brought them to his mother; and his mother made savory meat, such as his father loved. And Rebekah took the goodly raiment of her eldest son Esau, which was with her in the house, and put it upon Jacob her younger son. And she put the skins of the kids of the goats upon his hands, and upon the smooth of his neck. And she gave the savory meat and the bread, which she had prepared, into the hands of her son Jacob.
And he came unto his father, and said: My father! And he said: Here am I; who art thou, my son? And Jacob said unto his father: I am Esau thy firstborn; I have done according as thou badest me: arise, sit and eat of my game, that thy soul may bless me. And Isaac said unto his son: How is it that thou hast found it so quickly, my son? And he said: Because the Lord thy God brought it before me. And Isaac said unto Jacob: Come near, I pray thee, that I may feel thee, my son, whether thou be my son Esau or not. And Jacob went near unto Isaac his father; and he felt him, and said: The voice indeed is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau. And he discerned him not, because his hands were hairy, as his brother Esau's hands: so he blessed him. And he said: Art thou my son Esau? And he said: I am. And he said: Bring it near to me, and I will eat of my son's game, that my soul may bless thee. And he brought it near to him, and he did eat; and he brought him wine, and he drank. And his father Isaac said unto him: Come near now, and kiss me, my son. And he came near, and kissed him; and he smelled the smell of his raiment, and blessed him, and said: See, the smell of my son is as the smell of a full field which the Lord hath blessed! And may God give thee of the dew of heaven, and of the fatness of the earth, and plenty of corn and wine; and let nations serve thee, and princes bow down to thee; be lord over thy brother, and let thy father's sons bow down to thee: cursed be everyone that curses thee, and blessed be he that blesses thee!
And it came to pass, as soon as Isaac had made an end of blessing Jacob his son, and Jacob was scarcely gone out from the presence of Isaac his father, that Esau his brother came in from his hunting. And he also had made savory meat, and brought it unto his father, and said unto his father: Let my father arise, and eat of his son's game, that thy soul may bless me. And Isaac his father said unto him: Who art thou? And he said: I am thy son, thy firstborn, Esau. And Isaac trembled with an exceedingly great trembling, and said: Who then was he that took game and brought it to me, and I have eaten of all before thou camest, and have blessed him? Yea, and he shall be blessed! And when Esau heard the words of his father Isaac, he cried with a great and exceeding bitter cry, and said: Bless me, even me also, O my father! And he said: Thy brother came with subtilty, and hath taken away thy blessing. And Esau said: Is he not rightly named Jacob? For he hath supplanted me these two times: he took away my birthright, and, behold, now he hath taken away my blessing. And Esau said unto his father: Hast thou not reserved a blessing for me, O my father? And Isaac answered and said unto Esau: Behold, I have made him thy master, and all his brethren have I given to him for servants; and with corn and wine have I sustained him: and what shall I do now unto thee, my son? And Esau said unto his father: Hast thou but one blessing, my father? Bless me, even me also, O my father! And Isaac was moved with compassion, and Esau lifted up his voice, and wept. And Isaac his father answered and said unto him: Behold...

...of the fatness of the earth shall be thy dwelling, and of the dew of heaven from above; and by thy sword shalt thou live, and shalt serve thy brother; and it shall come to pass when thou shalt cast down and break loose his yoke from thy neck. And Esau hated Jacob because of the blessing wherewith his father blessed him.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 56 [Psalm 56:6 LXX], Tone 7:*
Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory above all the earth!
*Verse [Psalm 56:8 LXX]:* My heart is ready, O God, my heart is ready!

*Reading from Proverbs [Proverbs 19:16–25]:*[^1176]
He that keeps the commandment keeps his soul; but he that despises his ways shall die. He that has mercy on the poor lends unto the Lord, and according to his gift it shall be repaid unto him. Chasten thy son, for so shall he be of good hope, and let not thy soul be stirred up unto reproach. A man of great wrath shall suffer punishment; and if he do harm, he shall add yet more to it. Listen, my son, to the instruction of thy father, that thou mayest be wise in thy latter end. There are many devices in the heart of a man; but the counsel of the Lord endures forever. The fruit of a man is his mercy, and a poor righteous man is better than a rich liar. The fear of the Lord is unto life for a man; but he that is without fear shall abide in places where there is no discretion. He that hides his hands in his bosom unjustly will not so much as bring them to his mouth. When a pestilent man is scourged, a simple man becomes more prudent; and if thou reprove a man of understanding, he will understand knowledge.

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice: Tone 8:*
Deliver us, O Savior, from soul-corrupting greed, and number us with Lazarus the poor in the bosom of Abraham: for being rich in mercies, of Thine own will Thou didst become poor for our sake, and didst lead us from corruption unto incorruption, as Thou art a merciful God and Lover of mankind!

*Martyrikon:*
Thou Who receivest the endurance of the holy martyrs, receive also from us our hymnody, O Lover of mankind, and through their prayers grant us great mercy!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*[^1177]
[Deliver us from our distresses, O Mother of Christ] God, who didst bear the Creator of all, that we all may cry [unto thee: Rejoice, the only protection of] our souls!

[Headpiece: Rectangular ornamental woodcut headpiece band of scrolling foliate vines, rosettes, and clusters of grapes]

## On Tuesday of Palm Week, at Matins[^1178]

*The Hymns to the Trinity [Triadikoí] of the Tone.*
*After the first stichology of the Psalter, the penitential sessional hymns of the Octoechos, 2, with their Theotokion.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, of Master Joseph:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ):*
Wasting away through the disease of sin, I lie prostrate upon the bed of despair; therefore, O Physician of the sick, visit me in Thy love for mankind, and suffer me not to fall asleep grievously unto death, O All-Compassionate One, that I may fervently cry unto Thee: O Giver of mercy, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, the same again. Both now: Theotokion:*
The beginning of salvation was Gabriel's utterance unto the Virgin; for hearing the "Rejoice," she fled not from the greeting, nor did she doubt like Sarah in the tent, but spoke thus: Behold the handmaid of the Lord, be it unto me according to thy word!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn of Master Theodore:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Today shines forth" (Сїѧ́етъ дне́сь):*
Yesterday and today is the sickness of Lazarus, for his kinswomen make this known unto Christ; with joy prepare thyself, O Bethany, to entertain the Giver of life and King, crying aloud with us: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, the same again. Both now: Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
O All-Holy Mother of God, rampart of Christians, deliver thy people as is thy wont, who earnestly cry unto thee; withstand the vile and haughty thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Ever-Virgin!

*Canon of the Menaion, and the Triodion of Master Joseph. And the biblical ode is recited.*[^1179]
*Ode 2, Tone 6. Heirmos:*
See, see, that I am God, Who rained down manna, and gushed forth water from the rock of old in the wilderness for My people, by My right hand alone and by My strength!

*Troparion:*
Voluntarily didst Thou become poor, O Christ, Who art rich by nature; Thou didst hunger, having fasted, Who fillest every living thing; therefore satisfy me who hunger for Thy grace, and show me to be a partaker of the banquet there, O Word!

Show me to be Lazarus who is poor in sins, O Christ, and scatter the wealth that I have evilly gathered, and fulfilling Thy perfect love, O Compassionate One, deliver me from the fearful torment there!

Fasting of old sharpened the Children in Babylon to be stronger than the fire; therefore, having emulated them, O my soul, despond not, and thou shalt quench the fire of lusts by the dew of the Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
"Rejoice!" we cry unto thee who didst bear the Joy, O Virgin Theotokos, full of grace: pray unto God Whom thou didst bear, that they who ever praise thee may be delivered from tribulations and corruption!

*Another [Canon], of Master Theodore:*
*Tone 5. Heirmos: "See, see:"*
See, see, that I am God, walking on the other side of the Jordan, hearing that Lazarus is sick, and saying: He shall not die, but this is for My glory!

Enveloped in lamentation, the sisters of Lazarus make known his affliction unto Thee, the Knower of all; but Thou abidest a little while, that Thou mayest raise up a wonder, and show fearful things unto Thy disciples!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
O Consubstantial, Three-Hypostatic Unity, Dominion and Kingdom of the ages: the multitude of Angels and all human nature glorify Thee, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who would not marvel, beholding the Creator in thee, O Virgin, Who restored fallen Adam, born of thee in the flesh through an ineffable union, without change, for our salvation?

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Now adorn thyself with joy, O Bethany, having made a receptacle for the King of all, for He comes unto thee to show Lazarus once more running from corruption into life!

*Heirmos:*
See, see, that I am God, clothed in flesh of Mine own will, that I might save Adam from deceit, who fell into transgression through the serpent!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "From the flame didst Thou make dew gush forth for the venerable ones:"*
I contemplate the multitude of my transgressions, and wounded by the sting of conscience, I, the wretched one, cry out as in a flame: Have mercy upon me, O Word of God, in Thy compassion!

Having despised the virtues of Lazarus, I envied the disposition of the unmerciful rich man: O tender-hearted God, have compassion upon me who have become impoverished, that I may glorify Thee unto all ages!

Held fast by sickness of soul, and about to die through despair, I have need of Thy visitation, O Jesus, Who hast given life unto them that call upon Thee!

*Theotokion:*
O All-Holy Virgin, save me; be an aid unto my weakness, who didst bear the Mercy of the Father, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "Thy venerable children:"*
Rejoice, O Bethany, homeland of Lazarus: for Christ, coming unto thee, shall accomplish a great deed, having quickened Lazarus!

Lazarus is sick, that Thou mayest be glorified in him, O Son of God; and all the works of the Lord sing of Thee unceasingly!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Triadikon:*
Hymning the All-Holy Trinity in the Father, and the Son, and the Spirit, we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
We all hymn thine ineffable Childbirth, O Pure One, and honoring Him as God, all ye works, we bless Him as Creator and Lord!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Preparing branches of virtues to meet Christ, now we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
Thy venerable children in the furnace, chanting, said, O Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "It is not possible for men to see God:"*[^1180]
Of old the bitter eating grievously drove the first-created man out of paradise, and bound him in the snares of death: fast, O my soul, flee imitation, flee the enjoyment of grievous lust!

I consider Thy judgment seat, and the unquenchable flame, O most righteous Judge, and I condemn myself before the judgment; for I am dismayed and tremble, having transgressed against Thee immeasurably, as no other man on earth; therefore I pray Thee: Have compassion upon me!

Let us wash away the darkness of the passions by enlightening prayers, and bearing branches of virtues, let us hasten to meet Christ, Who is now expected to sit upon a foal, preparing to suffer, that He may save us!

*Theotokion:*
From thee, O Virgin full of the grace of God, unto them that are held fast in the night of passions, Christ the Giver of light and peace shone forth, loosing the transgression of the curse, and manifestly granting us deliverance!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah:"*
Now prepare the things of the dead and of the tomb, O wise Lazarus; for having departed from this life, being about to die tomorrow, behold the tomb wherein thou shalt dwell: yet Christ shall quicken thee again, raising thee up on the fourth day!

Rejoice, O Bethany, for Christ comes unto thee, working a great and fearful wonder; having bound death, He shall in every way, as God, raise up dead Lazarus, who magnifies the Creator!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
I hymn Thee as One Essence, O Unoriginate Trinity, honorable, life-originating, indivisible Unity: Unbegotten Father, and Begotten Word and Son, and Holy Spirit: save us who sing of Thee!

*Both now:*

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is thy childbirth, O Mother of God, for without a man was the conception within thee, and virginal was the birth; for He Who was born is God, Whom magnifying, we call thee blessed, O Virgin!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Come, let us prepare for the meeting of the Lord, carrying unto Him the branches of virtues; for thus shall we receive Him, as in the city of Jerusalem, into our soul, worshiping and singing praises unto Him!

*Heirmos:*
Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin was with child, and brought forth a Son, Emmanuel, God and man, Dayspring is His name; Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed!

*Exapostilarion.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 5:*[^1181]
Having stumbled through transgressions and bound with the cords of sins, O my soul, why dost thou despair? Why art thou slothful? Flee ever, like Lot, from the conflagration of the impurity of Sodom and Gomorrah; turn not back, lest thou become as a pillar of salt; save thyself upon the mountain of virtues; flee ever from the unmerciful rich man's burning heartlessness; pass over into the bosom of Abraham like Lazarus, and in humility of mind cry aloud: My hope and my refuge, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*For the Martyrs:*
Thy Passion-bearers, O Lord, having emulated the Angelic ranks, endured torments as though bodiless, having unanimous hope, the enjoyment of promised good things; by their supplications, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and unto our souls great mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Unto thee do we pray, as unto the Mother of God, O blessed one: pray for the salvation of our souls!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 5:*[^1182]
An invincible rampart hast Thou given unto our city, the Virgin who gave Thee birth; by her, O Savior, deliver, we beseech Thee, our souls from the surrounding evils!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 108, Tone 4:*
Help me, O Lord my God, and save me according to Thy mercy.
*Verse: O God, pass not over my praise in silence.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (49:6–10):*[^1183]
Thus saith the Lord: Behold, I have given Thee for a covenant of the race, for a light of the Gentiles, that Thou mayest be for salvation unto the end of the earth. Thus saith the Lord, the God of Israel that delivered Thee: Sanctify Him that despises His own life, Him that is abominable unto the nations, the servant of princes; kings shall behold Him, and princes shall arise, and shall worship Him, for the Lord's sake, because the Holy One of Israel is faithful, and I have chosen Thee. Thus saith the Lord: In an acceptable time have I heard Thee, and in a day of salvation have I helped Thee, and formed Thee; and I have given Thee for an everlasting covenant of the nations, to establish the earth, and to inherit the desolate heritages; saying to them that are in bonds: Go forth! and to them that are in darkness: Show yourselves! They shall feed along all the ways, and their pasture shall be in all paths. They shall not hunger, neither shall they thirst, neither shall heat nor sun smite them; but He that hath mercy on them shall comfort them.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 109, Tone 4:*
Thou art a Priest forever after the order of Melchizedek.
*Verse: The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand.*

## On Tuesday of Palm Week, at Vespers

*The usual stichology.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing the following prosomoia stichera of Lord Joseph:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^1184]
Fainting through all the assaults of the evil one, my much-suffering heart is grievously brought down to the grave of despondency, and is covered with insensibility as with a stone; O Savior, Who by the Wood of Thy life-bearing Cross didst quicken all that were in hades, raise me up, make me alive, that with fear I may glorify Thy Divinity!

Through the operation of the evil one, I ever loved the wealth of corruptive pleasures, vainly rejoicing, senseless that I am; and I disregarded my mind, as another Lazarus, groaning and hungering for divine meats, O Word; deliver me from the coming flame through Thy tender compassion, that I may glorify Thee, the Lover of mankind!

*Another [Sticheron] by Lord Theodore:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "They weighed thirty pieces of silver" (Поста́виша):*[^1185]
Today Lazarus hath breathed his last, and Bethany weeps for him, whom Thou, O our Savior, wilt raise from the dead, fore-assuring Thy friends of Thine awesome Resurrection, the slaying of hades, and the quickening of Adam; therefore do we sing praises unto Thee!

*And 3 from the Menaion.*

*And 3 from the Menaion.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 110, Tone 4:*
The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom.
*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart.*

*Reading from Genesis (31:3–16):*[^1186]
The Lord said unto Jacob: Return into the land of thy father, and to thy kindred, and I will be with thee. And Jacob sent and called Leah and Rachel to the field where the flocks were. And he said unto them: I see your father's face, that it is not toward me as yesterday and the third day; but the God of my father hath been with me. And ye yourselves know that with all my power I have served your father. And your father hath wronged me, and changed my wages of ten lambs; but God suffered him not to do me evil. If he said thus: The speckled shall be thy wages; then all the sheep bare speckled; and if he said: The white shall be thy wages; then all the sheep bare white. And God took away all the cattle of your father, and gave them to me. And it came to pass, when the sheep conceived in the womb, that I saw with mine eyes in a dream, and, behold, the rams and the he-goats that leaped upon the sheep and goats were streaked, speckled, and ash-colored spotted. And the Angel of God spake unto me in a dream, saying: Jacob! And I said: What is it? And He said: Lift up thine eyes, and behold the rams and the he-goats that leap upon the sheep and the goats, white, speckled, and ash-colored spotted; for I have seen all that Laban does unto thee. I am the God Who appeared unto thee in the place of God, where thou anointedst a pillar for Me, and where thou vowedst a vow unto Me; now therefore arise, and get thee out from this land, and go into the land of thy nativity, and I will be with thee. And Rachel and Leah answered and said unto him: Is there yet any portion or inheritance for us in our father's house? Were we not counted as strangers unto him? For he hath sold us, and hath quite devoured our silver. All the riches and the glory which God hath taken from our father, that shall be ours, and our children's; now then, whatsoever God hath said unto thee, do!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 111, Tone 4:*
Merciful, and compassionate, and righteous.
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.*

*Reading from Proverbs (21:3–11):*[^1187]
My son, to do what is righteous and to speak the truth is more pleasing to God than the blood of sacrifices. A haughty man is insolent of heart in his reproach; and the lamp of the wicked is sin. The thoughts of the diligent tend to abundance, but every slothful man is in want. He that gathers treasures with a lying tongue pursus vanity into the snares of death. Destruction shall fall upon the ungodly, for they refuse to do what is right. Unto the crooked God sends crooked paths, for pure and right are His works. It is better to dwell in a corner of an unroofed place than in plastered rooms with unrighteousness, and in a shared house. The soul of the wicked shall find no mercy from any man. When the intemperate man is penalized, the simple becomes wiser; and when the wise man is instructed...

...he shall receive understanding. The righteous man understands the hearts of the ungodly, and despises the ungodly in their evils. Whoso stops his ears from hearing the weak, he also shall cry, and there shall be none to hear him. A secret gift turns away anger, but he that is sparing of gifts stirs up strong wrath. It is joy to the righteous to do judgment, but the holy man is an abomination unto evildoers. A man that wanders out of the way of righteousness shall rest in the congregation of the giants. A needy man loves mirth, and he that loves wine and oil shall not be rich. The lawless man is a ransom for the righteous. It is better to dwell in a wilderness than with a contentious, talkative, and passionate woman. A desirable treasure rests in the mouth of the wise, but foolish men swallow it down. The way of righteousness and mercy finds life and glory.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 1:*[^1188]
Clothed in the divinely woven purple robe of dominion and in the scarlet mantle of incorruption, O my soul, thou hast disgraced thy dignity, turning wealth and enjoyment into sin, and despising thy fellow kinsman, even as the rich man despised poor Lazarus; but lest thou be tormented with him, become poor in spirit, and cry aloud unto the Lord Who became poor for thy sake: O Christ, Who before Thy crucifixion wast arrayed in the purple robe of mockery, and for my sake wast crucified naked upon the Cross, by the vesture of Thy Kingdom deliver me from eternal shame!

*For the Martyrs:*
O how fair is your barter, O holy ones! For ye gave your blood, and inherited the heavens; and having been tested for a time, ye rejoice eternally. Truly fair is your barter! For leaving behind corruptible things, ye received incorruptible, and dancing with the Angels, ye unceasingly sing praises unto the Consubstantial Trinity!

*Glory... Both now... Cross-Theotokion:*
*Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*
A sword hath pierced, O my Son, the Virgin said, when she beheld Christ hanging upon the Tree, and it rends my heart, O Master, even as Simeon foretold unto me of old; but arise, and glorify together with Thyself, O Immortal One, Thy Mother and handmaid, I pray Thee!

## On Wednesday of Palm Week, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the tone.*

*After the first stichology, two Cross Sessional Hymns from the Octoechos, with their Cross-Theotokion.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "The stone of the tomb" (Ка́мень гро́бный):*[^1189]
Weighed down by many sins as with a stone, I lie in the tomb of negligence, O Compassionate One; raise me up from thence, O tender-hearted Lord!

*Cross-Theotokion, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїа [сꙋ́щи]):*
Preserved by the precious Cross of thy Son, O pure Lady Theotokos, we all easily repel every assault of the slanderer; therefore, as is our duty, we glorify thee as Mother of the Light and the only hope of our souls!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^1190]
Today Lazarus, having died, is buried, and his kinsfolk sing a lamentation; as the Foreseer and God, Thou didst foretell the event, saying unto the disciples: Lazarus is fallen asleep, but now I go to raise up him whom I created; therefore we all cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to the dominion of Thy might! *(Twice)*

*Cross-Theotokion:*
The pure one who knew not wedlock, Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee hanging dead upon the Tree, weeping maternally, said: What hath the lawless, ungrateful assembly of the Jews repaid Thee, having enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I praise Thy divine condescension!

*Canon from the Menaion, and the Triodion by Lord Joseph:*
*Tone 2. Ode 3. Heirmos: "My barren mind:"*[^1191]
*Troparion:*
Having strengthened by the power of Thy Cross my mind, which hath grown faint through the assaults of the evil one, direct it unto Thy will, O Lord!

Raise me up, O Lord, who sleep upon the bed of pleasures in the slumber of despondency, and show me forth as an adorer of Thy holy things!

Illumined in our souls, purified by fasting, let us go forth to meet Christ as He comes in the flesh unto Jerusalem!

*Theotokion:*
O Pure One, who wast not consumed by the fire of the Godhead: burn up my material impulses of the passions, that with faith, O Pure One, I may glorify thee!

*Another [Triodion by Lord Theodore], Tone 3. Heirmos: "O barren soul:"*
*Troparion:*
Today Lazarus, having died, is not hidden from the all-seeing eye of Jesus; therefore He declares this unto His disciples, crying aloud: Lazarus our friend is fallen asleep; I go to raise him up!

Having said that Thou wouldst go again into Judea, O Lord, Thou didst make the disciples afraid; but Thomas cried aloud with boldness: He is Life! Let us go, and even if we die, we shall straightway live!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
Consubstantial Trinity, Almighty Father, co-unoriginate Son, with the enthroned divine Spirit, one worship, uncreated Godhead, we all, being mortal men, sing praises unto Thee!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Virgin Mother all-pure, root of Jesse, from whom Christ shone forth, the life-bearing blossom of earthborn men, whereby we were delivered from death, we all sing praises unto thee, O Pure One!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Rejoice, O Zion! Now thy King comes meek, as the prophet cried aloud; and the foal bears Him in the flesh Who upholds all things with His hand; we sing of His dominion!

*Heirmos:*
O barren and childless soul, acquire glorious fruit, and rejoicing cry aloud: I am established through Thee, O God; there is none holy, there is none righteous, save Thee, O Lord!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto Moses in the bush:"*
*Troparion:*
Roll away, O Christ, the heavy stone of grievous despondency from my humble soul, and raise me up from the tomb of insensibility unto Thy glory, O Word!

The death of Thy friend didst Thou foretell unto Thy friends according to the compassion of Thy mercy, O Foreseer and God; whom Thou wast about to raise up on the fourth day from the tomb unto Thy glorification!

Again Thou comest into Judea, O Christ, while they seek to kill Thee, the Tree of life, by a tree, desiring to make immortal them that were slain of old through eating!

*Theotokion:*
In thee, O Pure Virgin, were seen all the goings of God, Who preserved thy purity after childbirth sealed firmly unto all ages!

*Another [Triodion], Ode 8. Heirmos: "Begotten of the unoriginate Father:"*[^1192]
*Troparion:*
Today Lazarus, having died, is carried out for burial, the friend of Christ, and all that are with Mary and Martha weep; and Christ comes unto him with joy, to show unto the people that He is the Life of all!

Cutting branches of virtues as from the Mount of Olives, from the heights of almsgiving, come, O ye people, let us mentally prepare the coming of Christ unto us, singing, blessing Him, and supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Triadikon:*
O Three-Hypostatic Unity, Father, Son, and living Spirit, one Godhead, one Kingdom, the angelic hosts of unsetting light praise Thee, and we of the earth sing, bless, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Behold, all generations call thee blessed, O All-Pure One, beholding thy great deeds; for beyond nature thou didst bring forth the Creator of all, Who is God and man; therefore we bless and supremely exalt thee unto the ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
With the children let us also meet Christ our God, offering almsgiving instead of palms unto hearty prayer, crying aloud with branches: Hosanna! Bless and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*

*Heirmos:*
Him Who was begotten of the unoriginate Father before the ages as God, and in these latter times was robed in the flesh from the Virgin, as perfect man and true God: praise, bless, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Who among the earthborn:"*
*Troparion:*
Fenced by fasting, Daniel stopped the mouths of the beasts; emulating him, O my soul, drive away by the help of the Cross the serpent that roars like a lion and seeks to make every soul his food!

O Word of God, having raised by Thy life-giving word my soul, which hath been put to death by sins and shut up in the tomb of transgression, account me worthy to offer unto Thee, the Conqueror of death, the branches of virtues!

Having heaven as a throne and the earth as a footstool, the Word of God the Father, borne upon a foal, comes into the Holy City, to perfect out of the mouths of babes fair praise, as King of all!

*Theotokion:*
Among women thou alone wast adorned, O marvelous Pure One, having brought forth the most fair Word, Who appeared fairer than all men; therefore entreat Him, O Virgin, to cleanse the defilement of my heart!

*Another [Triodion], Ode 9. Heirmos: "Upon Mount Sinai:"*[^1193]
*Troparion:*
Lazarus' sisters pour forth funeral tears, beholding their kinsman today under the bitter stone; but my Christ, declaring this from afar, said unto His apostles: I rejoice for your sakes, for I was not there in the flesh!

Again Christ comes unto thee, O Judea, murderer of men, desiring as God to fulfill the saving Passion; Whom thou didst seek to kill with stones, behold, He comes unto thee willingly, of His own accord, unto thy slaughter, in order to save us!

*Glory: Triadikon:*
The Godhead is a Monad and a Trinity; O most glorious mystery! Being united in essence, It is divided by Persons, specifically; for being indivisible, It is divided, and being one, It is threefold; This is the Father, the Son, and the living Spirit, Which preserves all things!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who hath heard of a childbearing Virgin, and a Mother without a husband? O Mary, thou workest a wonder! But tell me, how? Search not into the depth of my giving birth unto God; this is most true, beyond the comprehension of human mind!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Having mentally plaited palm branches of the date palms, let us visibly make a meeting for the King as He comes unto us: for blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord and Father, as the true Son!

*Heirmos:*
Upon Mount Sinai Moses beheld thee in the bush, who didst unconsumed conceive the fire of the Godhead in thy womb; and Daniel beheld thee as the unhewn mountain; Isaiah cried aloud: A rod sprouted from the root of Jesse!

*Exapostilarion of the tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 1:*[^1194]
Israel was robed in purple and fine scarlet, shining with priestly and royal vesture, being rich in the Law and the Prophets, and rejoicing in the legal services; but having crucified outside the gates Thee, the Benefactor, Who becamest poor, and having denied Thee Who art alive after the crucifixion in the bosom of God the eternal Father: he now thirsts for a drop of grace, even as the unmerciful rich man thirsted for water from poor Lazarus; who, though once robed in purple and scarlet, hath entered into the unquenchable fire, and seeing is in anguish that the Gentile peoples, formerly destitute of even drops of truth, are now warmed in the bosom of Abraham's faith, wearing the purple robe of Thy blood with the scarlet mantle of baptism, renewed and taking delight in Thy gifts, and crying: O Christ our God, glory to Thee!

*For the Martyrs:*
Neither tribulation, nor distress, nor famine, nor persecution, nor wounds, nor the fury of wild beasts, nor the sword, nor threatening fire were able to separate you, O all-lauded martyrs, from God; but rather through love for Him, having contended as though in stranger bodies, ye forgot nature, despising death; therefore worthily according to your pains ye received your reward and became inheritors of the Heavenly Kingdom; pray ye for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Cross-Theotokion:*
*Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃еницы):*
Beholding Thee hanging, O Christ, Who didst hang the earth upon the waters, O Lover of mankind, the pure one who gave Thee birth cried aloud: Woe is me! What strange sight is this? How hath Thine immeasurable beauty set, O sweetest Son? I magnify Thy mercy, for of Thine own will Thou sufferest for all!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 5:*[^1195]
Deal Thy mercies with us, O Lord, and deliver us not up to our iniquities, O holy Master Almighty, we pray Thee!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 112, Tone 1:*
Praise the Lord, O ye servants, praise the name of the Lord.
*Verse: Blessed be the name of the Lord from henceforth and for evermore.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (58:1–11):*[^1196]
Thus saith the Lord: Cry aloud with strength, and spare not; lift up thy voice like a trumpet, and declare unto My people their sins, and to the house of Jacob their iniquities. They seek Me day by day, and desire to know My ways, as a people that had done righteousness, and had not forsaken the judgment of their God; they ask of Me now righteous judgment, and desire to draw near to God, saying: Why have we fasted, and Thou sawest not? Why have we humbled our souls, and Thou tookest no knowledge? For in the days of your fasts ye find your own pleasures, and afflict all that are under your hand. If ye fast for strifes and debates, and strike the humble with fists, why fast ye unto Me as today, that your voice may be heard in crying aloud? I have not chosen such a fast, nor a day for a man to humble his soul; not even if thou bow down thy neck as a ring, and spread sackcloth and ashes under thee, not even so shall ye call it an acceptable fast. Not such a fast have I chosen, saith the Lord; but loose every bond of iniquity, untie the knots of forced obligations, let the oppressed go free, and tear in pieces every unrighteous bond! Break thy bread to the hungry, and bring the poor that have no shelter into thy house; if thou seest the naked, cover him, and hide not thyself from the kinsfolk of thine own seed. Then shall thy light break forth early in the morning, and thy healing shall spring forth speedily; and thy righteousness shall go before thee, and the glory of God shall encompass thee, O man. Then shalt thou cry, and God shall hear thee, His servant; while thou art yet speaking, He shall say: Behold, I am here! If thou take away from thee the yoke, and the pointing of the hand, and the word of murmuring; and if thou give bread from thy soul to the hungry, and satisfy the afflicted soul; then shall thy light rise in obscurity, and thy darkness shall be as the noonday, and thy God shall be with thee continually.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 113, Tone 6:*
Blessed are ye of the Lord, Who made heaven and earth.
*Verse: The heaven of heavens is the Lord's, but the earth hath He given to the sons of men.*

## On Wednesday of Palm Week, at Vespers

*The usual stichology.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing the present Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 5:*[^1197]
Being rich in passions, I am wrapped in the deceitful garment of hypocrisy, rejoicing in the evils of intemperance, and displaying boundless pitilessness, despising my mind, which is cast down before the doors of repentance, hungering for every good thing, and sick through my negligence; but do Thou, O Lord, make me a Lazarus, poor in sins, lest in my want I fail to receive a finger moistening my aching tongue in the unquenchable fire, but establish me in the bosom of Patriarch Abraham, as Lover of mankind!

*For the Martyrs:*
With the insatiable love of your soul ye denied not Christ, O holy martyrs, who, having endured diverse wounds of passions, cast down the audacity of the tormentors; having preserved your faith unbending and unharmed, ye were translated unto heaven; therefore, having obtained boldness before Him, pray that He grant us great mercy!

*Other Stichera by Lord Theodore:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*[^1198]
Walking in the flesh on the other side of the Jordan, O Jesus, Thou didst cry unto them that were with Thee: Our friend Lazarus is already dead, and is now committed unto burial; therefore I rejoice for your sakes, O My friends, that ye may know that I know all things, being unchangeable God, even though I appear as a visible man; let us go therefore to bring him to life, that death may feel its defeat and utter destruction, which I shall openly bring to pass, granting great mercy unto the world!

Emulating Martha and Mary, O ye faithful, let us send divine deeds as prayers unto the Lord, that coming He may raise our mind, which lies dead and insensible in the tomb of slothfulness, feeling no divine fear whatsoever, and having now no living operations, crying: Behold, O Lord, and as of old by Thine awesome presence Thou didst raise Thy friend Lazarus, O Compassionate One, so quicken us all, granting great mercy!

*Another Sticheron by Lord Theodore:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "O Lord, standing at the tomb" (Гдⷭ҇и на гро́бѣ):*[^1199]
Lazarus passes his second day in the tomb; he sees them that have died from the ages; there he beholds strange terrors, an innumerable multitude held by the bonds of hades; therefore his kinsfolk weep bitterly, looking upon his grave; but Christ comes to raise His friend, to accomplish one harmony among all: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!

*And 4 in the Menaion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 114, Tone 6:*
I will please the Lord in the land of the living.
*Verse: I have loved, because the Lord will hear the voice of my supplication.*

*Reading from Genesis (43:26–31; 45:1):*[^1200]
His brethren brought Joseph the gifts which they had in their hands into the house, and bowed themselves before him with their faces to the earth. And he asked them: Are ye in health? And he said unto them: Is your father, the old man of whom ye spake, in health? Is he yet alive? And they said: Thy servant our father is in health, he is yet alive; and he said: Blessed be that man of God; and they stooped down and worshiped him. And Joseph lifted up his eyes, and saw Benjamin his brother of the same mother, and said: Is this your younger brother, whom ye said ye would bring unto me? And he said: God have mercy on thee, my child. And Joseph was troubled, for his bowels yearned over his brother, and he sought to weep; and he entered into his chamber, and wept there. And he washed his face, and went out, and refrained himself. And Joseph could not refrain himself before all that stood by him, but said: Send out everyone from me; and there stood no man...

...with Joseph, while he made himself known unto his brethren. And he wept aloud, and all the Egyptians heard, and it was heard in Pharaoh's house. And Joseph said unto his brethren: I am Joseph; doth my father yet live? And his brethren could not answer him, for they were troubled. And Joseph said unto his brethren: Come near to me; and they came near. And he said: I am Joseph your brother, whom ye sold into Egypt. Now therefore be not grieved, nor let it seem harsh unto you, that ye sold me hither: for God did send me before you for life. For these two years hath the famine been on the earth, and there are yet five years remaining, in which there shall neither be plowing nor harvest. For God sent me before you to leave you a remnant on the earth, and to nourish your great remnant. Now therefore it was not you that sent me hither, but God: and He hath made me as a father to Pharaoh, and lord of all his house, and ruler over all the land of Egypt. Haste ye therefore, and go up to my father, and say unto him: Thus saith thy son Joseph: God hath made me lord of all the land of Egypt; come down therefore unto me, and tarry not; and thou shalt dwell in the land of Goshen of Arabia, and thou shalt be near me, thou, and thy sons, and thy sons' sons, thy sheep, and thy oxen, and all that thou hast; and I will nourish thee there, for yet five years the famine shall be on the earth, lest thou perish, and thy sons, and all thy possessions. Behold, your eyes see, and the eyes of my brother Benjamin, that it is my mouth that speaks unto you. Announce therefore unto my father all my glory that is in Egypt, and all that ye have seen; and haste ye, and bring down my father hither. And falling upon the neck of his brother Benjamin, he wept over him, and Benjamin wept upon his neck. And kissing all his brethren, he wept over them; and after this his brethren talked with him. And the report was carried into Pharaoh's house, saying: Joseph's brethren are come; and Pharaoh rejoiced, and his servants.[^1201]

*Prokimenon, Psalm 115, Tone 6:*
I will pay my vows unto the Lord in the presence of all His people.[^1202]
*Verse: I believed, therefore have I spoken; but I was brought exceedingly low.*

*Reading from Proverbs (21:23–31; 22:1–4):*[^1203]
Whoso keeps his mouth and his tongue, keeps his soul from trouble. The bold and arrogant and boastful man is called a pestilence; and he that remembers injuries is a transgressor. The desires of the sluggard kill him, for his hands refuse to do anything. The ungodly man desires evil desires all the day long; but the righteous shows mercy and is generous without sparing. The sacrifices of the ungodly are an abomination to the Lord, for they offer them unlawfully. A false witness shall perish, but an obedient man shall speak with preservation. An ungodly man stands impudently with his face; but the upright man himself discerns his ways. There is no wisdom, there is no courage, there is no counsel with the ungodly. The horse is prepared for the day of battle, but help is from the Lord. A good name is better than much riches, and good favor rather than silver and gold. The rich and poor meet together; the Lord hath made them both. A prudent man, seeing the wicked punished severely, is himself chastened; but the foolish pass on and suffer loss. The generation of wisdom is the fear of the Lord, and riches, and glory, and life.

*And the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*

## On Thursday of Palm Week, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*

*After the first stichology, the Sessional Hymns of the Apostles from the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, we chant the Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph.*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginning with the Father and the Spirit" (Собезнача́льное):*[^1204]
Having yoked contemplation and action together, let us make haste to send them as a supplication unto Christ, that He may raise our dead mind, like another Lazarus, by His awesome presence, to bring unto Him branches of righteousness, and to cry: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord!

*Glory, the same. Both now, Theotokion:*
Heal swiftly the painful passions of my soul and the diseases of my flesh; stay the perversions of my mind, O All-Immaculate One, and in tranquility of thoughts grant me, O Mother of God, to offer pure prayers unto the King of all, and to entreat forgiveness of sins.

*After the third stichology, the Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore.*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "The angelic powers" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ):*[^1205]
Today Lazarus, having died, passes his second day, and over him his kinsfolk, Mary and Martha, shed tears of grief, beholding the grave stone; the Creator came forth with His disciples to despoil death, granting life; therefore we cry unto Him: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, the same. Both now, Theotokion:*
O Holy Lady and pure Mother of our God, who ineffably gavest birth unto the Creator of all, ever entreat His goodness together with the venerable Apostles, to deliver us from passions and to grant us remission of sins.

*Canon from the Menaion:*
*And the Triodion by Lord Joseph.*
*And the Ode is chanted with verses.*

*Ode 4, Tone 5. Heirmos: "Comprehending Thy divine:"*[^1206]
With the radiance of your consolation, O radiant Apostles, enlighten those shut up in the shadow of sin, who piously praise you!

Having yoked love with mercy, O ye faithful, let us make haste to send them for supplication unto Christ, that He may raise us also from the tomb of our secret passions.

Let us spare not fleshly love, being slain in soul, and let us cleave in love unto the Redeemer, that He may deliver us from the fearful condemnation of hades.

Thou didst foretell the falling asleep of Lazarus, whom after a little while Thou didst raise, receiving praise from them that bore palms, prefiguring Thy Passion, O my Savior.

*Theotokion:*
From thee, O All-Immaculate Birth-giver of God, flowed the divine water of immortality and life, tasting whereof, O Pure One, men are delivered from deadly thirst.

*Another Canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Heirmos: "I have heard:"*[^1207]
Lazarus is being buried, and they who are with Martha now lament and weep at the tomb, longing for Thee, the Giver of Life, to appear.

Death begins to tremble, sensing Thy descent unto it, O Christ, for Thou, being Life, hast shown it to be vain unto the ends of the earth.

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O simple, uncreated Unity, unoriginate Essence praised in the Trinity of Hypostases, save us who in faith bow down before Thy dominion!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Without human seed hast thou given birth in time, O Birth-giver of God, unto the Son Who is timeless from the Father, a strange wonder, having remained truly a Virgin.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Assemble, come, ye that are in the deserts and caves, to meet in hymns the Lord of glory coming upon a colt!

*Heirmos:*
*I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; I considered Thy works, and was astonished: glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto Thee, the Creator of all:"*[^1208]
Entreat the All-Compassionate One to take away the heavy stone from my heart, O divinely speaking Apostles of the Lord, who have shown yourselves to be stones of the Cornerstone!

Let us go, Thou didst proclaim unto Thy friends; My friend Lazarus is already fallen asleep; raising him, I put to sleep the all-destructive malice of death unto ages!

Having become gentle in soul and humble in disposition, let us through grace receive the gentle Master of all coming to crush the pride of the evil one!

*Theotokion:*
The Prophet beholds thee as the enlightened gate of the Light, O All-Immaculate One, for thou ineffably givest birth unto the Giver of Light, Who is made like unto us; Whom we exalt unto all ages.

*Another Canon. Heirmos: "Thy venerable:"*[^1209]
Mary and Martha now lament, seeing Lazarus lying in the tomb, and cry out in sorrow: If Christ had been here, our kinsman had not died!

Let hades expect to be destroyed, for Life comes to raise Lazarus, crying: Bless, sing, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Hymn:*
By unoriginate nature, by begetting, and by procession, I worship the Father Who begat, I glorify the Son Who was begotten, and I hymn the Holy Spirit, co-radiant with the Father and the Son!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O Virgin, cease not ever to entreat Him Whom thou didst ineffably bear, as the Lover of mankind, to save from perils them that flee unto thee!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Let us prepare virtues as boughs and as palms for the meeting, and let us, the faithful, cry unto Christ the King: Bless, sing, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*
*Heirmos:*
*Thy venerable Children in the furnace imitated the Cherubim, chanting the thrice-holy hymn: Bless, sing, and exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah:"*[^1210]
Deliver me from deceitful friendship, O Master Christ, Who didst reveal Thy wise disciples as Thy friends, with whom going forth, O Savior, Thou didst raise Thy faithful friend Lazarus, who thankfully hymns Thee!

With the fulfillment of the Fast, O Lord, fill our hearts and mind with joy, O Compassionate One, at the supplications of Thine Apostles, who purely loved Thee, the Savior of our souls!

Stinking with the putrefaction of malice, I live in the tomb of sloth, O Christ; therefore I cry unto Thee: Raise me up, save me, that I also may meet Thee with branches of virtues, crying: Hosanna to God!

*Theotokion:*
In two wills and two essences hast thou borne for us the Son, O Maiden, only-begotten of the Father, Who became man that He, the Transcendent God, might make us partakers of the divine nature, O All-Pure One!

*Another Canon. Heirmos: "Of the seedless conception:"*[^1211]
Lazarus passes his second day in the tomb today, and over him Mary and Martha, as his own sisters, shed tears; but Christ comes unto him with the divine Apostles, to show forth a great wonder!

O all-destructive death, receive thy dissolution, and let thy gatekeepers watch thy bars; for by a word Christ shall shatter thy gates, raising up Lazarus; drink this first draught, O hades, the Prophet cries with us!

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O Father, sole Begetter of the Only-begotten Son; and O sole Light, Radiance of the one Light; and O Holy Spirit, sole Lord from the one Lord God, truly existing, O Holy Trinity and Unity, save me who theologize Thee!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
The wonder of thy childbearing astonishes me, O All-Immaculate One: how dost thou conceive the Incomprehensible One without seed? Tell me, how dost thou remain virgin, having given birth as a Mother? Receiving in faith that which is above nature, bow down before the Born One, for whatsoever He wills, He also can do!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
The Lord comes to sit upon a colt, as it is written; O ye people, prepare to receive the King of all with palms in fear, as the Conqueror of death, Who hath raised Lazarus from hades!

*Heirmos:*
*The ineffable birthgiving of the seedless conception, the incorrupt Fruit of the unwedded Mother: for the birth of God renews natures; therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as the Bride of God and Mother.*

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice. Tone 5:*[^1212]
Come, all ye brethren, before the end let us draw near with a pure heart unto the compassionate God; having cast aside worldly distractions, let us take care for our souls, abhorring the pleasures of food through abstinence, and caring for almsgiving; for through it, as it is written, some have entertained angels; feeding the poor, let us feed Him Who hath nourished us with His own flesh, and let us clothe ourselves in Him Who covers Himself with light as with a garment; that having received forgiveness of sins through the prayers of the all-pure Mother of God and Virgin Mother, we may cry unto Him with compunction: O Lord, deliver us from standing on the left hand, and vouchsafe unto us a place at Thy right hand, as Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind!

*Martyricon:*
Being in torments, the holy martyrs cried out with joy: These things are for us exchanges before the Master; instead of present bodily wounds, a radiant vesture shall blossom for us in the resurrection; instead of dishonor, crowns; instead of prison bonds, paradise; and instead of condemnation with evildoers, dwelling with angels; therefore, by their prayers, O Lord, save our souls!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
*Podoben: "O full of grace" (Бл҃года́тнаѧ):*[^1213]
Fore-chosen from all generations, give ear unto our voice, and grant the petitions of our souls, deliverance from grievous passions and diseases, as thou art able to do all things, being the Mother of God!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 1:*[^1214]
Forget not the voice of Thy servants, O Lord; remember that we have ever put our hope in Thee, the Almighty One, and help us, we pray!

*Prokimenon, Psalm 117, Tone 4:*
This is the gate of the Lord; the righteous shall enter into it.[^1215]
*Verse: Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good:*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (65:8–16):*[^1216]
Thus saith the Lord: In like manner as a grape is found on the cluster, and they say: Destroy it not, for a blessing is in it; so will I do for My servant's sake, that I will not destroy them all for his sake. And I will lead forth a seed out of Jacob, and out of Judah, and he shall inherit My holy mountain; and Mine elect and My servants shall inherit it, and shall dwell there. And there shall be in the forest folds for flocks, and the valley of Achor shall be for a resting-place of herds for My people who have sought Me. But ye who have left Me, and forget My holy mountain, and prepare a table for the demon, and fill a mingled cup unto fortune: I will deliver you up to the sword, ye shall all fall by slaughter; because I called you, and ye gave no ear; I spake, and ye disobeyed; and ye did evil before Me, and chose the things wherein I delighted not. Therefore thus saith the Lord: Behold, My servants shall eat, but ye shall hunger; behold, My servants shall drink, but ye shall thirst; behold, My servants shall rejoice, but ye shall be ashamed; behold, My servants shall be glad with gladness of heart, but ye shall cry out for sorrow of your heart, and shall wail from contrition of spirit. For ye shall leave your name for a surfeit unto Mine elect, and the Lord shall slay you; but unto them that serve Me a new name shall be named, which shall be blessed upon the earth; for they shall bless the true God.

*Prokimenon, Psalm 118, Tone 4:*
I ran the way of Thy commandments, when Thou didst enlarge my heart.[^1217]
*Verse: Set before me for a law, O Lord, the way of Thy statutes.*

## On Thursday of Palm Week, at Vespers

*The customary stichology.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and chant prosomoia stichera by Lord Joseph.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^1218]
The most sacred pair of Apostles, knowing Thee, O Christ, to be One of the Trinity, is now sent by Thee to bring the colt, the foal of an ass, as it is written, whereon humbling Thyself Thou wilt sit, O Compassionate One, by Thy will in the highest preparing a throne for all that love Thee; and unto them that were stooped down unto brute beasts, harmed in mind, O Word, Thou grantest spiritual passions, who cry Hosanna unto Thee!

Receive thy King, O Sion! Behold, the Most Gentle One will voluntarily appear unto thee, He Who raised Lazarus and destroyed the bitter kingdom of death; O divinely assembled multitude of venerable monks, gather together with simple children to meet Christ with palms, crying: Blessed is He that comes to save mankind through the Passion of the Cross, and to bestow dispassion upon all according to His goodness!

*Another Sticheron Prosomoion by Lord Theodore.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "He Who in Eden" (И҆́же во Е҆де́мѣ):*[^1219]
Borne in the highest upon the dread Seraphim, O Christ, as God and Creator of all, Thou comest Thyself to sit upon a colt on earth, being as man like unto us; Bethany rejoices, having received Thee, O Savior; Jerusalem is glad, expecting to receive Thee; death is put to death, having sensed beforehand that Lazarus comes from the dead; and we, with palms, celebrating the meeting in joy, hymn the might of Thy goodness, O Lord!

*And in the Menaion 3.*
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

*Prokimenon, Psalm 119, Tone 6:*
Unto the Lord in mine affliction have I cried, and He heard me.[^1220]
*Verse: O Lord, deliver my soul from unrighteous lips, and from a deceitful tongue.*

*Reading from Genesis (46:1–4):*[^1221]
Israel himself rose up and all that he had, and came to the well of the oath; and offered sacrifice to the God of his father Isaac. And God spake unto Israel in a vision by night, saying: Jacob, Jacob! And he said: What is it? And He said unto him: I am the God of thy fathers; fear not to go down into Egypt, for there will I make of thee a great nation. And I will go down with thee into Egypt, and I will bring thee up at the end; and Joseph shall lay...

...his hands upon thine eyes. And Jacob rose up from the well of the oath; and the sons of Israel took their father, and their possessions, and their wives upon the wagons which Joseph had sent to take him. And having taken their goods, and all their substance which they had gotten in the land of Canaan, Jacob and all his seed with him entered into Egypt: his sons, and his sons' sons with him; his daughters, and his daughters' daughters, and all his seed he brought into Egypt.

*Prokimenon, Psalm 120, Tone 6:*
The Lord shall preserve thee from all evil; the Lord shall preserve thy soul.[^1222]
*Verse: I lifted up mine eyes unto the hills, from whence comes my help.*

*Reading from Proverbs (23:15–35; 24:1–5):*[^1223]
My son, if thy heart be wise, thou shalt make my heart glad also. And thy lips shall remain in words with my lips, if they be right. Let not thy heart envy sinners, but be thou in the fear of the Lord all the day long. For if thou keep these things, thou shalt have descendants, and thine expectation shall not depart. Give ear, my son, and become wise, and direct the thoughts of thy heart. Be not a winebibber, neither join thyself unto assemblies and purchase of meats; for every drunkard and whoremonger shall become poor, and every drowsy man shall be clothed with tatters and with rags. Listen, my son, unto the father who begat thee, and despise not thy mother when she is old. Acquire truth, and reject not wisdom, and instruction, and understanding. A righteous father trains well, and over a wise son his soul rejoices; let thy father and thy mother rejoice over thee, and let her that bare thee be glad. My son, give me thy heart, and let thine eyes observe my ways. For a strange house is a broken vessel, and a strange well is narrow; for this man shall perish speedily, and every transgressor shall be cut off. To whom is woe? To whom is tumult? To whom are lawsuits? To whom are bitternesses and contentions? To whom are bruises without cause? Whose eyes are livid? Are they not theirs that tarry long at wine? Are they not theirs that hunt after where banquets be? Be not drunk with wine, but converse with righteous men, and converse in public paths; for if thou cast thine eyes upon cups and flagons, afterward thou shalt go more naked than a winnowing pole. And at last he shall lie stretched out as if bitten by a serpent, and as by a horned viper venom is diffused in him. When thine eyes behold a strange woman, thy mouth shall then speak perverse things; and thou shalt lie down as in the heart of the sea, and as a pilot in a great surge. And thou shalt say: They beat me, and I felt no pain; and they mocked me, but I knew it not; when shall morning come, that I may go and seek them with whom I may meet? My son, envy not evil men, neither desire to be with them; for their heart meditats falsehoods, and their lips speak griefs. With wisdom is a house built, and with understanding is it established; with knowledge are the chambers filled with all precious and good wealth. Better is a wise man than a strong man, and a man having understanding than a great husbandman.

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice. Tone 8:*[^1224]
From the likeness of the unmerciful rich man and from an ungenerous disposition, deliver me, O Christ God, Who by Thy Cross hast made cleansing for our sins; and show me forth an emulator of the grateful patience of poor Lazarus, and estrange me not from the bosom of the Patriarch Abraham, for the sake of Thy great mercy!

*Martyricon:*
O Martyrs of the Lord, ye hallow every place and heal every infirmity; and now entreat, we pray, that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy!

*Glory, both now, Cross-Theotokion:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder!" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*[^1225]
O most marvelous wonder! O novel mystery! O dread beginning! The Virgin spake, when she beheld Thee hanging on the Cross between two thieves, Whom she had awesomely borne without pain, weeping and saying: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! How hath a fierce and ungrateful people nailed Thee to the Cross?

## On Friday of Palm Week, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*

*After the first stichology, the Cross Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, we chant the present Sessional Hymn by Lord Joseph.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^1226]
A pair of disciples is sent to bring the colt, as it is written, whereon Christ the Beautiful shall enter before all, to receive divine praise from babes; let us go therefore diligently to meet Him, bringing unto Him the branches of virtuous deeds!

*Glory, the same. Both now, Cross-Theotokion. Podoben:*
O Virgin all-immaculate, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God voluntarily crucified; cease not, O Blessed One, to pray Him to grant us remission of transgressions in the time of the Fast!

*After the third stichology, the Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore, Tone 8.*
*Podoben: "Risen from the dead" (Воскре́съ и҆з̾ ме́ртвыхъ):*[^1227]
A pair of disciples is sent today to bring the colt unto the Master of all; He Who is borne by the multitudes of Seraphim comes to sit thereon; the all-consuming death, ruler of this world, which formerly held the human race captive, begins to fear because of Lazarus!

*Glory, the same. Both now, Cross-Theotokion. Podoben.*
Standing by the Cross of her Son, the Pure One bitterly lamenting cried out in hymns: How have the Hebrew children, woe is me, delivered Thee unto the tribunal of Pilate, O Savior, Who art unceasingly glorified with angelic hymns? I hymn Thy manifold and ineffable compassion, O Word!

*Canon from the Menaion, and the Triodion by Lord Joseph.*
*And the Ode is chanted with verses.*

*Ode 5, Tone 4. Heirmos: "Bestow light upon me, O Lord:"*[^1228]
By Thy sleep upon the Cross, O Lord, Thou hast turned death into sleep, O Master; for Thou didst cry aloud: Our friend Lazarus is fallen asleep, but let us go now to awaken him!

Fulfilling the prophecies of the prophets' proclamation, Thou art come, O compassionate Christ, unto the prophet-slaying city, to be slain voluntarily, saving me who was slain by corruption!

Having subdued the disobedient body unto the Word, by abstinence and supplications, ye monks and simple people, go ye forth to meet Christ, Who is seated upon a colt and coming to suffer!

*Theotokion:*
Water my mind, dried up by the flame of sin, with the sprinkling of thy mercy, O All-Pure One, and rekindle the extinguished lamp of my heart, O Gate of Light!

*Another Canon by Lord Theodore.*
*Tone 8. Heirmos: "The gloom of my soul:"*[^1229]
When Thou wast about to reach Bethany, O Christ, Thou didst send a pair of Thy disciples to bring Thee the colt whereon no man had ever sat; for except Thee, O Savior, no one hath ever subdued the brute nations!

A multitude of Jews came together from Jerusalem unto Bethany, and today they mourn with the kinsfolk of Lazarus; but on the morrow, perceiving him leaping from the grave, they shall be stirred up unto the slaying of Christ!

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O Most Holy Trinity, Father, and Son, and all-creative Spirit: blessed Godhead, unoriginate Essence, and thrice-radiant Light, all-seeing Dominion, preserve Thy servants!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Seeing Thee nailed with nails upon the Cross, O Christ, and the blood and water flowing from Thy life-giving ribs, Thy Mother was wounded with maternal compassion, for she knew Thee to be her Son!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Behold, Christ hath prepared to appear in Jerusalem; all ye monks that are in the mountains and deserts, gather together to meet Him with joy with all the world beneath the sun!

*Heirmos:*
*Dispel the gloom of my soul, O Giver of Light, Christ God, driving out the original darkness of the abyss, and grant me the light of Thy commandments, O Word, that rising early I may glorify Thee!*

*Ode 8, Tone 4. Heirmos: "The earth and all that is therein:"*[^1230]

*Troparion:*
Possessing the Resurrection and the Life, O beloved women, why weep ye bitterly? The Benefactor of all comes to give life unto His close friend, proclaiming through His own Resurrection the rising of all!

*Troparion:*
O habitations of monks, gather together as rational lambs to meet Christ the Chief Shepherd, bearing branches now; for as a Lamb the Lord goes forth willingly to be slain, that He may deliver us from the tyrannical wolf!

*Troparion:*
As Thou drewest near unto Bethphage, the enemy of hades felt the treading of Thy feet, and he touched the feet of Lazarus, saying: If Life desires to call thee, be not slack, but come forth; for I have perceived my destruction quickly at hand!

*Theotokion:*
The sacred voices of the prophets proclaim thee prefiguratively, O Virgin: a door, and a mountain, and a tabernacle, a holy land, a radiant cloud, from which upon those who are in darkness and sitting in the shadow, the Sun hath shone forth, the only Giver of Light!

*Another Canon by Lord Theodore.*
*Tone 8. Heirmos: "The Angels and the heavens:"*[^1231]

*Troparion:*
The pair of disciples was sent to bring the beast of burden unto Him Who rids upon the clouds, Whom all creation hymns and exalts above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ is at the doors; henceforth weep no more, O Bethany! For He shall turn thy weeping into joy, raising thy nursling Lazarus from the grave, who hymns and exalts Christ above all unto the ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
One God is the Trinity, neither the Father passing into sonship, nor the Son changing into procession; but their personal properties and their unity as Light and God, Three, I glorify unto the ages!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Alone undefiled, thou givest birth; alone unespoused, thou sucklest an Infant; alone thou barest thy Creator and Master, though being both Mother and handmaid: thee, O Virgin Mother, we hymn unto the ages!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Let us all prepare to receive Christ in a twofold manner, with the gardens of virtues and visibly with branches, as He is borne upon a colt: Whom we exalt above all unto all ages!

*We praise, bless, and worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
*Ye Angels and heavens, bless Him Who sits upon the throne of glory and is unceasingly glorified as God, hymn Him, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!*

*Ode 9, Tone 4. Heirmos: "For He that is mighty hath done to me:"*[^1232]

*Troparion:*
Behold, Thou hast foretold, O Jesus: Let us go up unto the Holy City, and into the hands of murderers I shall truly be delivered unto the Cross, to be slain in the flesh!

*Troparion:*
Desiring, O Word, to snatch Thy friend from death, Thou art nailed to be put to death in the flesh for our sake, making faithful men immortal, O only Immortal One!

*Troparion:*
Let us bring branches of chastity unto Christ, Who humbled Himself in the flesh upon a colt, and let us say unto Him: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, Who comest unto Thy Passion!

*Theotokion:*
Awesome is thy childbirth, O Theotokos, Mother of Christ; therefore, we faithfully call thee blessed and reverently glorify thee, generations of generations unto the ages, amen!

*Another Canon by Lord Theodore.*
*Tone 8. Heirmos: "That which was prefigured on the mountain:"*[^1233]

*Troparion:*
Sending forth the disciples now, Christ said: Having loosed the colt for Me, bring it that I may sit upon it, that I may loose the Gentiles from irrationality, and subject them as the Son unto the Father!

*Troparion:*
The Lord comes; open thy doors, O Bethany, receive the Master in faith: for He comes to raise Lazarus from the grave, as the only Almighty One!

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O Lord, with the radiant splendors of Thy Tri-hypostatic Sovereign Rule enlighten our mind, turning us away from manifold deception unto unitive deification!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, palace of the King of all, through whom the Heavenly Kingdom was opened unto those upon the earth, O Virgin Theotokos, and life together with the Angels!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Ye that dwell in deserts, and mountains, and caves, come, gather together with us as branch-bearers, to meet the King and Master: for He comes to save our souls!

*Heirmos:*
*That which was prefigured on the mountain unto the lawgiver in the fire and the burning bush, the Childbirth of the Ever-Virgin, unto the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify!*

*Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*[^1234]

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice. Tone 7:*
*Composition of Emperor Leo:*[^1235]
Having despised divine anointings, O my soul, thou hast been overwhelmed by the wiles of the enemy, voluntarily surrendering thyself unto corruption; and having become heavy through much sin, thou hast defiled the divinely woven garment, making it unadorned for the royal wedding; but lest thou be dragged down with sin, and recline covered with the garment of passions, being held liable for retribution, and be cast out of the bridal chamber, cry aloud unto the Savior: O awesome Eye, Who didst take on what I am, and didst not abandon what Thou wast, and before the Cross wast clothed in the cloak of mockery for my sake, tear asunder my sackcloth, and clothe me in gladness, and deliver me from the outer darkness and from eternal weeping, and have mercy on me!

*Martyricon:*
Come, all ye people, let us honor the passion-bearers of Christ with odes and spiritual songs, the luminaries of the world and preachers of the faith, the ever-flowing fountains from which there flow forth...

...healings unto the faithful: by their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory, Tone 8:*[^1236]
Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls, we also pray to behold the Holy Week of Thy Passion, O Lover of Mankind, that we may glorify therein Thy majestic deeds, and Thine ineffable dispensation for our sake, singing with one mind: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Both now: the same again.*

*And the rest of the sequence as usual.*

*[Woodcut Ornamental Headpiece Band]*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 3:*
Intolerable, O Lord, is the wrath of Thy rebuke upon sinners, and we are not worthy to look up and ask mercy of Thee: let not Thine anger nor Thy wrath destroy us, whom Thou hast fashioned from the earth with Thine own hand!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 121, Tone 4:*[^1237]
Because of the house of the Lord our God, I have sought good things for Thee.
*Verse:* I was glad when they said unto me: We will go into the house of the Lord.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 66 [verses 10–19]):*[^1238]
Be glad, O Jerusalem, and keep festival in it, all ye that love it; and all ye that dwell in it, rejoice together with it in joy, all ye that mourn for it; that ye may suck and be satisfied from the breast of its consolation, that having milked it out ye may delight yourselves from the entrance of its glory. For thus saith the Lord: Behold, I incline toward them like a river of peace, and like a torrent overflowing, the glory of the Gentiles; their children shall be taken upon shoulders, and consoled upon knees. Even as when a mother comforts someone, so also will I comfort you, and in Jerusalem ye shall be comforted. And ye shall see, and your heart shall rejoice, and your bones shall spring up like grass; and the hand of the Lord shall be known unto them that fear Him, and He shall threaten the disobedient. For behold, the Lord shall come like fire, and His chariots like a whirlwind, to render His vengeance with wrath, and His rebuke in a flame of fire. For by the fire of the Lord shall all the earth be judged, and all flesh by His sword: many shall be wounded by the Lord. They that purify and sanctify themselves in the gardens, and eat swine's flesh in the porches, and abominations, and mice, shall perish together, saith the Lord. And I know their works and their thought, and I am coming to gather all nations and tongues; and they shall come and behold My glory. And I will leave a sign upon them, and I will send forth those of them that are saved unto the nations, to Tarshish, and to Pul, and to Lud, and to Meshech, and to Tubal...

...and to Greece, and to the islands afar off, who have not heard My Name, neither have seen My glory: and they shall declare My glory among the Gentiles. And they shall bring your brethren out of all nations for a gift unto the Lord, with horses, and chariots, and with litters of mules, under awnings into the holy city of Jerusalem, saith the Lord, even as if the children of Israel would bring their sacrifices to Me with psalms into the house of the Lord. And from them will I take for Myself priests and Levites, saith the Lord. For as the new heaven and the new earth which I make remain before Me, saith the Lord, so shall your seed and your name stand. And it shall come to pass from month to month, and from Sabbath to Sabbath, all flesh shall come to worship before Me in Jerusalem, saith the Lord. And they shall go forth, and see the corpses of the men that have transgressed against Me; for their worm shall not die, neither shall their fire be quenched; and they shall be a spectacle unto all flesh.[^1239]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 122, Tone 6:*[^1240]
Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us.
*Verse:* Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven.

*End of the Lenten Triodion.*[^1241]

*[Woodcut Engraving: Icon of the Most Holy Theotokos holding the Pre-eternal Christ Child, enclosed within an elaborate ornamental Baroque frame.]*

*[Woodcut Headpiece: Floral ornamental knotwork framing an emblem of Mount Sion and the Eye of Providence.]*

---

### Notes

[^713]: Monday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast (Mid-Fast Week / Cross-Veneration Week) at Matins: Sessional Hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph in Tone 8, to the automelon "Воскре́съ є҆сѝ" (Having arisen).
[^714]: Matins Sessional Hymn after the third stichology in Tone 3, to the automelon "Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства" (Beholding the beauty of thy virginity): "Преполови́вше ны́нѣ воздержа́нїе" (Having now reached the midpoint of self-control), marking the halfway point of the Great Forty Days.
[^715]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Monday, Ode 6 by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer in Tone 8: Heirmos "Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на" (The charioteer of Pharaoh), extolling the radiant light of the Cross shining forth at Mid-Fast.
