[^716]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Monday, Ode 6 by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer in Tone 8: troparia contrasting the deceitful snare of the serpent with the healing of Adam's fall through the saving Passion and Cross of Christ.
[^717]: Ode 6 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 3: "Пои́мъ Гдⷭ҇ви сотво́ршемꙋ ди̑внаѧ чꙋдеса̀" (Let us sing unto the Lord Who hath wrought marvelous wonders), recalling the victory over Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Exod. 15).
[^718]: Troparion marking the midpoint of the Great Fast: "Четы́редесѧте по́стныхъ ста́дїй преполовля́юще" (Having reached the midpoint of the forty-stage stadium of the Fast), exhorting the faithful to press onward with Christ toward Holy Week and the Resurrection.
[^719]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Monday, Ode 8 Heirmos incipit by Lord Joseph in Tone 8: "Седмери́цею пе́щь" (The furnace sevenfold), based upon the Song of the Three Holy Children in Babylon (Dan. 3:19 LXX).
[^720]: Classical patristic typology of the Cross: Moses extending his arms in the form of a cross on Mount Rephidim while Hur and Aaron supported him, vanquishing Amalek (Exod. 17:11–12).
[^721]: Ode 8 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 3: "А҆́ггли немо́лчнѡ" (The angels unceasingly), calling all cosmic creation to bless the Creator and Deliverer.
[^722]: Scriptural imagery of the "horn" as a symbol of divine strength and triumph (Ps. 17:3 LXX; Luke 1:69), here applied to the Cross exalting the power and defense of the Christian churches.
[^723]: Ascetical exhortation at Mid-Lent: "По́стъ преполови́вше, дерза́имъ дх҃омъ на грѧдꙋ́щее ю҆́ношески" (Having reached the midpoint of the Fast, let us take courage in spirit toward what is to come with youthful vigor), renewing spiritual zeal toward Holy Pascha.
[^724]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Monday, Ode 9 Heirmos incipit by Lord Joseph in Tone 8: "Оу҆страши́сѧ всѧ́къ слꙋ́хъ" (Every ear trembled), contemplating the unutterable condescension of God.
[^725]: Patriarchal typology from Genesis 48:13–14: the patriarch Jacob deliberately crossing his arms to bless Joseph's sons Ephraim and Manasseh, recognized by the Fathers (e.g., St. John Chrysostom) as a prophetic prefiguration of the Cross.
[^726]: Ode 9 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 3: "На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀" (On Mount Sinai), gathering Old Testament types of the Theotokos: the burning bush of Moses (Exod. 3), the unhewn mountain of Daniel (Dan. 2:34–35, 45), and the budding rod of Jesse from Isaiah (Isa. 11:1).
[^727]: Psalm 131:7 LXX (Ps. 132:7 MT): "На мѣ́стѣ, и҆де́же но́зѣ стоѧ́стѣ Влⷣки" (At the place where the feet of the Master stood), prophetically applied to the veneration of Golgotha and the Precious Cross.
[^728]: Prophetic proclamation from Isaiah 8:9–10 LXX: "Разꙋмѣ́йте ꙗ҆зы́цы и҆ покара́йтесѧ, ꙗ҆́кѡ съ на́ми Бг҃ъ" (Understand, ye nations, and submit yourselves, for God is with us), chanted here against demonic powers vanquished by the Cross.
[^729]: Matins Aposticha Idiomelon of the day in Tone 6: integrating the Gospel parable of the Publican and the Pharisee (Luke 18:9–14) with the Kenosis hymn of Philippians 2:5–8 ("having emptied Himself and taken the form of a servant").
[^730]: Rubric for the First Hour on Monday, Wednesday, and Friday of the Fourth Week: solemn veneration of the Precious Cross in the nave, replacing "Order my steps" (Ps. 118:133 LXX) with "Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master", and inserting the Kontakion of the Cross ("No longer does the flaming sword", Tone 7).
[^731]: Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy in Tone 7: based upon the penitential opening of Psalm 6:1 LXX and Psalm 37:1 LXX (MT 6:1; 38:1): "O Lord, reprove me not in Thine indignation, neither chasten me in Thy wrath."
[^732]: Sixth Hour First Prokimenon in Tone 6: Psalm 61:8, 2 LXX (Ps. 62:7, 1 MT): "Ѽ Бз҃ѣ спасе́нїе моѐ, и҆ сла́ва моѧ̀" (In God is my salvation and my glory).
[^733]: Sixth Hour Old Testament reading from Isaiah 14:24–32 LXX: the prophetic decree of the Lord Sabaoth concerning the broken power of Assyria, the impending doom of Philistia, and the everlasting establishment of Zion for the humble poor.
[^734]: Sixth Hour Second Prokimenon in Tone 4: Psalm 62:5, 2 LXX (Ps. 63:4, 1 MT): "Та́кѡ бл҃гословлю́ тѧ въ животѣ̀ мое́мъ" (Thus will I bless Thee in my life).
[^735]: Vespers on Monday of the Fourth Week: Lord, I have cried Prosomoia in Tone 8 to the automelon "What shall we call you" (Что́ ва́съ нарече́мъ), focusing on the spiritual comfort of the Cross at the midpoint of the Great Forty Days.
[^736]: Vespers First Prokimenon in Tone 6: Psalm 63:2, 3 LXX (Ps. 64:1, 2 MT): "Оу҆слы́ши Бж҃е, гла́съ мо́й, внегда̀ моли́тимисѧ къ тебѣ̀" (Hear my voice, O God, when I pray unto Thee).
[^737]: Vespers first Old Testament reading from Genesis 8:21–9:7 LXX: the Lord smelling the sweet savor of Noah's sacrifice, the perpetual covenant of the seasons, the post-diluvian blessing of Noah and his sons, and the sacred prohibition against eating blood and shedding human blood.
[^738]: Vespers Second Prokimenon in Tone 6: Psalm 64:6, 2 LXX (Ps. 65:5, 1 MT): "Оу҆слы́ши ны̀ Бж҃е Сп҃сителю на́шъ" (Hear us, O God our Savior).
[^739]: Vespers second Old Testament reading from Proverbs 11:19–12:6 LXX: ethical maxims contrasting righteousness and wicked folly, the proverbial golden ring in a swine's snout (Prov. 11:22), and the praise of the virtuous wife (Prov. 12:4).
[^740]: Vespers Aposticha Idiomelon of the day in Tone 8: meditating upon the humility of the Publican and the haughtiness of the Pharisee (Luke 18:9–14), invoking Christ's promise of grace unto the humble (James 4:6; 1 Pet. 5:5).
[^741]: Tuesday of the Fourth Week at Matins: Sessional Hymn after the second stichology in Tone 8, to the automelon "An unshakable foundation" (Непоколеби́мое ᲂу҆твержде́нїе), extolling the Cross as the sweetness of them that fast and destroyer of passions.
[^742]: Matins Sessional Hymn after the third stichology in Tone 8, to the automelon "As the first fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки): "Ꙗ҆́коже жи́знь тва́ри, тво́й крⷭ҇тъ предлежа́щїй" (As the life of creation, the inhabited earth kisses Thy Cross set forth).
[^743]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Tuesday, Ode 2 Heirmos by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer in Tone 8: "Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ а҆́зъ є҆́смь Бг҃ъ" (See, see that I am God), based upon the Great Canticle of Moses in Deuteronomy 32:39.
[^744]: Typological meditation: Moses' staff dividing the Red Sea (Exod. 14:16, 21) as a figure of the Cross guiding believers through the tempest of passions to the spiritual Promised Land and mystical Manna.
[^745]: Ode 2 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 8: "Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ а҆́зъ є҆́смь Бг҃ъ" (See, see that I am God), presenting the Cross as an unconquerable weapon for the faithful.
[^746]: Re-iteration of the Rephidim typology (Exod. 17:11–12): Moses' outstretched arms over the battle with Amalek typifying the sign of the Cross vanquishing demonic assaults in the air.
[^747]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Tuesday, Ode 8 Heirmos incipit by Lord Joseph in Tone 8: "Покрыва́й вода́ми превы́спрєннѧѧ своѧ̑" (He Who covers His chambers with waters), based upon Psalm 103:2–3 LXX.
[^748]: Contemplation of the side of Christ pierced by the lance on Golgotha, pouring forth blood and water (John 19:34), celebrated by the Fathers as the fountain of baptism and the Holy Eucharist that dries up sinful passions.
[^749]: Ode 8 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 8: "А҆́ггли и҆ нб҃са̀ на прⷭ҇то́лѣ сла́вы сѣдѧ́щаго" (Angels and the heavens, bless Him Who sits upon the throne of glory), from the Danielic Benedicite (Dan. 3:58 LXX).
[^750]: Mystical liturgical typology of the "three-part Cross" (трїсло́жный крⷭ҇тъ, composed of three woods according to Isaiah 60:13: cedar, cypress, and pine) as an earthly figure and reflection of the Holy Trinity.
[^751]: Christological miracle of the man born blind (John 9:1–7), interpreted ascetically as a model for Christ opening the blinded spiritual eyes of the soul during the Great Fast.
[^752]: Matins Triode for the Fourth Tuesday, Ode 9 Heirmos incipit by Lord Joseph in Tone 8: "Бл҃гослове́нъ Гдⷭ҇ь Бг҃ъ І҆и҃левъ" (Blessed be the Lord God of Israel), based upon the Benedictus Canticle of Zacharias (Luke 1:68).
[^753]: Penitential troparion invoking Christ's tenderhearted mercy to loose the fetters of sin through the power of the Cross.
[^754]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 8: sharing the same incipit "Бл҃гослове́нъ Гдⷭ҇ь Бг҃ъ І҆и҃левъ", celebrating the loosed ancient curse through the Cross.
[^755]: Acclamation of the Cross: "Цр҃кви похвала̀ ты̀ є҆сѝ чⷭ҇тный крⷭ҇те" (Thou art the boast of the Church, O honorable Cross, the weapon of kings, and the joy of the Orthodox).
[^756]: The maternal lament of the Mother of God at the foot of the Cross: "Что̀ стра́нное видѣ́нїе; Жи́зне, ка́кѡ ᲂу҆мира́еши;" (What strange sight is this? O Life, how diest Thou?), meditating upon the incomprehensible mystery of the Immortal One tasting death.
[^757]: Repentance of Manasseh (2 Chron. 33) contrasted with the unfaithfulness of Israel wandering in the wilderness, invoked by the penitent soul seeking divine forgiveness.
[^758]: Tuesday Matins Aposticha Idiomelon of the day in Tone 8: repeated from the previous evening's Vespers, reinforcing the spiritual lesson of the Publican and the Pharisee (Luke 18:9–14).
[^759]: Tuesday Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy in Tone 6: "Крестꙋ̀ твоемꙋ̀ покланѧ́емсѧ влⷣко" (Before Thy Cross we bow down, O Master), ancient liturgical chant of the Adoration of the Cross paired with the veneration of the Holy Sepulchre and the Resurrection.
[^760]: Tuesday Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon, Psalm 65:20 (LXX), Tone 6.
[^761]: Tuesday Sixth Hour Reading from Isaiah 25:1–9, the apocalyptic banquet of the Lord on Mount Zion, the swallowing up of death in victory (Isa. 25:8; cf. 1 Cor. 15:54), and the wiping away of tears from every face.
[^762]: Tuesday Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 66:7–8 (LXX), Tone 6.
[^763]: Tuesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, First Prosomoion of the Triodion in Tone 6: Podoben "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше / Ὅλην ἀποθέμενοι), celebrating the mid-fast exposition and veneration of the Precious Cross.
[^764]: Tuesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Second Prosomoion in Tone 6, meditating on Christ's thirst and tasting of gall as the mystical transformation of the bitter waters of Marah (Exod. 15:23–25).
[^765]: Tuesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron in Tone 1: Podoben "Of old Moses" (Дре́вле Мѡѷсе́й / Πάλαι Μωϋσῆς), typifying Moses' cruciform outstretched hands that defeated Amalek (Exod. 17:11–12).
[^766]: Tuesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Stichera in Tone 4: Podoben "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе / Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν), invoking the Cross as the invincible trophy of Christian peace against alien adversaries.
[^767]: Tuesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Doxasticon in Tone 8: "Дне́сь Влⷣка тва́ри, и҆ Гдⷭ҇ь сла́вы, на крⷭ҇тѣ̀ пригвожда́етсѧ" (Today the Master of creation and the Lord of glory is nailed to the Cross), an expansive liturgical meditation on the paradox of the Creator suffering humiliation at the hands of His creatures.
[^768]: Rubric directing that the stichera and canon of the saint of the day from the Menaion are omitted at Vespers and transferred to Compline, due to the paramount liturgical focus on the Precious Cross during the Mid-Fast Week.
[^769]: Tuesday Vespers, First Prokeimenon, Psalm 67:5 (LXX), Tone 6.
[^770]: Tuesday Vespers, First Paremia from Genesis 9:8–17, God establishing His universal covenant with Noah and every living creature, sealed with the rainbow in the clouds as a perpetual sign against another deluge.
[^771]: Tuesday Vespers, Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 68:30 (LXX), Tone 6.
[^772]: Tuesday Vespers, Second Paremia from Proverbs 12:9–22, contrasting the wisdom, gentle speech, and merciful kindness of the righteous with the treacherous lips, cruelty, and ruin of the ungodly.
[^773]: Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha Idiomelon in Tone 3 (chanted twice): penitential self-examination contrasting the speaker's sins surpassing the Publican with his pride imitating the Pharisee (Luke 18:9–14).
[^774]: Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha Martyrikon in Tone 3, commemorating the prophets, apostles, and martyrs who proclaimed the consubstantial Trinity.
[^775]: Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 5: "Зрѧ́щи тѧ̀ тва́рь всѧ̀ на крⷭ҇тѣ̀ на́га ви́сѧща" (Beholding Thee hanging naked upon the Cross), recalling the cosmic convulsing of all creation, the tearing of the temple veil, and the resurrection of the dead at Christ's Crucifixion (Matt. 27:51–53).
[^776]: Wednesday Matins opening: the Triadica (Trinity Hymns) of the current tone chanted during the Great Fast in place of "God is the Lord".
[^777]: Wednesday Matins, Sessional Hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 6: Podoben "O hope of the world" (Оу҆пова́нїе мі́ра), venerating the Cross set forth at the mid-point of the fast.
[^778]: Wednesday Matins, Stavrotheotokion after the second stichology, invoking Simeon's prophecy of the sword piercing the maternal heart of the Virgin (Luke 2:35).
[^779]: Wednesday Matins, Sessional Hymn after the third stichology by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 1: Podoben "O hope of the despairing" (Ѻ҆ча́ѧннаѧ / Ἡ ἀπηλπισμένη), praying for bodily and spiritual sanctification through the Cross.
[^780]: Wednesday Matins, Stavrotheotokion after the third stichology, Tone 1: Podoben "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й / Τοῦ λίθου σφραγισθέντος), the Virgin Mother mourning her dead Son stretched upon the Tree.
[^781]: Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross by St. Theophanes the Branded (the Graptos), Tone 4, bearing the acrostic: "Всечестно́мꙋ поклони́мсѧ дре́вꙋ" (Let us bow down before the all-honorable Tree), announced at the start of the canon on fol. 281v (Leaf p285).
[^782]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Ѿве́рзꙋ ᲂу҆ста̀ моѧ̑" (I will open my mouth), based on Psalm 77:2 (LXX).
[^783]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Твоѧ̑ пѣсносло́вцы" (Thy hymnographers, O Theotokos).
[^784]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 6: "Гдⷭ҇ь сы́й всѣ́хъ" (Being the Lord of all), celebrating Christ lifted up in the midst of the earth to raise fallen humanity.
[^785]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 1: "Оу҆твердѝ Гдⷭ҇и" (Establish, O Lord), based on 1 Samuel (1 Kings) 2:2 (LXX).
[^786]: Wednesday Matins, Sessional Hymns of the Cross in Tone 6 (Idiomela), sung after Ode 3 of the Canon, recalling Psalm 98:5 and 131:7: "Worship at the footstool of His feet, for He is holy."
[^787]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Сѣдѧ́й во сла́вѣ" (He Who sits in glory upon the throne of Godhead), based on the Canticle of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2 LXX), typifying Jacob crossing his hands to bless Ephraim and Manasseh (Gen. 48:13–14).
[^788]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́чєскаѧ" (All things were astounded at thy divine glory), from the Canticle of Isaiah (Isa. 26:9 LXX).
[^789]: Ode 5 Troparion: The Slavonic text "вѣ̑рнымъ носѧ̀ па́гꙋбꙋ безконе́чнꙋю" presents an evident epigraphic scribal inversion or misprint for demons/adversaries (бѣ́сѡмъ носѧ̀ па́гꙋбꙋ безконе́чнꙋю, Greek: τοῖς δαίμοσι φέρων ἀπώλειαν ἄληκτον), as the Cross brings salvation to the faithful and endless destruction to demonic foes.
[^790]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Возопѝ, прошбража́ѧ" (The prophet Jonah cried out, prefiguring the three-day burial), based on the Canticle of Jonah (Jonah 2:3 LXX).
[^791]: Wednesday Matins, Kontakion of the Cross in Tone 7 (Idiomelon): "Не ктомꙋ̀ пла́менное ѻ҆рꙋ́жїе храни́тъ вра́тъ є҆де́мскихъ" (No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden), sung during the third and fourth weeks of Great Lent (cf. Gen. 3:24).
[^792]: Wednesday Matins, Ikos of the Cross: dramatic personification of Hades lamenting its sudden wounding and despoiling when Christ was pierced upon Golgotha.
[^793]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Не послꙋжи́ша тва́ри" (The godly youths served not the creature), based on Daniel 3:28 (LXX); troparion typifying Elisha's miraculous floating iron axe-head (4 Kings / 2 Kings 6:5–7) as a figure of the wood of the Cross lifting submerged mankind.
[^794]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ" (The pious youths in the furnace), from Daniel 3:57ff. (LXX).
[^795]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 8: "За зако́ны ѻ҆те́ческїѧ" (For the laws of the fathers the blessed youths in Babylon stood in jeopardy), Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^796]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 1: "Се́й нарече́нный и҆ ст҃ы́й де́нь" (This chosen and holy day, the first of Sabbaths), Paschal irmologic incipit applied to the mid-fast veneration of the Cross.
[^797]: Troparion quoting Jeremiah 11:19: "Прїиди́те, вложи́мъ дре́во въ хлѣ́бъ є҆гѡ̀" (Come, let us put wood into His bread, and destroy Him from the land of the living), a locus classicus of Patristic Christological typology for the Cross and the Eucharist.
[^798]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 4: "Всѧ́къ земноро́дный" (Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy), celebrating the Virgin and the Cross.
[^799]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 6: "Недоꙋмѣ́етъ всѧ́къ ꙗ҆зы́къ" (Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee worthily), repeating the Elisha axe-head typology (4 Kings 6:5–7) and exalting Christ's dominion over demonic idols.
[^800]: Ode 9 Heirmos incipit of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 1: "Свѣти́сѧ свѣти́сѧ" (Shine, shine; cf. Isa. 60:1); the troparion summons the faithful to spiritual radiance before the wood of the Cross.
[^801]: Typological allusion to Psalm 67:16–17 (LXX): "The mountain of God, the rich mountain... the mountain wherein God is well pleased to dwell."
[^802]: Ode 9 Katavasia of the Triode: "Ѡ҆ Мт҃и Дв҃о, и҆ Бцⷣе нело́жнаѧ" (O Mother and Virgin, and true Theotokos, who without seed gavest birth unto Christ our God).
[^803]: Wednesday Matins Exapostilarion of the Cross, Tone 2, Podoben: "In the Spirit" (Дх҃омъ / Πνεύματι), celebrating the mid-point of the fasts summoning all to venerate the Precious Cross.
[^804]: Wednesday Matins Stavrotheotokion, the Virgin's lament at the Cross contrasting Gabriel's Annunciation greeting of joy (Luke 1:28) with Golgotha.
[^805]: Wednesday Matins Aposticha Idiomelon in Tone 8, linking the mid-point of the Fast to Abraham's joy at seeing Christ's day (John 8:56) and Isaac's delivery from Mount Moriah (Gen. 22:1–14).
[^806]: Typological interpretation of Abraham receiving Isaac alive from the sacrificial altar as a figure of Christ's Resurrection (Heb. 11:17–19).
[^807]: Rubric for the special liturgical veneration of the Precious Cross during Mid-Lent Wednesday at the Sixth Hour, chanting the Sunday veneration stichera in Tone 2: "Прїиди́те вѣ́рнїи, животворѧ́щемꙋ дре́вꙋ поклони́мсѧ" (Come, ye faithful, let us bow down before the lifegiving Tree).
[^808]: Wednesday Sixth Hour Prokeimenon, Psalm 69:5, 3 (LXX; Slavonic text gives ѱало́мъ ѯ҃д = Ps. 69 LXX / Ps. 70 MT), Tone 6.
[^809]: Wednesday Sixth Hour Reading from Isaiah 26:21–27:9, the Lord's holy sword slaying the dragon Leviathan and His cultivation of the fruitful vineyard of Israel.
[^810]: Wednesday Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 70:1–2 (LXX), Tone 6.
[^811]: Wednesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, First Idiomelon in Tone 4, celebrating the mid-point of the Fast as a mediator of heavenly blessings leading into Holy Pascha.
[^812]: Wednesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Second Idiomelon in Tone 5, paraphrasing the Sermon on the Mount (Matt. 6:1–18) regarding prayer and fasting in secret, concluding with the Lord's Prayer.
[^813]: Wednesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Stichera prosomoia in Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы / Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες).
[^814]: Wednesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Prosomoion in Tone 6, Podoben: "Today He watches" (Дне́сь бди́тъ / Σήμερον γρηγορεῖ).
[^815]: Wednesday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Doxasticon in Tone 8, combining Isaiah 53:2–5 with Simeon's sword prophecy (Luke 2:35).
[^816]: Wednesday Vespers First Prokeimenon, Psalm 71:18, 1 (LXX), Tone 4.
[^817]: Wednesday Vespers First Paremia from Genesis 9:18–10:1, the genealogy of Noah's sons after the deluge, Noah's vineyard and drunkenness, the filial piety of Shem and Japhs, and the curse upon Canaan.
[^818]: Wednesday Vespers Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 72:28, 1 (LXX), Tone 4.
[^819]: Wednesday Vespers Second Paremia from Proverbs 12:23–13:9, contrasting the discernment, life, and enduring light of the righteous with the deception, captivity, and extinguished darkness of the wicked.
[^820]: Pivotal liturgical rubric of the Great Fast: beginning on Mid-Lent Wednesday at the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, the second, intensified set of petitions for those preparing for Holy Baptism at Pascha (є҆ли́цы ко просвѣще́нїю, пристꙋпи́те / As many as are preparing for illumination, draw near) is added after the ordinary litany of the catechumens.
[^821]: Thursday Matins opening: the Trinity Hymns (Triadica) of the current tone chanted during the Great Fast in place of "God is the Lord".
[^822]: Thursday Matins Sessional Hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй / Χορὸς Ἀγγελικὸς).
[^823]: Thursday Matins Sessional Hymn after the third stichology by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 7, Podoben: "Lord, we are [Thy people]" (Гдⷭ҇и, мы̀ є҆смы̀ / Κύριε, ἡμεῖς ἐσμεν).
[^824]: Tone 7 automelon from Psalm 78:13 and 99:3 (LXX): "We are Thy people, and the sheep of Thy pasture."
[^825]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 1: "На бж҃е́ственнѣй стра́жи" (On divine watch / Ἐπὶ τῆς θείας φυλακῆς), based on Habakkuk 2:1 (LXX).
[^826]: Classical typological image from Exodus 17:11–12 of Moses with arms outstretched on the hill during the battle with Amalek, prefiguring the victory of the Cross over demons.
[^827]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 7: "Пло́тское прише́ствїе твоѐ Хрⷭ҇тѐ" (Thy bodily coming, O Christ), from Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX), quoting the "horn of our salvation" from Psalm 17:3 / Luke 1:69.
[^828]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 1: "Сѐй нарече́нный" (This chosen [and holy day]), Paschal irmologic incipit applied to the veneration of the Cross.
[^829]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 7: "Е҆ди́наго безначя́льнаго" (The only unoriginate King of glory), based on the Canticle of the Three Youths in Daniel 3 (LXX); the troparion addresses the current Fourth Week of Lent as the "Cross-bearing week".
[^830]: Poetic metaphor portraying the Twelve Apostles as a twelve-stringed harp (двоєнадесятострꙋ́нная цѣвни́це) sounding forth the wisdom of the Gospel.
[^831]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph in Tone 1: "Свѣти́ся, свѣти́ся" (Shine, shine / Φωτίζου, φωτίζου), based on Isaiah 60:1 (LXX).
[^832]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite in Tone 7: "Тя̀ пречи́стꙋю Мт҃рь и҆ Дв҃ꙋ" (Thee the most pure Mother and Virgin / Τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν).
[^833]: Typological reference to Exodus 15:23–25, where Moses cast the tree into the bitter waters of Marah to make them sweet.
[^834]: Troparion to the Apostles, "Ѻ҆́гненный ѕвѣ́къ" (fiery sound), alluding to the descent of the Holy Spirit at Pentecost as a rushing mighty wind and fire (Acts 2:2–3).
[^835]: Thursday Matins Aposticha Idiomelon in Tone 7, meditating on the Publican's repentance.
[^836]: Paraphrasing Christ's dictum in the Parable of the Publican and the Pharisee (Luke 18:14): "For every one that exalts himself shall be abased; and he that humbls himself shall be exalted."
[^837]: Thursday Sixth Hour Prokeimenon, Psalm 73:12, 1 (LXX), Tone 4: "God is our King before the ages; He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth."
[^838]: Thursday Sixth Hour Reading from Isaiah 28:14–22, the Lord overthrowing the false covenant with death and laying in Zion the sure foundation stone.
[^839]: Messianic prophecy of the precious cornerstone (Isa. 28:16) quoted Christologically in Romans 9:33 and 1 Peter 2:6.
[^840]: Thursday Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 74:10, 2 (LXX), Tone 4.
[^841]: Thursday Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Stichera prosomoia in Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе / Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν).
[^842]: Tone 4 sticheron prosomoion by St. Theodore the Studite, Podoben: "Of the spiritual adamant" (Оу҆́мнагѡ а҆дама́нта / Τοῦ νοητοῦ ἀδάμαντος), celebrating the veneration of the Precious Cross and contemplating the Passion instruments: the Crucifixion, lance, sponge, and reed.
[^843]: Thursday Vespers First Prokeimenon, Psalm 75:12, 2 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ ѻ҃е), Tone 8: "Make your vows, and pay them unto the Lord our God."
[^844]: Thursday Vespers First Paremia from Genesis 10:32–11:9, the post-diluvian division of nations, the journey to the plain of Shinar, the building of the city and Tower of Babel, and the confusion of tongues by the Lord.
[^845]: Thursday Vespers Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 76:2, 3 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ ѻ҃ѕ), Tone 8: "With my voice have I cried unto the Lord, with my voice unto God, and He gave ear unto me."
[^846]: Thursday Vespers Second Paremia from Proverbs 13:20–14:6a (Slavonic chapters і҃г–д҃і), on walking with the wise, the inheritance of the righteous, filial discipline, the wise woman building her house, and the pride of fools.
[^847]: Thursday Vespers Aposticha Idiomelon in Tone 6, exhorting the soul to learn humility from the Lord's Crucifixion rather than boasting like the proud Pharisee.
[^848]: Thursday Vespers Stavrotheotokion in Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ / Τριήμερος ἀνέστης), the Virgin's lament beholding the Giver of Life suspended upon the Tree.
[^849]: Friday Matins of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast: beginning with the Trinity Hymns (Triadica) of the Tone of the week instead of "God is the Lord", followed by the Cross Sessional Hymns from the Octoechos after the first stichology.
[^850]: Friday Matins Sessional Hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ / Ὁ ὑψωθεὶς ἐν τῷ σταυρῷ), honoring the mid-point of the Fast sanctified by the Precious Cross.
[^851]: Friday Matins Stavrotheotokion after the second stichology, the Virgin weeping at the Cross: "How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by angels, now voluntarily crucified by lawless men, O Savior?"
[^852]: Friday Matins Sessional Hymn after the third stichology, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ / Ταχὺ προκατάлаβε), beholding the Cross as the light of souls and glorifying Christ's voluntary elevation upon it.
[^853]: The Canon of the Precious Cross chanted at Friday Matins with the acrostic: "Покланѧ́емое всѝ пое́мъ дре́во" (We all sing of the Tree that is worshipped), while the canon of the Menaion is relegated to Compline.
[^854]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на" (The chariot-driver Pharaoh / Ἄρματηλάτην Φαραώ), based on the Song of Moses in Exodus 15:1–19.
[^855]: Typological interpretation of Moses' rod dividing the Red Sea (Exod. 14:16, 21) as a figure of the victory of the Cross over the tempestuous waters of sin.
[^856]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀" (Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens / Ὁ στερεώσας κατ' ἀρχάς), based on 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:2.
[^857]: Friday Matins Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 in Tone 6: "То́кмѡ водрꙋзи́сѧ дре́во" (No sooner was the Tree of Thy Cross planted, O Christ, than the foundations of death were shaken, O Lord).
[^858]: Friday Matins Stavrotheotokion after Ode 3, quoting Psalm 98:5 and 131:7 (LXX): "Behold, we worship at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord."
[^859]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Ты́ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость" (Thou art my strength / Σύ μου ἰσχύς, Κύриε), based on the Canticle of Habakkuk 3 (LXX).
[^860]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ" (Why hast Thou cast me off / Ἵνα τί με ἀπώσω), based on Isaiah 26:9–20.
[^861]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 4: "Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́чєскаѧ" (All things were astonished / Ἐξέστη τὰ σύμπαντα), based on the Song of the Three Youths in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^862]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 4: "И҆зъ но́щи оу҆́треннююще" (Early in the morning from the night / Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζοντες), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^863]: Classical patristic and hymnographic typological tradition based on Isaiah 60:13 (LXX), interpreting the cedar, pine, and cypress as the threefold wood composition of Christ's Holy Cross.
[^864]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ Сп҃се" (Cleanse me, O Savior / Ἱλάσθητί μοι Σωτήр), based on the Canticle of Jonah 2 (LXX).
[^865]: Friday Kontakion of the Precious Cross in Tone 7: "Не ктомꙋ̀ пла́менное ѻ҆рꙋ́жїе" (No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden), celebrating the abolition of the sting of death (1 Cor. 15:55) and the opening of Paradise to the thief (Luke 23:43).
[^866]: Friday Ikos of the Precious Cross, dramatically presenting Hades crying out to his infernal powers as Pilate erects three crosses on Golgotha, pierced by the wooden spear and compelled to disgorge Adam and all his lineage.
[^867]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Бж҃їѧ снизхожде́нїа" (The condescension of God / Θεοῦ συγκατάβασιν), based on the Prayer of Azariah in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^868]: Typological allusion to Exodus 15:23–25, where Moses sweetened the bitter waters of Marah with the tree, prefiguring the Cross sweetening the bitterness of the passions during the Fast.
[^869]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Седмери́цею пе́щь" (Sevenfold did the tyrant burn the furnace / Ἑπταπλασίως κάμινον), based on the Song of the Three Youths in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^870]: Typological figure from 4 Kingdoms (2 Kings) 6:5–7, where the Prophet Elisha cast a stick of wood into the Jordan and caused the submerged iron axe-head to float, prefiguring the Cross lifting fallen humanity from the depths of deception.
[^871]: Typological allusion to Genesis 48:13–14, where the Patriarch Jacob crossed his hands to bless Ephraim and Manasseh, prefiguring the form and power of the Holy Cross.
[^872]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph, Tone 4: "Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ" (The pious youths in the furnace / Παῖδας εὐαγεῖς ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ), based on Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^873]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 4: "Всѧ̑ дѣла̀ Бж҃їѧ" (All the works of God / Πάντα τὰ ἔργα), based on the Benedicite canticle in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^874]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6: "Оу҆жасе́сѧ" (Every ear was astonished at the ineffable condescension of God / Ἐξέστη ἐπὶ τούτῳ ὁ οὐρανός), based on Jeremiah 2:12.
[^875]: Poetic portrayal of the Cross as physically circumscribed in location yet encompassed by noetic angelic powers and illuminating all the fulness of the universe with divine uncreated light.
[^876]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the first Triode by Lord Joseph, Tone 4: "Всѧ́къ земноро́дный" (Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy / Ἅπας γηγενής), based on the Magnificat in Luke 1:46–55.
[^877]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the second Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 4: "Безсѣ́менное Ржⷭ҇тво̀" (The seedless conception / Ἄσπορος σύλληψις), based on the Magnificat.
[^878]: Friday Matins Aposticha Idiomelon in Tone 4: "И҆́стины и҆спыта́телю" (O Searcher of truth and Knower of secret things, O Lord), meditating on the Parable of the Publican and the Pharisee (Luke 18:9–14).
[^879]: Friday Matins Aposticha Martyrikon in Tone 4: "Кто̀ не оу҆жаса́етсѧ зрѧ̀" (Who is not astonished beholding the good fight wherein ye contended, O holy martyrs?).
[^880]: Friday Matins Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8, a solemn meditation upon Christ's Crucifixion: the Master of creation and Lord of glory crowned with thorns, clothed in a mock purple robe, struck by clay hands, and enduring spitting and wounds to deliver the world from deception.
[^881]: Friday Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon, Psalm 77:38, 1 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ ѻ҃з), Tone 6: "And He is compassionate, and will forgive their sins." Verse: "Give ear, O My people, unto My law."
[^882]: Friday Sixth Hour Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah 29:13–23, rebuking hypocritical lip-service, overturning the counsel of the worldly-wise, promising the deaf to hear the words of the book, and announcing Jacob's redemption.
[^883]: Friday Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 78:9, 1 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ ѻ҃и), Tone 5: "Help us, O God our Savior." Verse: "O God, the heathen are come into Thine inheritance."
[^884]: Liturgical rubric for the conclusion of the Mid-Lent veneration of the Precious Cross at the Sixth Hour on Friday of the Fourth Week: following the dismissal of the Hours and the final Trisagion, the stichera in Tone 2 "Come, ye faithful, let us bow down before the lifegiving Tree" are sung (cross-referencing printed folio 293 = physical leaf p297), after which the Precious Cross is reverently elevated from the tetrapod and returned to the skeuophylakion (vestry / sacristy).
[^885]: Friday Vespers Idiomelon in Tone 7: "Having enslaved the dignity of my soul to the passions", invoking the penitence of the Publican (Luke 18:13).
[^886]: Rubric for the Friday Vespers Doxasticon: St. John of Damascus's Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (hymn for the departed) from the Octoechos, introducing the commemoration of all the departed on the Fourth Saturday of the Great Fast.
[^887]: Friday Vespers First Prokeimenon, Psalm 79:2, 3 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ ѻ҃ѳ), Tone 4: "Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, Thou that leadest Joseph like a sheep."
[^888]: Friday Vespers First Paremia from Genesis 12:1–7, recounting the call of Abram, God's promise to make of him a great nation, his departure from Haran to Canaan, and his building of an altar at the oak of Shechem.
[^889]: Friday Vespers Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 80:2, 3 (LXX; Slavonic ѱало́мъ п҃), Tone 4: "Rejoice in God our Helper."
[^890]: Friday Vespers Second Paremia from Proverbs 14:15–26 (Slavonic chapter д҃і), on the simple and the prudent, the evil stumbling before the righteous, mercy to the poor, diligent labor, and the fear of the Lord.
[^891]: Saturday Matins Troparia in Tone 2: the universal Byzantine Saturday troparia for all saints, martyrs, and the departed: "Апостоли, мученицы и пророцы" (Apostles, martyrs, and prophets), "Помяни, Господи, яко благ" (Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, Thy servants), and "Мати Святая" (Holy Mother of the ineffable Light).
[^892]: The Hymns for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (funeral) stichology of Kathisma 17 (the Amomos / Psalm 118, "Blessed are the blameless in the way"), divided into two staseis with the Evlogitaria for the departed ("The choir of the saints has found the fountain of life"), followed by the small litany for the departed and the Tone 5 Sessional Hymn "Give rest with the righteous, O our Savior".
[^893]: The Saturday of the Fourth Week of the Great Fast is appointed as a General Commemoration of the Departed (Parental Memorial Saturday), with dual Four-Ode Canons (Tetraodia) composed by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer and St. Theodore the Studite covering Odes 6, 7, 8, and 9.
[^894]: Acrostic of Lord Joseph's Tetraodion: "Пѣ́нь на мч҃нки" (A song for the martyrs).
[^895]: Ode 6 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Пꙋчи́ною жите́йскою" (Tossed by the sea of this life / Ἐν πελάγει τοῦ βίου), based on the Canticle of Jonah 2 (LXX).
[^896]: Ode 6 Heirmos of St. Theodore the Studite's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Бꙋ́рею грѣхо́вною" (Submerged by the tempest of sin / Τῷ σάλῳ τῶν ἁμαρτιῶν), based on the Canticle of Jonah 2 (LXX).
[^897]: The solemn Funeral Kontakion in Tone 8: "Со святыми упокой, Христе, души раб Твоих" (With the saints give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is no sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting).
[^898]: The Funeral Ikos: "Сам един еси безсмертный" (Thou alone art immortal, Who hast created and fashioned man), cross-referencing printed folio 17 = physical leaf p21, where the full text is printed at the Meatfare Saturday memorial service.
[^899]: Ode 7 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Ѻ҃́бразꙋ злато́мꙋ" (Unto the golden image on the plain of Dura / Τῇ εἰκόνι τῇ χρυσῇ), based on the Song of the Three Youths in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^900]: Ode 7 Heirmos of St. Theodore the Studite's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Глаго́лавый на горѣ̀" (He Who spoke on the mountain with Moses / Ὁ λαλήσας ἐν ὄρει), based on Exodus 3 and Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^901]: Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Во ѻ҃́гни́ пла́меннѣмъ" (In the fiery flame / Ἐν πυρὶ φλογώδει), based on the Song of the Three Youths in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^902]: Ode 8 Heirmos of St. Theodore the Studite's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Землѧ̀, и҆ всѧ̑ ꙗ҆́же на не́й" (The earth, and all that is therein / Γῆ καὶ πάντα τὰ ἐν αὐτῇ), based on the Benedicite canticle in Daniel 3 (LXX).
[^903]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Ꙗ҆́кѡ сотвори́ мнѣ̀ вели́чїе Си́льный" (For He that is mighty has done to me great things / Ὅти ἐποίησέ μοι μεγαлеῖα ὁ δυνατός), based on the Magnificat in Luke 1:49.
[^904]: Ode 9 Heirmos of St. Theodore the Studite's Tetraodion, Tone 4: "Велича́емъ всѝ" (We all magnify Thy love for mankind / Μεγαλύномен πάντες), based on the Magnificat in Luke 1:46–55.
[^905]: Rubric for the Saturday Matins Exapostilaria: cross-referencing printed folio 107 = physical leaf p111 (Second Saturday of the Great Fast), where the universal Saturday Exapostilaria are written: "Thou Who hast adorned the heaven with stars" (О небеси звѣзды) and the martyria/Hymns for the Departed (Поко́инъ) exapostilaria.
[^906]: Saturday Matins Aposticha prosomoia by St. Theophanes Graptos (the Branded), Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ преславнагѡ чудесе).
[^907]: Saturday Liturgy First Prokeimenon, Tone 8: "Their souls shall dwell among good things" (Psalm 24:13 LXX).
[^908]: Saturday Liturgy First Epistle reading for the day: Hebrews 6:9–12 (Pericope 390), on inheriting the promises through faith and patience.
[^909]: Saturday Liturgy Second Epistle reading for the departed: 1 Corinthians 15:58–16:3 (Pericope 163), on steadfastness in the Lord and the resurrection of the dead.
[^910]: Saturday Liturgy Alleluia for the departed, Tone 2: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord" (Psalm 64:5 LXX).
[^911]: Saturday Liturgy First Gospel reading for the day: Mark 7:31–37 (Pericope 31), recounting Christ's healing of the deaf and mute man in the Decapolis ("Ephphatha").
[^912]: Saturday Liturgy Second Gospel reading for the departed: John 5:24–30 (Pericope 16), on hearing the voice of the Son of God, passing from death unto life, and the resurrection of the dead.
[^913]: Saturday Liturgy First Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn): Psalm 32:1 (LXX), "Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous; praise is meet for the upright."
[^914]: Saturday Liturgy Second Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn) for the departed: Psalm 64:5 and 101:13 (LXX), "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord, and their memorial is unto generation and generation. Alleluia."
[^915]: Liturgical rubric on the historical development of the Fourth Sunday of Lent: the older Stauropegion and Sabbaite Typikon prescribes 3 Resurrection, 4 Anatolian (Eastern), and 3 Menaion stichera, noting that "later typika" (the received Studite/Sabbaite synthesis) appointed the full proper office of St. John Climacus (author of *The Ladder of Divine Ascent*) to be sung alongside the Resurrectional hymnography.
[^916]: Vespers Prosomoia for St. John Climacus, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ / Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος), meditating on St. John's continuous prayer (Ps. 149:6 LXX), compunctionate tears, and ascetic discipline on Mount Sinai.
[^917]: Vespers Doxasticon for St. John Climacus in Tone 5, based on the Gospel commands of renunciation (Matt. 16:24, Luke 14:26) and the quest for heavenly rest.
[^918]: Vespers Aposticha Doxasticon for St. John Climacus in Tone 2, quoting Psalm 91:13–14 (LXX): "The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon, planted in the house of the Lord."
[^919]: Troparion (Troparion) of St. John Climacus in Tone 1: "Пꙋсты́нный жи́тель" (A dweller of the wilderness), the classic monastic Troparion celebrating St. John's ascetic life on Mount Sinai.
[^920]: Matins canon rule for the Fourth Sunday of Lent: Resurrectional canon with Theotokion from the Octoechos to 6, followed by the Triodion penitential canon (by St. Theophanes Graptos) to 4, and the Canon of St. John Climacus to 4.
[^921]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Triodion Canon, Tone 5: "Спаси́телю Бг҃ꙋ" (Unto God the Savior Who led the people through the sea / Τῷ Σωτῆρι Θεῷ), based on Exodus 15. The canon systematically interprets the Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–37) as an allegory of the human soul stripped, beaten, and wounded by passions and sinful demons, awaiting Christ the true Physician.
[^922]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of St. John Climacus, Tone 8: "Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ" (Having traversed the water as dry land / Ὑγρὰν διοδεύσας ὡсеὶ ξηράν), based on Exodus 14.
[^923]: The standard Sunday Katavasia of the Theotokos throughout Great Lent (except when Cross Katavasiai are prescribed): "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit" (Отверзу уста моя / Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου), Ode 1 of the Canon of the Akathist.
[^924]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Triodion Canon, Tone 5: "Оу҆тверди́ ны Бж҃е" (Establish us, O God our Savior / Στερέωσον ἡμᾶς ὁ Θεὸς ὁ Σωτὴρ ἡμῶν), based on 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:2.
[^925]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of St. John Climacus, Tone 8: "Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе" (Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee / Σὺ εἶ τὸ στερέωμα), based on Psalm 17:3 (LXX).
[^926]: Matins Sessional Hymn of the Precious Cross in Tone 5, Podoben: "Strange wonder" (Стра́нное чꙋ́до / Τὸ ξένον θαῦма), retaining the mid-Lenten Cross veneration theme on the Fourth Sunday of Lent.
[^927]: Matins Sessional Hymn for St. John Climacus, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ / Ὁ ὑψωθεὶς ἐν τῷ σταυρῷ), celebrating his ascent via the ladder of virtues unto the divine vision of God (*theoria*).
[^928]: Sessional Theotokion, Tone 4 (Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross"), completing the hymn begun on Folio 316v (Physical Leaf p320).
[^929]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Fourth Sunday (Habakkuk biblical canticle), Tone 4: 'I have heard, O Lord, Thy report' (Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭ҇и слꙋ́хъ тво́й / Εἰσακήκοα Κύριε τὴν ἀκοήν σου).
[^930]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon to our Venerable Father John Climacus, Tone 8: 'I have heard, O Lord' (Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭ҇и / Εἰσακήκοα Κύριε).
[^931]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Fourth Sunday (Isaiah biblical canticle), Tone 4: 'Rising early at the dawn, we cry unto Thee' (Оу҆́треннююще вопїе́мъ тѝ / Ὀρθρίζοντες βοῶμέν σοι).
[^932]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon to our Venerable Father John Climacus, Tone 8: 'Why hast Thou cast me off' (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ / Ἵνα τί με ἀπώσω).
[^933]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Fourth Sunday (Jonah biblical canticle), Tone 4: 'The abyss encompassed me' (Ѡ҆би́де мѧ̀ бе́здна / Ἄβυσσος ἐκύκλωσέ με).
[^934]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon to our Venerable Father John Climacus, Tone 8: 'Cleanse me, O Savior' (Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ Сп҃се / Ἱλάσθητί μοι Σωτήρ).
[^935]: Kontakion of our Venerable Father John Climacus, Tone 4 (Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" / Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь / Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον).
[^936]: Historical Synaxarion of the Fourth Sunday of the Great Fast by Nicephorus Callistus Xanthopoulos (14th c.), commemorating our Venerable Father John, author of the Ladder of Divine Ascent (Climacus), hegumen of Mount Sinai (d. ca. 603/649). The metrical iambic distich records his death on the thirtieth day of March.
[^937]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Fourth Sunday (Three Holy Youths canticle), Tone 4: 'The all-exalted God of the fathers' (Превозноси́мый Ѻ҆ц҃е́въ / Ὁ ὑπερύψωστος τῶν Πατέρων Κύριος).

[^939]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Fourth Sunday, Tone 4: 'Unto Thee, the Creator of all' (Тебѣ̀ Вседѣ́телю / Σοὶ τῷ Παντουργῷ).

The Typikon directs that the kathismata be read without omission despite the extraordinary length of the Great Canon and the Life of St. Mary of Egypt (see fol. 343 / Physical Leaf p347).
[^963]: Monday evening of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast (Vespers).
[^964]: In the Great Fast, Kathisma 10 of the Psalter (Psalms 70–76 [LXX 70–76]) is chanted at Monday Vespers.

[^981]: Ode 2 Heirmos of the Tuesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: 'Give ear, ye people' (Внемли́те лю́дїе / Προσέχετε λαοί).

[^983]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Tuesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: 'Of old Him Who bedewed' (Дре́вле ѡ҆роси́вшаго / Τὸν πάλαι δροσίσαντα).
[^984]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Tuesday Matins Triode by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 3: 'Thee, the unconsumed' (Тебѐ неѡпали́мꙋю / Σὲ τὴν ἀκατάφλεκτον).
[^985]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Tuesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: 'Supernaturally in the flesh' (Преесте́ственнѡ пло́тїю / Ὑπερφυῶς σαρκί).

[^1009]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 8: "Of the heavenly vault" (Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га / Οὐρανίας ἁψῖδος).
[^1010]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: "My barren mind" (Непло́дствовавшїй мо́й ᲂу҆́мъ / Στειρωθέντα μου τὸν νοῦν).
[^1011]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 8: "Sevenfold the furnace" (Седмери́цею пе́щь / Ἑπταπλασίως κάμινον).
[^1012]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: "With voices angelic" (Гла́сы а҆́гг҃льскими / Ἀγγελικαῖς φωναῖς).
[^1013]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 8: "Heaven was dismayed at this" (Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о / Ἐξέστη ἐπὶ τούτῳ ὁ οὐρανός).
[^1014]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Wednesday Matins Triode by St. Theodore the Studite, Tone 2: "Thy virginity, O Theotokos" (Дѣ́вство твоѐ Бцⷣе / Τὴν παρθενίαν σου Θεοτόκε).
[^1015]: Wednesday Matins Aposticha Idiomelon, Tone 3, drawing upon the Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–37).

[^1017]: Martyrikon of Tone 8.
